Number Category Short description Article body KB0014345 Academics Academics - REGIS (Rutgers Electronic Grading and Information System) "

The Rutgers Electronic Grading and Information System, also known as REGIS, is the online grading system used by faculty to submit grades.

Accessing Regis

To access REGIS, the user must have a Faculty, Staff, or Guest role; users will sign in to the system with their NetID and password. If users cannot view Class Rosters, verify their role in OpenRegistry. If they have an active Faculty, Staff, or Guest role, you should refer the user to their department chairs, as access has to be granted from the chair.

Task Specific Requests

Step-by-step documentation can be found on the REGIS Quick Reference Guide for calls regarding the following actions:

Viewing a roster
Adding students to a roster
Download and upload a roster
Granting/revoking privileges
Submitting warnings or grades
Downloading Rosters as Spreadsheets

REGIS users can download spreadsheets of their rosters. If someone reports problems with the downloaded spreadsheet (for example, the file is grayed out or blank), suggest the caller add https://sims.rutgers.edu as a trusted site in their browser settings and try again.

" KB0014167 Academics Academics - Transcripts & Grades "

Enrolled Students

Enrolled students (with a source of record in SRDB) can view their grades on their unofficial transcript through the Unofficial Transcripts and Grades site. Unofficial transcripts may be printed by selecting Print Full Transcript in the upper right-hand corner.

Enrolled students (with a source of record in SRDB) can view their grades through myRutgers Portal.

To view your grades:

Login to MyRutgers and navigate to Dashboard.

Navigate to My Grades.

To view your grades, select the desired year in the dropdown menu on the upper left-hand corner of the widget.

Alumni or Former or Non-enrolled Students

Alumni or Former students can obtain their transcripts at the Transcript/Enrollment Certification & Term Grade Report System site.

Alumni or Former students can also obtain their transcripts by contacting the Registrar's Office at 848-445-4636 for assistance.

Students who are not registered for classes during the semester cannot view their transcripts or grades online.

RBHS Students

Current RBHS students (with a source of record in Banner) can view their transcripts through the Banner app in the myRutgers Portal.

Note: RBHS students may view their unofficial transcripts through Banner, however, they cannot print them.

To obtain a printed copy, they should contact their respective registrar.

Contact information for RBHS registrars.

For reference: Tutorial: How To View Your Unofficial Transcript.

" KB0014103 Admissions Admissions - Class Registration "

Non-RBHS Students

Students can use their NetIDs and the case-sensitive passwords associated with those accounts to use the Web-based class scheduling system (WebReg). If they are trying to register for classes, they can also authenticate with their RUID numbers and PACs, usually their birth months and day (e.g., January 2 would be 0102). If they do not remember their PAC or wish to reset it, they can do so with their NetID/password or contact OneStop for assistance.

If the user tries to log into WebReg too many times unsuccessfully, they will be locked out of the system. The lock will be removed automatically in 24 hours. If they would like the lock removed sooner, they may contact OneStop for assistance and request it is removed.

If the user is a returning student interested in registering for classes for the Fall or Spring semester and does not have a role, they must contact their Dean to reinstate their role before they can register for classes.

During busy times (Fall, Spring, and Summer registration periods), users may find that the page will not load or it seems to load continuously for long periods. This is just a result of many users trying to access the system simultaneously. If a user calls up with this issue, tell them to wait and try again, explaining that many users are trying to access the page simultaneously. Additionally, users may find that after registering for their classes, the classes do not show up on their registered courses or under view/print schedule. Note that the courses should appear if the user logs out of webreg and logs back in.

RBHS Students

RBHS Student do not use WebReg to register for classes. They log in via the myRutgers portal and use the PIN provided to them by their advisor (who receives the PIN from the office of enrollment) to register for classes. If a user is unable to use their PIN and basic troubleshooting is unable to resolve the issue, direct the user to their advisor and/or their registrar.

" KB0014104 Admissions Admissions - College to College Transfers "

College-to-college (or school-to-school) transfers refer to currently enrolled Rutgers undergraduate students who wish to switch from one school or college within Rutgers University. This is separate from transferring between a non-Rutgers institution into Rutgers or transferring from a Rutgers institution into a non-Rutgers institution.


The following eligibility includes:

Transferring between Camden, Newark, or New Brunswick.
Transferring from one of the professional schools to a liberal arts school or from a liberal arts school to a professional school.

The application website is: School-to-School Transfer Application.

More information regarding school-to-school transfers can be located at the Undergraduate Transfer Portal.

" KB0014105 Admissions Admissions - Degree Navigator "

Access Overview
Undergraduate Students and Advisors
Degree Navigator is available exclusively for undergraduate students and Degree Navigator Advisors.
Graduate students do not have access to Degree Navigator.
Website Usage:
Both students and Degree Navigator Advisors use the same Degree Navigator website.
The platform is consistent across New Brunswick, Camden, and Newark campuses.
Eligibility and Access Instructions
Registered Students
Students currently registered for classes or those who have registered within the past two years may access Degree Navigator by logging in with their NetID and password.
Only degree-seeking students can view their past classes as applied to a degree program. Non-matriculating students can see their completed classes, but unless the class is from the current semester, it will not appear under a chosen degree.
Faculty Access
Faculty with Degree Navigator Advisor access must log in via the main Degree Navigator page by navigating to Advisor Login.
Advisors should navigate to nbdn.rutgers.edu and select Advisor Login, rather than using dn.rutgers.edu.
Data Refresh and Updates
Data Updates
Degree Navigator data refreshes every Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, and Saturday at approximately 4:00 AM.
If a grade update is not reflected, confirm whether enough time has passed since the last system refresh.
Grade Change Alerts
Students receive grade change email alerts within 24 hours but these updates refer to online transcripts, not Degree Navigator.
Troubleshooting and Support
Please note: Degree Navigator is unavailable from 8:30am-8:45am every day for system maintenance.
If a user cannot access the site during this time, please have them refresh the page after the hours listed have passed and see if they are still experiencing the issue.
Faculty Issues
Faculty without current Degree Navigator Advisor access or those unable to view specific classes should contact Degree Navigator support directly.
Contact information and additional resources are available on the Degree Navigator website.

" KB0014106 Admissions Admissions - Emergency Contact Information & Housing Site "

In order for someone with an A-number in OpenRegistry to access their Emergency Contact information or Housing Assignment information via the Residence Life Portal, the Residence Life Assignments office must be contacted for assistance with properly updating the ResLife Database to reflect an RBHS affiliation for such a person.

Housing Application process:

They rely on LDAP to allow the student to log in to the housing site.
Housing maintains their own database to determine the type of student logging in; they have their own codes for each type of student (new transfer, incoming freshman, etc.), which then determines if the student is eligible to apply for housing.
As far as RBHS (phi=y) students are concerned, RBHS relies on A-numbers as their primary identifier.
Housing is currently working on getting those numbers brought over, but as it currently stands, there is no process in place that automatically dumps that information. It is a MANUAL process, either manually dumped or manually built.
This means RBHS students (phi=y) will not be able to apply for housing until housing has their A-number in place.

First-year/admitted students must fill out their Emergency Contact information on the enrollment pathway. There are two separate links to update that information: one for Admissions and the other for Residential Life. Students with an Admit Coming role in OpenRegistry rather than a Student role will only be able to use the Residential Life link under the Housing and Dining section to update this information.

Please be aware that it takes 3-5 days AFTER the user has paid their admissions fee to log into the housing/dining website. If it has been over five days and they still can't log in, refer them to ResLife Assignments. DO NOT direct the user to Housing.

Method 1

Below are the general steps of the first-year housing application process, where this link is located:

Students will visit myRutgers and select the housing and dining link.
Students are then informed that they *must* sign up for their Rutgers NetID before proceeding to the housing link (they cannot log in with their RUID and PIN).
After giving this information, the Enrollment Pathway takes the student to the Housing Application website.
Students will log into the site and be brought to the Student Home Page.
At this point, they will see the housing application link at the bottom of the page.
Important: The first time they click on the link, it informs them they *must* add an emergency contact before proceeding.
Students will then select the My Emergency Contacts link on the left side of the screen.
Once they add an emergency contact, they will be directed back to the Student Home Page where they can now fill out the application.
Method 2

To update the Emergency Contact Information:

Log in to myRutgers and navigate to the Apps tab.
Utilize the Search Apps tool in the upper right-hand corner.
Search and select Emergency Contacts.
You will be redirected to an external page.

If the user continues to have a problem, refer them to the following:
Residence Life Assignments Office
Davidson Hall 90 Davidson Road, Piscataway, NJ 08854
Phone: 848-445-0750

" KB0014380 Admissions Admissions - Self-Reported Academic Record (SRAR) Site "

Rutgers University applicants can use the Self-Reported Academic Record (SRAR) site to submit additional academic records to supplement their applications.

Applicants can use the tool to self-report coursework, grades, GPA, and other academic information. High school seniors are required to complete the SRAR, while high school graduates and transfer students are not.

Users who have already completed the SRAR with another participating college or university may use their existing SRAR account to log into the Rutgers SRAR. Users without an existing SRAR account should click Create SRAR Account and follow the instructions on screen. Users should use the same email address they used or plan to use to complete the Rutgers Application. Users who will be utilizing this tool will not have a Rutgers NetID and password, and will not be in our system yet as they are only applying to the University.

If the user forgot their password, they can select Forgot Password? that allows them to retrieve the password with their name and relevant email address.

Beyond that, there is not much we can do to assist the user with logging in to the site as this is not a tool or database that we run. They can contact University Undergraduate Admissions for further assistance at 732-445-3777.

" KB0018579 AI AI - ChatGPT "

Table of Contents
Who can purchase a ChatGPT Edu License?
What are the benefits of ChatGPT Edu?
Why must users switch to the Rutgers ChatGPT Edu license?
FAQ
What if a department previously purchased ChatGPT licenses?
Should a department purchase subscriptions for ChatGPT?
What do departments need to do?
How will departments be charged?
Does the ChatGPT Edu license include access to Sora?
Can I purchase ChatGPT via a term subscription?
Can I migrate or merge my ChatGPT Free or Plus workspace over to my ChatGPT Edu subscription?
Can I license ChatGPT Pro or the ChatGPT API through the Software Portal?
What support is available for ChatGPT Edu licenses?

ChatGPT Edu licenses are available on the Software Portal. This version of the AI assistant from OpenAI includes higher message limits than the free version and allows users to build and share custom GPTs (the large language models developed by OpenAI). Using ChatGPT Edu also prevents Rutgers data and conversations from being used to train the company s AI models.

Any user with an individual or departmental ChatGPT license should switch to the ChatGPT Edu license on the Software Portal.

Who can purchase a ChatGPT Edu License?

ChatGPT Edu subscriptions must be purchased by a faculty or staff member. Subscriptions can be assigned to faculty, staff, faculty emeriti, students, and sponsored guests.

What are the benefits of ChatGPT Edu?

Purchasing a subscription through the Rutgers Software Portal provides several benefits:

Rutgers data is not used to train OpenAI models.
You can create and share GPTs (the large language models developed by OpenAI).
Prioritized support is available for ChatGPT Edu by contacting them as described in this OpenAI article. Users can also consult the OpenAI support page.
Coordinated management, data retention, and user authentication are provided for the software.
Why must users switch to the Rutgers ChatGPT Edu license?

Using individual accounts for institutional work presents the following risks:

Data Privacy: Rutgers has signed a contract with OpenAI, the parent company of ChatGPT. Individual accounts are not included in our agreements to ensure data protection for Rutgers information. Data processed in personal accounts may be used to train future AI models.
Compliance: Personal accounts may violate internal or contractual data handling standards at Rutgers.
Sensitive Information: Individual accounts limit Rutgers ability to safeguard sensitive work.
FAQ
What if a department previously purchased ChatGPT licenses?

Some units at Rutgers have previously purchased ChatGPT Plus accounts. Individuals identified with these accounts were sent a message with instructions to switch to a Rutgers-managed ChatGPT Edu account. This is essential due to the data protection and other features available with ChatGPT Edu.

Should a department purchase subscriptions for ChatGPT?

Unless a unit has faculty, researchers, or others with an immediate need for ChatGPT Edu, we recommend using the existing tools provided by Rutgers before using ChatGPT: Google Gemini and Microsoft Copilot.

What do departments need to do?

Sign up for ChatGPT Edu by completing the subscription process in the University Software Portal. The department's business manager must set up a payment account to finalize the transaction.

Once the new Edu account is provisioned, users can migrate their data from a free or Plus workspace into the Rutgers-managed ChatGPT Edu account.

Refrain from using personal accounts for any university-related activity.

How will departments be charged?

Usage under the Rutgers ChatGPT license will be centrally billed and reconciled monthly through internal chargebacks to departments.

Users do not need to pay out-of-pocket or submit reimbursements.

Usage reports are available upon request to help departments track activity and cost.

Does the ChatGPT Edu license include access to Sora?

No. ChatGPT Free, Enterprise, and Edu accounts are not eligible for Sora access.

Can I purchase ChatGPT via a term subscription?

No, only Monthly Subscriptions are available. Monthly subscriptions can only be purchased if you have been given access to a departmental payment account by the owner of that account. The payment accounts are tied directly to a General Ledger (GL) code for the department and must be set up by the department's Director/Business Manager.

Payment cannot be made with a purchase order or credit card.

Can I migrate or merge my ChatGPT Free or Plus workspace over to my ChatGPT Edu subscription?

Yes. Please view this article for more information.

Can I license ChatGPT Pro or the ChatGPT API through the Software Portal?

No. Users interested in leveraging these features must contract with OpenAI directly.

What support is available for ChatGPT Edu licenses?

To learn about options for support for ChatGPT Edu, visit this OpenAI webpage with resources and info. Users can also consult the OpenAI support page.

" KB0018731 AI AI - Creating a Help Desk/Labs Notebook on NotebookLM "

NotebookLM is a note-taking and AI research assistant that allows users to upload documents, webpages, videos, and other sources to a personalized notebook and then uses Google s Gemini AI to analyze, digest, and produce a variety of outputs from the uploaded material.

You can create up to 100 notebooks and perform 50 chat queries per day.

To create your own Notebook for assistance with troubleshooting user issues, you can follow these instructions.

This is an additional source of information and should not be your primary source. Responses may not be entirely accurate. Verify any responses by reviewing the related knowledge articles in their entirety.

Download the .txt files located below according to your position. There are multiple files you should download and use in your Notebook:
The Public Knowledge file contains all of the public knowledge articles.
The other has position-specific Help Desk/Labs articles based on your level. Download and use all files available to you in your Notebook.
Go to the NotebookLM site.
Ensure you are logged in with your ScarletMail account by clicking your icon in the top right, and confirm it shows your ScarletMail email address.
Click Create new on the NotebookLM main page.
You'll be prompted to upload sources. Upload the files you downloaded from below.
Allow it time to process the files. It will automatically name your Notebook for you.
Once the files are processed, you can submit questions in the chat, which will search the entire set of knowledge.
Note that you are limited to 50 queries per day across all of your Notebooks.
You will need to periodically come back to this KA and download new files that contain the most up-to-date information, since the .txt files in your Notebook are not updated automatically. Delete the existing files in your Notebook and upload the 2 new files when there is a more recent version.

" KB0017502 AI AI - Microsoft Co-Pilot "

Any Microsoft account can now log in and access a version of CoPilot. This includes Rutgers Connect accounts. To test your access, navigate to CoPilot and log in with your Rutgers Connect information. Only the chat function of CoPilot is available to Rutgers' Connect accounts.

This is not an endorsement of the CoPilot product, and Connect Support may reverse their decision later if alternate solutions present themselves. Please do not advertise this as a product supported by the University. Connect Support will continue to make changes as the product evolves.

More information about CoPilot can be found on the Microsoft CoPilot Support page.

Connect Support has enabled commercial data protection for the Microsoft CoPilot license feature for all Rutgers Connect users to minimize any potential security risks. This license aims to protect the data that users provide to CoPilot. Note that this decision was made to protect the privacy and data of our users by adding more administrative controls.

More information about AI at Rutgers, including AI use policies and AI services, can be found on the Artificial Intelligence at Rutgers page.

" KB0011792 Archive Archive - Covid Vaccine/Immunization Portal "

[Archived KA until 6/1/28]
COVID-19 Vaccine Requirement

Information on the current requirements can be found on the Rutgers Covid-19 website.

This KA is for historical records on policies during COVID-19.

How to upload COVID-19 vaccine records

Students must follow the below steps:

Go to https://rutgers.medicatconnect.com/
Choose Rutgers University.
Login with their NetID and password.
Select the upload tab.
Scroll down to documents available to be uploaded and choose Immunization records, then upload their vaccination record.
Go back to the top of the page and select Immunization, select COVID-19, and enter their vaccination dates.

Does the Help Desk have a way to see who is included in the campaign or who has successfully uploaded their vaccination information/exemption?

If a user is included in the vaccination mandate and their information has not been verified or their exemption processed, you will see the following in the NetID Management Admin Tool

If the user is not included in the vaccination mandate OR their information has been verified/exemption processed you will not any active campaign in the NetID Management Admin Tool

Canned Responses to Common Questions

Due to the sensitivity of the vaccine mandate it is important that we are only providing guidance related to the technology aspects of the campaign. We are not authorized to provide guidance on what options a student should select or if they are eligible for an exemption. Please use the language below when assisting a user to ensure that the guidance you are providing falls within OIT's jurisdiction. All specific questions regarding the vaccine campaign should be redirected to the appropriate group.

Which option should I choose at the vaccine campaign interrupt screen? OR None of the options apply to me? OR I don t want to choose any of the options.

The Office of Information Technology is unable to provide guidance on which selection to choose when you are prompted to confirm your vaccination status or intention to get vaccinated. If you have additional questions you must contact covidvax411@rbhs.rutgers.edu . You can also find additional detail and information on the Rutgers COVID resource site at https://www.rutgers.edu/covid19

My program should exempt me from the vaccination requirement because it is online OR I am only a summer student.

The Office of Information Technology is unable to provide guidance on whether you should be exempt from the requirement. Generally, students who are only taking summer courses or are enrolled in fully online degree programs (typically defined as having no access to on-campus facilities), as well as individuals participating in fully online or off-campus Continuing Education programs, will not need to provide proof of vaccination.

If you have additional questions you must contact One Stop via the One Stop portal:
https://rutgers.force.com/OneStopPortal/s/New-Brunswick

I already uploaded my vaccination or exemption status, why can t I login?

The Office of Information Technology is unable to provide confirmation of your vaccination or exemption status, but you can check the status using the Immunization Portal. If you have uploaded your vaccination data, please allow 24 hours for your NetID to be unblocked. You may also contact covidvax411@rbhs.rutgers.edu or call the VAX Hotline Monday - Friday 8am - 5pm at 848-445-3033.

Troubleshooting

According to Student Health, Google Chrome and Safari may present issues with uploading Vaccine portal information. Have the user try Firefox or Safari.

" KB0011251 Archive Curbside printing ServiceNow process "

Curbside printing workflow in ServiceNow [Archived until June 1st, 2028]
Setting up a filter for curbside printing tasks
In the All section of the toolbar, expand Service Catalog > Open Records and click Tasks.

In the top left corner of the screen, click Filter and add the following fields by clicking AND across the top:

Active > is > true
Assignment Group > is > NB-Labs - Busch, NB-Labs - Livingston, or NB-Labs - College Ave (Depending on the lab group you work for).

To run the filter once all fields are in place, click Run.
Now, the page should ONLY show the pending Curbside Printing jobs for the respective campus that you chose
Click on Save... next to the Run button to open the options for saving your filter.
Name the filter and select Save, on the right.

Once you have saved this filter, you can access it by pressing the List controls hamburger menu (three lines) next to the Filter button, and navigating to the Filters > option.

Curbside print process - Printing a job
When a user submits a print job to the curbside queue, each print job will create a new Task. If a user submits 5 print jobs, 5 Tasks will be generated, one for each print job. Each Task will have a SCTASK number associated with it.
Select the SCTASK number for the job that you will be processing.

Within the Task, you will see all the relevant information for the job including the document name, cost, and number of pages

Find the print job in PaperCut in Jobs Pending Release and release the job.
Mark the print job as Print Successfully in the dropdown menu.
**NOTE: If you accidentally select the wrong choice, you will have to discard your changes and go back into the Task in order to change your choice in this dropdown menu**

Set the State of the task to Closed Complete.
Click Update in the top right corner of the page to save your changes.
An email gets automatically sent to the patron through ServiceNow. A new Deliver job to patron task will now be created for the print job.

Curbside printing process Delivering a job
The patron will call into your lab to pick up their print jobs. After the job has been delivered to the patron, find the Deliver job to patron tasks that the patron picked up.
In the top right of the page, set the State of the Task to Closed Complete.
Click Update in the top right corner of the page to save your changes.
The Task will disappear from the list. Repeat for all print jobs the patron picked up.
Curbside printing process Error while printing the job

Use the following process if an error occurs while printing the user s print job. We will not be able to retry the print job. This process will have ServiceNow send an automatic email to the user saying that there was an error printing their print job and they receive a balance adjustment for the cost of the job.

While trying to print the print job, the print job does not come out or does not print properly.
Set the dropdown menu item for Printed Successfully to No.

Set the State of the Task to Closed Incomplete.

Click Update in the top right of the page to save your changes.
Task will disappear from the list. Managers will get a new task to issue a balance adjustment for the print job.
Curbside printing process Patron never picks up print job
If a print job has not been picked up after 72 hours, a new Discard job task will be created for the print job.

Discard the print job and then mark the Discard job task s State as Closed Complete. This will also automatically make the State for the Deliver job to patron task to Closed Skipped.
Click Update in the top right of the page to save your changes.

**NOTE: If the user comes to pick up the job before you physically discard the job but after the Discard job task was created, simply mark the original Deliver job to patron task as Closed Complete and the Discard job task as Closed Skipped**

" KB0016544 Archive Data Security Incident - Experian IdentityWorks "

[Archived until June 1st, 2028]

Rutgers recently concluded its investigation of an incident that involved unauthorized access to a Rutgers email account. Upon discovering the incident, Rutgers secured the email account and launched an investigation with the assistance of a cybersecurity firm. Through the investigation, Rutgers determined that an unauthorized actor may have accessed certain emails and/or attachments contained in the email mailbox. Rutgers reviewed those files, and on July 5, 2023, identified affected individuals where one or more file(s) containing their name and personal information may have been accessed.

The users who are affecting will receive letters identifying the information that may have been accessed. Rutgers has secured the services of Experian IdentityWorksSM to offer complimentary identity theft protection services for one year to those affected. Enrolling in these services will not hurt their credit score. More information on the Experian services, including instructions on how to activate the complimentary membership, as well as some additional steps they can take in response to this incident, can be found in the letter they received.

Information we cannot provide

Never provide the third party number. If they are affected they will get a letter and should only get the number from there. Only provide the number to the user if Full Time directs you to do so.

Never tell the user if they are or are not on the list of those affected. They will learn if they are affected based on whether they received the letter or not.

Steps to take

When a user calls in asking about the security incident:

If they got a letter:
They should call the number provided on the letter. We should not provide the number to any user that calls in.
If they believe they are affected, and they did not get a letter:

Consultant should escalate to Full Time who can check if the user is on the list of those affected:
If they are not on the list:
We should tell them they should wait for a letter for confirmation.
We should NOT tell them that they are not affected.
If they are on the list:
We should tell them they can call the number (Full Time can provide consultants with the number if needed) for more information.
We should NOT tell them they are affected.
Third party support number

If they have any questions about this incident, direct them to call the third party number (found in the letter), Monday through Friday, 8:00 a.m. to 5:30 p.m. Central Time, excluding major U.S. holidays.

For assistance enrolling in the complimentary identity theft protection services, they should contact Experian s customer care team (number is provided in the letter). They will need to provide the engagement number as proof of eligibility, which can be found in the letter.

Tag for the Incident

In ServiceNow, we have the ability to add tags to incidents. A tag has been created called Data Security Incident for our group. When you create the incident, please add this tag. Don't forget to save the incident after you add the tag:

KBB0010133FT - Data Security Incident - Experian IdentityWorks

" KB0016325 Archive ITS - Password Requirements "

[Archived until June 1st, 2028]

ITS (Echo) password complexity requirements are as follows:

Each password is valid for 365 days.
The new password cannot be the same as the new password.
The password must be at least 10 characters.
The password may not contain the user's account name or parts of the user's full name that exceed two consecutive characters.
The password must contain characters from three of the following four categories:
English uppercase characters (A through Z)
English lowercase characters (a through z)
Base 10 digits (0 through 9)
Non-alphabetic characters (for example, !, $, #, %)

" KB0014114 Bomgar Bomgar - Information and Policies "

Table of Contents

Bomgar Policies

Bomgar allows us to remotely view a user's computer so we have a better idea of what they're doing and guide them through using certain sites or setting up applications. We've included some brief guidance on using Bomgar below.

Make sure the user is aware that the file download only allows one-time access. Once the session has ended, we will have no further access to view the user's computer, and the program will be uninstalled. During the session, you can see everything they see on their screen. The user can cancel the remote session at any time by clicking the X in the chat window, which will give them the option to close the chat window as well or continue chatting without sharing the screen.
Let the user know that the entire session will be logged. They should not put any sensitive information, such as passwords or Social Security Numbers, into the chat.
If the user is from RBHS, remind them that they have access to PHI Information and advise that if there is any PHI information on their screen, to please close it.
After starting the session, include the user's ServiceNow ticket number in the chat so it can be easily linked back.
You should end the Bomgar session before ending the phone call. If the user disconnects the phone call prematurely (but not accidentally), close the Bomgar chat.
If you see anything that makes you uncomfortable or you find offensive please ask the caller to hold, and approach the CSq Supervisor for guidance.
Make sure you are not using screen sharing when the user enters potentially sensitive information, such as when entering answers to security questions.
After the chat session has ended, paste the entirety of the session's chat history into the ServiceNow ticket.
Bomgar Client Instructions
Explain that using Bomgar, we may view the user's screen but not control it and only see what is in the currently active window. Have the user close any sensitive information they would not like displayed before starting the session, and let them know if at any point they would like to discontinue the screen sharing, they may click on the red X in the chat window that will appear.
Login to the Bomgar web client.
Select Launch Bomgar Web Rep Console.
Select Start New Session in the top right.
Select Generate Session Key in the top right.
Have the user navigate to bomgar.edm.rutgers.edu, enter the Session key, and hit Submit.

If the user has trouble navigating to the website, you may send them the appropriate boilerplate (email) with the session key.
Have the user enter their NetID and ticket number when prompted for their name.

This will download the Bomgar program. The user will need to run this file to continue with the Bomgar setup.
Under All Queues, the user's session will appear. Select Accept on the right to start the session.

This will bring up the Bomgar console and allow you to begin the session.
Click the computer icon in the top left to begin the Screen Sharing process.

This will send a message to the user. They must click Allow to start Screen Sharing.
You will now be able to see the user's screen.

Send the main greeting message and any relevant secondary message(s).
Continue with the rest of the session.
Once completed, send the closing message and give the caller the ticket number.
Paste the chat transcript into the ServiceNow Ticket.
Hit the X in the top right, then End Session to close the Bomgar session.

This will uninstall the program from the user's machine and provide a window for feedback submission.

Please send the following messages during your Bomgar session.

Canned Messages
Greeting

Thank you for contacting the Help Desk. To assist you better, we will be connecting remotely to your computer. While connected, we will be able to view your desktop and see certain programs running on your computer. Throughout this session, you will still retain complete control over your computer. If you do not feel comfortable with us viewing your computer at any time, you may disconnect it by clicking on the X button in the chat window. All activity, including a video of all actions happening while we are connected to your computer, will be recorded.

Password Reset Without Activation Key

At this time, we will walk you through resetting your password. During this process, we may use annotations to draw arrows and other symbols on your screen to direct you to the appropriate links.

To reset your password, we will do the following:

Redirect your web browser to netid.rutgers.edu with your permission.
Go to Manage NetID.
We will show you where to enter your NetID and current password.
We will show you where to enter your new password. We will not be able to see what you are typing in for your new password.
While resetting your password, you will be prompted to reset your security questions. Although we will stay on the phone with you, we will disconnect from your computer at this time to ensure your information is kept private.

Once we have completed your session, our chat history will be included in your ServiceNow ticket for future reference.

Your ServiceNow ticket number is: [Ticket number]

At this time, we will walk you through resetting your password. During this process, we may use annotations to draw arrows and other symbols on your screen to direct you to the appropriate links.

Go to Manage NetID.
Go to Forgotten Password.
Assist you in filling out the Forgotten Password form. We will not be able to view your Social Security Number nor your RUID as it is entered on this form, but we will be able to see your name and birthday.
Your Activation Key will be provided to you in the chat.
Before resetting your password, you will be prompted to reset your security questions. Although we will stay on the phone with you, we will disconnect from your computer at this time to ensure your information is kept private.

Once we have completed your session, our chat history will be included in your ServiceNow ticket for future reference.

Your ServiceNow ticket number is: [Ticket number]

Now we will do the following:

Ask you to walk us through the steps you took, to reproduce the issue.
We may see what is causing the issue you are having and direct you to take additional steps.
If we need to take a screenshot, we will notify you ahead of time.
If you have any windows or programs open that you do not feel comfortable being displayed, please close these programs now.

Once we have completed your session, our chat history will be included in your ticket for future reference.

" KB0016437 Bomgar Bomgar - ITS Instructions and Admin Credentials "

The ability to elevate to take control of the user s mouse, entire computer, and/or to enter Administrator credentials will only work for ITS computers. Please keep that in mind when reading through this guide.

Look Up Computers in KACE

Kace has a database of desktops, laptops, and other devices, among other things. Looking up devices in KACE can provide further insight into things like IPs, Hostname, User, Model, OS, Software. Follow the instructions in the KACE knowledge article on looking up device info.

Start an ITS Bomgar Session
Explain to the user that you d like to use Bomgar to assist; explain to the user what Bomgar is, what you intend to do, and give them the chance to approve, ask questions, or object.
Log into the Bomgar Desktop Application or the Bomgar Web Client.
Start a Bomgar session. There are 3 possible ways to do start a Bomgar session
Reading off the Session Key
Email the Session Key
Jump Client (Note: This will only work for ITS computers)
Reading Off the Session Key
Select Session Key.




Direct the user to nbits.edm.rutgers.edu or its.rutgers.edu (preferred) and click on Begin Remote Support.
If they are prompted for the session key, provide it to the user. The user will enter it, select Submit > enter their name.
You should see an Incoming Connection prompt with the user s name and IP address.
Alternatively they may just be prompted to enter their name in which case they will appear in the Bomgar queue, double click on them in the queue to start the session.
Once you accept the incoming session, the user will see a Remote Support chat box and a Screen Sharing button in the middle of the Bomgar screen.
Select Screen Sharing, and the user will see "[Your/consultant s name] is requesting full access to your computer" along with Allow or Refuse options.
Note: though it will say you/the consultant is requesting full access to the user s computer, it will only share the screen (or whatever the user selects) initially
Once the user selects Allow, the user will be prompted with what to share (Browser tab, Window, or Entire Screen).
Instruct the user to select what to share. Typically it's Entire Screen > select one of the screens (if they have more than one) > Share.
Emailing the Session Key
Select Session Key.
You ll see Send with a small downward arrow.
Select Send > Send Local Email or Send Server Email.

Send Server Email is preferred and will send through the Bomgar Application itself (and you may not see it send/get a confirmation other than verbally asking the user (beyond beginning the session)).
Send Local Email will use the Outlook Desktop application.
Once the user clicks the link, you should see an "Incoming Connection" prompt with the user s name and IP address.
Once you accept the incoming session, the user will see a Remote Support chat box and a Screen Sharing button in the middle of the Bomgar screen.
Select Screen Sharing, and the user will see:
"[Your/consultant s name] is requesting full access to your computer" along with Allow or Refuse options.
Note: though it will say you/the consultant is requesting full access to the user s computer, it will only share the screen (or whatever the user selects) initially.
Once the user selects Allow, the user will be prompted with what to share (Browser tab, Window, or Entire Screen).
Instruct the user to select what to share. Typically it's Entire Screen > select one of the screens (if they have more than one) > Share.
Through the Jump Client
Find the desired computer in the bottom middle section that lists all connected computers (you can also do a search for it).
Select the row that has the desired computer > Screen Sharing.
If assisting a user this way, be aware (and let the user know) that if someone is logged on, they may be disconnected, if both parties allow.
If no one is logged in, it will have you log into the computer.
If someone is logged in, it will disconnect the user (you/the consultant will be prompted first, and then the user, if you/the consultant clicks Yes). Once both you/the consultant and the user click Yes/Ok, the user will be disconnected and you will be in the computer.
To end the session with the user, select X in the right-hand corner.
Elevating for control of the user's computer (will only work for ITS computers)
Select Full Customer Client (this icon is the person and the green arrow pointing down on the right-hand side of the screen).
Note: This will only work for ITS computers; If you do not have the right Bomgar permissions or group(s), you may not see the icon at all.
This will generate a different prompt for the user to accept or deny.
Make sure you inform the user that this will start a file download, that this file will allow Bomgar remote access control of the computer, and why you are attempting to gain access to the computer.
Unlike the Screen Share request, this one will say:
"[consultant s name] is requesting to elevate to full remote support which will allow more features like screen sharing and file transfer. You will be required to run an application that will be sent to you. Do you wish to continue? Accept or Decline".
Once the user selects Accept, this will initiate the file download. Instruct the user to open the file once it finishes downloading.
As the Bomgar client is elevating, it will show on the user s screen as two monitors with 3 dots in between.
Once the client is established, the user will see:
"This session is now being handled by a different client".
Select Screen Share. The user will get another prompt saying:
"[Your/consultant s name] is requesting full access to your computer".
Instruct the user to select Allow. Once they do, you should now be able to take control of the mouse/computer.
Elevating to enter Administrator credentials (will only work for ITS computers)

Once you see the user s screen (and have control of the computer as noted above), you may need to push a request to elevate permissions so you can input administrative credentials.

If you do not elevate, situations where administrative credentials are required may generate a black screen for you and you won t be able to do anything until the user closes out the prompt requesting the credentials. Note: If you do not have the right Bomgar permissions or group(s), you may also run into this issue but you may not see the shield icon at all (it should be in the upper right-hand corner).

Select the shield icon:
If device is on the RAD domain (most are), use your NetID-adm credentials to login:
Username: rad\NetID-adm
Password: Your ADM password. This password would be different than your regular NetID password. If you forgot your RAD-adm password you can reset it on this page by logging in with your regular NetID credentials.
If the device is on the ITS domain still, enter your ITS Admin credentials (netid_admin and your ITS admin Password) > Ok. Not everyone may have ITS Admin Credentials yet. Admin credentials for laptops are different, reach out to FT to get them.
If successful, you should see "Elevation in progress ".
The user will see a "Do you want to allow this app to make changes to your device?" prompt from Bomgar. Instruct them to select Accept and the elevation should go through.
Once the user accepts, it will say "Waiting for Reconnect" on your screen.
Once elevated, the text box in the upper right-hand corner will say "[your/consultant s name] can now have full access to the customer s computer".

Opening a session

" KB0016487 Box Box - Audit Unlocking and Extensions "

As part of the Box Audits, we may receive requests from users who were accessing files shared by a user who no longer holds a Rutgers role, stating they are unable to access these files because the owner's account has been locked.

Users can view a list of all files/folders shared with them, along with the owners of those shares, by going to the Service Management page and clicking Show Box Collaborators under the Box section.

If users need an account unlocked, a file unlocked, or access extended, they must submit the request through UEC. UEC will approve or deny and then work with Box Support to grant the user the approved access.

" KB0016710 Box Box - Determining Shared File/Folder Owners "

Determining Box shares in CAT

In the Cloud Admin Tool (CAT), under the Box section, select Collaborators. This tab will display any users that this user is currently collaborating within Box. This includes any shares this user is accessing from other users and any shares that this user is sharing with other users.

The Share Direction indicates whether the user is Sharing To the collaborator or Accessing From the collaborator. Remember that the collaborator may be a user or a Box shared account. If the collaborator is a user, you will see the collaborator's name, Box email address, their domain, and the number of objects shared.

If a collaborator's Box account is currently within the auditing/de-provisioning process, the row will be red to indicate the share will be going away soon.

The Count indicates the number of objects shared, each object can be either a folder or an individual file. If an object is a folder, the count does not include the number of files within that shared folder.

Please note that the collaboration report is generated once a week on Tuesday mornings.

Users' ability to determine their own Box shares in Service Activation

Instruct users to go to the Service Management Page and look under the Box section. Have them select Show Box Collaborators to pull up the same information we see in CAT. From here, the user can determine on their owner the individuals or groups that any of their Box content is shared to/from. Users can select CSV or Excel to export and download a spreadsheet of the collaboration information.

Even when a user's Box account is locked, a user can still view their collaborators here. That way, even if they are being audited and they ask for a list of their Box shares, the user can still get it themselves.

" KB0019535 Box Box - Virus Detection - Email Support Procedures "

Box automatically scans for malicious files and removes them from the online platform. When Box detects and removes a malicious file, it sends an automated alert to help@oit.rutgers.edu.

While the online file is handled by the Box technical team, IT support must intervene to ensure the user does not have a local copy of the malicious file on their personal or work device.

These alerts will automatically generate a ticket in ServiceNow. You can identify them by the following criteria:

Sender: noreply@box.com
Message Body Example:
"Virus detected for file 'example.css' Uploaded by: example@rutgers.edu File name: example.css Folder owner: example@rutgers.edu Path: [File Path]"

When you receive an incident matching the alert above, please perform the following steps:

Change the caller field to "Abuse"
Update the Assignment Group: Route the ticket to the local IT Support group responsible for the affected user identified in the Box message.
Use the Help@OIT Box Virus Detected Response response template.

" KB0014354 Connect Connect (Office 365) - Auto Provisioning "

Rutgers Connect accounts are automatically created for all faculty/staff who go through the NetID activation process. By activating these accounts, faculty/staff will also have the associated Microsoft Office products that come with Office 365. This information can be found on our Microsoft Office for Faculty and Staff page.

Activating Connect Account
All faculty/staff who activate their NetID are eligible to activate a Rutgers Connect account.
When the user gets to the Service Selection portion of the NetID Activation process, they will see the page below. Rutgers Connect will automatically be checked off for the user and they cannot uncheck it.

The NetID activation process will attempt to place a user into their correct domain in Rutgers Connect using their org code. If we are unable to identify the correct domain to put the user in, they will be asked to self-provide their campus affiliation. This will drop the user into a generic domain.

If a user selects New Brunswick/Other they will be put into the connect.rutgers.edu domain
If a user selects Newark they will be put in the newark.rutgers.edu
If a user selects Camden they will be put in the camden.rutgers.edu domain
Users will then select a personalized email address. This page will also provide the user with their sign-in address. This will be located in the line "The above email address will deliver email to: netid@domain.rutgers.edu".
Lastly, the user will be presented with a summary of their NetID activation. The first section of this page provides the user with their NetID and the second section will show the user their Official Rutgers Email Address (personalized email address) as well as directions on how to log in to their Rutgers Connect Account.
Note: After activation, users may need to wait up to 30 minutes before attempting to log into their account.
After a user activates their NetID, they are strongly advised to speak with their departmental IT support to ensure they are in the correct domain in Rutgers Connect.
If there are any questions regarding this process, please speak with a Supervisor.

" KB0014355 Connect Connect (Office 365) - Emeritus and Retirees "

Rutgers Connect delegated admins will be able to move users that have an Emeritus role to the @emeritus.rutgers.edu domain in Connect. That means users with an Emeritus role may keep their Rutgers Connect account if they wish and will not be forced to move to ScarletMail. The delegated admin can decide whether or not to leave their old @domain.rutgers.edu aliases attached to the user's account, or they can choose to remove them. Delegated admins will also be able to claim any user in the @emeritus.rutgers.edu domain and move them to the domain they administrate.

Users with the Emeritus role are permitted to have a ScarletMail and Rutgers Connect account, and are provisioned with a full Connect license. Users with the Emeritus role that activate a Connect account via the "Service Activation" link on the NetID Management page will automatically be placed in the @emeritus.rutgers.edu domain. Users with the Emeritus role are not permitted to set their delivery address to anything other than @scarletmail.rutgers.edu or @connect.rutgers.edu.

Users with a Retiree role are only permitted to have a ScarletMail account, and their delivery address will be set to their @scarletmail.rutgers.edu address. Retirees are not permitted to set their delivery address to anything other than their @scarletmail.rutgers.edu address.

Note: If a user has a Faculty, Staff, Guest, or Student role, the above does not apply to them because those roles always take precedence over the Emeritus and Retiree roles.

More information on these roles can be found at KB0014281.

" KB0016221 Connect Connect (Office 365) - Integrations for Microsoft Office Applications "

For security reasons, Microsoft Office 365 integrations need to be evaluated by Rutgers OIT before inclusion. To find what integrations have already been approved, you can click on add-ins in an Office application:

If they do not see the form listed, they may need to have their delegated admin fill it out on their behalf. There is no guarantee that their service will be approved. This is dependent on many factors, including security and demand.

You can view the KA on approved and denied Connect add-ins and inform the user if it has already been approved or denied.

" KB0014356 Connect Connect - Guest Account Provisioning "

If a Guest requires a Rutgers Connect account, the Guest should reach out to their Sponsor who would then contact their Rutgers Connect delegated administrator. The delegated administrator can license the user and should move the user to their appropriate domain.

Once a Rutgers Connect account has been created for the Guest, it will be treated like any other account and be deleted when the user no longer has any roles qualifying them for an account (i.e., when their Guest role expires and no other suitable role has been assigned).

" KB0014357 Connect Connect - Handling Connect calls "

When a user calls in with a Connect issue we need to determine whether we can properly assist them or if we should get their IT Departmental Staff involved.

General issues

For general issues that are covered in our documentation or Microsoft s documentation, such as setting up Connect on a mobile device or sharing a calendar, we can attempt to assist the user.

Please keep in mind that some departments may have their own set of rules for Connect that they want their staff to follow. We want to ensure that we are not overriding these rules when a user calls in. For example, some departments do not want their staff using IMAP when setting up Connect, so we should always instruct users to use auto-discovery and refer them to their department for IMAP setup.

More involved issues

For more involved issues, issues specific to the department, or issues taking a while for us to handle, we need to reach out to IT Staff for their department.

To do this, we let the user know that there is a dedicated team for their specific department that we need to escalate the issue to. To find the correct Rutgers Connect Domain Administrator for their department, navigate to the Rutgers Information Technology Help & Support page. Use the table to the right side of the How to find your IT support contact box to find the appropriate contact for the user; right-clicking on the Department name and selecting copy link address will allow you to copy the email for the user's delegated admin. We can then provide the user with this information.

Note: Any user calling from the Athletics or Scarlet Knight department with Connect issues, needs to speak to their IT Departmental Staff, independent of the type of issue. We can let the user know that we will connect them to the group that handles their issue.

" KB0016241 Connect Connect - Microsoft Unified Support "

Microsoft Unified Support is available only to IT Staff. This comprehensive support service offers several benefits that can improve IT operations and enhance productivity. Staff interested in this are directed to email software@rutgers.edu to activate their Microsoft Unified Support account.

Microsoft Unified Support includes:

Problem resolution support: With Microsoft Unified Support, we have access to a team of highly skilled technical experts who can provide fast and efficient support when issues arise. This can help minimize downtime and ensure that incidents are addressed quickly.
Advisory support: Short-term engagement sessions are designed to help implement Microsoft technologies in ways that avoid common pitfalls.
On-demand assessments: Microsoft Unified Support includes nearly 50 on-demand assessments covering services such as Windows client configuration, SharePoint, and SQL Server.
Access to Microsoft Education: With Microsoft Unified Support, we have access to over 6,000 on-demand videos and step-by-step labs for technologies like PowerShell, Teams, and Azure. Staff can also attend Microsoft-hosted online educational sessions on various topics.
Dedicated Customer Success Manager (CSAM): Microsoft Unified Support provides a single point of contact to coordinate Microsoft support cases and access services not covered under Unified Support.
How to handle these requests

Reassign the Incident to EAS - Connect Technical Support, who will coordinate with the user, as it is a two-step process to activate. They will create the account in Microsoft Hub. The user will then get an email from Microsoft, which can take up to 3 business days. They need to log in with their email to activate the account before Connect Support can assign our agreement to their account.

" KB0014358 Connect Connect - Software License Issues "

Sometimes, installations of Office 365 Suite via ProPlus License fail to assign properly on a user s machine. This tends to occur when a user signs in for the license with their ScarletMail address without properly removing their old account information from a previous subscription. If uninstalling Microsoft Office and reinstalling it does not work, please try the following.

DO NOT allow a user to do this on their own. Any assistance on these steps that is not done in-person is strongly discouraged.

Sign Out

Open Word, Excel, etc. File > Account > Sign out. Then click Yes.

After that, check again to see if old profiles may still be on there.

Remove the credentials from the Credential Manager

(Control Panel > User Accounts > Credential Manager)

Search for Credential Manager in the Control panel > Windows credentials > Microsoft Office 16 > Remove the credentials.

Edit Registry

Edit Registry and Remove the Identities folder - open regedit

Navigate to HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\Microsoft\Office\16.0\Common\ Identity\Identities and delete the Identities folder

AND

Navigate to HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Office\ClickToRun\Configuration(string) "SharedComputerLIcensing"="1"

Script

Use CSCRIPT.EXE to remove the license key. You will have to run as Administrator for the next steps.

Run cscript.exe command with the "/dstatus" switch to see if a product key is found. If so, it will report the last few characters of the installed product key. Use that as the XXXX of the "/unpkey" switch. The exact command is below.

The first command will give a product key, the second will clear the key

When BOTH Office and Windows are both either 32 bit or 64 bit, use the following two commands.

cscript.exe "%ProgramFiles%\Microsoft Office\Office16\ospp.vbs" /dstatus

cscript.exe "%ProgramFiles%\Microso? Office\Office16\ospp.vbs" /unpkey:XXXX

Use the following commands when Windows is 64 bit and Office is 32 bit, or vica versa. (try this if the above commands fail)

cscript.exe "%ProgramFiles(x86)%\Microso? Office\Office16\ospp.vbs" /dstatus

cscript.exe "%ProgramFiles(x86)%\Microso? Office\Office16\ospp.vbs" /unpkey:XXXX

" KB0014362 Connect Connect - Spam & Junk Email Filtering "

Rutgers Connect has a very good spam filtering system. Here are some common problems people might encounter. These steps must be followed in the Outlook Web Application (the connect.rutgers.edu website). Spam filtering changes made in a specific mail client, including a desktop copy of Outlook, apply only to those messages found in the Inbox when that mail client is running.

Too much spam/junk email is arriving in the Inbox

Exactly how many spam messages has the person received? 4-5 per day is not unusual.

Select how Outlook should handle new messages you receive.

Connect can handle incoming mail in two different ways, Standard or Strict.

Standard will automatically move any mail identified as possible junk or spam into the Junk Email folder.
Strict will only allow mail from senders and domains that have been marked as safe to be delivered to the inbox.

To view and edit these settings, please follow the steps below:

Click the gear icon.
Under Settings, click Mail.
Find and click Junk Email
Under Incoming Mail Handling select either Standard or Strict.

OIT recommends setting this to Strict if there is an excess of spam or junk email reaching the inbox.

See if a specific sender is allowed to bypass spam filtering.
In the same Junk Email section, under Safe Senders and Domains, see if the sender's address is listed.
If it is, select it, then click the Trash Can icon to delete the address.
Click Save to save and apply the changes.
Marking a message in Inbox as spam / Setting Rules
Select the message.
In the menu bar, select Move, then Junk Email (If the option is not available, type "Junk Email" into the search bar)

Please note that you can also setup rules within Outlook to automatically place all emails from a specific sender into the "Junk Email" Folder.

Blocking an entire domain or address
Copy the address or domain from the sender's email address (the portion after the @ sign).
Open the Junk Email section as described above.
Under Blocked senders and Domains, paste or type the sender's domain into the Enter a sender or domain here box.
Click Add Blocked Sender and paste the senders address or domain.
Click Save to save and apply the changes.
Changing a previously blocked domain or address
Open the Junk Email section as described above.
Under Blocked Senders and Domains, select the address to change.
Click the pencil icon
Edit the selected address to make the changes.
Press the Return key when done.
Click Save to save the changes.

" KB0014189 Email "Email - (IDM) Aliases, Official Rutgers Email Addresses, and Delivery Addresses" "

Aliases

An email alias is a customizable e-mail address which will forward emails to a user's delivery addresses. Aliases are not separate accounts that a user can log into. By default, during activation, the alias will be set to first.lastname@rutgers.edu. The alias must contain a period, start with a letter, and end with @rutgers.edu. If a combination of firstname.lastname is already taken, a number will automatically be added to the end of the address. After account and services activation, the user may modify or set up to a total of 3 Aliases by navigating to User NetID Management > Manage Email Addresses.

NOTE: If a user has chosen to use an alias as their Official Rutgers Email Address, they will not be able to delete it until a different address is used instead.

Delivery Address

All email received by a user's aliases will be sent to their delivery addresses. Users have the option to add a personal email account as an additional delivery option, but all users must have at least one Rutgers email account set as a delivery address. After account and service activation, the user may modify or set up a total of 3 delivery addresses.

NOTE: If a user is Faculty, Staff, or has a PHI = Y flag, they are required to have a Connect account set as their delivery address. They may still set an alias as their Official Rutgers Email Address.

Official Rutgers Email Addresses

Students and Student Workers can only have an official Rutgers email address that ends in @rutgers.edu. Therefore, it must also be one of their aliases. Faculty and Staff can use any valid @rutgers.edu account as their official Rutgers email address (this includes ScarletMail, aliases, and departmental addresses). Users cannot update their official Rutgers email addresses through personalinfo.rutgers.edu - changes can be made through the Manage Email Addresses page. If a user has multiple roles, updating a single official email address will update all of them.

NOTE: After updating an official Rutgers email address, the changes will immediately be reflected in the Manage Email Addresses tool, but it will take 5-10 minutes for these changes to be reflected in search.rutgers.edu.

More information about reporting suspected scams, breaches or theft can be found on the Information Security: Resources page.

" KB0014216 Email Email - Personalized Email Address "

Users can create a mail alias for their Rutgers email accounts by navigating to netid.rutgers.edu and selecting Manage Email Addresses on the left-hand side.

Once at the email address management screen, users can click Create New Personalized Email Address to input a new alias email address.

Use the Delivery Addresses box to control which inboxes the emails sent to an alias are delivered to.

Adding an alias does not change the user's listing in OpenRegistry (OR). The user must manually go into personalinfo.rutgers.edu and update their listed email address in the Address Update tab for changes to be reflected in OR.

If a user has a Rutgers Connect email account and would like to send mail as a different alias, they will also have to configure it through netid.rutgers.edu as well by going to the Manage Email Addresses page and locating Rutgers Connect Account Information at the bottom. Within that box, have the user select the desired alias address from the dropdown to the right of the Emails sent from Rutgers Connect will come from: section and then have them select Set Primary Address. Please be aware that the alias addresses are pulled from the ones listed in the Personalized Email Address box and that any changes made to the Connect send as address may take up to 2 hours.

Set accounts to show outbound mail as firstname.lastname@rutgers.edu
Thunderbird (v. 3+)
Open the Tools menu and select Account Settings.
On the left in the new window, select the e-mail account.
On the right, under Default Identity, type the alias in the Email Address: box.
Select OK.
Apple Mail
