In the new window, select Accounts on the top row.
Select Account Information.
Type the alias into the Email Address:
Close the new window and select Save when asked to save changes.
Outlook
Open a new email message.
Select Options.
Select From to enable the From field.
Select the drop down next to From and select Other Email Address.
Enter the alias you would like to send from in the field.
The alias will now be saved for future use.
Mail Application for iPhone/iPad/iPod Touch
Select Settings.
In the Settings screen, select Mail, Contacts, Calendars.
Select the account name (under Accounts at the top).
Replace the contents of the Address box with the alias.
Gmail in Web Browser
Select the gear in the right side of the screen.
Select See all settings.
Select the Accounts and import tab.
Under the Send mail as section, select Add another email address.
A screen will pop up, prompting you to input your alias. Make sure to treat it as an alias.
The next screen will prompt you for the following information:
Smtp Server: smtp.gmail.com
Username: netid@scarletmail.rutgers.edu
Password: Mobile IMAP password.
Put a check mark near Secured Connection using TLS
Select Next Step (Send through Rutgers University Mail should be selected).
Select Next once again.
Select Send Verification. You will receive a verification email prompting for a code. Input that code once you receive it.
Select Verify and then close the window.
Users may additionally make the alias their default account to send mail as by clicking on make default.
Aliases for shared accounts (departmental accounts, mailing lists) and non-OIT email accounts

Full-time staff can create additional @rutgers.edu mail aliases for addresses other than those of personal accounts.

Aliases longer than 8 characters or containing punctuation such as periods, dashes, or underscores are permitted.

Aliases can only be delivered to a single address. If someone wants an alias to deliver to multiple addresses, they must first set up some sort of mailing list, either on Mailman or a departmental mail server, and have that mailing list delivered to the requested addresses. Full-time staff can then set the alias to deliver to the mailing list's address.

Aliases do not need to resemble the address to which they deliver unless a) the requested alias looks like a NetID, in which case they must own an account on our systems with that name (as described above), or b) the alias has the potential to confuse (e.g., "academicdean@rutgers.edu" for the Dean of a particular school). In such cases, suggest clearer or non-conflicting alternatives, such as "sebs.dean".

Always speak with a full-time staff member about this before promising that a particular alias will be created.

" KB0014141 Email Email - Phishing "

Phishing is the attempt to steal someone's account or personal information through e-mail masquerading as an official message.

For more details on information security and how to recognize threats like this, please visit the Rutgers IT Information Security Resource page.

Reporting Phishing
For Users

If a user calls in to report they have received a phishing email, instruct the user to forward the email with full headers, ONLY to abuse@rutgers.edu.

Obtaining full headers varies based on the email client. To determine how to obtain full headers, first check what email client the user is utilizing. Then search on Google for how to obtain full headers for the specific email client.

For Consultant/Supervisory Staff

If you personally receive such a message, the message should be forwarded, with full headers to abuse@rutgers.edu, and CC: hd_supervisors@oit.rutgers.edu and operator@oit.rutgers.edu. There is no need to report the same message more than once, so check with a supervisor to see if anyone has already reported such a message that day.

If you or the user don't know how to forward a message with full headers, Google has an Instructions for Gmail and other common mail services page.

To forward headers for webmail clients:

Follow instructions on how to obtain full headers for your specific email client.
Copy the full header to your clipboard.
Paste the text into the forwarding message above the original email text.
Email headers

An email header is the section at the beginning of an email that contains technical and routing information, such as the sender, recipient, subject, date, and unique message ID. While some information is visible to the user, the full header includes details about the email's journey across different servers, making it a useful tool for troubleshooting, verifying sender authenticity, and detecting spam or phishing attempts.

Full email headers include:

The standard fields that are visible in all emails to users
From
To
Subject
Date
Technical details
Message-ID which is a unique identifier for the email.
Delivery path which is a record of every server the email passed through.
Authentication results which are the results of security checks like SPF, DKIM, and DMARC to confirm the sender's authenticity.
Compromised Accounts

Our system administrators keep a close eye on such attempts. Once they learn of a phishing message, they track who sends email back to the address listed in it. Under no circumstances should someone ever reply to such a message. Remind our callers that they should never share their passwords with anyone, nor should they ever send any personal information through e-mail, and that our office will never ask for their passwords.

If the user clicked on a link in the email or responded to the email, then it is strongly suggested that they reset their password. Please let a supervisor know if the user believes their information was compromised, as this may need to be reported to abuse@rutgers.edu.

If the user has a Kerberos lock in the NetID Management Tool, this may be related to the phishing attempt. All calls regarding accounts flagged for phishing should follow the Probshell/Kerblock procedures, and the ticket should be given to a full-time staff member after verification. Full-time staff can follow the Kerblock Checklist procedures to give phishing talks and unlock the account.

" KB0014147 Email Email - Troubleshooting Email Related Issues "

There are a number of things that can go wrong with email. Fortunately, mail servers are set up to send detailed error messages to the sender if they cannot deliver messages, commonly known as "bounceback messages". If a user cannot send mail to someone, check if they received a bounceback message and confirm its contents. Below are examples of the most common types of bounceback messages - sending an email to a nonexistent address in Gmail/ScarletMail or Office365/Connect.

Gmail/ScarletMail







Office365/Connect







As you can see, these messages are very detailed, giving an exact error code and plain text explanation of the issue. To assist a user, it can be beneficial for the user to send a screenshot of the message. Users can send screenshots by directly replying to the email informing them a ticket has been opened for them. When troubleshooting email-related issues, the CAT tool is very resourceful and should be used. If unfamiliar with the CAT tool, reference the CAT getting started documentation.

Once the bounceback email has been received, troubleshooting can begin. Essential questions and scenarios to keep in mind when troubleshooting are as follows:

Is there another way you can confirm the user's email address, such as Rutgers University Search?
Is there a current outage noted in outage_degradation within Slack that might be responsible?
If the user is sending to an alias address, perhaps the alias system is currently experiencing issues. In this case, you can use Rutgers University Search to provide a direct delivery address for the user.
If a user cannot receive mail, the first thing to determine is how they access their email and what method is appropriate for them. You should utilize the CAT tool to see what the user has as their delivery email address.

If the user is a staff or faculty member trying to use ScarletMail instead of Connect, you must direct them to utilize their Connect account.
If the user is a student who thinks they use Connect as their email client and is not associated with RBHS, they should be using ScarletMail, and you need to direct them to the appropriate mailbox. If the student is affiliated with RBHS, ensure they log in with the correct UPN found within the CAT tool.

For further troubleshooting, please refer to the Rutgers Connect and ScarletMail knowledge base.

" KB0014156 Five9 Five9 - Fac/Staff overflow calls procedure "

When users call the Faculty/Staff Help Desk and wait more than 2 minutes, some callers will receive a message offering the option to transfer to a student worker. This option will only be made available to users who indicated that their issues are related to NetID/password, ScarletMail, Webex, or Connect.

If a user opts to be transferred to a student worker, the call will be transferred to an available Level 1 consultant. You are expected to assist these users to the best of your ability.

If their issue is not something you can assist with and requires the support of a Faculty/Staff Help Desk staff member, you can redirect the call to the FSHD using MAX's internal transfer option labeled "Faculty Staff Overflow Return".

Please remember that this option is only to be used when returning an overflow call back to FSHD and not when transferring users who called into the Student Help Desk.

When it has been determined that the call will need to be redirected in this manner, the following response can be used to inform the user:

I am sorry, as a student worker I do not have access to the tools required to assist with that issue. I will need to transfer you back to the Faculty Staff Help Desk. You will be transferred to the next available agent.



" KB0014111 Five9 Five9 - Phone Call Etiquette "

When answering the phone it is always important to introduce yourself as well as the place of where the user is calling into. In addition, within the first minutes of the phone call, it is important to also gather information about the user such as their NetID, full name, call back phone number and email. Below is a sample script in order to have an efficient and professional phone call.

Start the phone call by saying:

Thank you for calling the [LOCATION]. This is [YOUR FIRST NAME HERE]. May I please have the spelling of your NetID?"
(Or, if callers don t know their NetIDs or they haven t yet created one, ask for the spelling of their first and last name). When repeating back spelling to a user, it is important to use the NATO alphabet.
Note: If you cannot find the user in ServiceNow by their last name, it is possible they do not yet have a NetID. You may create a ticket under Guest.
Once you've confirmed the spelling of the NetID or user's name, get the user's phone number. You may do this by saying:

"May I have a phone number where you can be reached in case we get disconnected?"

It is always important to repeat the phone number back to the user to ensure the phone number is correct.
Next, ask for the user's email address. You may ask for the user's email by saying:

"Thank you and may I have a current e-mail address where we can send your ticket confirmation to?"
If the user is a Guest, this will be entered in the "alternate contact information" section of the ticket.
After that, ask the user if they have called about the issue before or have an existing ticket number by asking:

"Have you called about this issue before and if so, do you already have a ticket number?"
If the user states no, please continue with creating a new ticket.
If the answer is yes, look for a previous ticket about this issue and review that ticket. If the ticket was created recently and is still open, you should continue commenting on that ticket (i.e. do not create a new ticket).
If the ticket is closed, you should review it briefly for troubleshooting tips. Then, cut a new ticket and begin annotating your troubleshooting steps in the new ticket.

Remember to speak clearly and at a pace that's comfortable for your callers. Be patient with callers who may not be native speakers of English. If the user has trouble understanding a word, spell it using the NATO phonetic alphabet (e.g. A as in Alpha, B as in Bravo, etc.). Only after you complete the above steps should you begin troubleshooting the user s problem.

Note: Some users may be put off by what they perceive as a bureaucratic delay and try rushing directly to the problem. Try to avoid letting yourself be directed in this way. It is a reasonable expectation that when people call a support line for any issue, technical or non-technical, they often must give this kind of information to get the best possible service. If we do not get a contact number and the call drops, the user will have to wait in the queue again. If we do not have a valid email, they will not get a feedback form.

" KB0017656 Five9 Five9 - Phone Tree "

" KB0017642 Five9 Five9 - User guide "

Five9 is a software based phone system that processes calls using either phone audio or designated external phone. Five9 will be our new our new VCC (Virtual Contact Center) that is replacing Verizon.

Table of Contents
Logging in
Receiving a Call
Outbound Call
Hold and Mute
Warm Transfer
Cold Transfer
Ending Call
Logging Out
Logging in
Log into ServiceNow.
Click Service Operations Workspace.




Click the phone icon in the top right corner.
Click SSO Log In





You'll automatically be logged in via SSO.
Choose PSTN.
Enter the PSTN number listed on the sticker on your desk phone formatted as: +1 PSTN
If not at a desk phone you can enter the number of any phone in place of the PTSN.
Under Connect to Station click Yes.
Click Confirm.




The phone with the number you entered will receive a call. Answer it and you will hear 3 quick beeps to confirm it has connected properly.
Once you hear the beeps you can hang up the call on your phone.
For Level 2 and above: you'll always be prompted to select the Skills that you will be using. The first time you login, you'll have to check off the appropriate skills, these preferences will be saved for the next time you login. Update your list accordingly so the following skills are the only ones check marked. Click Confirm when done. (note: OIT_Supervisors line is now called OIT_Level3; and the IT Direct line is OIT_Level3ITD)

The first time you log in, everyone will need to disable the Automatic Answering options from the Settings.
Note: You may need to disable third party storage partitioning in Chrome to launch the Settings menu.




Use the Ready state when you are ready to start taking calls.
Receiving a Call
You must be in the Ready state to receive calls.
When you receive a call, your phone will ring.
If you're phone has a headset connected:
Click Accept and you'll automatically be connected to the call over your headset.
If your phone does not have a headset connected:
The Accept button will be greyed out. You'll automatically be connected to the call once you answer your phone.
A new ServiceNow Interaction will pop up with the caller data.

If you do not answer or click Accept in time, the call will go back in the queue and your state will be automatically put into the Not Ready status.

Outbound Call
Select New Call
Input the name or number of the new participant
Choose the correct Campaign (OIT - Outbound)
Click Dial to start the call


Hold and Mute

Activating Hold will temporarily pause the conversation, during which hold music will be played for the caller.

Activating Mute will silence your microphone, resulting in silence for the caller on the other end.

Warm Transfer
Select Conference.
Input the name or number of the new participant.
Choose Warm.
Click Speak with Participant to temporarily place the initial caller on hold and talk with the new participant.
After the new participant answers, click Add Participant to conference them into the conversation.
Confirm all parties can now speak with each other.




To end a conference call, you could either:
Click Leave Conference to drop from the call. This option will keep the other callers connected.
Click on the participant name or number and choose Remove to remove one participant.
Click End Call to disconnect everyone.



Note: To receive a transferred call, you need to be in the Ready or Not Ready - Outbound Call state.

Cold Transfer
Select Transfer.
Input the name or number of the new participant.
Choose Cold.
Change timeout duration to zero. This will make the receiving agent s station ring until the call is answered or the user hangs up.
Click Complete Transfer.
Select Disposition and choose Complete Transfer.



Note: To receive a transferred call, you need to be in the Ready state.

Ending Call
Click End Call.
You will disconnect with the user and be required to select a Disposition before ending the Interaction.
While choosing a Disposition, you will be in a Wrap Up state.
Finish up any work needed on the Interaction/Incident before proceeding.
Once done, choose a disposition and click End Interaction.
After ending the Interaction, you will be reverted back to your previous state.


Logging Out

Please note that closing the Five9 Adapter does not log you out of the phones, instead it minimizes the display. Click on the phone icon to re-open the Adapter.

To sign out of the Five9 Adapter, click on the power button to the left of the agent state.

You can change where the Adapter is positioned on the three dots in the right hand corner.

" KB0016896 General General - Changes to Television Services "

Rutgers has significantly updated the television services provided on campus in January 2024. In response to the evolving media landscape and changing viewing habits, Rutgers phased out traditional cable television service. This change affected both residential areas, including rooms and apartments, and public spaces throughout the university.

Discontinuation of Traditional Cable Television Service

As of January 2024, Rutgers has discontinued traditional cable television service in all on-campus residence hall rooms, apartments, and community lounge spaces.

Supporting Access to Streaming Services

There is a comprehensive guide of available streaming plans that offer either free content or attractive student discounts.

These options are tailored for use on personal devices within the residential buildings. To use these plans, students must show proof of current enrollment at Rutgers.

New IPTV Streaming Service
Residence Halls

Internet Protocol television (IPTV) streaming service will be made available in the public lounges of the residence halls. This innovative streaming service of over 80 channels brings a diverse range of programming.

Departments responsible for other public spaces

Local Departmental IT can setup this new IPTV streaming service is spaces they are responsible for. They can follow the instructions in the Activation Guide on how to set it up.

Additional Questions

If any questions need to be routed, you can direct these students to the IP&O Central Service Center at 848-445-1234. The IP&O Central Service Center will have FAQs to help them further navigate the inquiry with the student.

" KB0017464 General General - enhanced Institutional Review Board (IRB) system (eIRB+) "

The mission of the Human Research Protection Program (HRPP) is to support the University's research enterprise by ensuring the protection of individuals who participate in research; ensuring compliance with all pertinent federal and state laws and regulations; fostering the ethical conduct of human subjects research; and providing education and other services related to regulatory requirements and best practices to Rutgers researchers.

An Institutional Review Board (IRB) ensures the protection of the rights and welfare of human research subjects based on the rules and regulations of the Food and Drug Administration (FDA) and Department of Health and Human Services (specifically Office for Human Research Protections) by approving, requiring modifications in, or disapproving Human Subject Research projects.

Faculty and Staff make use of the eIRB+ system to document the process and receive the necessary approvals for their research.

Support requests for the eIRB+ system should be directed to the contact emails below:

IRB Submission Help:
IRBOffice@research.rutgers.edu

IT Tech Support:
eirb@research.rutgers.edu

eIRB Training Workshops:
HSPP/IRB Educational Resources | Rutgers Research

eIRB+ manuals and user guides can be found on their website:
eIRB Manuals and User Guides

" KB0014157 General General - Freshman iPad Program "

What is the iPad Program?

The Chancellors of Rutgers- New Brunswick and Rutgers-Newark launched an initiative to distribute iPad Air 256GB devices. This program is in partnership with SHI, who assisted in securing the devices and handled all fulfillment. The devices were customized to include Rutgers-approved apps and web links to be used as resources throughout the students' time at Rutgers, such as the Rutgers Mobile App, Blackboard, Canvas, Google Suite, Microsoft Suite, and more. Note: This program has been discontinued.

Note: Once a user registered for a free iPad, their NetID/ScarletMail account was associated with the MDM profile. Therefore, if they have a smart phone or tablet with an RU profile already installed, the phone/tablet will try to sync with the profile. They would see extra apps installed on their phone/tablet even if they had not yet received their free iPad. To avoid this, they would've needed to delete the profile from their phone/tablet if they did not want the devices to sync. This will also occur when they buy a device in the future and still have their NetID/ScarletMail account associated with the MDM profile and install an RU profile on the new device.

Official wording for why MDM cannot be removed from iPad:

The iPad was gifted to enrolled first-year students as a tool to keep students connected to campus, to the Scarlet Knight community, and to assist with coursework. With a Rutgers-managed device, all resources and Rutgers-approved technology is automatically pushed to your iPad to assist with your transition to college life. Given the purpose of this iPad, Rutgers management can not be removed.

The management profile will only be removed once a student is no longer associated with the university (i.e. a student transfers, graduates, etc.). To remove the profile the device will have to be wiped to factory settings. The management profile does not track your usage and you can use your iPad for non-Rutgers purposes. You also have complete access to the app store to purchase or download any applications you choose. A Supervisor or FT can follow up if the device does not allow for removal of the profile.

" KB0011772 General General - Job Scam Notice "

The ScarletMail system admins may email FT & Case Notes with an email similar to the below when they notice students conversing with known scam addresses/mail content that is determined to be a scam. One such notice is included below.

When we receive such a notice, it is our job to reach out to the affected user and inform them they were conversing with known scammers and the resources available for identity theft protection.

Create a ServiceNow Incident for Guest
Alternate Contact Information: use N/A or - for all fields
Service Offering: ScarletMail
Short Description: Notice Regarding Employment Scam
Description: blank
Assign to relevant Assignment Group you are in, and assign to yourself.
Include original email from system admins in Work Notes.
The notification to the user(s) should be sent via an email message (see example below) with each user BCC'd on the message. If the system admins provide extra information (i.e. other links that can be provided to the user) the message can be modified with that information.
In the Response Tool there is a template called Help@ Notice Regarding Employment Scam which should be used.
Once we have emailed the users, please reply-all to the message from the system admins informing them we have reached out to the users.
The incident can then be resolved.

" KB0016874 General General - Library Resource & Service Access "

Who has access
Faculty/Staff/Students

Faculty, Staff, and Students may access online library services using their NetID login information. Checking out books is done using your physical Rutgers ID card. Current Faculty, Staff, and Students have access to the full suite of online library services.

New Faculty/Staff can check out items up to one month in advance of their start date. This requires either a Rutgers ID card, or other proof of current employment to be provided to the front desk of their desired library.

Course reserves

If a Senior Auditor in a course wants to access a resource on reserve, the faculty member or the auditors themselves provide the library their NetID and they will enter them into the file so they can have access to e-reserves. Kay Schechter in the Senior Auditor office is aware of the process. She requires each auditor to obtain an RU ID card and NetID. She handles the activation of the NetID.

Course reserves for students are available through the Reading List in students' Canvas courses. They can also be searched for directly within the reading list tool.

Alumni, Guests, Retirees, and Faculty Emeriti

Individuals who have an ALUMNI, GUEST, or Retiree GUEST roles can not access restricted library services online. They, and members of the general public, can continue to use the services from an on-campus location where login is not required. Faculty Emeriti may use their Rutgers login information to access library resources.

Accessing library facilities

Rutgers University Libraries include libraries, centers, and collections at physical facilities distributed across the Rutgers campuses. The following pages contain more information for each particular campus:

Visit and Study at Camden
Visit and Study at Newark
Visit and Study at New Brunswick
Visit and Study at the Health Sciences

In general, most libraries allow any individual to enter their facilities; however, individuals may be asked to show the contents of their backpacks, bags, or briefcases. Additionally, some library locations limit access to Rutgers students, faculty, and staff during late night and weekend hours - which would require a Rutgers identification card for admittance.

Accessing library services

The Libraries' online services are available to current faculty, students, staff, and faculty emeriti of the university. These individuals can access the services through their NetID login information. Some of these services include:

Booking A Space
Interlibrary Loans
Contacting Subject Specialists
Course Reserves
Databases

Anyone may use Ask A Librarian, the electronic reference service, wherever they are located. Anyone with Rutgers borrowing privileges may use My Account to view their library record and renew their books online. University affiliates may also qualify to request Rutgers materials, use instructional services, and article deliveries.

Borrowing privileges and library access

Current faculty, students, staff, and emeriti can check out books by using their physical Rutgers ID card and may access online library services using their NetID login information. Current faculty, students, staff, and emeriti have access to the full suite of online library services. Non-university affiliates may be granted guest borrower statuses at the libraries - where they would have to check in with the Library's Borrow and Return policies.

New Faculty/Staff can check out items up to one month in advance of their start date. This requires either a Rutgers ID card or other proof of current employment to be provided to the front desk of their desired library.

" KB0014205 General General - LinkedIn Learning "

What is LinkedIn Learning?

LinkedIn Learning (the upgraded platform of Lynda.com, shutdown in 2021) features thousands of video tutorials and training resources about business, technology, and creative skills. LinkedIn Learning offers industry-leading content and courses taught by expert instructors in leadership, project management, graphic design, photography, software development, and more. Courses range from beginner to advanced levels and can be taken to obtain a professional certificate or learn more about a personal hobby.

Who can access this service?

Rutgers provides free access to LinkedIn Learning for active Faculty, Staff, Students, and Guests.

Qualifying members of the university login to LinkedIn Learning using their NetID and password. KB0012583 is available for assistance with logging into LinkedIn Learning.

How can individuals get additional help?

If you have confirmed that a person knows their NetID and password but still cannot log in to LinkedIn Learning, you may open an incident task with ESD - Software Site Licensing for further escalation. If you are unfamiliar with opening incident tasks, reach out to a supervisor or a full-time staff member.

Important notes

If an individual has a personal LinkedIn Learning account, they should make a Rutgers LinkedIn Learning account to switch back and forth between their personal and Rutgers account to transfer history. For additional information, LinkedIn Learning provides a knowledge article on how to switch accounts and to transfer their account history.

" KB0014321 General General - Qualtrics Support "

Login

To log in or to set up an account, go to rutgers.qualtrics.com. The user will be directed to CAS to enter their NetID and password. If the user can see the terms of service page but is not redirected to Qualtrics after agreeing, have them try going to rutgers.qualtrics.com to restart the authentication process.

Troubleshooting

For general troubleshooting, search the Qualtrics Support website since it contains detailed step by step instructions: qualtrics.com/support.

For more information about Qualtrics, see it.rutgers.edu/qualtrics or check the Qualtrics KB.

To access an existing project or survey on Qualtrics, contact the person running that project or survey in order to get access. The owner must add them as a collaborator in order to access the survey. If they do not know the owner, reassign Incident to NB-HD - Qualtrics to investigate.

User groups

Only Rutgers brand admins can create/manage user groups. View this article for instructions.

Escalating

If you are unsure how to answer a Qualtrics question and it isn't covered by the documentation linked above, speak with a FT staff member. Most questions regarding how to set up or use Qualtrics are handled by the Help Desk, although questions regarding licensing, survey ownership, or Rutgers-based issues may be reassigned to the NB-HD - Qualtrics assignment group.

If it is a complex technical troubleshooting issue, users may contact Qualtrics Support directly for expert help by following these instructions.

" KB0014323 General General - RAD (Rutgers Active Directory) "

The Rutgers Active Directory (RAD) managed environment is based on Active Directory by Microsoft. All users within RAD have RAD admins in place to support them. The Help Desks should follow the guidance below for any user questions they may receive.

Those with access to CAT can search for the delegated admins of a specific computer that is connected to RAD and view all RAD admins associated with the OUs.



RAD Support - General Users
Account locked out / Can't log in to a machine on RAD

RAD accounts will only be locked out after 50 login attempts. The locks last 15 minutes. A possible cause of 50 login attempts could be a script continuously running that is using cached credentials. Users who are locked in RAD should be directed to their local RAD admin. If a RAD admin cannot be located, the Help Desk can send mail to rad-support@oit.rutgers.edu for assistance.

Forgot password

RAD uses the standard NetID and password. The user should be directed to reset their password at netid.rutgers.edu

Network drives
Users may need access given or removed for a user
Users may have existing access, but trouble connecting
Users may need help accessing from home
Users may need help retrieving backup or previous version of file

If a user is having any of the above issues with network drives on RAD, they should be directed to their RAD admins. If a RAD admin cannot be located, the Help Desk can send mail to rad-support@oit.rutgers.edu for assistance.

Network Printers

Problems with connection/installation/troubleshooting

If a user is having any of the above issues with network drives on RAD, they should be directed to their RAD admins. If a RAD admin cannot be located, the Help Desk can send mail to rad-support@oit.rutgers.edu for assistance.

Connection issues at login not related to account

If a user is having the issue above, they should be directed to their RAD admins. If a RAD admin cannot be located, the Help Desk can send mail to rad-support@oit.rutgers.edu for assistance.



RAD Support - RAD Admins

We may receive calls from domain administrators regarding this service. If you receive a call from a non-IT staff member regarding RAD, have them speak to their IT staff. However, we do have the following support guidance in the event that IT support calls in looking for assistance. Information can also be found at:

Rutgers Active Directory (RAD) page

Email address: rad-support@oit.rutgers.edu

ServiceNow Assignment Group: EAS - Rutgers Active Directory

If a RAD Admin calls the Help Desk looking for assistance please follow the procedure below:
For regular hours connect the OU Admin to RAD support. (2-hour response time)
Create a ticket in the EAS - Rutgers Active Directory assignment group
Send an email to rad-support@oit.rutgers.edu with the ticket number.
Escalated issues call directly.
Off-hours connect the OU Admin to RAD support. (response next morning)
Create a ticket in the EAS - Rutgers Active Directory assignment group
Send an email to rad-support@oit.rutgers.edu with the ticket number.
Escalate for enterprise-wide outages call directly.
How to Reset RAD Admin Password

If a user needs to reset their RAD Admin password they can do so by following the below instructions:

Direct the user to NetID Management page
Select Manage NetID Password from the left column
Log in with NetID and Password.
Select RAD Admin Account on the left
Log in with their NetID and Password
Once logged in they will be presented with a password change screen for the RAD admin account
How to Request RAD Admin Access (Elevated Access)
The requester can go to Rutgers RAD Admin Request System and submit their request.
Once the request is processed, the requester will need to log into the NetID Management page, and they will see an option for their ADM account.
They will need to set up their ADM account password before we're able to see that they have a RAD admin account in our NetID Management Tool.



NOTE:

There were some manually created ADM accounts that existed before the IDM website was setup.

If someone had an ADM account prior to the website going up they would still have to go through the process to request an ADM account. Instead of creating a new one, it would associate the existing one with their NetID account.

They should visit this site Rutgers RAD Admin Request System, and go through the steps to request it.

Once this process has been completed, they will then see the option for ADM account when they log into NetID Management page, and it should also be available in LDAP.



" KB0014324 General General - Raspberry Pi OS/Linux install "

Raspberry Pi OS

Raspberry Pi OS (formerly Raspbian) is slightly different from other Linux OSs. Flashing it onto a SD card or USB drive will have the machine boot directly into the OS rather than an installer (a traditional desktop OS like Windows or CentOS will boot into an installer to install the OS onto a hard drive).

The following steps should be done on a separate machine (Ex: a laptop with an SD card slot like the Resnet laptops. You may need an adapter from microSD to standard SD).

To install Raspberry Pi OS, download the ISO (zip in this case) file of choice from the Raspberry Pi software page; there are three different packages (Note: Current versions run on Debian 12/Bookworm at the time of writing but this is subject to change with new updates):
Raspberry Pi OS with desktop and recommended software
This is the heaviest version of Raspberry Pi OS. The OS comes bundled with the full software suite - this includes software like LibreOffice, Minecraft, VNC Viewer and more. If not everything included in this package is needed (most of these are games or programming environments and can be installed later if needed) check the versions below.
Raspberry Pi OS with desktop
This version only includes the base OS. It has some software such as PDF viewers, Chromium, and video players, but does not include any of the additional software listed above - though they can always be installed individually later.
Raspberry Pi OS Lite
The lightest version of Raspberry Pi OS. This is a headless (terminal only) install. Only use this version if the SD card is 4GB or less, or battery life is of concern, and you know what you are doing without a GUI.
Download and Install BalenaEtcher. The Resnet laptops will have BalenaEtcher installed.
Once BalenaEtcher is opened, select the Raspberry Pi OS ISO that you downloaded, select the SD/USB to flash it onto, and press Flash.
Once it s done flashing, insert the drive into the Raspberry Pi and boot. It should load into Raspberry Pi OS on its own.

If the device does not boot into the desktop, you can find troubleshooting steps here.

Other Linux OS

Follow the same steps above, except you will be downloading the OS from its respective site and step 4 is now:

Insert the drive into the machine that will be using the OS. Boot from the USB/SD card and follow the installation instructions. Make sure to install the OS onto the correct hard drive, otherwise you will wipe all data already on the drive.

" KB0014348 General General - RUHere Campaign "

A student will be considered enrolled if they complete the RUHere process, whether or not the student has submitted a term bill or has an outstanding balance. All registered students who log into a website utilizing the Central Authentication Service (CAS) will be automatically redirected to a RUHere dialog box (pop-up) prior to the start of each term. All students must indicate either Yes or No to the RUHere question to verify their intention to enroll for the given semester.

Below is a screenshot of the RUHere prompt:

If a student responds Yes to the RUHere dialog, they will be considered enrolled, financial aid will be disbursed to their account, and they will not be deregistered for classes for non-payment or an open balance on their term bill. If a student responds No to the RUHere dialog, they will be considered not enrolled, financial aid will not disburse and their registration will be canceled at the conclusion of the add/drop period.

Please note that this semester, all students will receive a confirmation email whether they selected yes or no to the campaign. Below are sample messages that will be generated by the system for students who respond based on the Spring 2019 semester:

System generated message for students who respond no from email: ruhere@rutgers.edu: Subject: Confirmation of RUHere Process Thank you for completing the RU Here process. Based on our records, you do NOT plan to attend the spring 2019 semester. As a result, your course registration will be cancelled. For information on the withdrawaln policy and process, visit our website at studentabc.rutgers.edu. We would like to know more about what prevented you from attending this semester. Please take a few minutes to provide your feedback. If your intentions have changed, and you plan to attend the spring 2019 semester, email ruhere@rutgers.edu.Thank you, The RUHere Implementation Team
System generated message for students who respond yes from email: ruhere@rutgers.edu. Subject: RUHere | Successful Completion Thank you for completing the RUHere process and confirming your attendance for spring 2019. We would greatly appreciate feedback on your experience. Please take a few minutes to complete the following survey. We look forward to hearing from you. Thank you, The RUHere Implementation Team

If a student does not respond to the RUHere question, they will not be able to proceed to the destination website and will be blocked from accessing any application that requires CAS authentication, including, but not limited to email, myRutgers, and learning management systems (Canvas).

Some students may also see a pop up asking them to agree to the Financial Responsibility Statement (FRS). Most students will have already agreed to the FRS by the time the RUHere campaign starts because it is required to agree to the FRS before viewing your term bill. Below is a screenshot for the FRS statement:

You can also tell how and when the user responded to the campaign by using the "Audit Campaigns" option in the NetID Management Admin Tool. Below is a sample screenshot from the NetID Management Admin Tool.

If a user has any questions regarding what these campaigns mean OR if they want to change the answer they have provided, please direct them to email ruhere@rutgers.edu. Technical issues with the RUHere campaign should be directed to idm_support@email.rutgers.edu. If technical issues arise with RUHere, please be sure to note this in Slack so that we can coordinate support procedures.

" KB0014379 General General - search.rutgers.edu "

Search.rutgers.edu is a great way to help locate resources within the Rutgers Community. The page allows users to search for people, websites, buildings, and RBHS faculty and staff. The tool is available to the public and can be used to look up contact information, find helpful links, and obtain building addresses.

Note: For machines with an IP address that are not in the RU network, search.rutgers.edu will not allow them to perform more than 200 searches in a 24-hr period. There is an exceptions list, and there may be a way for Administrative Computing Services (ACS) Database Admins (DBAs) to manually unlock someone.

" KB0014381 General General - Senior Citizen Audit Program "

This special program was created by the Rutgers University Board of Governors to permit retired New Jersey residents, age 62 or older, to attend courses on a space-available, noncredit basis. There are no tuition costs for auditing courses.

Only courses offered during the spring and fall semesters on the Camden, Newark, or New Brunswick campuses are open to auditors. No other courses offered at the university are available under the Senior Citizen Audit Program, therefore Winter Session and Summer Session courses are not covered by this program. Additionally, the cost of textbooks is not included in the program.
Auditors are permitted to audit on-line courses.
Courses listed as closed in the online Schedule of Classes those that have reached maximum capacity are not open to auditors.
Auditing is a courtesy offered at the discretion of the professor. If the professor informs the Auditor that auditors are not permitted, they will need to select another course.
Deadlines to submit the Permission-to-Audit Form as well as other forms can be found on the Senior Citizen Audit Program website.
The Auditor's name will not appear on the professor's roster. The Auditor's status is that of a nonregistered auditor.

If auditors have further questions, they may reach out to the Senior Citizen Audit Program directly.

" KB0014386 General General - Student Self Reporting Absences "

The Student Self-Reporting Absences (SSRA) Application allows Rutgers University students to report their absences during a semester.

How to Access the SSRA Application

Students can access the SSRA application in two ways:

Direct link: https://sims.rutgers.edu/ssra/
Via myRutgers Portal:
Log into myRutgers.
Navigate to the My Apps tab.
Under the Classes & Degree section, click on Self-Reporting Absence Application.
Reporting an Absence
Students may report up to 7 consecutive days of absence, beginning with the current day.
Students should contact their dean for further guidance if the absence is longer than 7 days.
Students can select a reason for their absence:
Bereavement
Medical
Personal Issue
Other (default)
Notifications and Confirmation
A confirmation email will be sent to the student s Rutgers email address (associated with their NetID). Students can also choose to send the confirmation to an additional email address.
Notifications are automatically sent to all graders of the affected courses.
If no graders are listed or do not have associated email addresses, the student will see a send failure message on the confirmation page.
If no success or failure message appears, the student has no scheduled courses on the selected absence days.
These days are still recorded as absence dates without course conflicts.
Need Help?

For additional details, review the support document: [ SSRA-Support.pdf ]

" KB0014434 General General - Website Creation/Hosting (Sites@Rutgers) "

What is sites.rutgers.edu?

Sites@Rutgers is the "site-in-a-box" solution that offers hassle-free, no-fuss website creation Rutgers Style. Now, professors, student organizations, and project initiatives can build their own feature-rich Rutgers University website, all in a matter of days.

The Sites@Rutgers page can be found at: https://sites.rutgers.edu/

Who can access this service?

Professors, student organizations, and project initiatives are eligible to use this service. Students and Departments cannot request a website through Sites@Rutgers.

Who provides support?

The Help Desk uses the information found at https://sites.rutgers.edu/frequently-asked-questions/ to provide support to end-users who will be directed to call or email the Help Desk for support. If Help Desk Team Members need to escalate issues to Web Consulting Services (WCS), please email sites-at-rutgers@oit.rutgers.edu with all details regarding the issue. WCS will then evaluate the problem and provide an appropriate response for the Help Desk to relay to the user. The Help Desk will respond to the user and resolve the issue.

Common Questions and Answers
User Management
Adding Users to Sites
User Role Access
Support Agent User Role
Launching Site
Domain Names
Custom Domains
Site Functionality
Issue Escalation Process
User Management

Sites@Rutgers Sites include various user roles, allowing different access levels to a site's pages, blog posts, and more. Refer to the User Roles documentation page for a more detailed breakdown of what each role can see and do.

Adding Users to Sites

Site Owners are the only type of users able to add or edit other users' roles on their site.

I want to add another user to my site as an editor or author, but the dashboard says they must first log into Sites@Rutgers. How can they do that?

New users can log into Sites@Rutgers by visiting sites.rutgers.edu subdirectory /login. After logging in, they can be added to the site and assigned an appropriate user role by the Site Owner.

Check the User Roles documentation page for more information on adding and editing users' roles.

I don't know how to add users. Can you add users to my site?

No, only the Site Owner may add users to a site or modify an existing user's role.

Can I add a non-Rutgers-affiliated user to my site?

Yes. They will first need to submit a request for a NetID with guest access by visiting https://requests.rutgers.edu/guest/guest.htm. Once they have a NetID, you can add them to your site like any other user.

User Role Access

Some users experience confusion about what level of access their role provides them, and expect site privileges they do not have. The Site Owner needs to provide them with access to these features accordingly.

I am an undergraduate working in a professor's lab, and I've been tasked with editing her new lab's Sites@Rutgers website. I can only access the site's blog and events through the dashboard. Why don't I have the option to create or edit pages?

You must have the Editor user role to create or edit site pages. Only the Site Owner themself can provide users with this role.

For more information on permissions for each user role, check the User Roles documentation page.

I need to have more access to this site. Can you upgrade my role?

No, only the Site Owner may add users to a site or modify an existing user's role.

Support Agent User Role

This role provides the user the same access as a Site Owner, except they cannot manage users and their roles.
If a Help Desk team member needs to access a Sites@Rutgers site, the Site Owner may add that team member to their site as a user and assign them the Support Agent role. Once the Help Desk team member is done providing support, they may remove themselves from the site.

Launching Site

When Sites@Rutgers Sites are created, they are set to Maintenance Mode. Maintenance Mode only makes a site visible to logged-in users who have already been added. This allows Site Owners to fully create their site before it has been made publicly accessible. Site Owners can turn Maintenance Mode on and off as they please.

How do I remove Maintenance Mode on my site?

You can remove your site from Maintenance Mode by navigating to the Customizer via the dashboard menu. Under Site Settings, uncheck the box that enables Maintenance Mode.

For more information on toggling Maintenance Mode, check the Site Settings documentation page.

Domain Names

By default, Sites@Rutgers Sites are provided with a subdirectory under sites.rutgers.edu. Based on the information submitted through the Site Request form, such as site name and purpose, WCS provides the site with a relevant subdirectory. For example:

sites.rutgers.edu/person-name
sites.rutgers.edu/organization-name
sites.rutgers.edu/project-name
Custom Domains

A custom domain for a Sites@Rutgers site is defined as a subdomain of rutgers.edu. While other Rutgers web services may provide subdomains at no cost or allow site owners to submit their domain requests directly to Rutgers NOC, this is not available for Sites@Rutgers sites.

Can I get a custom domain name for my Sites@Rutgers site? How much does this cost?

Custom domains on Sites@Rutgers are available at an additional cost and cannot be purchased for a single site. Departments can buy these domains as a bundle of 25 custom domains, each costing $2,500 annually. Departments may purchase additional bundles as needed. Departments approve and request custom domains on the website owner's behalf. These requests should be addressed to the WCS Sites@Rutgers Team via sites-at-rutgers@oit.rutgers.edu. WCS will be responsible for all DNS changes and custom domain setup before site launch.

What constitutes a custom domain name?

A custom domain for a Sites@Rutgers Site is a subdomain of rutgers.edu. Departments approve and request custom domains on the website owner's behalf.

Site Functionality

While the Sites@Rutgers platform is a WordPress Multisite, not all WordPress features are available to our Site Owners. This includes adding custom code, plugins, or styles. On the other hand, some functionalities of Sites@Rutgers are constrained by WordPress' capabilities and cannot be changed.

Is there a way to organize my site's media into subfolders?

No. This folder structure is determined automatically as files are uploaded to the site. This is a WordPress functionality and cannot be manually changed.

Can I add custom CSS to my site?

No. At this time, it is not possible for Site Owners to add custom CSS to their Sites@Rutgers sites.

Can I upload files to my site via SFTP?

No. The Sites@Rutgers platform does not allow SFTP file transfers or uploads to individual sites. All user files must be uploaded via the WordPress media library.

Can I add WordPress plugins to my site?

No. Sites@Rutgers does not support custom plugins beyond what comes packaged with the platform. If you want to add additional WordPress plugins to your site, it must be hosted outside the Sites@Rutgers platform. If you require a more flexible solution with custom features, Rutgers WCS provides consulting services for a fee. For more information, visit the WCS website.

Can I have [additional feature not included with Sites@Rutgers] for my site?

Currently, that particular feature is unavailable on the Sites@Rutgers platform.

Issue Escalation Process

If Help Desk Team Members need to escalate issues to WCS, open a ticket with the EAS - Sites@Rutgers Service group or email sites-at-rutgers@oit.rutgers.edu with all details regarding the issue. WCS will then evaluate the problem and provide an appropriate response for the Help Desk to relay to the user. The Help Desk will respond to the user and resolve the issue.

" KB0014201 IDM IDM - Identity Management "

To access most services at the University, people will need an account, which consists of a NetID and a password. NetIDs are automatically generated using letters and a unique number when they are added to OR (Open Registry). Before 2011, users were allowed to create their own NetID. Therefore, not all NetIDs follow this format.

During the NetID activation process, Users will be able to select the services (ScarletMail, Box, Zoom, etc.) that they would like to activate along with their NetID. They can only choose the services for which they are eligible. At that time, they will also be able to set their alias email address and a delivery email address.

Graduate students' admission information is processed from their application to the SRDB (Student Records Database). When a student responds to the admission letter, the admit role is generated from the SRDB and sent to the PDB (People Database), which then generates a NetID. This process takes overnight to process. The next day, the student can activate their NetID. This process generally takes 1 to 2 business days.

" KB0014988 IDM IDM - Chosen Name Initiative "

Please note: it is critical to always respect a user by calling them by their indicated name and pronouns. If a user tells you their name or pronouns, use them when addressing the user and updating the incident.

To assist users with setting their chosen name, the following resources are available:

For students, directions are available at https://scarlethub.rutgers.edu/registrar/personal-information-updates/name-change/
For faculty and staff, directions are available at https://uhr.rutgers.edu/worklife-balance/submitting-chosen-name

If a user is a student worker and has updated their name through the student name change process, they will still need to complete the steps above for faculty and staff to see their chosen name reflected in relevant systems.

It is important to note that a chosen name will replace a legal name in multiple administrative systems. However, not all systems utilize the same information source for a user's name, and subsequently, will not update automatically once a chosen name has been set. The following systems will be updated within 24-48 hours:

Rutgers Search Directory
myRutgers Portal
Class Rosters
Canvas
REGIS

We can also provide directions to assist a user with setting a chosen name in the following platforms:

Email (How to set a personalized email alias)

ScarletMail/Google Apps (edit your profile)
Zoom (How to edit your Zoom display profile name)
WebEx (How to edit your WebEx profile name display)
ID Cards (update your RU ID through Identity and Access Management)

The following needs a manual intervention to get the chosen name updated:

Adobe Creative Cloud requires cutting a Task for ESD - Software Site Licensing to have an Adobe System Admin manually update the chosen/legal name change.

If a user calls or emails regarding setting a chosen name, you can direct them to the above resources and assist them with completing the steps outlined above.

After they have set a chosen name using the above steps, they should wait 48 hours before it is reflected in Rutgers systems.

If a user has waited that amount of time and still does not see their chosen name in certain systems, please note in the incident which systems they do not see the correct name and escalate to FT staff, who will reach out to the service owner for the service to be corrected.
