" KB0011212 IDM IDM - Deprovisioning "

When a Rutgers affiliate no longer has an active role at the university, their NetID is set to an inactive state. This will immediately revoke access to any and all services that use their NetID to log in, including Rutgers Connect and ScarletMail.

For faculty and staff, this process is immediate upon losing all active roles.
For legacy (non-RBHS) Rutgers students, they will have a one semester grace period before they are deprovisioned.
RBHS Rutgers students will have four months before they are deprovisioned.
The Student Worker role does not qualify as an active role if it is the only active role the user has.
Users who graduate or retire should have active roles (retiree and alumni) which allow them to remain active and will not be deprovisioned. Faculty, staff and RBHS students using Rutgers Connect will only have access to ScarletMail with these active statuses.

If a user is deprovisioned because they do not have an active role, their status in the NetID admin tool will show User record is not in LDAP.

If a person with a deprovisioned account gets an active role again, their account will be added to LDAP but inactive until the user activates it again. You can assist these users by directing them to activate their NetID through the standard NetID activation flow. The account will be unlocked (only when the reason code is Deprovisioned ), and NetID will be activated. The user will also be able to reset their password during the activation flow.

" KB0012275 IDM IDM - DUO Hardware Token pickup process "

The user places an order for a token (or token replacement) from software.rutgers.edu according to their current role:

Faculty/Staff
Students
Replacements

Depending on their role, users will be presented with "Delivery Method" options to receive their token (delivery or pickup).

Pickup at the local Help Desk or mail to the home.
Delivery may incur a charge of up to $7.95 for mailed delivery to the user's home.
'Help Desk' option will require the user to come in for token pickup at their specified location and is free of charge.

Help Desk options are New Brunswick, Newark, and Camden.
If a user contacts the Help Desk and states they cannot come on to campus for token pickup:

If the user has already purchased a token from the software portal, the Help Desk will leave a comment in the auto-generated incident created by the software portal (Software Portal Request #, Security Hardware Token) stating that the user is requesting token shipment. Help Desk will collect the requested shipping address from the user.
Help Desk will create an incident task and assign it to ESD - Software Site Licensing
The token will then be shipped to the address by the Software team. The user will not incur any shipping costs.
If the user has not yet purchased a token, the Help Desk will guide them to the software portal to purchase the token and then follow the steps above.

Once an order is entered and submitted by the user, the processing of the order will follow the workflow, dependent on the delivery method chosen:

The delivery option will be processed by the Software team staff and processed/mailed to the user within 1 business day.
Help Desk pickup will be processed by Help Desk FT staff using the instructions below.
Help Desk location pickup - Processing orders:
Software.rutgers.edu will send the DUO Token pickup order to ServiceNow for processing.

The order will generate an incident in ServiceNow
The inbound address is set as "tokenpickup_sn@oit.rutgers.edu"
Service Offering set as "Software Portal"
The assignment group is set as "ESD - Software Portal Fulfillment" (accessible to HD FT staff)
The description will contain full-order info from software.rutgers.edu
User will receive an email notification with the subject "Rutgers University Software Portal - Order xxxxxx Updated" with a link to Help Desk hours and location
The Help Desk FT will provide them with a token, accompanied by an instructions sheet, to activate, add the serial number to the incident, and resolve it in ServiceNow.

" KB0011230 IDM IDM - DUO Opt-Out Rules for Roles "

The opt-out rules for DUO depend on the roles the user has.

Roles that prevent a person from opt-out by default are:

Admit
Admit Coming
Student
Staff
Faculty
Guest
Student Worker
Emeritus
Retiree

Roles that can opt-out unless they have additional Staff, Faculty or Guest roles:

Alumni

Users who are restricted from opting out by default are required to call the Help Desk. There may be business/technical reasons that require a user to opt-out. If a user calls in, a full-time staff member can grant the user back the ability to opt out of two-step authentication. If a user is requesting the ability to opt-out of DUO, please contact a full-time staff member or a member of the Faculty/Staff Help Desk.

" KB0014158 IDM IDM - Early Employee and Guest NetIDs Requests Status "

Occasionally users and or sponsors will call in asking for an update on their Early Employee NetID or Guest Account requests. To look up the status of one of these requests do the following

Go to http://registry.rutgers.edu and log in
Click on View Status/Reporting under the type of account you are looking for the status of.
Requests can be searched by

Request ID (Which should have been provided to the sponsor)
Sponsors NetID (Guest Requests only)
Organizational Unit (Guest Requests only)
For Sponsor NetID and Organizational Unit selection, a date range will also be required before you can search
A request has two types of status

Approval Status - This is the status of the sponsor's approval
Process Status - This is the status of the processing of the request
Each of the statuses can be:

Finished

If Approval Status is listed as Finished that means that the sponsor has approved it.
If Process Status is listed as Finished that means that the request was processed.
Pending

If Approval Status is listed as Pending that means the Sponsor has not approved it.
If Process Status is listed as Pending that means that the request is waiting to be processed.
Escalated

If Approval Status is listed as Escalated that means that the request has been waiting for a few days without any action from the sponsor.
If Process Status is listed as Escalated that means that the request has been waiting for a few days without any action from the person processing the account.
Clicking on the request ID number gives more information about the request. This could include any notes as to why a request was denied or any special handling instructions for that request.

" KB0014867 IDM IDM - Early Employee NetID Request "

OIT will provide Early Employee NetIDs to Rutgers employees (including RBHS) with Social Security Numbers who need access to electronic services to perform their assigned duties and whose on-boarding process has been delayed. In practice, a department can use Early Employee NetID process for anyone that is a future F/S member but needs access ASAP. Early Employee NetID does NOT give full access and it does not replace the need to complete on-boarding via the Rutgers Integrated Administrative System (RIAS). As such, it is expected that the employee and their department will work with HR to ensure the on-boarding issues are resolved within 30 days.

If a person calls regarding obtaining a NetID and they are a future faculty or staff member, we should first investigate why they have not yet received roles. If the (pending) employee is confident that their department has started the on-boarding process, then the Help Desk should first check the HR Data Error Logs see if the problem lies with conflicting database information. IDCheck can be checked for roles when RBHS may be involved.

While any person with an active faculty or staff role can submit a request for an Early Employee NetID for another, the (pending) employee should speak to their departmental Human Resources representative or a hiring authority about using the following link to request an Early Employee NetID.

Requirements

To obtain an Early Employee NetID, a person must be a future Faculty/Staff member and MUST have a Social Security Number. This criteria is put in place to avoid issues that may arise when the user is properly on-boarded via RIAS. When being paid by the University, you must have a valid Social Security Number, so having a database entry without an SSN may lead to problems in the future.

Turnaround

The Help Desk provides their best effort to complete Early Employee NetID requests in one business day. A full time staff member is assigned to process Early Employee NetID requests each weekday.

Giving out NetID policies

NetIDs are not to be provided to anyone. The user should look up their own NetID using the NetID Lookup tool. If requested, please direct the user to the How do I find my NetID? public knowledge article.

" KB0014198 IDM IDM - Faculty Staff Onboarding Issues "

In order for a new faculty or staff member to activate a NetID (or for student employees to have access to Employee Self Service) they must be added to payroll by their department. This information is then pushed to OR (Open Registry), which allows the user to activate their NetID or access role-based services. Depending on what type of employee they are hired as, this process can take anywhere from 1-3 days.

If the employee has an existing entry in OR and the information entered into payroll conflicts with this entry, one of two things may happen:

OR catches the conflict and generates a report about the issue (this is added to a logfile accessible to CSq and FT staff only). The user's payroll entry is held until the conflict can be resolved. Once we have confirmed with the user what information is correct, FT staff can contact IDM support to have them merge the two entries.
OR does not catch the conflict and creates a second OR record for the user, complete with a second NetID. The user may also be informed by email that they can activate a second NetID. If this occurs, we will need to confirm what information is correct AND what NetID the user wishes to keep. After FT contacts IDM support and the records are merged, the extraneous NetID will be deleted and accounts associated with that NetID will be merged to the user's accounts for the current NetID.

If a user is calling who you believe has already been entered into payroll, (i.e., 5-7 business days after the payroll information has been submitted), but can't activate their NetID or log into services, please consult with FT for additional guidance. We may have them reach out to their department's HR team for further assistance on delay in processing the NetID. They can also reach out to Rutgers One Source for more information on their NetID as well, but should only do so after the incident has been reviewed by FT.

" KB0014169 IDM IDM - Guest Access "

The guest process is relatively complex. Before taking any action in regards to providing answers about guests please consult a Supervisor first. The following information will provide a base understanding of the guest process. However there are exceptions to all occasions. Any unanswerable questions should be directed to FT.

What is a guest?
What access does a guest get?
What is the difference between a Guest NetID, Guest Account, and a Guest Role?
What is a Herd?
How Are Guest Requests Submitted?
Updating Guest Information
Services:
ID Cards
ScarletMail
RU Wireless
VPN
Canvas
Connect
Libraries
Special Cases
Senior Citizen Auditing Program
Retirees
Housing Residents
RBHS Guests
Audits
Auditing the Guest Role
What is a guest?
Typically a guest is anyone who is affiliated with the University but is either not being paid by the University or officially enrolled as a student.
A guest must be sponsored by an active faculty/staff member.

This means a guest cannot sponsor another guest nor can a guest sponsor themselves. A student worker also cannot sponsor a guest.
Note that other campuses also process guests (i.e. Newark, Camden, and RBHS) for which the guest processing is slightly different. It is important to identify which campus the guest is affiliated with.
Many guests are affiliated with certain programs such as Senior Citizen Auditing, or may be Retirees.

It is important to ascertain as much detail as you can as to what a guest needs and from where are they coming from because each has a slightly different process.
What access does a guest get?
The level of access a guest obtains is unique to each and every guest.

Examples of access include but are not limited to RU Wireless, Rutgers Email account, and ID cards.
Guests will have a sponsor that is responsible for maintaining the account and providing access.
Guests have the option to provide their Social security Number (SSN) when submitting a guest request.
If they provide their SSN, it will be on their record in OR and should be used to activate their NetID.
If they do not provide their SSN, a Pseudo one is created for them in OR. The sponsor provides their guest with their own pseudo-SSN that is meant to be used in place of an actual SSN during NetID activation and password reset procedures.

The sponsor has access to this pseudo-SSN and is able to provide it to the respective guest whenever necessary.
The pseudo-SSN should be located in a prior email sent to one of the guest's personal email addresses.
You should ask what services the guest needs access to.
Many guests will only require a minimal subset of the services possible.
Certain services will require additional steps to obtain. Some of these services will be outlined below.
What is the difference between a Guest NetID, Guest Account, and a Guest Role?
All of the above are individual items but tie in to each other.
Guest Role - A role is an individual's affiliation with the University. An individual can have multiple concurrent roles. Examples of such roles are faculty, staff, student, admit coming, student worker, alumni, and guest.

In most cases a guest role will not be assigned to anyone with an active faculty/staff role.
By having an active guest role in Open Registry, a guest is able to activate their NetID and obtain access to certain services.
Guest NetID - A guest NetID is the same as any other NetID in that it is the unique identifier assigned an individual when they are entered into Open Registry.

If a guest is brand new to the University they will be assigned a NetID.
If a guest had a prior affiliation, they will assume their old NetID, but they will now have an updated role to go with the NetID
Guest Account - An account is an entry for a particular service.

An example of this is a Connect account. A guest can request additional access to a service like Connect which would give them an account on the email system.
Accounts can run independent of roles. In fact it is quite possible to have an active account without having an active role.
Some accounts are dictated by Herds.
What is a Herd?
A herd is a specific grouping used to manage service access.
You can view what herds an individual is in through Open Registry.

To do this look up a user and then select on of their roles.
On the next page find where it says Network Herds. Under this section will be all the herds the user has had.
Looking at the dates will let you know how long that herd is active for.
The standard herd that a new guest will be put in is ASC_MISC.
How Are Guest Requests Submitted?

Active faculty/staff members can submit guest requests electrically by using the form found at requests.rutgers.edu

Short-term Guests (those equal to, or less than 14 days), like those created for RCPID Requests, can not have their Guest NetID renewed.

Updating Guest Information

Rutgers guests can contact the Help Desk to notify us of desired changes. FT staff can update Guest info.

Services
ID Cards
In order to apply for an ID card a guest must first have an active role in Open Registry
For this reason a guest NetID request will need to be submitted
Note:
A guest is not required to activate their NetID in order to obtain an ID card
Once the guest has been entered into the registry they must still take additional steps.
An ID card can still work even if a role is inactive so long as whatever building or service the ID card is being used for does not check for active roles.
Admit Coming users can only obtain ID cards after a certain date. If a sponsor wants to create a guest NetID to get an ID card early, this is the wrong course of action to take. Ask the sponsor for what reason they need an ID card early. If the guest is going to be employed then the sponsor should have that person on-boarded instead.
ScarletMail
By default a guest can create a ScarletMail account during NetID activation or afterwards by going through Service Activation
RU Wireless
Once a guest has been entered into Open Registry and they have activated their NetID they will be able to log on to RU Wireless Secure
VPN
Once a guest has been entered into Open Registry, they will need to activate their NetID and also setup Duo two-factor authentication before they will be able to utilize Rutgers VPN.
Once they have Duo setup, they can go to the Service Activation page to enable VPN.
Guests are NOT authorized to use the VPN to access services they are not entitled to (such as libraries resources)
Canvas
Once a guest has been entered into Open Registry and they have activated their NetID they will be able to log in to Canvas.
However unless they have been added to a course by their NetID they will not see any courses listed.
Connect
To gain access, their sponsor should reach out to their local Delegated Administrators to provision an account for the Guest and to have their UPN changed to the correct Domain.
Libraries
By default, guests are restricted from off-campus access to University library resources (borrowing books or accessing articles/journals online). They may use the libraries in-person, and can check out up to 25 books for up to 6 weeks.
Guests who require access to Library resources will need to contact the libraries directly; see https://www.libraries.rutgers.edu/public_service_policies/guest_borrowe for details.
Guests are NOT authorized to use the VPN to access services they are not entitled to (such as libraries resources)
Virtual Labs
Virtual labs requires a Rutgers connect license, either student or fac/staff. Guest will need their sponsor to reach out to their Rutgers Connect Delegated Administrators to request a Rutgers Connect be provisioned for the Guest, as this is not done automatically.
With the exception of Community Affairs Guests, as outlined below, who get a student Connect license automatically.
Special Cases

The following are a few exceptions to the standard guest process. You should refer to FT if any questions arise in taking calls of this nature.

Senior Citizen Auditing Program
Rutgers Community Affairs runs this program in which senior citizens can sit in on Rutgers classes. These guests are only allotted access for one semester.
For more information look here: https://community.rutgers.edu/programs-services/program-directory/senior-citizen-audit-program
Effective 6/11/24 MaCS has evaluated the Senior Citizen Audit Program (https://community.rutgers.edu/programs-services/program-directory/senior-citizen-audit-program) and believes this population is eligible for Microsoft Student licenses. Therefore, we have updated Service Activation to provide GUESTs of "COMMUNITY AFFAIRS" a Rutgers Connect LIMITED license (OneDrive/Office/Virtual Computer Labs/etc.; no mailbox). Senior Citizen Audit participants are PHI=N, so those requiring Rutgers email should still be directed to ScarletMail. (This remains unchanged.)
Retirees
Some guests of the University are actually retirees.
Historically faculty or staff members who have been with the University for over 10 years are eligible for a Retiree Guest Account.
However this is not an automatic process. To obtain an retiree account the retiree should contact the Benefits Department, University Human Resources ASBII, 57 US Highway 1, New Brunswick, NJ 08901-8554 (848 -932-3990) who will verify the person's retirement status.
If someone asks about converting their account to guest/retiree status before they have actually retired, have them contact OneSource.
Retiree Guest accounts must be renewed yearly in September. There is no way to renew the account for longer periods of time or at any other part of the year. Retirees, like other Guests, may not have access to some University services (e.g., online library services or software.rutgers.edu), depending on the criteria each service checks for.
Housing Residents
Rutgers offers family living solutions on-campus in various residence halls. These residences mostly reside on Busch campus. If the spouse of a Rutgers affiliate is residing on campus with the user and requests a guest NetID in order to have swipe access to the building as well as access to RU Wireless networks, the spouse and Rutgers affiliate must go to the Assignments Office, located in the Davidson Hall on Busch Campus.
More information regarding this office, such as location and hours, can be found below:

https://ruoncampus.rutgers.edu/about/contact-us
RBHS Guests
Guest requests can be submitted through the standard requests.rutgers.edu process. They get approved by specific RBHS staff after being submitted.
If a caller from another RBHS school/unit needs a guest account they should be directed to their local IT. For RBHS units that do not have a school designation or the RBHS local IT support is asking for assistance, then ask the Fac/Staff HD managers as they may process those.
Audits
In order to maintain accurate records and to preserve system security audits are conducted on both Guest Roles and certain accounts
Auditing the Guest Role
Anyone with an active guest role in Open Registry will receive 2 automated emails (one at 30 days out and one at 15 days out) when their role is near expiration. This applies only to the guest role. You may receive calls from users stating they have already taken action in one aspect or another. As a supervisor you should:
Verify the actual message by having the caller forward it.
Look up the caller and see what their role says and what herds they are a part of.
Depending on what you see you may take different action.
If they only have an acs_misc herd they need to have their sponsor reply to the automated audit email
If you are ever unsure please speak with FT.

Here is an example of what the automated email looks like for the guest:

Dear Jayesh Ratnam,

Your Rutgers Guest role will expire in 30 days. If you continue to need access to Rutgers resources, please notify sponsor John Brennan. Your sponsor will receive a similar email to remind them to provide approval to renew the Guest role if the Guest role is still needed. If you wish to submit a new Guest request with a new sponsor, you may do so by following the main guest request link https://requests.rutgers.edu/app/ui/#guestrequest. Otherwise no action is required.

Please contact the Office of Information Technology Help Desk if you have questions:

Office of Information Technology (OIT) Help Desk

https://it.rutgers.edu/help-support

833-OIT-HELP

" KB0015602 IDM IDM - Identity Theft "

A user may have their account flagged for identity theft by the Information Security Office. The ISO is the one to determine if this should be added to their account and are the only ones that can add/remove this flag to a user's account. The user will have the identity theft flag on their account for 3 years. During this time they have the following restrictions:

Unable to use the forgot password feature via two factor authentication
Unable to use the forgot password feature via security questions
Their NetID will not show up when searched on the NetID Lookup Tool.
Flags you will see regarding identity theft

Auditing a person in NetID Admin will show Added Identity Theft status in the logs.

Searching a NetID in NetID Admin will show WARNING: User with Identity Theft Status.

Searching a user in Open Registry (OR) will show **WARNING: User with Identity Theft Status** under Generate New Activation Key.

Communication with User

Users with the identity theft flag will receive the following communication when it is added to their account:

Subject: Rutgers Added Identity Theft Status

Dear Student,

Identity Theft Status has been added to your account.
This means you cannot reset your password using Forgotten Password method with Two factor Authentication method. Also, NetID lookup will not show your NetID.
This change will be in effect for the next 3 years.

If you have any questions, please contact OIT Help Desk by phone at 833-OIT-HELP (833-648-4357)

This is an automated message. Please do not reply.

They will receive this communication when it is removed:

Subject: Rutgers Removed Identity Theft Status

Dear Student,

Identity Theft Status has been removed from your account.
This means you can reset your password using Forgotten Password method with all available methods. Also, NetID lookup will show your NetID.

If you have any questions, please contact OIT Help Desk by phone at 833-OIT-HELP (833-648-4357)

This is an automated message. Please do not reply.

Resetting Password

User will not be able to use the forgot password feature via two factor authentication or security questions, and will be unable to find their NetID on the NetID Lookup Tool. This message will be shown:

Information not found. Please contact Help Desk if you believe the information entered is correct.

They will need to contact the Help Desk for assistance with resetting their password.

Help Desk should use extreme caution when assisting users with resetting their password. When in doubt, the user must come in to the Help Desk with valid government ID for us to be able to provide them an Activation Key.

When assisting a user with this issue, we do not inform them of this flag on their account. You may use the following message if the user needs assistance resetting their password:

"Due to additional security requirements to ensure the integrity of your account, it's required that you contact the Help Desk to reset your password. You may come into the Help Desk with Valid government ID and we can assist you in person."



" KB0015279 IDM IDM - Identity/Data mismatch issues "

If a user is trying to activate their NetID or having other issues related to a data error (ex: wrong DOB) you should reach out to FT to investigate and verify the issue. Only once approved by FT can they be directed back to where the data was entered to have it corrected.

Students: OneStop
Faculty/Staff: Departmental or University HR
Alumni: OneStop
Guests: Help Desk FT

For other specific cases on where to redirect patrons to please view the ID Number Information Guide.

If a user has multiple roles and the data between them do not match up, FT should check the data error logs:

Navigate to the error logs
Select the appropriate Error Log to view based on the role having the data mismatch.
Confirm user shows up in the log.
Verify ID.
Confirm correct record and data.
Open a Task for EAS - Identity and Access Management.
Include which record and data is correct.
Never include sensitive information in the Task (ex: SSN).
If necessary, you may indicate sensitive number changes while not providing the full number and being as specific as possible (ex: "change last digit of SSN from 1 to 2").

" KB0014045 IDM IDM - NetID - Password reset process "

Before proceeding with a password reset, please have the user try the self-service options first.

This now includes using two-factor authentication with Duo, which most users will be required to have set up on their accounts. To reset a password with Duo, the user should select the Two Factor Authentication box on the Forgotten Password page.

First Half

When creating the incident, fill out the following fields:

Caller: User's NetID
Subject Person: User s NetID if different from the Caller
Callback phone number: Number you verified they can be reached at
Service offering: NetID
Channel: Phone
Assignment group: NB-HD
Assigned to: Your NetID
Short Description: NetID Password Reset
Description: A brief explanation of the issue they are experiencing
Click Save in the top right.
IMPORTANT: Remember to post the OpenRegistry (only the PHI flag and Roles) and NetID Admin information as a Work Note before the password reset!
Check for the following
Do they have an active role listed in OR?
Only users with at least one active role in OpenRegistry can reset their password.
Check that one of the Roles listed in OR says it is Active.
If their role should be active but is currently inactive:

Students should contact the Registrar/OneStop.
Faculty/Staff should contact their IT department.
Guests should contact their sponsor.
Do they have an active NetID?
Only users with an active NetID can reset their password.

In the NetID Admin Management Tool, verify the "NetID LDAP Status:" field shows as active.
If a "NetID LDAP Status" is inactive, or "Is NetID locked in Kerberos?:" is Y and shows locked due to deprovisioning"; AND the user has an active role, the user should be able to reactivate their NetID by going to https://netid.rutgers.edu/ > NetID Activation.
If "Is NetID locked in Kerberos?:" is Y and it is locked due to any other reason, such as "phishing", please notify a Supervisor or FT.
What service are they trying to access?

Make sure the user has the appropriate role for the service they are attempting to log in to. Alumni, for example, will not be able to access their unofficial transcript.
Has the user attempted to reset their password on their own?

If not, direct the user to https://netid.rutgers.edu/ > Manage NetID Password > Forgotten Password. If the user can reset their password themselves via this form, make sure they can now log in with their new password.
Is the user probshelled?

Check a user's role using the NetID Admin Management Tool and look under "LDAP Campus Services". If the user has student:nb:probshell, the user is probshelled. Please notify a Supervisor/FT if this is the case.
Is Kerberos under maintenance?

This is not common and is typically announced.
During the scheduled Kerberos maintenance, all Rutgers and RBHS users will be unable to set or reset their Rutgers NetID password. They will see the following message displayed on the password page at this time:
"Your Rutgers NetID password cannot be changed at this time, due to scheduled system maintenance. Please try again later today after <specify time> EST."
Verify Identity

If none of the above apply, ensure the user has attempted to reset their password by visiting https://netid.rutgers.edu/ > Manage NetID Password > Forgotten Password.

If they are unable to reset their password using Duo and do not remember their security questions, put this in the Incident as a Work Note and proceed with verifying identity.

Follow the procedures on Verifying Identity.

Do NOT share the information you see in OR with the user. The user should be providing you with the information, and then you verify that it matches one of the methods listed below.

If there are any concerns regarding the identity verification, supervisors also have access to a photo of the user in OR, which could be used as an additional layer of security to verify a match with the user's in-person appearance. Matching the photo in OR should never be the primary way of verification.

IMPORTANT: Always post how you verified the user's identity. If identity has been verified via an alternate phone number or email in OR, post the following in the work notes:

"Verified email/phone: <Enter email/phone here> from role: <Enter role here>"

" KB0011129 IDM IDM - NetID - Security Questions and Answers "

The Security Questions and Answers allows students, student workers, faculty, staff, alumni and guest to reset their password without the need to come to a NBCS Computer Lab or the Help Desk. All Rutgers affiliates are now enrolled into the program when they first activate their NetID. At that time, they must define answers to three of the many questions predefined by the University that they can choose from. The answer entered in for each of the three questions selected must contain a minimum of three characters each to be accepted. If there are less than three characters then the following error message will be displayed:

"Please enter at least 3 characters long answer to question <1>"

If at a later date the student cannot remember their password, they can use this tool to answer the security questions they setup and reset their password online.

On the Security Questions and Answers page users will need to provide their NetID, First and Last Name, DoB, and SSN/RUID/A-Number in the format requirements displayed on the page. The Activation Key field can be left blank. After the user has reset their password by answering their three Security Questions then have them login to their webmail or cas.rutgers.edu to verify that it was successful. Also check the Netid Admin Management Tool for the last password change date, which should reflect that date if the password reset was successful and then copy & paste that whole section into the ticket to resolve the ticket.

If a user answers a question incorrectly several times while attempting to use this tool, it will become locked for 24 hours. After 24 hours from the time of the lockout, the Security Questions and Answers feature will automatically unlock and can be attempted again.

See the Password Resets section for additional ways to reset passwords.

If a user has used an Activation Key to support a Password Reset, they will be prompted and required to reconfigure their Security Questions and Answers before moving on to reset their Password.



" KB0011130 IDM IDM - NetID Activation "

To begin the activation process, users should go to http://netid.rutgers.edu. This page gives users a brief overview of the NetID & services offered. Users should follow the below steps:

Click on Activate NetID on the left-hand side of the page.
The next page will ask the user for the following information: first and last name, Identifier (SSN, RUID, or A-Number), and DoB.
Remember to inform the user of the format requirements for each field - first letter of first and last name must be capitalized, DoB must be entered in the format listed below the field (mm/dd/yyyy).
Note that the first and last name must be entered the same way that it appears in Open Registry (RUST can also be checked and is useful for special cases as it gives the exact number of characters in the user's first and last names).
Once all fields have been filled, complete the captcha and click Continue .
The Captcha is used to prevent automated activation attempts and is hosted by Google. If the user is on a network that is actively blocking Google services, they will not be able to proceed with activation until they are using a network that allows Google services.
If there were no errors in the provided information and this is the first time that the user is activating a NetID, the next page should ask them to create a password. Users who have previously had an active NetID will keep the same password associated with their account the last time it was active. The password must be 10-63 characters long and contain 3 different character classes, and must also be substantially different from their past 5 passwords. These requirements are also listed on the page for the user to view.
Once the password is set the next page will allow the user to select and provide answers for three security questions which may be used to reset their password remotely should they ever forget it. The answers are case sensitive. Users can also manage their security questions at any time by going to netid.rutgers.edu > Manage Security Questions and logging in.
Once the questions are set, the user will be able to activate whichever services they are eligible for, as determined by their role at the University. These include services like Rutgers Connect and ScarletApps. Once the user has selected the appropriate check-boxes for the services they want, they should click Activate Services . If the user does not want to activate any services at the time, they may click "Skip Services Activation" instead. The user may manage service activation at any time by going to netid.rutgers.edu > Service Activation.
After selecting services to activate, the User must select a Rutgers Email Address also known as an Alias . The default alias is netid@rutgers.edu. Users may also set personal aliases which may also include their middle initial or suffix. For example, john.doe@rutgers.edu, john.j.doe@rutgers.edu, or john.doe.md@rutgers.edu. This information is listed above the alias tool.
The process will also ask the user to set a Delivery Address . This could be an address they have at the University (Rutgers Connect, ScarletMail, etc.) or a personal address of their choosing. All emails sent to the Alias address will be forwarded to the Delivery address selected by the user.
Account and Services activation is complete at this point. Note that some services can take up to 24 hours to activate completely.

" KB0014192 IDM IDM - NetID Activation (Summer and Winter Session) "

To take summer/winter courses, prospective students must register first. Session registration information can be found on the Summer Session Registration Page or the Winter Session Registration page.

Non-matriculated students who have been accepted for the Summer or Winter sessions must first register for classes using their RUID and PAC and pay their term bill before they are listed as a student in OR. Once listed as a Summer/Winter student, they can activate their NetID within 24 hours.

Students should receive an email like the one listed below upon being accepted for the Summer/Winter term.

Dear [STUDENT NAME]:
Congratulations! Your Registration Request Form has been received and your information has been added to our student database.
You have been assigned the Rutgers University Identification (RUID) number [RUID].
You may register online at http://webreg.rutgers.edu, using your RUID and Personal Access Code (PAC). Your PAC Number is a four digit code representative of your date of birth. The first two digits represent the month and the second two digits represent the day. For example, if you were born on Oct.22nd, your PAC would be 1022.
To pay your term bill online, please visit http://studentabc.rutgers.edu and click on pay your bill online.
Once you have paid your bill, please wait 24 hours, then visit http://netid.rutgers.edu to create your Rutgers NetID. This will enable you to open email and computer accounts, view course materials and your grades online, and access other services. We encourage you to create a NetID and Rutgers email address so your email address appears on an enrollment roster or in the online directory.
To locate a building on the New Brunswick campus please visit http://maps.rutgers.edu.
For information about parking, transportation, university services and facilities, and more, please visit summerwinter.rutgers.edu or contact the New Brunswick Office of Summer and Winter Sessions at wsru@docs.rutgers.edu or 848/932-7565.
Please note: If you are a matriculated student at another college or university, we recommend that you confirm course transferability with your home institution before the first class meeting. Brief course descriptions are available at http://sis.rutgers.edu/soc. If more detailed course information is required by your home institution, please contact the appropriate Rutgers academic department. You can find department contact information at http://search.rutgers.edu.
Welcome to Rutgers! We wish you a successful Summer or Winter Session.

" KB0014191 IDM IDM - NetID Activation - New Students "

Admit Role

This role is for newly Admitted Students that have NOT yet committed to attending Rutgers (by accepting their offer of admission). This provides very limited access to services and systems.

Newly admitted students need to wait 3 to 5 days after they receive their offer of admission before they can activate their NetIDs. They should receive an email with instructions to activate when they are in the system and able to do so. If they do not show up in OR, or they have issues activating their NetID, they just need to wait and try a few days after they receive their offer of admission.

Admit students do NOT need to accept their offer of admission to activate their NetID. However, activating a NetID is required to view financial aid information, which usually becomes available in early March.

Admit Coming Role

This role indicates a new student has committed to going to Rutgers and accepted their offer of admission. Access to most services requires an Admit Coming role.

Prospective students who have Accepted to start Rutgers in the Fall or Spring sessions must pay a $150 acceptance fee ($200 for graduate students) before they are listed as an incoming student in OR (OR will show them as "ADMIT COMING"). The timeline for application deadlines, admission decisions, and acceptance deadlines can be found on the Admissions Deadlines site. It may take up to 7 days after paying the acceptance fee before they are listed as an incoming student.





" KB0015172 IDM IDM - NetID Change Request "

If a user calls and asks for a NetID change, please notify your FT supervisory team right away (offer a call back if no one is available) and collect the basic information needed to create the incident.

Please do not guarantee that we can change their NetID and do not ask the user for a reason for the change.

If the user requests a NetID change, let them know you have escalated the request to FT staff for follow-up and offer in the meantime to assist with updating the email alias/displayed name, etc. Sample script:

"We will have to escalate your request to a full-time supervisor for approval. Let me create an incident for you and in the meantime, I can help you update your email address alias and display name if you desire."

Make sure they are aware of other alternatives that are available:

If their email name is a concern: set up an alias/updating their display name; to make their email more professional or to match any email format standard their department may require.
If spam is a concern: block junk mail senders, create rules or filters, etc. Spam is an unavoidable consequence of using email, and they will be back at the same level of spam given enough use of a new NetID.

If the user decides against a NetID change at any point, you can update the incident and resolve it as appropriate.

KBB0010107NetID Change Request



" KB0014735 IDM IDM - NetID Identity Merge "

If current or returning users are onboarded for a new role with incorrect or incomplete information, a secondary record may be created for them in the OpenRegistry. When this occurs, these records must be merged into a single record. Having more than one NetID can cause access issues for a user and needs to be addressed as soon as possible.

The identity merge process involves multiple groups: IDM, Help Desk, the ID Card office, and possibly Enterprise Messaging.

Please note that the group initiating the merge request is responsible for confirming that both records refer to the same individual, not two similar but distinct individuals.

Identity Merge Process Checklist

If an identity merge incident is identified, please follow the steps below.

The subject of the Incident should be: Merge NetID - NetID1, NetID2
Service Offering should be: NetID
Investigate the request and document the following:

Confirm which NetID includes the correct information.
Determine which, if any, of the NetIDs are active.
Determine what, if any, email accounts are associated with each NetID.
The Incident should be assigned to the OIT-HD - Identity Merge Assignment Group with the State set as New.
Steps for FT

*If you need any assistance with submitting a NetID merge request, please post a message in the esd_csq_redzone Slack Channel.

Assign yourself to the Incident.
Add directions in the Incident on which primary NetID to keep.

Example formatting of this process is:
Person has two entries in OR.

NetID: bk135 (keep active)
RcpID: 9999209
First Name: Billy
Last Name: Kee
DOB: 09/26/2016 - Correct
SSN: Fake (RUID)
NetID LDAP Status: active
LDAP Campus Services: student:nb:enabled, student:google:enabled
SOR Source: Rutgers
PHI: N

NetID: bk212
RcpID: 9987841
First Name: Barkha
Last Name: Kip
DOB: 09/09/2016 - Wrong
SSN: Fake (Psuedo)
NetID LDAP Status: inactive
LDAP Campus Services: none
SOR Source: Rutgers
PHI: N

Please merge under NetID: bk135. There are no accounts affected by this merge so it can be done at any time.



Email IPO-ID Card office for input on ID card info using the email feature in Service Now:



TO: ID Card Office <iam@ipo.rutgers.edu>

SUBJECT: Identity Merge: <netid1>, <netid2>, <netid3>, etc. (INCxxxx)

BODY:

Hello ID Card Office,
Please provide ID card info for the NetID(s) listed below that are related to the identity merge ticket: INCxxxx
The first NetID listed at the top is the NetID to remain/keep, and all other NetIDs will be deleted.
<netid1>
<netid2>
<netid3>
...
Kindly reply to this email to ensure the information in the reply is associated correctly with the ticket referenced above.
For any questions, please refer to the above-mentioned ticket and contact the OIT-Help Desk.
Thank you
The
IPO-ID Card office would reply to emails received from ServiceNow with info about ID cards.


After the IPO-ID Card office replies, Help Desk must then create an incident task and assign it to EAS - Identity and Access Management, summarizing the analysis and identifying when to schedule the NetID change.
Incident Task Short Description:
Identity Merge: <netid1>, <netid2>, <netid3>, etc. (INCxxxx)
Description:
NetID to keep - There should only be one.
NetID(s) to delete/join - There can be one or more.
Other identifiers to keep (e.g., if multiple SSNs, which SSN is correct) - If not applicable, then write "n/a" instead of leaving it empty.
Paste the reply from the IPO-ID Card office into the task. If you forget, IdM will ask for their reply before processing the task, causing a delay.
Timing - A specific date or date range in the future (with at least 3 days' notice, preferably). This is dependent on email services:

If no email accounts are affected by the change, it can be noted that "the merge can happen at any time".
If the user keeps the NetID active, with the corresponding email accounts they wish to keep, this is considered not to affect any accounts.
If the NetID being kept active is NOT the NetID that has the email accounts they wish to keep, you will need to schedule the NetID change.

ScarletMail: Changes are only made on Tuesday and Thursday. They MUST be logged out of everything on that day between Noon and 5 PM, and cannot log in on ScarletMail until after 8 PM that day.
Rutgers Connect: After the merge, the user's 365 User Principal Name (UPN) must be updated. The user will need to wait 30 minutes after the change is made before they can access their Rutgers Connect Account.
Send an email from your dedicated campus full-time email group at least 24 hours in advance:
To: root@{affected systems}
CC: <your dedicated campus full-time email group> & hd_support@oit.rutgers.edu.
Requesting the email merge at the scheduled time, and it should include the details:
Old NetID
New NetID
Incident Number
RP (responsible person)
The onus will fall back to the Help Desk after the merge is complete to contact the user and resolve the Incident.

" KB0010777 IDM IDM - NetID Lock Request "

Departments may request to lock a NetID for faculty or staff for their department. If calls come in through the Help Desk or through UCS Direct, create a ticket for the caller, but do not include any information identifying the faculty/staff member or the reason for locking the NetID. Keep in mind that the caller may specify that they want to lock, delete, close, or suspend the faculty/staff member's NetID, account or services, and we will need to clarify what they mean.

To lock a NetID, request the user to send a written notice to hd_mgr@oit.rutgers.edu.

The request must come from a person of authority (dean, director, or department chair) in the same department as the user.
If the request is coming from a department's IT staff, a person of authority must be CC'ed on the request.
The request should include what they want to be restricted (all NetID services, email only, etc).
The request should include when they want the NetID to be restricted (immediately, "after 5pm", etc).

" KB0015708 IDM IDM - NetID+ and Duo Support "

Table of Contents

Support Notes

If any users call in with questions regarding support for Duo, please refer to the Rutgers IT site documentation or the Duo user guide:

Two-step Login - Rutgers IT Website
Duo Guide to Two-Factor Authentication

If your question cannot be answered by Rutgers IT or Duo documentation, please refer to a full-time staff with Duo Admin access for assistance.

Bypass Codes

A bypass code is a temporary passcode created by full-time staff for a specific user. These are generally used as backup codes, so that users who are having problems with their mobile devices (e.g., mobile service is disrupted, the device is lost or stolen, etc.) can still access their Duo-protected systems. Bypass codes can also be used to allow a temporary user access to applications that do not support self-enrollment without having enrolled a device. Bypass codes expire after being used 2 times or after 120 minutes, whichever comes first.

Bypass code policy: to maintain security the following guidelines for issuing bypass codes should be observed. The user's identity needs to be verified, only FT can generate bypass codes, and a second person needs to handle the code. Similar to the procedure for password resets.

To enter a bypass code select the Bypass code option when prompted to choose your authentication method.

DUO documentation for generating a bypass code

Removing Lost or Stolen Devices

Always stress to users that they should contact the OIT Help Desk immediately if their 2FA authentication device is lost or stolen.

If a user reports a lost or stolen device, that device should be removed from the system immediately by full-time staff. Let the user know that the device can be added back once it is found or recovered. Remember: deleting a phone in this manner removes it from all associated users immediately (an important consideration if a phone is associated with multiple users, whether this is the case can be ascertained by clicking on the number in the user s profile and looking at the top of the page) therefore it is important that you talk to the user and make sure that the device is actually lost/stolen and not just in the possession of another associated user. When the device is recovered, it can be added to the user again and Duo Mobile can be reactivated.

DUO documentation for dealing with lost or stolen phones

Adding or activating a new authentication device

Users may add a new device via the self-service portal. Users may only add a new device via the self-service portal if they have access to another previously-activated authentication device or a bypass code. When adding a new device, if it is not successful at first attempt, use an incognito window. If they do not have access to either, then a full-time staff member must remove the previously-activated device (if the user no longer has it in their possession) or provide them a bypass code.

Excessive Authorization Failures

If a user initiates two factor authentication via any of the supported methods, but failed to complete the action then that counts as an Authorization Failure. If a user accrues 50 failure attempts, then the account will be locked and the user will need to wait 15 minutes until they can try again.

If the user calls within the 15 minute lockout window then an admin can login and manually change that user s status from Locked Out to Active or Bypass .

DUO documentation for changing a user s status

Images of DUO Tokens

Duo Hardware Tokens

Users who want to use a hardware token to authenticate using Duo can request using the Software Portal. On software.rutgers.edu search for the type of token and click on the product page that applies to them.

Security Hardware Token for Faculty/Staff

Security Hardware Token for Students

Security Hardware Token for Guests, Retirees and Emeritus

Out-of-sync Hardware Token / Resync DUO Token

A user's token may become out of sync if the button is pressed too many times. If a token becomes out of sync, the user can reset it by following the below steps:

Login to the NetID Management application by clicking on Manage NetID Password and entering their credentials. DUO authentication is required.
This step is required to display the next step.
The left-hand side of the window will update. Choose Resync Hardware Token in the left-hand menu.
The serial number drop-down will list the serial numbers of all the DUO hardware tokens the user has enrolled in. User has to select the token they want to sync from the drop down and enter 3 different passcodes generated through the hardware token in the text boxes and click Resync Hardware Token. There is about a 30 second wait for DUO tokens to get the next passcode. If all passcode values are entered correctly, the token will be resynced and the user will see a success message.

If a user is unable to do this, a person with duo admin access will need to follow the directions listed here:

DUO documentation for resynchronizing tokens

Encourage Duo Push

Always encourage your users to use the Duo Push, if possible. Duo Push is the best option: it is the most secure way of authenticating and the cheapest (there are no telephony charges for push authentication although data usage charges may apply). Users can also push if they do not have cell service as long as they are connected to WiFi, and it works in any country.

Traveling Abroad

All available authentication methods should work. If cellular service or internet access are not available, the push method will not be available. In this case, mobile app generated passcodes or a hardware token can still be used.

Why am I not receiving Duo Push notifications?

First, ensure that the user allows notifications on their phone.

You may have trouble receiving push requests if there are network issues between your phone and our service. Many phones have trouble determining whether to use the WiFi or cellular data channel when checking for push requests, and simply turning the phone to airplane mode and back to normal operating mode again often resolves these sort of issues, if there is a reliable internet connection available. Similarly, the issue may be resolved by turning off the WiFi connection on your device and using the cellular data connection. A Duo Push notification is only 2 KB.

Check the time and date on your phone and make sure they are correct. If the date and time on your phone are manually set, try changing your device's configuration to sync date and time automatically with the network.

In some cases, a user may experience issues using duo while abroad in other countries. Have the user do the following:

In a browser, please attempt to reach the following link: https://api.duosecurity.com/auth/v2/ping

Successful access will display a response code like this: "response": {"time": 1445354727}, "stat": "OK"} If you are able to access the URL above, check the certificate used to access that site by clicking on the icon beside "https://" in your browser bar. The certificate should be issued by duosecurity.com. If it is issued by some other site, then there is likely an SSL proxy. In that case, make an exception in the SSL proxy for duosecurity.com. If you are not able to access the URL above, or do not receive a JSON response code similar to the example, there may be a firewall rule in place preventing communication. Unfortunately, we do not have the ability to assist with firewall issues as the restrictions are set in place by your local ISP.

In-depth troubleshooting guides for Duo Push delivery for iOS and Android.

Link for DUO Help Desk Guide PDF

" KB0015014 IDM IDM - Processing Early Employee NetIDs "

The Early Employee NetID Request System is an enterprise self-service tool that enables an employee (faculty or staff) who has not been on-boarded through RIAS to submit a request to obtain a digital identity at Rutgers University. The request will be processed by the appropriate campus help desk. The employee will then be notified with instructions to obtain and activate their digital identifier called a NetID. *For AH FT only: Please process all EEs, no matter the campus.*

Any faculty member, full-time staff member, or graduate assistant whose information has been entered into the RIAS III payroll system ("on-boarded") and approved by University Human Resources can activate a NetID. If the faculty/staff member needs a NetID before the entry is approved then the departmental payroll person can apply for an Early Employee NetID. A valid SSN is required for an Early Employee NetID.

Early Employee NetIDs can be processed by doing the following:

Go to http://registry.rutgers.edu and log in
Under Process Early Employee NetID click on Search Pending Requests
Click Find Requests
Click on the Request ID to read any special notes
Click Claim
Click Process
Compare the department listed on the EE NetID Request to the Department/School/Unit listed on the Sponsor's active role in OR (click on the sponsor's NetID in the guest request list to see their active roles). If they are not similar (does not have to be an identical match but should be close), deny the request with a comment that the departments must be the same.
Click Add Person to search for the user
Look through the pages of results that come up to see if the person is already in the system. Pay attention to the birthdate and SSN.
If the person is listed and they do not have an active Faculty or Staff role click on Add Role
If the person is there and has an active Faculty or Staff role Deny the request
If the person is not listed click on Add New person and role

For instructions on reviewing or looking up the status of a request, please read: Early Employee and Guest NetIDs Requests Status KA

" KB0015028 IDM IDM - Processing RCPID Requests "

An RCPID is an identification number that can be found in Open Registry.

Every Rutgers user has their own unique RCPID. RCPID's are typically used for F1/J1 International visitors coming to the university. The form to request an RCPID request can be found at http://rcpid-requests.rutgers.edu. We do not accept RCPID's via e-mail due to the information that is listed on it. Typically when a RCPID account is made, there will be a Guest NetID that is visible in Open Registry. These accounts are only valid for 7 days. The account is solely made to give the user the RCPID number. The usual RCPID request is handled within two business days. If there are any issues beyond this make sure to e-mail accounts@oit.rutgers.edu or contact a Full-Time staff member.

Process RCPID Requests by doing the following:

Go to https://rcpid-requests.rutgers.edu/ and log in.
Click on the name of the person the RCPID is being requested for.
Go to http://registry.rutgers.edu and log in.
Click on Add Person.
Fill out the users First Name, Last Name and Birthdate.
Click Add Person to search for the user.
Look through the pages of results that come up to see if the person is already in the system. Pay attention to the birthdate and SSN.

If the person is listed and they do not have a Guest role click on Add Role.
If the person is listed and they had/have a role, note the NetID and skip to step 11.
If the person is not listed click on Add New person and role.
Choose Guest and Add Role.
Fill out the form using the information from the request:

Role End date: should be 1 week after the request was made.
Department: find the best match if the exact department is not listed. Looking at the Sponsor's Department in OR can help and you may use that one if appropriate.
Account Reason: should be J1 Visa.
Email: use no-reply@oit.rutgers.edu and check the box for Don't Send Account Creation Notification Emails to Guest and Sponsor.
Sponsor: enter the NetID of the sponsor.
Herd Expiration Date: set to be the same as the Role End Date.
Click Add Role and note the NetID.
Look the user up on http://registry.rutgers.edu and note the RCPID number.
Create an incident in Service Now for the sponsor.

Service Offering: NetID
Assignment Group: NB-HD - Full Time Supervisors
Short Description: RCPID Request
Description: Processed request for [NAME]
Save the incident.
Use the response template for RCPID requests and resolve the incident:


------------------------------------------------------------------------

Subject: RCPID Requests

Hello,

Thank you for contacting OIT. The RCPID Number you requested is:

Name:

NetID:

RCPID:

Thank you
Account Support

Approve the request in the RCPID request system.

" KB0014318 IDM IDM - Pseudo SSNs "

Faculty/Staff

A payroll pseudo SSN is assigned to a user who does not have a valid SSN in the system. By leaving the SSN field blank, a string of 9s is entered instead by the payroll system automatically. To activate their NetID, the user would input "999999999" as their SSN for the time being. They should speak with their payroll personnel in order to update their SSN in the system, as they should have applied for an SSN when starting their position.

Guests

University Guests who are added to Open Registry without a SSN will be provisioned with a unique pseudo SSN. The accounts office will send the 9 digits of the pseudo SSN to the sponsor who should then provide it to the Guest.

The Help Desk can provide a pseudo SSN to the Guest if they verify identity by one of the standard verification methods we would use for a password reset (non-Rutgers email, phone number, CVC). We may also provide it to a Rutgers sponsor if they request it and verify their identity through the same methods.

The pseudo SSN can be provided by consultants over the phone or in person. Optionally, a FT who processes Guest Requests can send the pseudo SSN from the accounts@oit email account.



" KB0014197 IDM IDM - RBHS Guest Info Corrections "

If a guest associated with RBHS's information has been incorrectly entered into the registry, the user should contact the sponsoring department/sponsor or the person who entered the guest into the registry. If the user does not have this information, the sponsor's NetID may be found in Open Registry. You may use their NetID to find public-facing information for the sponsor on Rutgers Search.



" KB0014342 IDM IDM - RCPID overview "

An RCPID is a standardized identification number that can be found in Open Registry.

Every Rutgers user has their own unique RCPID. RCPID's are typically used for F-1/J-1 International visitors coming to the university. The form to request an RCPID request can be found at this RCPID Requests page. RCPID requests are typically handled within two business days. We do not accept RCPID's via e-mail due to the information that is listed on it.

Typically when an RCPID is created, an additional Guest NetID will be made that is visible in Open Registry. These accounts are only valid for 7 days, and are solely made to give the user their RCPID number. We do not accept RCPID requests via e-mail due to the identification information that is required. If you encounter any issues beyond these examples, contact a Full-Time staff member for assistance.

" KB0019351 IDM IDM - Retiring the Duo Phone Call Option "

As part of the ongoing commitment to protecting the Rutgers University community from evolving cyber threats, the Office of Information Technology (OIT) retired the "Call Me" (telephone callback) authentication option for Duo, effective Wednesday, March 25th, 2026.

Why is this change happening?

In recent months, higher education institutions nationwide have seen a significant increase in sophisticated phishing attacks. The telephone callback method is increasingly vulnerable, making it a primary target for attackers attempting to compromise university accounts.

To safeguard Rutgers' systems and protect personal data, we are aligning with industry best practices by deprecating the phone call option and transitioning to more secure verification methods.

What are the recommended alternatives?

To guarantee uninterrupted access to University services and resources, users must switch to one of the following supported methods:

Duo mobile app with Verified Duo Push (Highly Recommended)

The most secure and convenient way to authenticate is by using the Duo Mobile app on your smartphone or tablet. Verified Duo Push provides an extra layer of security.

The Duo mobile app can also generate offline passcodes if you are traveling or lack cellular/Wi-Fi service.

Learn how to use Verified Duo Push.

SMS Text Passcodes (For Basic Mobile Phones)

If the user has a mobile phone that can receive text messages but cannot or prefers not to use the Duo Mobile app, they can choose to authenticate using SMS passcodes. They must ensure your registered device is set up in Duo as a mobile phone capable of receiving SMS messages.

Learn how to use SMS passcodes with Duo.

Hardware Token (For Landline or Non-Smartphone Users)

If they do not have a smartphone or if their primary authentication device is a landline that cannot receive SMS text messages or run the Duo app, they will need to use a hardware token.

Learn how to obtain and use a Duo Hardware Token.

What do users need to do?

Users should still have an alternative authentication method. Follow the instructions on how to enroll a new Duo device. This will guide users in viewing their current Duo authentication methods and adding a new one if needed.

Exempt users

An exception list will allow approximately 600 users who rely exclusively on phone call verification to continue using that option until April 22nd. An email message to the 600 exempted users will provide guidance on moving to options other than phone calls by April 22. If they did not get an email with subject: Action required: Change your Duo verification method they are not exempt. If Duo had registered the phone as a device type that supports Duo Mobile, they would not be exempt. Only users who use the phone call option on a landline, with no other registered Duo devices, would be on the exempt list.

" KB0012272 IDM IDM - SecurID Tokens "

SecurID tokens are used to access the Rutgers University Administrative Mainframe System (RUTADMIN). The OIT Help Desk is a location in New Brunswick where faculty/staff can pick up their SecureID token if they cannot wait for one to be mailed to them. These tokens expire every few years.

The recipient is here to pick it up

Spare SecurID tokens will be stored in Center Square in the drawer labeled SecurID . If a user calls, emails, or walks into the Help Desk with a question about picking up their token, please notify Level 3 or FT. The Level 3 or FT will check their ID and look up the incident. If they are picking up a SecurID token for someone else, make sure this is stated in the incident. Please do not give a user more than one token.

If they have an incident

Move the incident into Assignment Group: NB-HD - Level 3

" KB0014413 IDM IDM - Turnaround Database "

The Turnaround Database is a tool for determining what credentials a user needs and how long it takes for a user to be able to log in to a certain application. To log into most applications, a user will need an active NetID and password.

When an application states that it checks LDAP, that means that it first looks to see if the user has an active account. If they do not, it will check to see if they have an active role in the PDB (People Database). If a user does not have an active account and/or role, the user will not be able to authenticate into anything that uses LDAP.

Applications that use CAS (Central Authentication Service) for authentication allow a user to log in 30 minutes after NetID activation. For a user to log into CAS, the user must have an active NetID and the correct password.

Applications that use CAS for authentication are the following:

Rutgers Portal https://my.rutgers.edu/portal
SAS - Gradebook https://secure.fas.rutgers.edu

Faculty/Staff are able to add courses. Students are able to view grades.
Ethics Reporting Web page https://uec.rutgers.edu/

Allows Faculty/Staff to log in.
Final Exams Web page https://scheduling.rutgers.edu/exam-scheduling/final-exams/

Allows students to log in to view their customized exam schedule. There is a white list for administrators (Faculty/Staff) that can also log in.
RIAS III http://rias.rutgers.edu/
ScarletMail http://scarletmail.rutgers.edu/index.php

User must have an active ScarletMail account.

Other Applications:

Rutgers Software Portal https://software.rutgers.edu/

User must have a Faculty, Staff, or Student role. Guests cannot log in.
Canvas https://canvas.rutgers.edu
Computer Labs Login

Uses LDAP. Does not allow users with only an Alumni role to log in.
Libraries

Not available to users with only Alumni, Guest, and Retiree Guest roles.
Parktran Web Services https://ipo.rutgers.edu/dots

Check to make sure user has valid NetID. Users can log in 48-72 hours after NetID activation.
Type 1 & Type 2 Employees can use the online webform to obtain a parking permit. Type 3 and Type 4 must go to the Public Safety Building and obtain the permit in person.
Resnet Registration it.rutgers.edu/resnet

Uses LDAP.
RU Wireless

Uses LDAP and user must have Student, Faculty, Staff, or Guest role.
SSH Login

User must have an active account on the system they are trying to access.
VPN https://vpn.rutgers.edu

Uses LDAP, any role.

" KB0015085 IT Help Desk - Application Support After-Hours - ITS Escalation Procedures "

The NB-ITS Help Desk closes at 4:30 pm Monday-Friday and is closed on weekends. Users who call the NB-ITS Help Desk after it has closed will be presented with the option to speak to an NB HD After-Hours consultant. This article will provide you with guidance on how to handle some common ITS issues. If you cannot assist the user, please let the user know that you will be moving their ticket, and an ITS specialist will assist them during regular business hours. If the user indicates their issue is an emergency, please use the FT on-call list. If you are unsure if the user is supported by ITS know that ITS supports most users who land in the Echo domain on Connect.

Incidents that need to be handed off to the NB ITS Help Desk should be placed in the ESD - Information Technology Services (ITS) assignment group.

Unlocking ITS Account

If a user's ITS account is locked, you must reassign the incident to the ESD - Information Technology Services (ITS) assignment group. After-hours staff cannot unlock ITS accounts.

Creating/Recreating New ITS User Account

If a user needs to create a new ITS user account, you can get them started on the process by doing the following:

The user will be asked to log in
Select the link for the request form
The form will generate a sys admin ticket for ITS staff to follow up on
At this point,t you can notify the user that the ITS Help Desk will be reaching out to them to schedule a new user orientation
New User Orientation

If a user asks to schedule a new user orientation, you will need to reassign the incident to the ESD - Information Technology Services (ITS) assignment group.

If a user asks what is part of a new user orientation you can provide the following information:

Set up Outlook, also inform them of connect.rutgers.edu.
Change Default PDF user to Adobe Reader (or Acrobat if their computer has it).
Take note of programs on the desktop, if they don t need something you can get rid of it and remove the label off KACE, if they need a piece of software they don t have, start the process of getting it.
Change the Default Mail program to Outlook.
Change the default browser to their desired default.
Show users the Snipping Tool. This will help capture error messages when they want to talk to the Help Desk.
Make user aware they should not Shut Down ever. We do updates at night, and we must keep their computers updated with security patches.
Show them how to log off/lock the computer instead.
Teach them how to interact with the help desk.
Point out the stickers on their monitor, the contact info, and the computer name.
Explain its a good idea to reboot first before calling with a support issue.
If emailing, include call back number, department, computer name, and suitable time to call back.
Set them up with remote desktop and VPN instructions if they need them. This can be found on its.rutgers.edu/documentation.
Do the same for email on the phone.
Show them the shortcut to the ITS website that is on their desktop.
Connecting to RUWireless secure

https://ithelp.rutgers.edu/sp?id=kb_article&sysparm_article=KB0012539&sys_kb_id=b5d76e158737f01056be426d8bbb355f&spa=1

Cisco VPN (AnyConnect)

Hardware Delivery

If a user wants to have new or used hardware delivered to them, a ticket will need to be created with the following information:

What the item is, and is it used or new
If it is a used item, such as a keyboard, mouse, or monitor, the ITS help desk will work with the field team to deliver and install the item
If it is a new item, the request is sent to field support so that they can begin the procurement process

Once new item is received after procurement, field support schedules pick up or delivery with the user (depending on the item)
