If a user requests a computer renewal, the incident will need to be triaged to the ESD - Information Technology Services (ITS) assignment group.

" KB0014806 IT Help Desk - Application Support "After-Hours - Support Board (Visual Task Board, VTB) " "

The After-hours Support Board is a visual task board on ServiceNow dedicated to tracking events that may directly impact users and services during your shift.
All after-hours consultants are expected to create, update, and transition cards through lanes as events occur.
Applications/AH Support Board
Lanes
The support board contains 5 lanes, in which status cards can be dragged and dropped as you progress through an event:
Scheduled 1 Day: Single day events that are scheduled in advance.
Scheduled 2+ Days: Multiple day/month-long maintenance events that are scheduled in advance.
Critical/Active: Sudden events that impact operations (systems/services/environment).
Operational Changes: Alterations to IT services and infrastructure to improve service quality.
Completed: Events that occurred in the last 2 months.
Creation of Cards

A card can be created by selecting +Add card in any appropriate lane.

Scheduled events will be received via email and copied/pasted into a status card.
Pertinent emails are sent to the following email addresses:
ei_maintenance@email.rutgers.edu
KACE Support (and similar KACE emails that start with kbox followed by a number).
ECS HD - Afterhours Support (operator@oit.rutgers.edu).
Any other email that may come in announcing maintenance or a change to a Rutgers service (e.g. Canvas, CAS, servers, etc.)
The naming convention of new tasks is:
Scheduled 1 Day	day, date - title/subject
Scheduled 2+ Days	month - title/subject
Operational Changes	Change numbers
The card should be expanded, and Description should include the details of the event or a copy/paste of the email received.
The Assignment field will not be used at this time and should be left blank.
The Due Date field is mandatory and should reflect the start time/first day of the event.
Please note use the first day of a multiple day/month-long event.
Additional comments can be used when needed for relevant notes of the event.
Cards should be created for events immediately when:
Service degradation is confirmed.
The service outage is experienced.
Email is received with scheduled maintenance.
An environmental issue has been noticed.
Example:

1 Day:

2+ Days:

Updating Cards
Cards should be updated and transitioned through lanes as events progress. Any additional actions taken by a consultant are expected to be included in the Additional comments of the card(s). Additional actions may include additional communications (email, phone, Slack) sent/received, action(s) taken because of noted call load impact, VIP inquiries, etc.

*Please note Multiple day/month-long events will show Due Dates in red while the time frame is active. Only move such cards to Completed once the entire maintenance time frame is complete. Example:

Lane Changes
Scheduled events shift to Completed when the timeframe of a scheduled event ends or there is updated communication stating maintenance is finished.
Critical/Active shifts to Completed when the system/service returns to full functionality and there is no lingering impact.
Operational Changes shift to Completed when the timeframe of a scheduled event ends or there is updated communication stating maintenance is finished.
Deleting Cards
Under no circumstances should the cards be deleted from the board. Management will clear the completed events promptly (events more than 24 hours old). Please note that all changes to this board are logged/archived.
*Please work with your co-workers or speak to management if you have any questions or uncertainties on proceeding with status board updates.*

" KB0014877 IT Help Desk - Application Support General - Student Data Warehouse Refresh "

Within the hd_notify inbox we will receive emails with a status on Student Data Warehouse (SDW) and Course Data Warehouse (CDW) Load processes. These feeds control both course information and student data (classes, transcripts, etc.). When the load process is delayed, then obviously updates are not available to the databases, which in turn has an impact on the course load and student data information available to students.

Ops should watch for emails within hd_notify. In the event you receive this message:

Help Desk, the Student Data Warehouse refresh will be late

You can then inform callers that There was a delay in overnight processing, please try again tomorrow".

" KB0014960 IT Help Desk - Application Support Help Desk Communications Newark - Camden "

Background Information on current protocol

Within 15 minutes of a system slowdown, failure, or outage the system administrator is to notify the campus help desk of the situation. When the situation is resolved, the system administrator is to notify the campus help desk with the status update.

The help desk receiving that call is responsible for notifying the other two campus help desks of the situation. The help desks are to notify other support units according to their procedures. Users are to be notified within one hour of the problem.

Operations Role

Currently, Operations is responsible for notifying the NB Help Desk of any possible system problem after 15 minutes of continuous occurrence. The Help Desk will then notify the Help Desks of Newark and Camden. This process can only happen during HD business hours (when the NB Help Desk is open).

The Camden and Newark Help Desks have been supplied with the phone number of the Operations Department, to be used when they experience a degradation in service and the NB Help Desk is closed. Should Operations receive a call from either Help Desk reporting a degradation in service, Operations should collect as much information as possible from the caller (Service degradation information, as well as contact information). The Ops Consultant should then contact their Supervisor and/or Manager to relay the information and seek guidance on proper handling. The supervisor will likely ask the consultant to send an email to sc_ft@email.rutgers.edu with all the gathered data.

An email will also need to be sent to Operator with all collected information and details of the call. You may be asked to relay this email to other parties - so please be sure it contains as much information as possible.

Campus Help Desk Information

The following contact information has been distributed to each of the Campus Help Desks.

Camden contact info

856-225-6065 enter 0 at prompt
help@camden.rutgers.edu

Newark contact info

help@newark.rutgers.edu

Camden - After-Hours Support

Name, NetID, and Email of the requester.
Phone number where this person can be reached.
A short description of the issue.

Please let the user know that someone from the Camden support team will be in touch the following morning, during our normal business hours.

Escalation Procedure

Camden - Law School Exam Support

The Law School in Camden provides students with the opportunity to self-schedule their exam during the exam period. These self-scheduled exams do not require any specialized software. Students have access to their exam through the Law School s course webpage system (law.rutgers.edu/course). This site requires a NetID login and is restricted to only students, faculty and staff of the Law School.

The most important part of helping a law student with an exam question is to do your best to help the student stay calm. In most cases, law school classes have only one exam for the entire semester, so it can be extremely stressful for a law student to have a technical issue that could have a serious impact of their grade that semester.

Law Exams are also graded anonymously, so all law students are forbidden from contacting their professor for help.

Business Hours

If a law student is calling during the normal operating hours of the Camden Help desk, please transfer the call through the Internal Transfers - Camden option, so that a member of the Camden Help desk can assist the student.

After Hours

If a law student calls with a question about an exam after hours, please review the below FAQ to see if the question can be answered using the information below. The website is restricted to only students taking that exam, but there are a number of general questions that you can answer without having direct access to the exam itself.

Helpdesk staff should only assist with questions related to the process of retrieving their exam and uploading their answer. Any questions related to the exam itself or timing issues like how much time do I have left should never be answered by HD staff. The Law School appoints a Dean on Duty each day and night during exams and students should be directed to contact the Dean on Duty for any non-technical issue. The Dean on Duty Schedule can be found by directing the student to this site: https://law.rutgers.edu/current-students (Restricted to Law Students only) and clicking the Exam Packet for that semester under the Camden Resources tab. There they will find information about the exam session, as well as a link for the Dean on Duty Schedule.

If a student has completed their exam but is having trouble with uploading it, they should email a copy of their final submission (as an attachment) to lawexamsend@camden.rutgers.edu. This email will capture the date and time of submission so the student is not penalized for having issues with uploading their exam.

A record of every after-hours call regarding an exam should be sent to help@camden.rutgers.edu with the Subject CMD - HD After Hours Support from the operator@oit persona, this will create a ticket in their system. This includes calls where the student s question was answered by the FAQ.

Law School Exam FAQs
I forgot to attach my anonymous # to my submission.
Answer: The number is automatically attached to your exam when submitted.

I put the wrong exam number on my final, what should I do?
Answer: Don't worry, the system automatically puts your correct number on the exam. It won't remove the incorrect number, but your correct number will appear in bold as a footer of each page.

I forgot to note on my exam that I am a graduating senior, what should I do?
Answer: Don't worry, in the spring semester the system automatically notes that you are a graduating senior.

Should I contact my professor for questions on the exam or problems with downloading or uploading?
Answer: You should never contact your professor if you are experiencing technical difficulties with this system or the exam; instead notify help@camden.rutgers.edu or the dean on duty.

The text box would not accept my document when I attempt to cut and paste; what should I do?
Answer: Please use the file upload method.

I reviewed my exam in the box (cut and paste method), and it was fine. When I submitted it, all of a sudden there are no tabs at the beginning of the paragraph, is there something I can do about this?
Answer: Since you used the cut/paste method to submit your exam, you were extremely limited to what formatting you could use (essentially none). In the future, using the file upload method will preserve your tabs. You should not worry about the formatting of your exam. Professors are aware of the limitations of the system with respect to advanced formatting.

The professor has placed a word count on our exams. When working in my local word processor, I was within the word count, when I check my submitted exam online, the count is different; it s possibly even over. What Gives?
Answer: Unfortunately, word count varies from word processor to word processor. The count method takes a simple straight forward approach, and is why we state "approximately". In reviewing student's exams, we find that often simple spacing issues are overlooked (i.e. Sect.208 instead of Sect 208).

The professor requested that the exam be submitted in double space formatting. When I review the submission, the exam is single spaced, why is this?
Answer: We store the document in single space format. However, the professor has the option of receiving the exams in single or double space from us.

I am using Microsoft Word on an Apple Computer and am wondering if I will experience any issues?
Answer: The Law School fully supports the word document format available on PC and Mac.

HELP! I don't know my RUID number and the system will not give me my anonymous exam number until I enter it.
Answer: You can lookup your RUID number by following the link that appears on the Online Grade Reporting System webpage. Alternatively, you can access it directly at the following url: https://my.rutgers.edu/

" KB0011795 IT Help Desk - Application Support LMS - After-Hours LMS Escalation Procedures KB0015672 IT Help Desk - Application Support Machine Room - Fan Placement "

Please use the map below as a placement guide for fans in the data center during cooling issues. This setup should cover the three most common scenarios we have faced in the past:

Complete loss of cooling and airflow (CRAC blowers are off) due to a power failure.

In the case of #1: Fans should be deployed immediately; open exterior doors; open cold-isle doors.
Complete cooling loss - All CRACs display high head pressure.

In the case of #2 - Fans should be deployed immediately; Open exterior doors; Monitor cold-isle temps; Open cold-isle doors if/when temps inside/outside the isle equalize.
The loss of one or two CRACs of seven.

In the case of #3, fans should be deployed to the area of heat buildup and placed to direct the warm air toward a working CRAC, avoiding pushing warm air into cold aisles.

In all cases, fans should be directed according to the blue arrows, pointing upwards (~30 ) to prevent heat from accumulating at the ceiling in the data center.

" KB0015671 IT Help Desk - Application Support Machine Room and Environment Information "

You'll want to (ctrl+F) for the information you're looking for in this KA!

Table of Contents
Data Center Contacts
Generator
Loading Dock
Power Distribution Units (PDUs)
HVACS
Fan Placement
Fire Systems
Cage (COLO)
Rear door heat exchangers (RDHXs)
Data Center Event Notification Procedure
Rounds
Facilities
Fire Alarm Procedures
Data Center Contacts

Only the After Hours FT team, FT supervisors, and managers have access to the Contact List. If you are a student OIT consultant and no AH FT is on shift, please call the FT on-call list if an escalation to the data center contacts is needed.

Generator

The Hill Center is equipped with a diesel generator capable of powering the machine room for approximately 12 hours in the event of commercial power loss. In the event of unstable power, the generator will start automatically after 20 seconds and continue running until power is restored. For immediate backup power, we also have a limited UPS (Uninterruptible Power Supply) battery system. This system functions as a continuous source of electricity while the generator is cycling up. You will receive auto-generated emails whenever the generator powers on or off.

The mechanical room is where the UPS is located, and the audible alarms stem from there. The battery room is where the UPS battery system is located. The UPS is located in H019. The batteries are located in H047 (off the loading dock).

Loading Dock

The loading dock is where many deliveries to the Hill Center are made. During business hours, Application Support usually receives phone calls regarding deliveries to the Hill Center. There is a phone on the loading dock with the Operations phone number listed. The extension for this line is (5-4507).

Power Distribution Units (PDUs)

There are five PDUs (Power Distribution Units) located throughout the machine room, supplying conditioned power to the various equipment at all times. Each unit has an LCD that lists the current voltages and the device's status. The doors should remain closed at all times to avoid any electrical hazards. Audible alarms may occur under certain conditions.

Should you notice a PDU alarm during your round/shift, don't panic. Follow the LCD instructions to silence the alarm. Check your email for an automated message titled "Device Alarm". If no email was received -OR- if the email you received does not include specific instructions to escalate the issue, then send an email to ei_dcts@email.rutgers.edu and cc: operator@oit.rutgers.edu with the error information. These errors do not need to be escalated further; however, if you do have any questions or concerns, contact a supervisor.

"Communications Lost" alerts are typically caused by momentary network errors and resolve themselves when the device is polled again (~5 min). If the device is located in a remote data center (Liv-MDC), you should only reach out to John Wieczorek if the alert doesn't resolve within 15-20 minutes.

HVACS

Six HVAC Units are strategically located throughout the machine room. They maintain the room temperature at approximately 63 F year-round and also act as dehumidifiers. It is a closed system that requires regular maintenance. During a machine room round, a consultant must check under the floor of each unit for leaks and check the LED displays on each NC.

Overview

The machine room is equipped with 22-ton Liebert units with copper piping. The cooling capacity is 132 tons, with diffusers on each unit to evenly distribute airflow. In addition, their placement on the floor is to help reduce airflow". Each unit sits on a stand rather than on the floor to help reduce vibration in surrounding areas.
Product Information via Liebert: Liebert Deluxe System/3
Service

The monitoring system (Nform) has been set up to send an email if any alarms are triggered on any AC Unit. There are approximately 15 possible alarm conditions for each unit. Rutgers Facilities Maintenance will continue to maintain the HVAC system for the machine room.
Rutgers Facilities Maintenance maintains a service contract with Stillwell-Hansen, which includes quarterly PMs, parts, and labor.
For service during business hours, call:
1-800-LIEBERT (1-800-543-2378).

Any major problems after hours should be reported immediately to Public Safety at 2-7211.
Should you experience any problems with the units, please refer to the "CRAC/HVAC Problem Reporting" section below.
Temperature & Humidity

Each unit is equipped with a deluxe display & microprocessor that displays the current temperature, relative humidity, & unit status (e.g., cooling, heating, humidifying, dehumidifying, or an alarm state). The current settings for temperature & humidity are 69 degrees & 45% RH, respectively. These settings should not be changed. Doing so will result in one of the other units working harder to make up the difference.
Issues with Cooling/Power

If an air conditioning problem is suspected, be aware of the polar booths. Polar booths are located in front of some of the higher-density racks and direct cold air from under the floor into the rack. This works well when there is positive air pressure coming from under the floor and the air is cold. Where there is no normal positive air pressure coming from under the floor, the booths restrict airflow into the rack and should be removed from the front of the rack. Airflow can occur when power to the CRACs is lost or when the CRACs are down for maintenance. Generally, only one CRAC is offline at a time, but even with one CRAC down (#5 &#6), there is an impact on these racks. Just to be safe, if an air conditioning problem is suspected, remove the polar booths -- currently, there are 2 in use at locations 4Z and 4FF.
Power-on/Power-off
To start or stop a unit, press the blue On/Off button under the display. DO NOT use the ON/OFF switch on the upper left front of the unit unless it's an emergency.
Emergency Power Off (EPO)

In the event of a fire or another situation requiring you to quickly shut down all units, use any of the red/yellow buttons on the walls in the machine room. There are three, one outside the Machine Room Office (H004) and one to the right of either of the main entrances to the machine room. NOTE: These buttons will bring down the entire room - they remove power to both the HVACs and the room's Power Distribution Units (PDUs).
Power-on After An Emergency Power Off
Go to each unit and place the On/Off switch located on the front upper left in the OFF position.
Inspect each EPO button. If you note one pushed in, pull it out.
Go to the air conditioning panel box located to the left of room 10 (the old IO counter). Open the door. There is a large breaker (500 amp) at the bottom. Reset the breaker by pressing the handle down as far as it will go, then back up to the ON position.
Go around to each unit, placing the On/Off switch located on the front upper left in the ON position & press the blue On button below the display.
Monitoring

90% of the water supply lines have been moved out from under the floor and placed above the ceiling, so it should be fairly easy to spot a leak. There is, however, a small amount of pipe still under the floor on the left side of each unit that could leak. Gone undetected, this can still cause us problems. It needs to be checked during routine console checks.

If you find a unit has a substantial leak (flooding the floor under the tiles), contact a Supervisor immediately. You may be requested to shut the unit down by pressing the blue on/off button on the control panel and close the YELLOW supply & return valves located on the wall behind the leaking unit. Do this by rotating the yellow handle clockwise 90 degrees (or 3 o'clock). You may then be asked to contact Facilities.

NOTE: If attempting to clean a leak, verify that the Vacuum is labeled as the Wet Vac. If you cannot find the Wet Vac, notify a supervisor before attempting to clean the leak.
Systems Overview

The HVAC system for the computer room utilizes freon-based air conditioners. Like those in your home, these systems require a means to dissipate the heat generated during the cooling process of freon-based systems. In an in-home system, the condenser is placed outside the house & a fan cools the freon, changing it back to a liquid. To make a long story short, we can't do this, so we need another way to dissipate the heat generated. We do this by using condenser water. Cool water from the towers enters each unit, which is equipped with a heat exchanger. Cool water enters the heat exchanger, cooling the freon as warm/hot water exits. The warm water is cooled by the small cooling towers outside. If an HVAC reports "High Head pressure", it's a good indication of a condenser water flow issue to the unit. If ALL units report "High Head pressure", there is a water flow issue with one of the two water loops. Check tower water levels; pumps in mechanical rooms - there should be one pump running for each loop. Pumps are located in H033 for the open loop and H049 for the closed loop. The Leibert units can run with up to 110 degrees F condenser water without losing any cooling capacity.
NOTE: This system utilizes condenser water. Not to be confused with "chilled" water.
Contact your Supervisor if you have questions regarding the procedures below.
CRAC/HVAC Problem Reporting
Non-emergency issues
Non-critical air conditioner issues (such as noises, minor leaks, or warning alarms) should be reported via email to operator@oit.rutgers.edu and ei_dcts@email.rutgers.edu, including details of the situation. A Supervisor and/or John Lyons will determine whether a service call should be placed to facilities. Should a situation arise outside normal business hours (late nights/weekends) and you are unsure of the proper course of action, please contact your Supervisor for direction. Make sure to monitor the situation carefully, as it may escalate into an emergency.
If there's a temperature fluctuation of 5 degrees at the thermostat near PDU3, please treat it as a non-emergency issue. However, please check the status of the CRAC units to be safe. Please also follow the instructions in the first bullet point above: email operator@oit.rutgers.edu and ei_dcts@email.rutgers.edu to inform them of the situation. If outside of normal business hours and you are unsure of a proper course of action, please contact your Supervisor for direction. Make sure to monitor the situation carefully, as it may escalate into an emergency.
The degradation or loss of a single CRAC/HVAC unit in the Machine Room may not constitute an immediate emergency. This would be a failure local to the unit a failed compressor circuit, a blown fuse, etc. A failure of units 2, 3, or 4 is currently less problematic than that of units 6, 5 & 1 (in that order), given the potential heat loads in these areas. Moving the PDB equipment to a "hot pod" configuration shortly will improve this situation. A failure on a single CRAC unit should not create panic. Assess the situation as best you can and note the machine room temperature. Please also follow the instructions above in the first bullet point. Email operator@oit.rutgers.edu and ei_dcts@email.rutgers.edu to inform them of the situation. If outside of normal business hours and you are unsure of a proper course of action, please contact your Supervisor for direction. Make sure to monitor the situation carefully, as it may escalate into an emergency.
When we receive an auto-generated email from the HVAC units, the Application Support consultants on duty should go to the unit in question and conduct a physical inspection. Check the unit for leaks and any alarm conditions. You should then report your findings to operator@oit.rutgers.edu and ei_dcts@email.rutgers.edu.
Emergency Issues / AC Failures
The following scenarios will constitute a CRAC/HVAC EMERGENCY:
A significant and/or steady increase in temperature of the Machine Room.
Extreme flooding under the floor (unable to contain/control leak and/or flooding an area of 6+ floor tiles).
Loss of use/power to 2+ HVAC units at any given time.
Emergency issues require a call to Facilities, John Lyons, and/or RUPD. If it is business hours, speak to a supervisor. If it is after hours, call a supervisor, who will likely tell you to call RUPD, which can get in touch with the on-call Facilities people. Be sure to gather as much information as you can both before you make the call and when/if facilities personnel and/or RUPD arrive. Send mail to operator@oit.rutgers.edu and ei_dcts@email.rutgers.edu and cc all the people involved in the situation.

If *All* CRACs are in ala, follow the procedure below:

This scenario requires swift and immediate action. This indicates a problem with the water loop serving all the CRACs.

There is a loss of water flow in the loop due to: (most common)

Failed pump/motor
Low/lack of water makeup/ emptied tower
Leaking/broken pipe somewhere
Clogged strainer(s)
Clogged heat exchanger
The supply water is too warm due to (less common)

Restricted heat exchanger
Failing pump/motor
Blocked water flow in the tower(s)
After Standard Business Hours:
Contact RUPD immediately and request assistance from Facilities.
Contact John L, John W & then David until you get a hold of one of them on their cell phone numbers. Do not wait to move on to the next. If John Lyons doesn't answer, call John W immediately and so on. Stop the contact chain when you reach one of them unless they mention otherwise. Please also call a Manager. If you're unable to reach a Manager, proceed to call Ana.
After placing these calls, please try to keep line 2293 clear in case any of them are returning your call.
Continue to send mail to operator@oit.rutgers.edu and ei_dcts@email.rutgers.edu and cc all of the people involved in the situation.
During Standard Business Hours:
Attempt to locate John and/or John/David in their office.
If you're unable to locate them, call John's cell phone.
If no answer, call David at his office, then call again if he doesn't answer.
If you're unable to reach any of them, please notify a Full-Time Supervisor or Manager.

Fire Systems

Next to the UPS alarm panel is the machine room sprinkler alarm panel. This system is separate from the building alarm panel, which is located next to the loading dock door. Smoke detectors and sprinklers are located on the ceiling and under the floor of the machine room and are all tied to a CO2 suppression system. This panel is not to be reset or touched at any time. In the event of a fire, emergency shutoffs have been placed at the main entrances. There is a fire extinguisher at the end of the machine room, opposite the Application Support office.

Cage (COLO)

A fenced, locked area of the machine room is available for organizations and departments that wish to house equipment in a controlled environment. After hours, they are not primarily responsible for the equipment. After hours, have access to the area for monitoring the HVAC in the cage.

Fire Alarm Procedures

Building fire alarms: - Building alarms are located in the hallways and the data center. These are the large red strobes (and bells in the data center). These alarms are tied into the panels in the Fire Command Center, located to the left of the 5th-floor elevators. This alarm panel is tied directly to the Rutgers Fire Department and will notify them in the event of a problem. A remote panel is located at the loading dock entrance to help emergency personnel quickly identify the alarm's location in the building.

Emergency Power Off (EPO) - These are located within the machine room, next to the Operations door, and both main entrances. They are red with yellow plastic around them. Properly labeled as "Liebert Emergency Shutoff". They will immediately shut down all HVAC units and PDUs within the Machine Room by tripping the main breaker. Once pushed, you cannot quickly restore HVAC service, as facilities will need to reset the breaker. FOR THIS REASON, these buttons will only be pushed if you actually *see* a fire or visible smoke, after which you should call 911 immediately.

FAMILIARIZE YOURSELF WITH THESE PROCEDURES, AS YOU WILL NOT BE ABLE TO REFERENCE THE WIKI IF THESE EVENTS OCCUR

Building fire alarms:

Secure door to Machine Room Office
Exit the computer room via the nearest exit, securing all doors behind you
Contact RUPD (2-7211) from the loading dock phone or your cell phone

C02 System dump:

Secure door to the operations consultant's office
Exit the machine room via the nearest exit, securing all doors behind you
Contact RUPD (2-7211) from the loading dock phone or your cell phone

In every situation, you are to EVACUATE the building IMMEDIATELY!

Sprinkler System:

The sprinkler system installed in the Hill Center is a two-stage dry system (unlike the rest of the building, which is always filled with water). During the first stage, the pipes above the ceiling become flooded with water. The second stage involves a component in the sprinkler heads that melts at a certain temperature. When the component melts, the water is then released.

The sprinkler system is activated when one or more smoke detectors in the computer room detect smoke. These detectors are mounted in the ceiling and contain a red LED. The LED will light if smoke is detected in that area. When a ceiling smoke detector detects smoke, the system floods with water. This activates the alarm above the panel in the DC as well as the building alarms (notifying RUPD).

" KB0014914 IT Help Desk - Application Support Parking Permits "

Parking Guidelines for Application Support Staff Lot 64

This article outlines the parking privileges and expectations for Application Support staff, including the use of Lot 64 on the Busch Campus.

General Parking Guidelines

Application Support staff may be eligible for a special parking pass to park in designated stalls in Lot 64 if they regularly work early-morning or late-evening shifts.

If you do not qualify for special parking, you are expected to park in the lot appropriate for your permit type:

Commuter lots for commuter permit holders
Student lots for student permit holders
Faculty/Staff lots for staff permit holders

If you believe you have an exceptional circumstance that requires parking in Lot 64 during your shift, speak directly with your management team.

Lot 64 Parking Rules (In Effect at All Times)
All University parking policies must be followed.
Do NOT park at the loading dock under any circumstances.
Pulling into the loading dock to sign in when late,d then moving your car is strictly forbidden.
Violations may lead to disciplinary actions.
Abuse of parking privileges can result in revocation and/or further disciplinary action.
The four stalls in Lot 64 are reserved for Application Support staff only.
To report unauthorized vehicles in these stalls during off-hours, call Parking Services at 848-445-2978.
Additional Parking Privileges

Some Application Support consultants may be granted additional parking privileges through management.

Conditions for Additional Parking Access
Privileges are only valid after you file the required paperwork with the Parking Department (parktran).
Tickets issued before your paperwork is submitted are your responsibility.
These privileges only apply while working for Application Support.
They are not valid while attending class.
If you have class before or after your shift, you must move your vehicle to a valid student lot before or after work hours.
Parking in Lot 64 is only allowed while actively working for Application Support.
Parking Citation Appeal Process

If you receive a citation while working a scheduled shift, copy the form below, fill in the required information, and email it to hd_mgr@oit.rutgers.edu to request an appeal:

Subject: Request to Revoke Parking Citation

Request to revoke parking citation for <Full Name>

License plate #: <Your Plate Number>
Citation #: <Citation Number>
Date & Time of citation: <MM/DD/YYYY HH: MM>
Lot & Campus: Reserved Stalls in Lot 64, Busch Campus

<Name> confirms they were not attending class when this citation was issued.
<Name> also confirms that they filed the additional parking paperwork with the Parking Department before receiving this citation.

Reminders
Only use the Application Support stalls when actively on duty.
Ensure your car is moved before or after your shift to avoid violations.
Contact your manager if you're unsure about your eligibility or parking status.

" KB0014917 IT Help Desk - Application Support Security Operations Center (SOC) and RU CIRT Esclataions "

Security Operations Center (SOC)


The SOC's mission is to maintain a secure information environment at the University. Critical areas include computing incident response, regulatory compliance, information assurance, and overall best practices for secure computing.

Rutgers University Computing Incident Response Team (RU CIRT)

The Rutgers University Computing Incident Response Team (RU CIRT) is a part of SOC and handles computer security complaints, such as spam, Denial of Service (dos) Attacks, and abuse of email. Email complaints are generally directed to abuse@rutgers.edu.

More information can be found at the RUSecure website

SOC and After Hours

After Hours serves this division as an Emergency Contact, generally during off-business hours. Consultants will contact SOC and/or RU CIRT, as requested, to pass along information. RU CIRT handles computer security complaints, such as spam and abuse email.

Reasons After Hours would contact SOC and/or RU CIRT

Directed by law enforcement officers
Issues involving safety
Issues that impact the operations of RUNet (TD informs Apps of the incident)
Issues that impact the Internet as a whole (TD informs Apps of the incident)
Issues that put the University in a negative light or publicity that would be an embarrassment to the University
Escalation Procedure

After Hours may get calls from other departments looking to speak with RU CIRT or SOC. For these inquiries please create an incident for EI - Security Operations Center and if necessary escalate to SOC@OIT.rutgers.edu

" KB0015027 IT Service Delivery and Support IDM - Processing Guest Requests "

Guest Request System is an enterprise self-service tool that enables a guest to submit a request to obtain a digital identity at Rutgers University. The request will be submitted for approval to the appropriate sponsor. Once the request is approved, the departments' guest administrators process it, and the guest is notified with instructions on obtaining and activating their digital identifier called a NetID. *Do not claim a guest request if you are not processing it before you leave for the day. Once claimed, no one else can see it.*

The person's Guest NetID can be processed by doing the following:

Go to OR and log in
Under Process New Guest Requests click on Search Pending Requests
Click Find Requests

The OIT Help Desk can process RBHS requests. However, if the Department has any of the following listed, we DO NOT process them, as these departments have their own admins to process Guest requests:

CINJ
GSBS
NJMS
RWJMS (RWJ)
SDM (unless it is "SDM OIT Health", then Help Desk would process it)
SHP
SN
SPH
UBHC (UCHC)
The OIT Help Desk always processes Guest Requests for the department: "OIT - IT Health Sciences" regardless of Campus. (they get PHI=Y flag based on dept level)
Click on the Request ID to read any special notes
Verify the following:

Compare the Department listed on the Guest Request to the Department/School/Unit listed on the Sponsor's active role in OR (click on the sponsor's NetID in the guest request list to see their active roles). If they are not similar (does not have to be an identical match but should be close), continue the steps below, and at step 9, Deny the request with a comment that the departments must be the same.
If the Department listed on the request would be affiliated with RBHS (such as those listed in step 3), then the Campus listed on the Request must also be RBHS. If it is not, continue the steps below, and at step 9, Deny the request with a comment that the Campus must be RBHS.
Click Claim
Click Process
Click Add Person to search for the user
Look through the results pages that come up to see if the person is already in the system. Pay attention to the birthdate and SSN.

If the person is listed and does not have an active Guest role, click on Add Role.

When processing an RBHS guest request, you must provide a reason and select Other from the drop-down menu.
If the person is there and has a Guest role that lasts longer than the requested role and is not an RBHS Guest role, Deny the request.
If the person is there and has a Guest role that is shorter than the role being requested and the current role is not an RBHS Guest role, do the following:

Note the existing NetID
Go to http://registry.rutgers.edu and click on Update Person
Search by the NetID and click on the entry
Click on the Guest Role and update its end date to the day before the new request's start date. For example, if the new guest request begins on 1/15/2025 and the current guest role ends on 1/31/2025, change the current role's end date to 1/14/2025. This ensures that the new guest request can be added to the individual.
Go back through steps 6, 7, and 8 to approve the request
If the person is not listed, click on Add New person and role

When processing an RBHS guest request,, you must provide a reason and select Other from the drop-down.
For the OIT Help Desk, if an SSN was not provided, send the email below to the sponsor from accounts@oit.rutgers.edu.
If requested by your department, please keep track of how many Guest NetIDs you have done via the Guest NetID Tracker.

For instructions on reviewing or looking up the status of a request, please read: Early Employee and Guest NetIDs Requests Status article

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Please note that since a government-issued SSN was not provided we have created a pseudo-SSN entry in our system.

Please contact OIT Help Desk at 848-445-HELP (4357) or email accounts@oit.rutgers.edu if you require additional information or assistance.

" KB0016782 ITS ITS - Computer Naming Formats "

All ITS machines should have a sticker on it with the computer name. Alternatively, you can walk the user through finding the computer name on Windows or Mac.

Desktops
NBITS prefixes are included in the Hostname for those migrated to the RAD domain.
If no prefix is included in the Hostname (or the prefix is ITS), it has not been migrated yet (is still on the Echo (ad-its) domain).
The format is [Prefix (NBITS, if there is one)][Building Abbr.][Room #][Computer #].
Ex. CASC303C3 is in the College Ave Student Center, Room 303, and is the 3rd PC;
When a PC is imaged (setup a new machine), it is logged and physically labeled on the PC with non-T object name.
For new devices, the letter T is added at the end of the computer name to denote that it is being tested/setup/configured so it does not conflict with the existing device currently installed at the user's desk.
When setting up/installing the PC (at the designated location/office), the field tech changes the name on PC from T object to non-T object. Example: "RFO121C1T" changes to "RFO121C1".
Laptops
The prefix changes depending on what image it uses.
The prefix ITS- indicates the old ITS autopilot image.
The prefix NBITS- indicates the new RAD image.

All laptops are named after the ticket number they are purchased from.

Macs
Macs follow similar naming rules.
iMacs follow the desktop rules, only they use M at the end instead of C. (ex. RFO102M1 vs RFO102C1 (now NBITSRFO102C1)).
Macbooks are always [prefixed with ITS] [ticket #] [machine #].
There are a few exceptions to this rule such as:
Signage
Labs
Kiosks

" KB0016326 ITS ITS - Computer Replacement "

Computer Upgrade Cycle

All of our machines are in a computer upgrade cycle. This ensures that our users computers are the latest available model and have the most up to date Operating System (OS) and software. Currently, ITS replaces staff computers on a 4-year cycle.

Full Time Staff Undergraduate Academic and Student Affairs (UAA/SA)
Computer is upgraded for each user who is a full-time staff member on a specified scheduled basis.
As of Jun 2023 Dell OptiPlex 7000s are the standard desktop model used.
As of Nov 2025 Dell Latitude 7440/3440s are still under warranty and are the current laptop models used.
Student Staff UAA/SA
Student PCs are recycled old staff PCs. Replacements occur on a first come, first serve basis and there may be a waiting list.
Current models used OptiPlex 7050s and OptiPlex 7060s. These usually have hard drives in them. Replace with 120GB SSD
OptiPlex 7050s will soon be included into the upgrade cycle.
There might still be a handful of older computers out there (9020s and 7040s).
If you have one or find someone who does, please let us know and we'll prepare a newer PC for upgrade.
Full Time Staff External Departments
These computers are not replaced until they are bought again by the department. We will usually notify the department of any computers that are due for upgrades.
External Departments: Honors College, Veterans Affairs, Occupational Health (this is a department under Student Health), ROTC and more
Replacement procedure for deploying new computer
Boot computer to BIOS Setup (F2)
Go to General, select System Information, tab to page, and scroll down to find the MAC address (listed as LOM MAC). Add the MAC address to the ticket.
Tab back to the Settings column, arrow down to System Configuration, Integrated NIC, Enable w/PXE should be selected.
Tab back to the Settings column and arrow down to Power Management.
AC Recovery should be set to Power On
Wake on LAN should be set to LAN with PXE Boot
Tab back to the Settings column and arrow up to Boot Sequence under General.
Disable Diskette Drive, USB Storage, and CD/DVD/CD-RW Drive.
Apply and Exit to save these settings.
Create a new ticket for Sys Admins with the Short Description set as Create "T" object. The following information should be included in the ticket:
Whether it's a new or reimaged computer
Computer Name with "T"
If the computer you re preparing is a replacement, the name you give to Sys Admin should have a T at the end.
There cannot be 2 computers with the same name.
Ex: You re replacing "GTV402C1". The name of the computer you re deploying should be named "GTV402C1T". Once the new computer is setup, the name will be changed to remove the "T". If the computer is a new installation, you do not need the "T".
Department
MAC address
Contact the user to inform them that a replacement computer is being set up.
Ask the user if they currently have any special software installed on their computer.
Get the user's phone number.
Change the phone number in the ticket to the user's phone number, if not already set.
After "T" Object has been created, set up a replacement computer on the service desk and boot to NIC (F12) to install Windows.
Map replacement computer to users c:\ on the old computer, to verify that the requested software existed on the computer that's being replaced.
If needed, list the verified special software on the ticket and assign to Sys Admins to have the needed software pushed out to the computer.
Leave the computer on and connect.
Verify that software has been installed. If it still needs software installed, contact Field Support for instructions.
When the replacement computer is ready, contact the user to schedule the install date and time.
Add install to ITS Calendar.

See a FT Supervisor if you are unsure of any step.

Common User FAQs
Do I need to contact my department head/supervisor when I m notified by ITS that my computer is scheduled to be replaced?

Yes. Once your computer replacement has been scheduled, please notify your department head / supervisor that the replacement will be taking place.

How long does a computer replacement generally take?

Computer replacements generally take 1-2 hours to complete. During this time, you will not have access to your computer as your old computer will be removed and your new computer will be setup, with a brief overview of your new computer provided. Note: To avoid any data loss, we recommend that you backup your data via OneDrive prior to replacement date.

Do I need to be present during my computer s replacement? If so, why?

Yes. You will need to make yourself available during the entire replacement time slot. Although every attempt will be made to limit your time involvement during the process, please plan on being available during the entire time slot. During the replacement, questions may arise as to where to place the new components, etc. Your availability will be needed to answer any such questions. In addition, we will also need you to be present in order for you to log into your new computer.

What additional information will I need to provide ITS regarding my current computer configuration?

ITS will need to be informed as to any specialized software that you need to be installed on your new computer (i.e.: Event Management software, SPSS, any previously-purchased software, etc.).

What if I currently have specialized equipment connected to my computer (i.e. ergonomic keyboard, trackball mouse, widescreen monitor, etc.)?

If you currently have any equipment that is not standard, you are free to keep this equipment after the replacement and use it; however, in the event that any of it stops working, you will be responsible for its replacement unless you request standard equipment from us.

How often does my computer get replaced?

Currently, ITS replaces staff computers on a 4-year cycle.

What happens to my old computer?

Your old computer will be returned to our office and the contents of its hard drive will be erased. Depending on its hardware level, the computer will be surplused or prepped to be re-utilized.

" KB0016305 ITS ITS - Importing bookmarks from different computers "

Please follow the steps below if a user needs bookmarks from another computer:

Go to File Explorer.
Connect to the department's network drive, typically in the form of \\rad.rutgers.edu\dfs\Nbits\<Department abbreviation>
Click Backups and navigate to the specific computer.
Go to Favorites.
Open the Show All Bookmarks menu on the specific browser.
Drag and drop the files in the Favorites folder into the browser menu.

" KB0016370 ITS ITS - Installing a check scanner for business accounting "

To begin, the user will navigate to the Wells Fargo portal. Additionally, there is a backup link that users can use. For the backup link, they will select Sign On. User should have the sign on, as it should have been emailed to them before troubleshooting. Sign-on will be RUTGERS888 or something similar. Once the user is signed in, users will select Make a Deposit. On this page, the user can download the drivers, however, it is important to figure out which driver is needed for the user.

When going over installing a check scanner for a business account, it is important to note the following:

Bomgar will be necessary for these incidents.
You must be logged in as your admin account for this to install correctly.
Have the user scan a voided check to ensure their driver is up to date and they are good to go.
There are tickets involving required updates for desktop deposit scanner drivers that can be referred to, to assist with the process if need be, as well as contact information for Wells Fargo IT personnel:

" KB0016490 ITS ITS - KACE - How to Lookup Devices "

Kace (also known as kbox) has a database of desktops, laptops, and other devices, among other things.

In this database is extensive information including, but not limited to:

IP addresses
Who the device is assigned to
Hostname
Last user (displayed by NetID)
Date of last inventory (useful for determining if the device is possibly unreachable through network)
Amount of RAM
System model (ex. "OptiPlex 7090")
Operating system
How much space is available on the hard drive
Open Kace and log in:
Username: NetID
Password: NetID password
Organization: NBITS

When you click Login (if your login credentials were correct/accepted), it may appear like nothing is happening; it is logging you in though. To speed up the process, press F5 on your keyboard.
If you do not have the right permissions, you may not see a column on the left-hand side (and if you do, you may not see any other category than Home).
Kace may appear as though you don't have the right permissions if the url is entered as kbox3.rutgers.edu/userui instead of kbox3.rutgers.edu/adminui.
Below is an example of having the correct permissions:



Select Inventory > Devices.
Use the search box in the upper right hand corner to search for the device. You can search by:
IP Address
Hostname (the computer name)
Assignee name (can do a search by NetID)
Description (rarely used; need to know the description beforehand)
Select the device to see an overview of information, with more information listed below (with expandable sections):
Inventory Information
Software
Activities
Security
Dell Command | Monitor
Dell Updates
Logs and Diagnostics
Asset

" KB0016331 ITS ITS - KACE - Software Deployment "

Log into Kace.
Select Inventory > Devices.




Search for the computer you are trying to deploy software and click it to go to the device details of the machine.
Go to Activities > Labels to expand the menu.
Click Manage Associated Labels at the bottom of the label list.




Search for the software deployment label until you find the software you are trying to deploy. Drag the label from the left to the right within the dotted line box with the heading Assign Label.




Verify that the label you chose is showing up in the Assign Label column. Then click Ok. Click the Save button.
The software will now try to install the next time it checks into Kace. You can expedite this process by clicking Force Inventory.

These instructions will work as long as the software you are attempting to deploy has already been set up in Kace. If the software is not already in Kace, assign to ESD - ITS Field Support.



" KB0016746 ITS ITS - Mail Merge "

Purpose: Mail Merge is a handy feature that incorporates data from both Microsoft Word and Microsoft Excel and allows you to create multiple documents at once, such as letters, saving you the time and effort of retyping the same letter over and over.

Mail merge lets you create a batch of documents that are personalized for each recipient. For example, a form letter might be personalized to address each recipient by name. A data source, like a list, spreadsheet, or database, is associated with the document. Placeholders--called merge fields--tell Word where in the document to include information from the data source.

You work on the main document in Word, inserting merge fields for the personalized content you want to include. When the mail merge is complete, the merge document will generate a personalized version of itself for each name in the data source.

Setup access to a different email account through the Outlook Desktop app

Setup an alternate email address in Outlook (must be as a new profile) if the user wants to use that email to send from instead of their own.

How to complete a Mail Merge
Ensure the User/you are signed into the Outlook Desktop app under the desired source profile/email they want to send from.
Note: In the upper right-hand corner, you will still see your profile. That is because the Microsoft 365 Office license for using Word is on your account; this will not mean that your email will appear on the emails being sent via the Word Mail Merge as long as you selected the correct profile in Outlook (ex. "Oncampus").
In Word, go to Mailings. At the top to the right of File, Home, etc.
Select Start Mail Merge.
From the dropdown menu, Click Step-by-Step Mail Merge Wizard...
An internal window/menu will appear on the right-hand side of the screen. Select the desired document type.
There will be a description below the options for clarification; it changes based on the currently selected option.
Once a document type has been selected, click Next: Starting Document.
Under Select starting document, select the desired option and click Next: Select recipients.
Under Select Recipients choose the desired option depending on who the document is being sent to:

If Use existing list is chosen:
When you click Next: Write your e-mail message, In a new window you will be prompted to select an existing Excel file containing the recipient list.
If Select from Outlook contacts is selected:
When you click Next: Write your e-mail message, a small window will open prompting you to select a profile.
Click OK
A new window will open saying Select Contacts while showing the number of contacts and the location (the email address associated with the Outlook profile).
Click OK.
The small window will pop up again for you to select a profile.
Click OK and a window with Mail Merge Recipients at the top will appear.
Select the desired contacts and click OK.
If Type a new list is selected:
When you click "Next: Write your e-mail message", a window titled New Address List written at the top will appear.
Fill out the fields (Title, First Name, Last Name, etc. The most important being the email address (to be put in address line 1))
Click OK.
A new window will pop up asking where the new list should be saved. Enter a name, select the desired location, and then click Save.
A window titled Mail Merge Recipients appears showing the recipients. Click OK.
Click Next: Write your e-mail message.

It will tell you to write your e-mail/prepare your document/etc. if you have not already done so.
Click Next: Preview your e-mail message.
Here, you can see how the e-mail/document/etc. will appear to the recipient and also allows you to change/edit your recipient list or exclude a recipient on the right-hand side.
Click Next: Complete the merge.
It will now tell you Mail Merge is ready to produce your e-mail messages.
Under Mailings click Finish & Merge.
A drop-down menu will appear. Click Send Email Messages...
A small window will appear that titled Merge to E-mail. Select an option under To and in the Subject line put a subject and click OK.
Another window titled Check Names will appear. Find and select your recipient(s)
Click Ok.

With that, your emails are sent.

" KB0016479 ITS ITS - Naming Convention for ITS Machines "

Desktops
NBT or NBITS prefixes are included in the Hostname for those migrated to the RAD
domain.
If no prefix is included in the Hostname or the prefix is ITS, it has not been migrated yet (is still on the Echo (ad-its) domain).
There should be none left on the ad-its domain. If there is, notify Shanna Spurlock (ss4458) or other Full Time to contact ITS Field Support.
The format is Prefix (if there is one), building abbreviation, room number, computer number.
Ex. "NBITSCASC303C3" is in the College Ave Student Center, Room 303 and is the 3rd PC.
Laptops
The prefix changes depending on what image it uses:
The prefix ITS indicates the old ITS autopilot image.
There should be none left on the ad-its domain. If there is, notify Shanna Spurlock (ss4458) or other Full Time to contact ITS Field Support.
The prefix NBITS indicates it's on the RAD domain.
All laptops are named after the ticket number they are purchased from.
Macs
Macs follow similar naming rules

iMacs follow the desktop rules, only they use M at the end instead of C. (ex. "NBITSRFO102M1" vs "NBITSRFO102C1").
MacBooks are always prefixed with ITS ticket number, machine number.
There are a few exceptions to this rule such as:

Signage
Labs
Kiosks
When a PC is imaged, the student logs and physically labels the PC with a non-T suffix name (For example, "NBITSRFO121C1T" gets labeled as "NBITSRFO121C1").

When setting up/installing the PC (at the designated location/office), the field tech changes the name on PC itself from the T-suffix name to the non-T-suffix name.
Example: "NBITSRFO121C1T" changes to "NBITSRFO121C1".
Other special purpose machines
Otherwise, everyday computers usually follow this naming scheme.

Note: If there are any questions regarding the above, contact Shanna Spurlock (ss4458) or ITS Field Support.

" KB0016492 ITS ITS - New User Account/Reactivation "

Submitting a request (User's Supervisor/Manager)

Supervisors/managers for new users must submit a New User Account Creation form. They do this through the request form on the IT Help portal.

Go to the NB-ITS New Staff Onboarding form or you can navigate to the IT Help Portal > Request Service > search for "ITS".
From there, the requestor can fill out the form and submit it.
Receiving a request (ServiceNow)

Once the request is submitted, a few things happen in ServiceNow:

The form automatically generates a REQ (Request), which acts as a parent to the subsequent RITM.
The form automatically generates a RITM (Request Item), with the short description listed as the completed form name "NB-ITS New Staff Onboarding", and acts as a parent to any subsequent SCTASKS.
The form automatically generates 1 or 2 SCTASKs (Service Catalog Tasks), depending on the information in the request, under the RITM.
One of the SCTASKs will have the short description listed as "Account setup, service activations, and integration for User" and will be automatically assigned to the ESD - ITS System Admins queue.
If there is another SCTASK generated, it will have the short description listed as "Repurpose, reimage or purchase equipment for User" and will be automatically assigned to the ESD - ITS Field Support queue.
Processing a request (System Admins)

When the requestor submits the form, it automatically generates a SCTASK (Service Catalog Task) in the ESD - ITS System Admins assignment group s queue.

The System Administrator does the following:

They create a ticket from the SCTASK in their queue.
They check the NetID in CAT to make sure they have the correct delegated email address (may be connect.rutgers.edu at first) and licensing allocated.
If this was an internal transfer and user still retained their old email (ex. "ipo.rutgers.edu"), the corresponding delegated admin group assigned to the new user's previous department may have to be contacted to initiate the process for the release of the email to "connect.rutgers.edu".
They make sure the user has a mailbox and migrate the email account into the appropriate domain.
This may be "echo.rutgers.edu" or department specific (such as "zimmerli.rutgers.edu")
They copy the permissions (from the person that the requestor says to copy from), and grant access to any requested shared resources which may include:
Shared Drives
Departmental Calendars
Departmental Emails
Software accounts, which the System Administrators would also create if necessary.
Once the above tasks are complete, they may move the ticket to ESD - Information Technology Services (ITS) for a New User Orientation (NUO) to be conducted.
Processing a request (Field Support)

When the requestor submits the form, it may automatically generate a SCTASK (Service Catalog Task) in the ESD - ITS Field Support assignment group s queue.

Field Support does the following:

They check whether there's a device that needs repurposing/reimaging (usually, a computer that was previously used by someone else).
If there is, they acquire the computer, reimage it, deliver it to new user, and assist them with setting it up.
If not, they get a leased machine that fits the needs of the new user's role, they image it, deliver it to new user, and assist them with setting it up.
If the requestor needs to purchase any devices, Field Support will work with them throughout the purchasing process, image the device, deliver it to new user, and assist them with setting it up.
Processing a request (Consultants)
Once the ticket reaches the ESD - Information Technology Services (ITS) queue, the consultant will make an incident task for dispatch requesting a New User Orientation.
The consultant will reach out to the new user or the requester to get a direct contact number for the new user.
When the consultant gets in contact with the new user, they schedule a dispatch appointment for an in-person New User Orientation.
Conducting a New User Orientation (Consultants)

The goal in mind for conducting a New User Orientation is to help set up the new employee/user with everything they need to make their start as smooth and easy as possible.

The dispatch consultant should follow the checklist for the New User Orientation (NUO) with the user and answer any questions they may have.

Note: If anything is still pending, it should be noted in the work notes. Consultants are kindly reminded that tickets should not be resolved following a New User Orientation if something is still pending, such as the user s email being migrated from a different domain. After the New User Orientation is complete, and there's no other pending items to address, the incident can then be resolved.

" KB0016491 ITS ITS - New User Orientation "

Has the user been able to log in?
Make sure the new employee/user can log into the computer for the first time, if they have not already.
Outlook
Help them set up Outlook on the desktop application.
Help them get on Outlook through the website/inform them about connect.rutgers.edu
Activate Services

Direct them to https://netid.rutgers.edu/index.htm then click on Service Activation, to activate services (Rutgers Connect (Office 365), Webex, Box, Adobe, Rutgers ScarletApps/ScarletMail (Gmail), Zoom, Rutgers VPN).

Computer
Explain how to connect any peripherals (mice, keyboards, cameras, etc).
Setup their default programs.
Go to Control Panel > Default Programs > Set your default programs. In this window, you can set the default programs for things such as email and web browser based on their preferences.
To set defaults for things such as PDFs, the best way to do so is by file type. You can do this by going to Control Panel > Default Programs > Set your default programs > Choose default apps by file type > (find .pdf and click next to it to set the default program to Acrobat, Acrobat Pro, or Acrobat Pro DC)
The list might take a bit to load.
Under Name (towards the top left-hand side of the window), you ll see file extensions listed, such as .386, .3fr, .3g2, etc. (common extensions include .jpeg, .bmp, .pdf, .html, etc.)
Set them up with Remote Desktop and VPN instructions if they need it (put a screenshot of device name and IP on the desktop).
Show users the Snipping Tool. This will help capture error messages when they want to talk to the Help Desk.
(For laptops) Install/set up RU Wireless Secure.
Explain they are not administrators on the computer, but we will help them out to get what they need.
If they ask why, it s because it's a security risk. If hackers get into an admin account, they could seriously harm our network and access sensitive information.
Ask if there is a printer they need to connect to; assist them with this.
Make user aware they should never shut down their computer, as we push updates at night and it's important that we keep their computers up to date with the latest security patches.
They can, however, restart their computers as needed and should do so periodically to prevent their computers from running slow.
Show them how to log off, lock, or restart the computer instead.
Software
Install any software they need/may need (ex. Cisco AnyConnect, Teams, Slack).
Explain that some software is provided by ITS through our group policy.
Show them the Software Portal (software.rutgers.edu) and explain that this is where they can download additional software.
Remember to inform them that some software may need to be purchased, in which case, they are advised to go through their department for approval.
Getting IT Help
Teach them how to contact and interact with the help desk.
Show them how they can submit tickets.
Point out the stickers on their monitor, the contact info, and the computer name.
If not done previously, show the new employee/user the Snipping Tool and explain that this will help capture error messages and other details when they want to submit a ticket to the Help Desk.
Explain that they need to save any snips/screenshots and attach the images to the incident.
Offer to show them how to do this.
Remind them to always try to reboot first when they run into an issue, then call us for additional support. Rebooting their computer can fix many issues, especially if they haven't restarted their computer in some time.
They can select restart on the computer if it s running slow or running into strange issues, but never select Shut Down due to the reasons mentioned in step 8 located above.
Inform them that it is best to call us if they have any issues; give them our phone number ((848) 932- 7248).
Inform the new employee/user that if they emailing, please include a call back number directly to them, include their department information, and their computer name (should be located on the top right side of their desktop). Request that they also put a direct callback number in their email signature.
Explain that for resetting voicemail pins, line transfers and other telephone services, they get more information and assistance at it.rutgers.edu/voice.
Ensure you have answered all their questions and that they don't need any additional assistance.

Note: If anything is still pending, it should be noted in the work notes. Consultants are kindly reminded that tickets should not be resolved following a New User Orientation if something is still pending, such as the user s email being migrated from a different domain. After the New User Orientation is complete, and there's no other pending items to address, the incident can then be resolved.

" KB0016272 ITS ITS - Outlook - 365 Shared Calendar Not Updating "

When an individual creates or edits a shared calendar entry in Outlook and "Download shared folders" is enabled in their mail account, Outlook may not upload changes to the?Office 365?cloud server. Thus, no other individuals will see those changes synchronized to their version of the shared calendar.

Disable "Download Shared Folders" on the advanced tab under "More Settings" in the change account window in outlook.

Go to File > account settings, and then account settings in the drop-down menu.
Select your mail account.
Click More Settings on the exchange account settings window.
Click the Advanced tab then disable Download shared folders by removing the check from the box.
Click Apply.
Click Okay.
Restart outlook.

" KB0016329 ITS ITS - Outlook - Only Opens in Safe Mode "

This describes the procedures that one can take if they are only able to access Microsoft Outlook in safe mode.

Remedial Action 1

Try restarting the computer and launch Microsoft Outlook again.

Remedial Action 2

Disable Add-Ins:

Click on File > Options > Add-ins > Go to the right of Manage: COM Add-ins.
Check if there are add-ins listed, and clear the checkbox.
Close the Office program and restart it.
Enable each add-in one at a time, restart Outlook, and repeat the above procedure. If the issue reappears again, it will help you to determine which add-in is causing this issue.
Remedial Action 3

Remove/Re-add Outlook Profile:

Go to Control Panel > Mail.
Select Mail > Show Profiles and remove the one that's there.
Add a new profile and name it Outlook
Fill in the name and email fields; leave the password fields blank.
Select Next.
The CAS log-in screen will appear, where NetID and password can be entered.

" KB0016327 ITS ITS - Printer - Add a Network Printer/Copier "

This describes how to add an ITS copier and/or network printer.

Using the Start Menu search (Magnifying glass icon - lower left taskbar) type Control Panel.
In the Control Panel window select View Devices and Printers.
In the Devices and Printers window click on Add a printer.
Select The printer that I want isn't listed.
Choose: Add a printer using TCP/IP address or hostname, then click Next.
Under Hostname or IP address: Type in the IP address or the hostname (ex: RFO105ND1.its.rutgers.edu)
Click Next and follow the prompts on the screen.
How to find the IP Address / Hostname for an ITS Printer

ITS Printers should have a tag on them. An example tag is RICOHMPC4504EX-BSC106ND1

The bolded section is the hostname for that Printer. The full hostname would be BSC106ND1.its.rutgers.edu

Open Command Prompt: Press the "Windows Key + R", type "cmd" and press enter.

To find the IP address, ping the full hostname on the Command Prompt as shown below:

The IP address for RICOHMPC4504EX-BSC106ND1 would be 172.16.129.193

" KB0016308 ITS ITS - Printer - How to Deploy a Network Printer "

This article outlines the procedures on how to deploy a network printer.

You will need
Fluke
Ethernet cable
New printer
Label maker
Before you leave
Call up to network admins to see if they are in. You will need to call them when you are at the area to have them unlock the data port for the printer that will be plugged in.
Call user and find out which computers will need to have access to the printer.
Find out if there are any other printers in the room.
Once you're on site
Install the printer where the user would like it to be.
Be sure to remove all packaging materials from inside the printer trays, toner, etc.
Do not plug the network cable in yet.
Label the printer with the building name, room number, & the printer number i.e. (12MOR001P1)
Do not install any drivers as they will be deployed by active directory.
Call a network admin to unlock the data port.
Once the port has been unlocked, you may plug the network cable into the printer.
Wait 5 minutes and print out network config page. Update ticket # with MAC and IP and pass ticket to Network admins.

The remaining computers will obtain the drivers for the network printer once the group policy is updated on each of those computers. There are 2 ways to trigger a group policy update, one is by restarting the computer and the other is by running "gpupdate /force" at an elevated command prompt. If you choose to run the command, the user will need to log out and in for the change to take effect.

" KB0016320 ITS ITS - Printer - Issues Troubleshoot Checklist "

When you first get a call about a problem with printers, there are a few basic things you must check before you create a ticket and send someone out into the field. The following is a checklist of information you must get and things to try over the phone before doing anything else

Local Printers
Find out the model of the printer.
Find out the name of the printer (should be on a sticker).
Get the computer name the printer is connected to.
Make sure the printer is on and active.
Power cycle (turn off and on).
Make sure the connection between the computer and printer is not loose on either end.
Make sure the power cord is not loose.
Check to make sure the printer has paper and toner.
Check to make sure there are no paper jams.
Find out if the printer is showing up on the print menu as a printing option.
Ask about any messages the printer or computer may be displaying.
Replace ink/toner.
Verify the toner s protective strip is removed (plastic strip).
Verify the toner is genuine (aka not a third party vendor).
Network Printers
Find out the model of the printer.
Find out the printer name (should be on a sticker).
Find out the number of the port the printer is connected to.
Make sure the printer is on and active.
Power cycle (turn off and on).
Print a configuration page and give IP/MAC address to System Admin.
On the printer, set IPv4 configuration to Automatic or DHCP (automatic IP address configuration).
Ping the printer using command prompt to see if it is connected to the network.
Ping the computer using command prompt to make sure there are no connectivity problems.
Make sure correct drivers are installed and up to date.
Make sure the network cable is connected to the wall solidly.
Make sure the power cord is not loose.
Check to make sure the printer has paper and toner.
Check to make sure there are no paper jams.
Ask if anyone else connected to the printer network is having difficulty.
Find out if the printer is showing up on the print menu as a printing option.
Ask about any messages the printer or computer may be displaying.
On the printer, set IPv4 configuration to Automatic or DHCP (automatic IP address configuration).

If there is a connectivity problem where a network printer will not respond to pinging, ask the System Admins to check the port. Remote into the computer and check to make sure all drivers are properly installed.

" KB0016322 ITS ITS - Printer - Request to Move "

All large copiers must be moved by Material Services or the leasing company (whichever is more cost-effective). This applies to moves within the same building and floor. Moving copiers by non-authorized personnel may void warranties, as copier companies need to track their machines' locations.

Steps for a printer/copier move
Have department requesting move contact Material Services (or leasing company).
Get all information on the copier move date such as:
Current IP Address
Current Data Port
Destination Data Port
Date/times of moves
Schedule a dispatch appointment to be on site to rename/relabel and reconnect copier to network when it is going to be moved.
During the move
Provide System Admins with new copier IP/Data port information.
Verify IP is whitelisted for scan-to-email with a System Admin.
Verify that users can still print to the printer after it is moved.
