" KB0016477 ITS ITS - Remote Desktop to Work Computer Setup "

Before starting, the user must have been granted access to remote into the computer. Ensure the user is set up with permission to remote into the specific computer by following the instructions on how to grant access to the remote desktop.

Steps to remoting in
Get the required computer information. This means the computer/device name (also called the hostname), the Full device name (which would include the domain (ex. RAD, ECHO, etc.), and/or the IP address (using the IP address to remote in tends to work more often than using the device name or the full device name.
The full device name will be in the format of computername.rad.rutgers.edu
To get the full device name, navigate to the Start menu > Settings > System > About and look under Device Specifications.
To get the IP address, navigate to the start menu > type CMD (the command prompt should come up) > type ipconfig or ipconfig/all.
The IPv4 Address is what is needed.
Start the VPN.
Navigate to the Start menu > open Cisco AnyConnect Secure Mobility Client (not to be confused with Cisco Secure Client).
A small box will pop up. In the empty field (under VPN: Ready to connect), type in vpn.rutgers.edu > select connect.
Another small box will pop up with Cisco AnyConnect | vpn.rutgers.edu at the top. It will have fields for Username, Password, and Duo Action.
For Username, this will be the user's NetID.
For Password, it will be the user's NetID password.
For Duo Action, this will either be push (for a push notification), or SMS (for passcodes to be texted). They ll need to check their phone for this part.
Select Ok and the user should get something on their phone.
For a push, hit accept (most people choose this; sometimes the user might have to open the DUO app itself if they don t see the notification).
If the user chooses passcodes, have the user enter the passcode when prompted.
On the computer screen, another small box will pop up saying "Please click Accept. You are receiving this notice because you are a member of the ESD Help Desk with additional departmental access. If you are not a member of this group, contact SOC@oit.rutgers.edu so your membership can be adjusted". Tell the user to click Accept.
Navigate the user to go to the Start menu and search for, and open Remote Desktop Connection.
In Remote Desktop Connection, in the empty field next to Computer, type either:
The device name (also called the hostname)
The full device name (which would include the domain (ex. "RAD").
Or, the IP address (for more information, refer back to step 1).
For User name, have the user type domain\NetID
For example, "RAD\ss4458" (for the RAD domain)
If the user needs to change the username and it appears that it can't be edited, they will need to select the dropdown arrow (that has Show Options next to it).
This will expand the small window and unlock the field for the username to be entered or changed.

Select Connect when done.
If it is successful in reaching the desired computer/device, it will open a window that says "Windows Security" at the top as well as "Enter your credentials" and "These credentials will be used to connect to [XXX.XXX.XXX.XXX] (if connected by IP), computer name (if connecting using the computer name), or computername.rad.rutgers.edu (if connecting using the full device name)".
Underneath that, the user should see their name (if a domain\NetID was entered) and an empty password field. Below that, the domain\NetID should appear if they entered it.
If the domain\NetID was not put in yet (before the "Windows Security" window popped up), then no name will show and there will be two empty fields rather than one (for NetID and their RAD account password).
If the domain, username, or both are listed but incorrect, they can change it by selecting More choices at the bottom of the box above Ok.
This will show one option as:
[First name & Last name] and/or [domain]\[NetID]
The second option will show as Use a different account.
Select this option and it will allow the user to enter new information as described in step 5.
Select Ok.
If successful, it will show the computer login screen and log them in.
If someone else is already using into the same computer, it will show the log in screen but will say the (remoting) user s name and then below it will say "Another user is signed in. If you continue, they ll be disconnected. Do you want to sign in anyway?".
Clicking Yes will force the person current logged in to log out and is not advised without notifying them first (if possible).
In general, it is recommended to remote into a different computer instead, if possible, to avoid any issues.
Sometimes, they might get a small pop-up box that says "The identity of the remote computer cannot be verified. Do you want to connect anyway?".
For this, verify the user typed the computer/device/hostname or full device name correctly under Certificate name and click Yes at the bottom of the box if it is correct. It should proceed with connecting and they should see the log in screen or the desktop.
Troubleshooting

If a small box pops up saying "Remote Desktop can t connect to the remote computer for one of these reasons":

"Remote access to the server is not enabled"
"The remote computer is turned off"
"The remote computer is not available on the network"
"Your computer cannot connect to another console session on the remote computer because you already have a console session in progress"

Double-check and ensure the following is true:

The computer they are attempting to remote into is turned on.
Connected to the network (including being signed into and connected to the Cisco AnyConnect VPN)
Remote access is enabled for the user.
To ensure the user has the access to remote in, you can remote in with your RAD Admin account or connect to the destination computer with another computer, using the instructions in the how to grant access to the remote desktop KA.
The user is not in another existing session. You can tell if the error message "Your computer cannot connect to another console session on the remote computer because you already have a console session in progress" comes up.
A consultant can kick the user off by signing in with their admin account and waiting 30 seconds. Then sign out and the user should be able to remote back in to the device.

Typically, some of the potential causes not already mentioned on the popup could be:

The name was mistyped.
The wrong domain was typed in sometime through the remoting process.
The IP address was incorrectly typed in.
There is an IP-hostname mismatch (which would mean that the DNS would need updating).
Let a staff member know if you run into this issue so we can assist in troubleshooting the issue; if the DNS needs updating, it d go through one of our System Administrators to be resolved.
They did not launch Cisco AnyConnect and the computer they re trying to remote into is on a different domain than the computer they re attempting to remote with.
For example, they re trying to remote into a work computer or any computer on the Rutgers network from home or anywhere outside the university.
Check the RD Gateway settings. (Sometimes the settings in the section are set incorrectly.)
This would be changed through the small box that pops up when the user first launches Remote Desktop Connection. Select the dropdown arrow that has Show Options next to it > Advanced > Under the Connect from Anywhere section, select Settings.... and a new small box will pop up.
Under Connection Settings, there will be 3 options:
Automatically detect RD Gateway server settings
Use these RD Gateway server settings:
Do not use an RD Gateway server
Select Automatically detect RD Gateway server settings or Do not use an RD Gateway server and click OK. Continue the process mentioned at the beginning of step 4.
If a small box pops up saying "The connection was denied because the user account is not authorized for the remote login", it is because they need to be added to the Remote Desktop Users group in Computer Management.
If a small box pops up saying, "The remote computer that you are trying to connect to requires Network Level Authentication (NLA), but your Windows domain controller cannot be contacted to perform NLA. If you are an administrator on the remote computer, you can disable NLA by using the options on the Remote tab of the System Properties dialog box", verify they have the right RD Gateway settings mentioned above and the right domain preceding their NetID (Example: RAD\netid)
The domain should be RAD\ due to everything being migrated from the ITS/Echo domain and the device name should reflect that. If you see this message and the computer starts with ITS or you're not sure the computer was migrated, you may refer to The Naming Convention for ITS Machines.
This message should not appear anymore. However, if it does and/or you come across a computer with a hostname that begins with ITS, annotate it in the ticket and contact Shanna or other Full Time staff. It may need to be moved to Field Support.

" KB0016346 ITS ITS - Request for Additional Computers "

Below are guidelines for when a user/department places a request for additional computers. Collect any required info noted below and incidents can be assigned to ESD - ITS Field Support.
Request for New Staff

If the computer requested will be utilized by a NEW full time staff member then they will be supplied with a new computer that ITS purchases.

We will need to know the full name and NetID of the newly hired staff member and also what building/room number the computer will be deployed to.

Request for Existing Staff

If the computer requested will be utilized by an EXISTING staff member (in addition to their regularly used computer) or a STUDENT staff member, then the user\department must wait until a used 'off-cycle' computer is available.

Note: If the department does not want to wait for a used 'off-cycle' computer to become available they may purchase a computer out of their funds through our office.

" KB0016312 ITS ITS - Request for Additional Monitor "

Second monitors needed to be purchased by the requesting department. ITS has a stock of used 22" square monitors that can be used for this purpose with no charge to departments. ITS is still only responsible for each computer having one monitor. In the event that the second monitor fails, IT Services will only be able to replace it if we have another used monitor available. If no monitors are available and the user's department wishes to purchase a new monitor, they will need to provide their account codes for the purchase.

Change Short Description to "Full-time staff members request for second monitor"
Collect any info on their current monitor setup, and whether they are fine with a 22" square monitor if available, or if they wish to purchase another one.
Escalate incident to ESD - ITS Field Support.
Second Monitor Deployments

All second monitors are to be labeled Second Monitor placed under the stand. This label should be installed before taking the monitor to the client. Only use 22" square monitors. Never use a new monitor. Never use a widescreen monitor.

User purchase of additional monitor

NB-ITS has partnered with SHI to provide direct access to pre-approved technology. This includes essential IT accessories such as keyboards, mice, webcams, and chargers; and may include monitors. This partnership aims to streamline the ordering process and enhance access to the needed tools. The custom catalog is available directly through RU Marketplace. Follow the instructions below to access the custom catalog.

Navigate to MyRutgers
Launch RU Marketplace
Select SHI International
Select Company Standards
Select ITS Supported Accessories
Review approved options and check out. Items can be delivered directly to the user's office.

" KB0016341 ITS ITS - Request for Additional or Newer Student Computer "

Student computers are upgraded as viable staff computers become available from upgrades and replacements. If a department requests an extra computer, it will be put in the request queue.

Collect any info available for the request such as:
Current computer setup (model)
NetID of the user it's for (if it's for a specific user)
Building and room number
Reassign Incident to ESD - ITS Field Support.
Student Computer Upgrade Requests

Student-use computers are changed as better replacements become available. The computers used for this purpose are staff computers that were exchanged for new computers by our department. There are many reasons a computer cannot be used as an upgrade. New computer upgrades are taking place all the time; however, it is not known if the replaced computer can be used until it is checked, making it impossible to predict when replacements will become available.

We understand that in some cases this presents a problem for our users. Departments that require expedited or scheduled replacement have the option of asking ITS to purchase a computer to replace or supplement computers for student use. The funds required to purchase these computers are the responsibility of the requesting department.

Additional Computer Requests

An additional computer for non-full-time staff members can be requested and placed on a waiting list. The computers used for this purpose are staff computers that were replaced with new computers by our department. Most of the above information applies to additional computer requests, including estimated delivery and responsibility for funding.

" KB0016504 ITS ITS - Request for Computer Peripherals "

Optional Accessories available for purchase

NB-ITS has partnered with SHI to provide direct access to pre-approved technology. This includes essential IT accessories such as wireless keyboards/mice, webcams, and chargers. This partnership aims to streamline the ordering process and enhance access to the needed tools. The custom catalog is available directly through RU Marketplace. Follow the instructions below to access the custom catalog. Basic wired keyboards and mice can still be obtained through Dispatch.

Navigate to MyRutgers
Launch RU Marketplace
Select SHI International
Select Company Standards
Select ITS Supported Accessories
Review approved options and check out. Items can be delivered directly to the user's office.


Other Devices
Keyboards and mice

Users can get basic ones through dispatch. Wireless ones can be purchased on RU Marketplace.

Speakers

Users can get basic ones through dispatch. Users can purchase specific/special speakers through ESD - ITS Field Support.

Webcams

Users can purchase these through the RU Marketplace

Swipe Card Readers and Signature Pads

Users can purchase these through ESD - ITS Field Support.

Troubleshooting
Check USB connection.
Install appropriate drivers
Routers

Not supported.

Scanners

Users can purchase these through ESD - ITS Field Support. These are USB connections. Typically need a specific Cannon driver called CaptureOn Touch or Capture Perfect can be downloaded from the website.

Printers
All printers must be purchased through ESD - ITS Field Support.
We do not support all-in-one, multifunction, or ink-jet based printers.
For office copiers (i.e. Xerox, Ricoh, etc.), we support the scanning and network printing functions, not fax.
Network Printers
Network printers are on our network connected via Ethernet.
Naming convention: LocationP1/ND1. Example: LSHA301P1 or LSHA301ND1.
If there are multiple printers in one room, follow naming pattern like computers. Example: LSHA301P1, P2, etc.
Local Printers
Local printers are not on the network. They are connected to an individual PC via USB.
These printers are not assigned names, but you can rename once installed.
Laptops
We don t service personal laptops, only laptops bought through ITS departments.
You can identify if a laptop is ours by asking for the name of the laptop, which should be on a label.
Caveat: We can assist in setting up VPN and Remote Desktop on personal laptops.
External HDDs
Users can request purchasing external hard drives through ESD - ITS Field Support.
Send user a note asking what the purpose for the device and that they are responsible for any Rutgers data loss or misuses.
Mobile Devices
We support Androids, iPhones, and Windows mobile devices (Surface Pros).

" KB0016394 ITS ITS - Residence Life Access to Odyssey "

In order to access Odyssey, users must first have a HARS username and password. If they don't have one, they can request it from either Bill O'Brien (William.OBrien@rutgers.edu) or Ryan Kassis (rk745@echo.rutgers.edu).

Once they have their HARS account, they will need to log into a terminal server, which is where Odyssey runs. This is the only way to access Odyssey. If there are issues with the server itself, for example if you ping hmstsrl.ufcp.rutgers.edu and the ping requests times out, then Ryan Kassis needs to reach out to the Housing IT contact to let them know.

To access Odyssey
Make sure the user has a Remote Desktop profile file called Odyssey.RDP.
If the user's computer does not have this, they will need to have their System Admin deploy it via Group Policy.
Right click and edit Odyssey.RDP
Expand Show Options if it's visible
Ensure the Computer field is pointed to Odyssey server's address: ipohmsrds3.rad.rutgers.edu and the user name field is filled as follows.




Hit Save and click on Connect
Check the box that says Don't ask me again for connections to this computer then click Connect.




Click OK on the next screen.




At this point, they should be able to see the Odyssey icon on the desktop of the terminal server where they are logged in. In the future, if they need to remote access the server again, they can simply open Odyssey.rdp and enter their HARS account password.

" KB0016686 ITS ITS - Scheduling ITS Dispatch Appointments "

There will be times when troubleshooting or addressing an issue via phone or email does not result in a resolution. If that is the case, please speak with a Help Desk Supervisor so they can determine if the issue can be dispatched. Dispatch is a service offered by the labs on all campuses, where consultants visit the staff office area of the caller in order to assist them with their issue in person.

If ITS Field Support or Shanna requests via Slack for an appointment to be scheduled with a user, follow the instructions below starting at step 2. When it has been scheduled, reply as a comment to the Slack message and note that it has been scheduled, including the date and time of the appointment.

The groups that are supported by ITS can be found here.

For scheduling ResNet/RUWireless Residence Hall dispatch appointments, view that knowledge article.

L2/Supervisors scheduling appointment
Determine if dispatch is an option that can help the user.
Typically, dispatch can handle issues such as:
Computer move/set up
Software installation that cannot be completed over Bomgar
Cable or computer peripheral replacement
Determining if there are network/physical issues with a port
New User Orientation
If dispatch is not suitable, the incident should be reassigned to ESD - ITS Field Support. Examples of incidents that should be reassigned to Field include:
Equipment replacement/order
Machine migration to RAD
Access to display TVs
Once dispatch is deemed suitable, create a Task for the Incident.
In the Task, be sure to include the following:
Equipment to bring to the appointment and full details of the issue
For most appointments with port-related issues, they will want to bring the Dispatch Laptop, Fluke, and Ethernet cable.
The peripherals/cables needed for replacement
Location
Contact information
Contact person
Availability
Assign the task to the proper campus
NB-Labs - Busch
NB-Labs - College Ave
NB-Labs - Cook/Douglass
NB-Labs - Livingston
Schedule the Dispatch with the user.
Dispatch hours vary. If communicating with the user via email, inform them to call the Help Desk to schedule an appointment to avoid back-and-forth email.
If it is during dispatch hours, call over to the corresponding lab. Dispatch consultants can either take the dispatch right now if the availability works out or work with the user to schedule for a future date. If no dispatch consultant is available, a Lab Supervisor should be able to assist.
Busch - (848-445-2760)
College Avenue (Academic Building) - (848-932-0310)
Cook/Douglass - (848-932-9750)
Livingston - (848-445-3465)
Do not schedule same-day appointments without speaking to local staff.
If there is no dispatch consultant or Lab Supervisor available, the Help Desk can schedule the appointment with the user via the Bookings calendar.
If the Help Desk schedules an appointment, post in the esd_nb_hdlabs_dispatch channel on Slack with the following information:
Tag the appointments with [ITS] and [campus]
Incident number and Task number
Appointment date/time
After the dispatch appointment:
If the dispatch consultant resolves the incident, they will also resolve the TASK.
If they are unable to resolve it, they will reach out to the HD for further escalation (ITS Field FT).

Note: ITS Field Full Time staff may also schedule work directly on the bookings calendar with at least 48 hours' notice when there is pre-scheduled work and additional help is needed. This may include computer installations/moves, office moves, inventory, etc.

Canceling/rescheduling a dispatch appointment
Verify the user's contact info and appointment information vs what's in their Incident/Task and the Bookings calendar.
Add a Work Note to the Incident Task listing the reason why the appointment is being cancelled/rescheduled.
Reach out to Full Time and request that they cancel the appointment on the bookings calendar.
Post in the #esd_nb_hdlabs_dispatch slack channel:
Incident Number/Task Number [CAMPUS] Dispatch appointment for [DATE/TIME] was cancelled by the user
Handle the Task:
If cancelling, mark the Task as Closed Skipped. Resolve the Incident if the user no longer needs assistance regarding their issue; otherwise, handle the Incident appropriately.
If rescheduling, assist the user with booking a new appointment and update the Task accordingly. Post the new appointment info in the #esd_nb_hdlabs_dispatch Slack channel.

" KB0016296 ITS ITS - Shared Drive - Restoring Deleted Folders "

Restoring deleted shared folders. Only recently deleted files can be restored.

Access the shared drive. Navigate to the folder in which the files were deleted.
Right-click and choose Properties
Select the Previous Versions tab. A list of "Folder versions" will appear.
To restore the entire folder, choose the appropriate folder (which is sorted according to date) and click on Restore.
To restore a specific file only, choose the appropriate folder (which is sorted according to date) and click on View.
Copy and paste the appropriate files.

" KB0016310 ITS ITS - Shared Drive - Troubleshooting/Mapping "

Note: Before proceeding, we need to know the name of the folder the user needs (Ex. "Zimmerli", "Oasis", "Disability", etc.). Often times, users will request access to "the I drive", for example. This does not give sufficient information that is needed, as any letter can be assigned to the mapped shared drive, as long as that letter is not assigned to something else; it does not tell us the specific folder the user needs access to. Therefore, we must obtain name of the folder from the user before proceeding.

Ask if the user has been granted access to the shared drive or had used it previously?

If the answer is no, they would need to request access.
If they are Full Time Staff, escalate to ESD - ITS System Admins to grant access.
If they are a Student, ask for their FT supervisor to email the Help Desk for the request.
You can search ServiceNow incidents with the user's name to see if they have been granted access before.
Sometimes users may have issues accessing the shared drive, even though they've accessed it before.
One reason this can occur is the user is working from a non-Rutgers location and they are trying to access the shared drive but have not connected to the VPN (Cisco AnyConnect)
Alternatively, they may see the Cisco AnyConnect icon in the bottom right of the task bar and believe they are signed into the VPN but are not.
Another reason the user may not be able to access the shared drive is because the drive isn't mapped to the correct location.
This shouldn't anymore since the drives were migrated and when they were, most users already already reached out to us and and had the drives remapped.
However, if this issue does come up, the shared drive needs to be disconnected and readded.
If it doesn't begin with \\rad.rutgers.edu\dfs\Nbits for the path, it's likely mapped incorrectly.
You should see the name of the folder with \\rad.rutgers.edu\dfs\Nbits\folder next to it in parenthesis)
How to disconnect a shared drive

It is best to do this for the user, in a Bomgar session, incase any issues arise.

In File Explorer, on the left side bar you should see This PC (or My Computer), Network, and the mapped shared drive.
Right-click on the shared drive.
Select Disconnect.
You should see the shared drive disappear.
How to map a shared drive path

It is best to do this for the user, in a Bomgar session, incase any issues arise.

In File Explorer, Right-click on My Computer (or This PC).
Select Map Network Drive...
Select any drive letter (ex. "A", "I", "Z", etc.).
The letter does not matter, so long as it's not assigned already.
Write the path for the shared folder in the Folder: field (\\rad.rutgers.edu\dfs\Nbits\folder)
Make sure to check Reconnect at sign-in so the drive automatically connects each time the user logs in.
Let the user know that if they are working from home and wish to access the shared drive, they will need to launch Cisco AnyConnect and sign into it.
Select Finish.

If these steps are completed successfully, the shared drive should pop up after selecting Finish, though sometimes it can take a minute or two to connect.

" KB0016476 ITS ITS - Software licenses "

Most licenses can be procured from software.rutgers.edu.

If it is generic software (Firefox, Zoom, etc), it can be deployed to a computer via KACE. If you do not have access to KACE, reassign to ESD - ITS Field Support.
If users are requesting other free software that is available from the Software Portal, assist them with obtaining it.
If the user wants software from the Software Portal that requires payment, reassign the incident to ESD - ITS Field Support.

When it comes to software purchase requests, they go to ESD - ITS Field Support. While we will process these requests, departments will need to fund the purchases of software.

" KB0016314 ITS ITS - Synchronization Issues in Outlook "

When synchronization issues occur in Microsoft Outlook or in Microsoft Outlook Web App (formerly Outlook Web Access), you may experience the following symptoms:

You see differences or mismatches between the messages that you receive in Microsoft Outlook compared to Microsoft Outlook Web App.
You have an issue with Outlook, but the issue does not occur when cached mode is disabled.
Occasionally you receive error messages when you synchronize your Offline Folder file (.ost) in Outlook with your mailbox on a server that is running Microsoft Exchange Server.
You notice that some items such as email messages, appointments, contacts, tasks, journal entries, notes, posted items, and documents are missing from your .ost file or from your mailbox after you synchronize your .ost file and your mailbox.
Solution

Rebuild the .ost file; to do this, delete the .ost file, and then let Outlook download the information again from the Exchange Server.

The OST Integrity Check Tool runs only on .ost files and can be used to diagnose and repair synchronization issues. It scans your local copy of the .ost file and your mailbox on an Exchange Server. The tool compares items and folders in the file and in your mailbox and tries to reconcile synchronization differences between the files and the mailbox.

The OST Integrity Check Tool does not change your mailbox on the Exchange Server. The tool records any differences in a scan log so that you can see what differences the tool found and resolved. The scan log also identifies any situations that the tool could not resolve and that you must fix manually. The scan log is in your Deleted Items folder.

The OST Integrity Check Tool (Scanost.exe) is installed when you install Outlook in the following locations, as appropriate for the Outlook version that you are running.

For Outlook 2003: drive:\Program Files\Common Files\System\MSMAPI\LocaleID folder. Be aware that in this example, LocaleID is the locale identifier (LCID) for the installation of Microsoft Office. The LCID for English - United States is 1033.

For Outlook 2007: drive:\Program Files\Microsoft Office\OFFICE12

How to repair errors by using Scanost.exe
Exit Outlook 2003 or Outlook 2007 if it is running.
Double-click Scanost.exe.
If you have set up Outlook to prompt you for a profile, the tool also prompts you for a profile. In the Profile Name list, select the profile that contains the .ost file that you want to check. If you are prompted to Connect or to Work Offline, select Connect.
Select the options that you want. To have the tool automatically resolve differences that it finds during the scan, select the Repair Errors check box. If this check box is cleared, the tool logs the problems. However, the tool does not make the necessary corrections.
Select Begin Scan.

Note:

To view the scan log, start Outlook, and then open the Deleted Items folder. The tool does not scan the Deleted Items folder. Any problems are noted in a message that has "OST Integrity Check" as its Subject.
If you have problems opening your .ost file, you can use the Inbox Repair tool (Scanpst.exe) to diagnose and repair errors.
The Inbox Repair tool can be used on both .ost files and on Personal Folders (.pst) files. The Inbox Repair tool scans the .ost or .pst file and makes sure that the file structure is intact. The tool also tries to repair the internal data structures, if it is necessary. The tool does not interact with your mailbox on the Exchange Server.
How to rebuild the .ost file
Locate the following folder, as appropriate for the operating system that you are running.
For Windows Vista and later versions: C:\Users\<alias>\AppData\Local\Microsoft\Outlook
If the folder is not displayed, unhide the folder.
To do this, follow these steps (For Windows 8 and Windows 10)
On the View tab, select the Hidden items check box, and the File name extensions check box.

Note: This OneNote page information was copied from this link (to an article from 03/31/2022):
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-US/outlook/troubleshoot/synchronization/synchronization-issues-occur-in-outlook-owa

Additional note: The link/article applies to Outlook 2019, Outlook 2016, Office 2016, Outlook 2013, Microsoft Office Outlook 2007, Microsoft Office Outlook 2003

" KB0016321 ITS ITS - Transfer User Data Remotely "

This article explains how to transfer user data remotely when the user is requesting to have their files moved after relocating their office. This method does not require the client to be present at the computer; however, it assumes the user has logged onto the workstation at least once.

Open my computer and click on Map Network Drive.
In the Folder section, type in \\Source_Computer_Name\c$
example name: pcname.its.rutgers.edu
Check off Connect using different credentials and then input your admin credentials when prompted.
After the drive opens, go to the Users folder and select the user's NetID. This will take you to their user data directory.
Repeat steps 1-4 for the replacement computer, with the folder section \\Destination_Computer_Name\c$
Once both locations are mapped, open up the source and destination windows side by side and perform the following actions until all of the user directories are copied to the destination computer.
Open the same folder on the source and destination computers.
i.e. Desktop folder on both computers
Right-click and choose Select All to choose all of the files from the source computer.
Open up the folder within the destination computer and right-click to paste the information.
If you are prompted to merge existing folders, choose yes.
Repeat until all of the directories are copied over to the destination computer.

This method assumes that the client has logged into the new computer at least once beforehand. This is so that the user's directory can be created and a few other registry changes can take effect.

" KB0016311 ITS ITS - Windows Expiring License Error Message "

Upon reboot, user would get notification that the Windows key was expiring. To fix this, follow these steps:

Go to the Software portal.
Search for Microsoft Windows 10 Enterprise
Click the down arrow next to Get Product.
You will see Single user license (University Equipment only). Select Add to Cart at the bottom of the box and click check-out.
Select a delivery method (on the right-hand side, in the grey section)
Hover over My Account in the top right-hand corner of the site and in the drop-down menu, click My Software.
The Microsoft Windows 10 Enterprise License should show there and the key should be listed (you might have to select license information, more information, or something to that effect)
On the user's computer, go to Settings (the cogwheel on the left when selecting the Start menu).
Go to System and then on the very bottom of the left-hand column, click About.
Scroll down to Change product key or upgrade your edition of Windows.
Click Change product key and then click Next.
The License Key should be updated and the user should be all set.

In addition to personal RU Zoom accounts that students, staff, and faculty can provision, users can request Shared Department Zoom accounts to be used for Departments

If a user is requesting a Shared Department Zoom account for their department, please direct them to the following form: https://go.rutgers.edu/nbitsdeptzoom

" KB0015716 Labs Labs - Adding paper to Ricoh and Kyocera printers "

When to add paper to printers

Adding paper to printers can occur at any point in time, and it is especially beneficial to add paper before a printer is all out of paper. If you are unsure if a printer is low on paper, check the printer trays, the display screen on the printer, or Icinga. Icinga will display an "unhandled service" message regarding the paper trays if it is low or out of paper.

Checking paper levels on Incinga

To check toner levels & page counts, you will follow the steps below:

Navigate to Icinga. When logging in, log in with your NetID-adm account.
Select Overview > Hostgroups once you are logged in.
Select the appropriate campus.
The page will display all the printers located on the selected campus. Selecting each printer will provide relevant information about each printer. If a printer displays the error message "Unhandled Services", selecting the message will indicate the current issues a printer will have. An example error message regarding low paper in a paper tray is as follows:

Adding Paper

In the Rutgers Computing Labs, there are Ricoh and Kyocera printers. Ricoh printers have four separate paper trays that each need paper, while the Kyoceras only have one tray for paper. Each printer has its trays adjusted to fit 8.5x11 paper, and this is the only size they can print. Refrain from completely filling the paper trays when adding paper, as this will cause frequent paper jams.

Common Issues After Adding Paper

Sometimes after adding paper, the printer status will not update to reflect the new paper level. This will continue to cause an "unhandled service" error in Icinga and may also prevent the printer from printing. In this scenario, try restarting the printer using the power button. This will clear the error and allow the printer to determine an accurate paper tray level.

" KB0018106 Labs Labs - Alarm Pins and Printer Lock Codes for College Avenue and Livingston "

This knowledge article will review the alarm pins and codes for College Avenue and Livingston. Alarm pins are needed to arm and disarm the alarm for the computer labs. Each consultant should be familiar with the alarm system. Printer Lock Codes are used to lock and unlock the printer paper trays.

College Avenue
Alarm Pin

Each College Avenue consultant will have a unique pin. An alarm pin is only needed for the Academic Building, as the library staff opens the DLC for consultants. Consultants are granted PIN access by full-time staff members. If you are unsure if you have been granted PIN access, please check with a supervisor or full-time staff member. Once PIN access is granted, consultants must contact Identity Access and Management to receive their code. The number for Identity and Access Management is 848-445-5050. Their office hours are Monday through Friday, 8 am to 4:30 pm. Consultants will need their Rutgers ID on hand when you call. The department will ask for the CVC number on the back of your Rutgers ID Card.

Printer Lock Codes

The DLC, College Avenue Student Center, and Art Library printers utilize locks on the paper trays. The code is 5625. The paper trays should remain locked at all times and be unlocked only when paper is added to the printer.

DLC-Specific Codes

All College Avenue consultants will have swipe access to the supply closet in the DLC. Inside the closet is the lock box, which can be unlocked by using the code 0319E. Inside the lockbox, there are keys for the consultant station cabinets and the iPad used to complete the room reservation forms. Please be sure to immediately return the keys to the lockbox after they have been used. The code to unlock the iPad is 454357. Please fill out the iPad Tracking form during shift change so we can track who has the iPad when it is not locked up. Remember to return the iPad and place it on the charger when closing.



Livingston
Alarm Pin

The Livingston Computing Center has a shared alarm pin that is the same for every consultant. The alarm pin for the Livingston Computing Center is 84331 for disarming at opening. The alarm code to arm it at closing is 84332. The library staff at Carr Library will open the building for consultants.

Printer Lock Codes

The printers at the Carr Library and the Livingston Student Center have locks on the paper trays. The code for the paper tray is 5079. The paper trays should remain locked at all times and be unlocked only when paper is added to the printer. At Carr Library, the paper is in locked cabinets, and a key is in a lock box to the far left of the cabinets. The code for the lockbox is 1235. The key must be returned to the lock box, which must be locked when not used. If the key is missing, please report the issue to the supervisors right away.

" KB0016858 Labs Labs - Audio Booth Troubleshooting "

The Alexander Library Digital Learning Commons (DLC) audio booth is available for patrons. To reserve the booth, patrons must schedule an appointment. For more details on how to book a room, please refer to the knowledge article on Bookable Rooms for Patrons.

General troubleshooting steps

If a patron is unable to record audio, follow these steps:

Ensure all cables are correctly connected to the mixer and the mixer is powered on.
Test each microphone by speaking into it and verifying its connection on the mixer.
Open Audacity and make a test recording with each microphone to confirm the audible playback.

If additional troubleshooting is required, please refer to the rest of this article for more information.

Accessing the computer

Patrons should log in to the audio booth computer using their NetID and password. If they encounter any login issues, check the network connection and verify the NetID and password.

Microphone setup

There are two microphones available in the audio booth. The microphones should be positioned close to the patron for optimal sound capture. A distant microphone may fail to pick up sound clearly, resulting in poor recording quality. When troubleshooting microphone issues, ensure the patron speaks clearly and is close to the microphone. The microphones are attached at the base of the microphone arm and the base of the microphone head. Please ensure these connections are secure.

Please refer to the following photos for reference:

Audacity guide

The audio booth is equipped with Audacity software for audio recording. For software troubleshooting, consult the Audacity Common Troubleshooting Guide. If further assistance is required, please reach out to a supervisor.

For reference, here is an example of what a successful audio test looks like in Audacity:



Here is an example of what an audio test looks like in Audacity when the microphones are muted:





Audacity settings

To ensure that the mixer is being used correctly by the software, please ensure that the following audio setup settings are being used in Audacity:

Audacity > Audio Setup > Host > MME




Audacity > Audio Setup > Playback Device > Speakers (Realtek(R) Audio)




Audacity > Audio Setup > Recording Device > Line (2- ProFX)




Audacity > Audio Setup > Recording Channels > 2 (Stereo) Recording Channels




Mixer guide

Below is a map and key describing most of the settings and features of the mixer:


Key:

GAIN: (first row of white nobs) Adjust these as needed, depending on how far away users sit from the microphones and whether their voices are picked up properly.
Compressor (COMP): (second row of grey nobs) This controls volume normalization - increasing it will cause louder sounds to decrease in volume and quieter sounds to increase in volume (more or less aggressively).
Equalization (EQ): (array of three rows of blue nobs in the center) Adjust these to enhance or remove specific frequencies. Patrons should adjust to their desired levels.
Effects (FX): (row of yellow nobs, fourth row from the bottom) Keep these turned off.
Panning (PAN): (row of white nobs, third row from the bottom) Adjust these depending on whether you want certain voices to be on the left or right; keep them in the middle otherwise.
MUTE: (row of red buttons with the word MUTE, second row from the bottom) To mute a mic, press the MUTE button. If a mic has no output, check that it is not muted and that it is properly plugged in.
LEVEL: (last row of large white nobs) Use this to adjust the actual volume of each microphone, and make sure not to set it too high since distortion and clipping can occur.
Power: (switch is on the back right side of the mixer above the MAIN OUTS label) The Power switch turns the mixer board on and off. Ensure it is turned on before use.
MAIN METERS: (two columns of indicator lights) A visualization of the audio levels, the mic may not be connected or muted if this is not lighting up.
BLEND: (single white nob to the left of the main meters) When the TO PHONES/CR button is depressed, the BLEND knob adjusts the CONTROL ROOM mix between inputs.
TO PHONES/CR: (square grey button below the BLEND nob) When depressed, the CONTROL ROOM mix is adjusted by the BLEND knob. When not depressed, the CONTROL ROOM mix will mirror the Main Mix.
CONTROL ROOM: (small white nob below TO PHONES/CR nob) When the TO PHONES/CR button is depressed, the CONTROL ROOM mix is adjusted by the BLEND knob. When the TO PHONES/CR button is not depressed, the CONTROL ROOM mix will mirror the MAIN mix.
MAIN: (single white nob on the lower right-hand side) Collectively adjusts the volume of all microphones.
Mixer settings

The audio booth features a mixer for adjusting recording settings. It is important to reset the mixer to its default settings before and after each use. When adjusting the mixer, ensure all cables are correctly plugged in and the system is powered on.

Please note that the outlined mixer settings are shown in the Mixer guide image above. The following settings should be used:

Mixer is plugged into the USB port on the back of the computer, not on the side.
M-002 (black cord) is plugged into Line 1.
M-003 (green cord) is plugged into Line 2.
M-004 (green cord) is plugged into Line 3.
HI-Z is pressed on.
LOW CUT is turned off.
GAIN is set to the maximum at +40, to the far right.
COMP is off, all the way to the left.
EQ is set to 0, in the center.
PAN is in the center.
MUTE is disabled, not lit up in red.
LEVEL is set to the maximum at +10, to the far right.
LINE IN 9/10 is turned off.
FX MUTE is turned off.
48V is turned off.
BLEND is in the center.
TO PHONES/CR is turned on.
CONTROL ROOM is in the center.
FX LEVEL is set to 0, in the center.
BREAK is turned off.
PHONES is turned to the minimum at 00, all the way to the left.
MAIN is set to the maximum at +10, to the far right.
Windows settings

If additional troubleshooting is required, you can confirm that the following audio settings are correct in the Windows Control Panel:

Control Panel > Hardware and Sound > Sound > Recording > Line 2 - ProFX is selected.




Select Line > Properties > Listen > Playback through this device, and select Default Playback Device.

General Information

Computer labs provide access to technology and support activities such as research, group projects, coding, and multimedia production. Patrons may reserve bookable rooms in the Digital Learning Commons (DLC) at Alexander Library and the Livingston Computing Lab in Tillett Hall. Because each room contains different equipment and technology, consultants should be familiar with the features and functionality of each space.

To use a bookable room, patrons must reserve a timeslot through LibCal and check in with an OIT consultant. Consultants can view and manage reservations through the LibCal staff portal at studyrooms.libraries.rutgers.edu.

Before granting access, consultants must remind patrons of the following policies:

Food and uncovered beverages are prohibited in all bookable rooms.
Rooms must remain locked when not in use.
Patrons may not use an unreserved room; a valid reservation is required, and standard check-in procedures must be followed.
Room lights must remain on at all times and may not be turned off.
Livingston Computing Lab

The Livingston Computing Lab contains two bookable Breakout Rooms (106F and 106G). Each room is equipped with a Mac Mini connected to a 55-inch television. Using Intel Unite, patrons can connect their personal devices to the room's system and share documents, websites, videos, and other content for collaboration and presentations.

Additional information about the Tillett Breakout Rooms is available on the Rutgers IT Computer Labs website.

Alexander Digital Learning Commons (DLC)

Alexander Library offers several reservable spaces within the Digital Learning Commons. While Rutgers Libraries manages the library's general study rooms, OIT consultants are responsible for the DLC bookable rooms.

Audio & Visual Booths (Rooms 132 and 133)

These rooms allow patrons to create professional-quality video recordings using integrated audiovisual equipment, including cameras and green screens. Lighting, audio, and video settings are preconfigured, enabling users to begin recording with minimal setup. The booths are available to Rutgers students, faculty, and staff with a valid NetID and RUID card.

Audio Booth (Room 130)

The Audio Booth is designed for podcasting, interviews, class assignments, and other audio projects. The room includes:

Four Shure SM7B microphones
Audio recording and mixing equipment
A computer with Audacity pre-installed
Seating for up to three users
Multiple power outlets

The room is available to Rutgers students, faculty, and staff with a valid NetID and RUID card.

DLC Conference Rooms
Room 126G

This conference room accommodates up to 12 users and is equipped with:

Cisco Webex Codec Plus system
Cisco Spark Room Kit Quad Camera
Two Samsung displays
Two table microphones
Two whiteboards
Adjustable podium
Room 131

This conference room accommodates up to 5 users and includes:

Television display
Whiteboard
DLC Huddle Rooms

Huddle Rooms provide a collaborative workspace for small groups. Each room includes a table, office seating, a Samsung TV, and a whiteboard.

Reservable Huddle Rooms:

Room 128H
Room 128M

Self-Service Huddle Rooms:

Room 128S
Room 128T

Each Huddle Room accommodates up to four patrons.

Additional information about the reservable spaces in the DLC is available on the Rutgers Libraries website.

Consultant Expectations

Consultants are responsible for monitoring and maintaining all bookable rooms at lab facilities:

Inspect bookable rooms at opening and before closing.
Report any room issues to a supervisor or full-time staff member as soon as possible.
At the end of each reservation:
Ensure the room is clean.
Verify that all equipment is functioning properly.
Room Reservations and RUID Card Collection

Consultants must complete the reservation form for all campuses. The reservation form is not required for self-service rooms in the DLC.

When patrons use a reservable room:

Collect the patron's RUID card.
Hold RUID cards at the DLC or Tillett at either the consultant station or the supervisor's office.
Never turn over RUID cards to library staff or security.

Before returning a patron's RUID card:

Inspect the room to ensure it is orderly and in the same condition in which it was provided.
Collect the Room Pass.
Document any discrepancies in the room assignments on the reservation form.
Notify a supervisor or full-time staff member of any issues as soon as possible.
End-of-Day Responsibilities

Before the last shift at the DLC or closing at Tillett, consultants must ensure all reservable rooms have been vacated.

Return RUID cards to patrons who are still present at closing time.
Turn over any unclaimed RUID cards to a supervisor for processing in accordance with standard lost RUID card procedures.
Additional Resources

Information about group study rooms and other bookable spaces across the New Brunswick campus can be found through the Rutgers Libraries Site, Bookable Rooms and Technology resources page.

Patron Self-Service Rooms

Within the DLC, there are rooms where consultants do not need to swipe patrons in. These spaces, called self-service rooms, allow patrons to enter without assistance from a consultant. Patrons reserve these rooms on the LibCal Site to promote fair and equitable utilization. Due to the self-service nature of these rooms, the reservation log is not applicable and does not need to be filled out, and RUID card collection is not necessary. Consultants are responsible for ensuring rooms are kept orderly and clean. In instances where verification of patron reservations or conflict resolution arises, consultants may use studyrooms.libraries.rutgers.edu.

The patron self-service rooms are as follows:

128D Pitch Room "Red Room"(Group Study Space)
128H Huddle Room (Group Study Space)
128M Huddle Room (Group Study Space)
128S Huddle Room (Group Study Space)
128T Huddle Room (Group Study Space)
131 Conference Room (Group Study Space)
Bookable Rooms that Require Patron Check-in & Check-out

These rooms require consultants to let the patrons in by swiping them in. When letting patrons into these rooms, consultants will follow the reservation log protocol to check patrons in & out.

The rooms that require patron check-in & check-out are as follows:

Breakout Rooms 106F and 106G (Livingston Computing Center at Tillett Hall)
DLC Audio Booth (Room 130)
DLC AV Rooms (Room 132 and Room 133)
DLC Conference Room (Room 128G)
Policies and Procedures Scripts

When letting patrons enter the bookable rooms, ensure you inform them of all room policies and procedures using the following scripts:

General Script

At the end of your reservation time, ensure you clean up and leave the room the way you found it. Please do not remove any furniture from the room or bring any furniture into the room. If there are any technical difficulties with any Rutgers devices in the room, please let a consultant know and do not try to fix it yourself. The door may not be propped open at any time, and the lights must remain on within the room at all times.

AV Room Script

Use the General Script and then add:

Ensure all mixer dials are reset to baseline after use. Make sure all equipment remains in its proper location and is handled with care. If there are any technical issues during your reservation, please escalate to a consultant; do not attempt to troubleshoot on your own.

One Button Room Script

Use the General Script and then add:

To use the One Button Studio, you will need a flash drive to insert. Do not move or tamper with the One Button Studio equipment. If you need assistance adjusting the equipment, please ask a consultant for help. To enable the green screen, press the green button. To start or stop recording, press either of the red buttons in the room.

Conference Room Script

Use the General Script and then add:

The conference room includes a tablet on the table and a stylus pen located beneath the TV. Please do not remove any items from the room. Instructions for connecting the tablet to the TV are printed on the tablet. If you need assistance, please ask a consultant.

How to Use the Reservation Form & Check Patrons In

When using the reservation check-in/out forms, begin by filling out the Check-In section. When a patron leaves a room, you will fill out the Check-Out section for that patron. The most up-to-date Alex DLC Reservation Form and the Tillett Reservation Form can both be found on the Level 1 and Level 2 Consultant Dashboard in ServiceNow.

Check-In Steps
Confirm patron reservation on studyrooms.libraries.rutgers.edu.
Request the patron's RUID card and confirm it is the correct one for the patron checking in. When taking the patron's RUID card, remind the patron that it is kept at the consultant station and will be returned upon check-out inspection. Remind patrons they cannot bring food or uncovered beverages into the bookable rooms.
Place the RUID card into the designated basket at the consultant station, and attach it to the colored card corresponding to the reserved room. Please provide the patron with the correct Room Pass for their reservation.
Open the reservation form for the respective location.
Within the form, fill out the appropriate fields.
Before letting patrons enter the room, inspect it to ensure it is ready for patron use. Refer to the inspection guidelines for what is expected in each room. Any discrepancies with the room should be noted in the reservation form with a photo and brought to a supervisor immediately.
Once the room is deemed ready for patron use, you may let the patron in.
Check-Out Steps
Open the reservation form for the respective location.
Within the form, fill out the appropriate fields.
Inform the patron that you will inspect the room before giving back their RUID card.
Inspect the room to ensure it is functional and ready for the next patron. Refer to the inspection guidelines for what is expected in each room. Any discrepancies with the room should be noted in the reservation log and brought to a supervisor immediately. Once the room is inspected, note it in the reservation log accordingly.
Return the patron's RUID card, collect their Room Pass, and indicate in the form that the RUID card has been returned.
Room Closure Reminders
If patrons are present in a room at the end of the final consultant shift, consultants must complete all room check-out procedures before leaving.
Patrons must be informed that the room closes when consultants leave for the night and that all RUID cards and Room Passes must be returned before departure.
Under no circumstances may patrons remain in any room after consultants have left for the night. All rooms must be vacated before the end of the final shift.
Consultants must provide patrons with advance notice of closing, including:
A reminder 30 minutes before the end of the final shift.
A final reminder 15 minutes before the end of the final shift.
Consultants are responsible for ensuring that all patrons have exited the room and that all access materials have been returned before leaving for the night.
What to do with Patron RUID Cards

Patron RUID cards are to be kept at the consultant station in the designated basket until patrons check out of the rooms. If a patron leaves their RUID card and does not check out, please report it to a supervisor so they can follow our guidelines for recovered patron RUID cards. Consultants should note that the patron did not check out in the Comments section of the reservation log. The room is still to be inspected and updated in the log.

" KB0015732 Labs Labs - Carr Library Printer Lock "

These locks should be locked at all times, and should only be unlocked when adding paper or servicing the printer. Please speak to a supervisor if you do not know the Carr Library Printer Lock Code!

To Unlock
Enter lock code as illustrated (pattern order shown in yellow).
Rotate dial clockwise to disengage the latch (same direction as red arrow).
Carefully pull the front piece forward to remove it.

To Lock
Carefully slide the front piece back into the metal bracket (metal bracket shown in first picture).
To avoid damage to lock and printer, do not use force. Rotate dial counterclockwise, and scramble the code.

" KB0018781 Labs Labs - Conference Room 128G Guide "

Table of Contents
Introduction
Screen Sharing
Connecting with Mersive Solstice
Connecting with Webex
Wired Connection
Conference Calls
Introduction

In the Alexander Library Digital Learning Commons, Conference Room 128G is an available room for patrons to book. The room includes a tablet that connects to the TV and supports multiple connection methods. Additionally, the TV is touchscreen and comes with a stylus pen for drawing, which is located on the bottom shelf.

Important note: The TV input must be set to HDMI 3 before utilizing the following functions.

Screen Sharing
On the tablet's home screen, one option is screen share. This will allow personal devices to be screen-shared onto the TV.




Connecting with Mersive Solstice
First, make sure Solstice is selected under the share screen tab.




Open the Mersive Solstice app, and enter the IP address in the search bar at the top of your screen.




Then there will be a prompt to enter the code that is displayed on the TV to connect the device.
Connecting with Webex
Rutgers students, staff, and faculty have access to Webex; however, they may need to activate their account first. Once activated, they can either access Webex through a web browser or by downloading the app. On the tablet, select Webex and open it on the device you wish to connect to.




Navigate to share.webex.com and enter the code as shown on the tablet to connect the device. Alternatively, open the Webex app and select the screen-sharing button in the top-left corner.




After selecting the share screen button, a new page will come up that should show the conference room TV (CAC-AL-128G). Select Connect and follow the prompts to begin screensharing. If the TV does not come up on this screen, select the blue button at the bottom to enter the 9-character code, then follow the prompts.




Wired Connection
The TV also has a USB port and an HDMI port for screen sharing from a personal device. These cables are located on the conference room table next to the tablet.




Conference Calls
The tablet also comes with Zoom, Teams, Webex, and a video-calling application installed, all accessible from the tablet's home page. To make calls on Zoom and Teams, enter the meeting ID and password if applicable. For the call function, enter a phone number to make the conference call. For Webex, enter a phone number, Webex account, or Webex room to join or start a conference call.
Important note: The TV input must be set to HDMI 3 when making a conference call.

Check all of the printers on Icinga to make sure they have enough paper
Walk around the DLC and ensure that all chairs and whiteboards are where they should be and are undamaged
If you noticed anything that was not correct, please reach out to a supervisor either in person or on Slack
Closing
Fill all printers with paper
Check each reservable room and make sure everything is where it should be, and all patrons have exited the room
Reservations end 30 minutes before closing, so ensure all patrons have exited by that time
When leaving the rooms, make sure the door closes fully so the rooms are secure overnight

Ensure that no RUIDs from reservations remain in the consultant station
Place all tools (hole puncher, staplers, paperclips, and tape dispensers) back into the cabinets and lock the cabinet
Take the iPad and cabinet keys back to the supply closet and place them in the lockbox for safe storage overnight

The iPad should be plugged into the charger located inside the lockbox
Walk around the DLC and ensure that all chairs and whiteboards are where they should be and are undamaged
If you noticed anything that was not correct, please reach out to a supervisor either in person or on Slack

" KB0017359 Labs Labs - Headsets for Patron Use "

The labs provide headsets for patron usage within the labs. This article outlines the necessary guidelines for student workers to follow regarding the use of headsets by patrons.

Logging and Tracking

When issuing or receiving headsets, student consultants must update the Headset & Adapter Loaning Tracking Sheet. It is important to log pertinent details is during the issuance and return of headsets. When issuing headsets, student consultants must record the date, time, patron name, and corresponding headset number. Upon the headset's return, consultants are required to note the precise time of return, if the patron ID was returned, and provide any pertinent comments if applicable. Before closing the labs, consultants should collect headsets from patrons to ensure all equipment is properly accounted for and to prevent loss or damage.

Collection of Collateral

To ensure the return of headsets, patron IDs will be collected as collateral. These IDs will be securely kept at the consultant station until the headset is returned.

Hygiene and Maintenance

Patrons are encouraged to use the provided covers on the headsets for hygiene purposes; however, they are not mandatory. Covers will be provided upon request. Following each use, it is imperative that student consultants clean the headsets meticulously using alcohol wipes, adhering to designated procedures.

Reporting Issues

Any issues with the headsets, such as damage or malfunction, should be reported to the supervisor immediately. The issues should also be reported within the tracking sheet within the comments section. Timely reporting allows for prompt maintenance or replacement of equipment, ensuring uninterrupted service for patrons.

" KB0015622 Labs Labs - Network Troubleshooting "

Labs troubleshooting can be necessary for a variety of reasons. Some symptoms may include but are not limited to, the following: patrons unable to log into computers, not being able to access websites, black screen with PXE boot messages including "TCIF" loading.

To troubleshoot lab machines, follow the steps below:

Check the ethernet cable. Make sure both ends are connected to the computer and wall port. Try unplugging and replugging the wire.
Testing port and computer by swapping the working cable and connection from another computer. Testing another cable and port will narrow down the issue to the original port or the computer port that is the problem. If the test passes, then the computer port is working fine. If the test fails, the computer port is likely the issue.
Test the cables by replacing the cable. You can either replace the cable or remove the existing cable and plug it into the working port. This will eliminate the wire as the problem.
Once you determine the test results and do not have networking, please escalate using the Down Machine Form. When contacting, please include the tests you did and the results.

" KB0018936 Labs Labs - PaperCut Print Release Guide "

PaperCut Print Release

Patrons may need their print jobs manually released by a consultant if there is a physical problem with their RUID card or if their RUID card is too old. RUID cards have a chip in them that interfaces with the printer when tapped. If the card is damaged or was created before Rutgers started installing the chips, but they have the card physically with them, then we may release their print jobs. We are not able to release print jobs for patrons who do not have their RUID with them. If you are unsure about releasing a print job, consult a supervisor first.

To release a print job, utilize the following steps:

Open PaperCut and log in with your admin account. If you log in with your student account, you will not be able to release the print job
On the left side of the screen, select Printers > Jobs Pending Release to view active print jobs




Search for the patron's print job by looking for their NetID under the user column
Select Print on the right side of the screen to force the print job through




Select one of the printers from the provided list of available printers to print the job. Once the job is sent, it will print immediately, so do not send it to a printer that is actively in use to avoid confusion



" KB0016196 Labs Labs - Plotter maintenance and troubleshooting - HP DesignJet T730 "

The HP DesignJet T730 printers can be found on the Busch and Cook/Douglass campuses.

Please refer to the other guide for maintenance and troubleshooting for the RICOH IM CW2200 plotter printer on the College Avenue campus.

The OIT plotters on Busch and Cook/Douglass are similar in the basics to other lab printers, with a few key differences outlined below:

Documentation for how to print to the plotter can be found in the Poster printing in the OIT computing labs guide.
The major process difference is that once users choose the proper queue (ARC Computing Center: arc_plotter_q, Loree Computing Center: lor_plotter_q), they cannot swipe their cards to release their print jobs.
Instead, they will talk to a consultant to release their jobs. Consultants will release the job through Papercut and charge the user for the indicated amount.
Each plotter has a piece of paper or laminate in the single-page feeder on the back right side of the plotter. This must be done so that the plotter can print from large-format paper.

As with regular printing, problems with print jobs should be resolved with a free reprint whenever possible. If it won't fix the issue, follow the standard procedure to request a printing credit.

Please talk to your local supervisor staff for the location of paper and toner supplies.

Loading a new roll of paper

Type of Paper: Roll HP Bright White Inkjet Paper

Open the roll cover.
Remove the spindle from the printer.

WARNING! Do not insert your fingers into the spindle supports during the removal process.
The spindle has a stop at each end to keep the roll in position. The blue stop can be removed to mount a new roll; it slides along the spindle to hold rolls of different widths. Remove the blue paper stop from the end of the spindle.




If the roll is long, rest the spindle horizontally on a table and load the roll on the table.
Slide the new roll onto the spindle. Make sure the paper type is oriented as shown. If it is not, remove the roll, turn it 180 degrees, and slide it back onto the spindle. There are labels on the spindle showing the correct orientation.




Roll the paper into the printer until you feel resistance and the paper flexes slightly. When the printer detects the paper, it beeps and feeds in the paper automatically.




The front panel prompts you to select the paper category and type.




Touch More paper types if you do not see your paper type in the front panel.
The printer checks the alignment and measures the width. If the roll is not correctly aligned, follow the instructions on the front-panel display.
Close the roll cover.
Touch OK when ready.
Replacing Ink
Make sure the printer is turned on.
Open the ink cartridge cover on the rear right-hand side of the printer.
Remove the ink cartridge.
Insert the new ink cartridge. Make sure you insert the ink cartridge into the slot with the same colored letter as the cartridge you are installing.
Close the ink cartridge cover.







Troubleshooting paper issues

There are various reasons why you may have issues loading the paper.

Ensure that no paper is already loaded.
Ensure the paper is far enough inside the printer; you should feel the printer grab the paper.
Do not attempt to straighten the paper during the alignment process, unless instructed by the front-panel display. The printer automatically attempts to straighten the paper.
The paper may be crumpled, warped, or have irregular edges.

Loading a roll of paper can fail for several reasons.

Check the leading edge of the paper: if it is not straight or clean, it should be trimmed. Remove the initial 20 mm (1 in) from the roll and try again. This may be necessary even with a new roll of paper.
Check that the roll ends are tight against the spindle ends.
Check that the spindle is correctly inserted.
Check that the output tray is closed. The roll will not load when it is open.
Check that the paper is correctly loaded on the spindle and loaded over the roll towards you.
Check that all roll end plugs have been removed.
Check that the paper is wound tightly on the roll.
Do not touch the roll or paper during the alignment process.
If the paper has not been inserted straight, follow the instructions on the front panel.
If you want to start the load process again, cancel the process on the front panel and rewind the roll from the end of the spindle until the edge is removed from the printer.

NOTE: The printer cannot load the paper if the roll has become loose around the core.

Paper error loading messages

Front-panel message:

Paper loaded with too much skew
Suggested action: During the paper load process, the printer detected that the paper had too much skew. Touch OK to unload the roll automatically, then restart the process.
Paper not detected
Suggested action: During the paper load process, the printer did not detect any paper. Ensure the paper is inserted fully and is not transparent.
The paper is too far from the lateral edge
Suggested action: Reload the cut sheet, aligning the right edge against the black stop on the spindle.
Paper load failed. Paper width is smaller than the minimum size supported by the selected paper source.
Suggested action: Cut sheets smaller than ISO A3 must be loaded using the multi-sheet tray.
Sheet too big
Suggested action: During the load process, the printer has detected that the sheet of paper is either too wide or too long to be loaded correctly.
Sheet too small
Suggested action: During the load process, the printer detected that the sheet of paper is too narrow or too short to be loaded in the printer.
Paper jams

You usually see a message about a paper jam in the front-panel display when a paper jam occurs. Be sure to remove all paper pieces and fragments from the printer.

Open the roll cover.
Open the top window.


Remove any jammed paper you can lift from the printer's top.
Cut the paper if needed




Try to move the printhead carriage out of the way.




Carefully remove any jammed paper you can lift from the printer's top.

 


Remove any of the jammed paper from the top window.
Close the top window.




Load the paper roll again.
Close the roll cover.
