Loading a new roll of paper

Type of Paper: HP Universal Bond Paper (Q1397A)

Removing the spool and paper roll
Lift the paper cover until it clicks.




Remove the bar under the paper roll cover (Bar A) and hang it in front of the catch basket.




Press the rewind button on the right side of the spool location for one second or more.




After you release the button, the paper is rewound automatically.
If the paper is not rewound, press the button again.
If no paper roll is loaded, insert the spool into the paper roll.
Hold the left and right flanges of the spool firmly, and then lift and remove the spool.
Important: When removing paper rolls from the paper input locations, lift the flanges on either side of the paper roll before removing them. Do not lift one side only. This could damage the machine.




Removing the spool from the paper roll
Place the spool on a flat surface.
Raise the lock lever on the left flange of the spool.
Remove the left flange from the spool.
Slide out and remove the right flange and spool from the paper roll or paper tube.
Inserting the spool into the paper roll
Insert the spool into the paper roll for replacement. Ensure that the paper is oriented so that it unrolls from and towards the opposite side facing you and unrolls from the bottom, not the top of the roll (see picture below).




Place the paper roll horizontally, and then slowly and gently insert the right flange and spool into the paper roll from its right until the flange touches the roll paper.
Important: Do not insert the spool into the paper roll while standing upright/vertically. Doing so can damage the flange.




Insert the left flange into the spool slowly and gently so as not to damage it, and then lower the lock lever on the left flange once it's inserted all the way in.
Important: Two people are required to assist with this step. This way, one person can steady the right end of the flange while the other inserts the left.
Loading the spool and paper roll into the printer
Hold both flanges and place the paper roll on the paper input location.
Place the spool so the black shutter covers its right edge.




Lift Bar A and hook it back into place.




Lower the paper cover.




When a message appears that confirms that bar A has returned to the correct position, press Exit.
Confirm that the paper type and thickness of the paper you loaded are the same as those displayed on the screen. If the paper type and thickness are different from those shown, press Change Settings. Select the correct paper type and thickness you want to use, and then press OK.
If you want to cut the edge of the paper, press Cut End of Paper, and then select Yes.




Press Start Feed. If the paper loading settings screen does not appear, open and close the paper input location cover. If the paper roll is not fed correctly and it is rewound, remove the paper roll and set it up again.
Ensure that the size of the paper roll you loaded is correctly displayed and that Ready appears on the Printer screen.
To make another cut

Sometimes, after loading the paper, an extra cut is needed to have a clean edge on the paper. The cut paper function cuts a paper roll at the specified length from the paper's leading edge.

From the Home screen, go to Settings.
On the right, select Maintenance > Cut Paper.
Select the paper input location and length to cut the paper, then press Start.
When you select Paper Input Location in Use, the cutting length is set to Short.
Press Close to close the dialog box.

To help determine how much paper needs to be cut for a clean edge, you can use a pen or pencil to mark the defective area to help make it more visible. Then, you can repeat cuts until the marked area has been removed.

Adding ink

The following messages may appear depending on the amount of ink.

When the Check Print Cartridge replacement appears, approximately 35% of the original amount of ink remains in the print cartridge.
When Printing is not possible even if only one cartridge is depleted appears, approximately 20% of the original amount of ink remains in the print cartridge.
When No ink appears, replace the corresponding color print cartridges.
See the animation on the machine's control panel for replacing the print cartridge.

Important things to remember when handling printer cartridges:

Replace the print cartridge after the message prompting replacement is displayed on the control panel.
Do not turn off the power to the machine when replacing the print cartridge. If you turn off the power when replacing the print cartridge, the specified settings are canceled, and printing will not resume afterwards.
Keep the ink horizontal when storing it.
After removing the print cartridge from the machine, please do not shake it while its opening faces downward. The remaining ink may spill out.
Do not pull out and put back the print cartridge repeatedly. The remaining ink may leak.
Do not touch the print cartridges' IC (integrated circuit) chip.
Do not open the cartridge cover other than when replacing the print cartridges. Doing so may result in misfeeds.
If, after replacing the print cartridge, the add ink messages continue to appear, turn off the machine's power, and then turn it back on again.
Push in the print cartridge until the lever clicks. If it does not click into place, ink may leak inside the machine.




How to replace the ink cartridge
Check the screen to see which print cartridge needs to be replaced.
If you have turned off the screen, on the Machine Status tab, press Check Status > Check.
Add ink by following the procedure shown in the animation.




Testing the print quality

The print quality should be tested after each time the ink is changed. Weekly print testing will be conducted by a supervisor. It may need to be tested more frequently depending on usage. The primary print quality test method will be the print nozzle check pattern. If this test comes back clean, then no further action is required.


If there are any issues with the print nozzle check pattern, please follow the troubleshooting steps below:

Print the nozzle check pattern.
Clean the print heads if the pattern is patchy, then print the test pattern and check the result.
Do not use the machine for at least 10 minutes, then print the test pattern again and check the result.
Clean the print heads, and then print the test pattern. Then, clean and print a second time, then check the result.
If the pattern is still patchy, flush the print heads, then print the test pattern again and check the result.
Important: Please note that flushing the print heads consumes a very large amount of ink. Perform this function only when necessary.
Do not use the machine for at least 10 minutes, then print the test pattern again and check the result.
This should resolve the problem. If the test pattern is still patchy, contact your service representative.

Additionally, an error will occur and print quality testing cannot continue if:

There is a misfeed.
One of the machine's covers is open.
The machine is performing maintenance operations.
The machine has run out of ink.
The machine has run out of paper.
The ink collector unit is full.
Print nozzle check pattern

Use this action to print the test pattern to check for non-firing nozzles. This maintenance task should be conducted after each time the ink is changed, as well as weekly by a supervisor to keep the print nozzles functioning well.

From the Home screen, go to Settings.
On the right, select Maintenance > Print Nozzle Check Pattern.
Select the paper input location from which you want to print the test pattern, then press OK.
Press Exit to close the dialog box.
Check the print results of the test pattern:
Normal print results:




If a nozzle is not firing:




Interpret the results. For example, if a print result indicates that the test pattern of K1 is not firing, select the option for Black (K1) when performing the Clean Print-heads action to reactivate the nozzle.
Clean print heads

Use this function to clean the print heads if a specific color is not printing or prints out unclearly.

From the Home screen, go to Settings.
On the right, select Maintenance > Clean Print-heads.
Select the color of the head you want to clean, and then press OK > Start.
Press Exit to close the dialog box.
Check the print results of the cleaning by printing the nozzle check pattern.
Important: Cleaning the print-heads should only be done twice per print-head before a flush is warranted.
Flush print heads

Use this function to clean the print heads far more thoroughly. It consumes much more ink than head cleaning. Use this function only if repeated head cleaning fails to reactivate a mis- or non-firing nozzle.

From the Home screen, go to Settings.
On the right, select Maintenance > Flush Print-heads.
Select the color of the head you want to flush, and press OK > Start.
Press Exit to close the dialog box.
Check the print results of the flush by printing the nozzle check pattern.
Important: If flushing the nozzle did not work to correct the issue, you should not perform any more print quality tests. If this is the case, contact RICOH support.
How to use the home screen

Press the blue Home icon at the bottom center to display the Home screen, on which icons for each function are shown.

System message

This section will be updated with any error messages or alerts. Depending on the error, it may show an animation with additional information on resolving the issue. Messages may also appear on the top right-hand side of the Control Panel. Check the status and fix the problem accordingly.

Check status

If there is a problem, such as a paper jam, the Check Status indicator lights up or flashes along with a message displayed on the screen. Press Check Status to check the status of the machine or application, and resolve the problem accordingly.







Press Check Status on the Home screen to confirm the following items: Machine's status

Statuses of the error and network
Operational status of each function
Status of a running job
Job history
Printing troubleshooting

A misfeed can happen for various reasons, such as the paper roll being misaligned or the sensors not detecting correctly. When this happens, the Control Panel will display a message indicating specific troubleshooting steps, depending on the type of issue. Please ensure you follow all messages, including closing the basket when instructed.

You may want to do a test cut if the paper is misfeeding due to an uneven edge on the paper roll. Please clear all residue from the tape so the paper is clean and smooth without any wrinkles or tears. You may additionally need to manually feed the paper off the roll into the catch area. Please be careful when taking this action so as not to touch the sensors inside the machine.

Important: If the error message Paper Misfeed is displayed, do not touch the encoder sheet, the ink tubes, the metal shaft, or the sensors inside the machine when removing misfed paper. Touching these parts may result in loss of print quality or machine failure.







Encoder sheet

Ink tubes

Metal shaft

Sensors

Important things to note about troubleshooting printing issues:

When clearing misfeeds, do not turn off the power. If you do, your printer settings will be lost.
Do not turn off the power to the machine when removing the jammed paper. If you turn off the power, the specified settings values are reset.
Only perform the operation described in the animated illustration. Failure to do so may degrade the print quality or cause a malfunction.
If the error message remains displayed even after you remove misfed paper, fully open the front cover, and then close it.
When removing jammed paper, you can touch only those areas explicitly indicated in the manual.
To prevent paper misfeeds, do not leave any torn scraps of paper inside the machine.
Do not load paper immediately after you remove misfed paper from the paper bypass location. Close the front cover after removing the paper, and wait until the Please wait message disappears from the screen before loading paper into the paper bypass location again.
Wrap the paper tightly around the paper core so there is no gap between the paper and the core. If there is a gap between them, the paper holder may spin while the paper roll remains still, which may cause a paper jam.

" KB0018913 Labs Labs - Reservation Room Active Check Procedure "

General Check-In Procedure

When checking a patron into a room, conduct an active inspection to ensure that everything is operational and in proper condition at the start of their reservation. This process helps identify when incidents or issues occur within the room.

During the inspection, complete the following steps:

If the room had a prior reservation, verify that the previous occupants have left and that the room is empty.
Turn on the room lights.
Inspect the room for any trash, spills, or other cleanliness issues.
Confirm that all furniture assigned to the room is present and undamaged.
Verify that any Rutgers-owned devices in the room are powered on and functioning properly.
Active Check Guidelines

An active check must be completed each time a patron is checked into or out of a room.

" KB0015351 Labs Labs - Toner Changes "

When to change toner

There are several instances when a toner change will be necessary. Toner change can occur when printouts are light/streaking or if toner is leaking out of the cartridge. Typically the printer will tell you that the toner is empty with the message "Replace Toner." If the toner is not affecting print quality, such as streaking or leaking, we do not change toners until it is empty; therefore, double-check the printer's toner levels to ensure it is empty.

If you decide to change toner, notify a supervisor that you are changing toner.

Checking toner levels & page count

In order to check toner levels & page counts, you will follow the steps below:

Navigate to Icinga. When logging in, log in with your NetID-adm account.
Select Problems > Service Grid once you are logged in.
Using the filter icon you will select Host Campus and then the appropriate campus. The campuses are listed below

Cook/Douglass you will enter the letter "D"
Livingston you will enter the letter "L"
College Avenue you will enter the letter "C"
Busch you will enter the letter "B"
Then select the appropriate printer. Once selected you will see Printer Toner Levels which will indicate what level the toner is at & Paper Pagecount will display the page count of the printer.
Toner Change Form

In the event, toner will need to be changed you will need to fill out the Toner Change Form for each campus.

Please note that Busch and Cook/Douglass does not have a toner tracker due to switching to Ricoh printers. As the Labs continue to transition to Ricoh printers, we will not use the toner change form, however until the printer change occurs, the listed campuses will continue to track toner.

The appropriate form for each campus is as follows:

Livingston
College Avenue
Changing Toner

In order to change toner, please follow the general steps below:

Wait until the printer gives an error that says, "Replace Toner" or "Empty Toner".
Printers that give the error "Low toner" will continue printing.
Open the Toner Change Form for the appropriate campus and get the page-count information of the printer.
Open the new toner kit, take out the new toner, an empty waste toner, and a plastic bag.
Get a blank toner label from the appropriate location (consultant station or from a supervisor) and put it on the new toner. Fill in the "In" information.
Take out the old toner and the old waste toner.
Fill in the "Out" information on the old toner. It will be the same as the "In" information on the new toner.
Place the old toner in the plastic bag and then in the box that you took the new toner out of. After this, throw away the waste toner.
Fill out the toner change form with the information from both labels.
Place the new toner and empty waste toner in the printer.
Seal the box and write "used" all over it and place it in the appropriate area for the campus. If you are unsure, please reach out to a supervisor.

Each printer type will have a video displaying how to change the toner. If you are unsure which kind of printer you are handling, reach out to a supervisor.

Plotter Printer
Further information regarding Plotter maintenance and troubleshooting can be found in the Plotter Printer troubleshooting Knowledge Article.

" KB0014259 Labs Labs - Tools USB Descriptions "

Each lab location has several USB thumb drives with tools for various purposes. Included on each is a suite of software and documents that can be useful, including:

Antivirus - Self-explanatory for the most part, just antivirus installers. There are also specific virus killers for TDSS and ZeroAccess. NPE is a general anti-rootkit.
Browsers - Portable browsers if the original browsers on the target device are compromised, and you need to access the internet. Not typically used.
Documentation - Some policy documentation, CIRT & waiver handouts, and other instructions for removing specific infections. Read these over.
Logs - Empty, useful to copy CIRT logs into if no internet access
NIC Drivers - Drivers for certain models of NIC, the current NetGear NIC is not included
Removal Tool Supplements - More specific infection removers
RUWireless Secure - All of the secure installers and certificates for manual setup
Supplementary - Other useful utilities, some repetitions
CCleaner - Cleans registry, temp files, and other temp files
CPU Z - Gets info about CPU hardware
Default File Extensions - Used to reset extensions to the standard programs
Assorted targeted virus removal tools
Various AV tools
Misc network analysis tools
Troubleshooting Tools
HijackThis - Analyzes general vulnerable programs
Wireshark - Packet monitor
Putty - SSH installer
Inssider - Signal strength analysis

If you expect to need a specific tool, you are free to upload it onto the drive before using it. Check the USB before going out to make sure it is still a tools USB and has not been converted by someone else into other media.

" KB0013972 Labs "Labs - Troubleshooting ""Login is Invalid"" Error When Scanning RUID on the Card Reader" "

What could be the issue?

On rare occasions, when a patron scans their RUID at the card reader to print, they could receive a "Login is invalid" error message, preventing printing.

There are three possibilities for why this error occurs:

The user's card is damaged and therefore, the encoded data from their card no longer matches the 9 or 15 digit identifier associated with their NetID
The backend system that holds NetID/RCN information has not been correctly updated
There is another issue with the user's account that we are unaware of, but PaperCut Support may have more info
How Can I Diagnose The Issue?
Verify Rutgers ID Card Encoded String

Here is an overview of how to troubleshoot issues where one or more users are not able to authenticate via swipe at a print release station or MFP:

Connect card reader to a Mac or Windows computer. Both operating systems should automatically recognize it as a keyboard device
Open an app like Notepad in Windows, or TextEdit in Mac OS
Have the patron swipe/wave their card, and the encoded data should appear in the text window

The data returned should be in one of two formats:

Likely format: a 15 digit number. This could be in between start and end characters ( ; and ? ), or just the 15 digit number

Example: ;123456789012345? , or 123456789012345
There could still be some older cards in use that will display a 9 digit number, with some ending control characters

Example: ;123456789=1234?

Copy either the 15 digit number from the first instance, or the 9 digit number in the second instance, and enter it in the Quick Find search section of the "User List" page in the PaperCut admin web interface. If the number is correct, the patron's NetID/name should be returned:

If the information returned from the swipe doesn't match the two formats listed above, the likely root cause is an issue with the patron's ID card, and they should have it replaced.

Verify Back End NetID/RCN Information

If the data encoded on the card does match either of the card formats above, but the user cannot authenticate and/or the quick search test fails, it's possible that the backend system that holds NetID/RCN information has not been correctly updated. The updates to the backend card system usually happen on a regular basis, so an option is to have the patron wait 15 to 20 minutes and try swiping again. If the above tests fail again after waiting 15-20 minutes, then the patron will need to have their card replaced or re-encoded to rectify the issue.

Escalating to NetOPS

If the issue cannot be resolved after completing the troubleshooting steps above, escalate to NetOps for further investigation.

Create an incident by following the Reporting Incidents to NetOps for Computer Labs using ServiceNow knowledge article.

Contact PaperCut Support

If these tests are inconclusive, the PaperCut support group can parse the PaperCut log files for possible clues. The following information should be collected and sent to the PaperCut support group at papercut_support@oit.rutgers.edu:

The patron s name and NetID
Which release station(s)/MFP(s) the patron swiped at
Approximate date/time(s) of the swipe attempts


" KB0018092 Labs Labs - USB-C to HDMI Adapters for Bookable IML Rooms "

The labs provide USB-C to HDMI adapters for use during bookings. Currently, the adapters are available at College Avenue and Livingston. This article outlines the necessary guidelines for student workers to follow regarding using these adapters.

Logging and Tracking

When issuing or receiving USB-C to HDMI adapters, student consultants must update the Headset & Adapter Loaning Tracking Sheet. Logging of pertinent details is imperative during the issuance and return of adapters. When issuing adapters, student consultants must diligently record the date, time, patron name, and corresponding adapter number. Upon the adapter's return, consultants must note the precise time of return and whether a patron ID was collected, as well as provide any relevant comments if applicable. Before closing the IML rooms, consultants should collect adapters from patrons to ensure all equipment is properly accounted for and to prevent loss or damage.

Collection of Collateral

Patron IDs will be collected as collateral to ensure the return of USB-C to HDMI adapters. These IDs will be securely kept at the consultant station until the adapter is returned.

Hygiene and Maintenance

Patrons are encouraged to handle the adapters with care; however, there are no specific hygiene requirements for the adapters themselves. Following each use, student consultants must inspect the adapters for any visible damage or wear. Any issues should be reported immediately.

Reporting Issues

Any issues with the adapters, such as damage or malfunction, should be reported to the supervisor immediately. The issues should also be documented within the tracking sheet in the comments section. Timely reporting allows for prompt maintenance or replacement of equipment, ensuring uninterrupted service for patrons using the IML rooms.

" KB0014209 LMS LMS - Google Classroom "

What is Google Classroom?

Google Classroom is available to anyone with an active ScarletMail account. It is a free suite of productivity tools, including Gmail, Drive, and Docs. Classroom is designed to help teachers create and collect assignments virtually, including time-saving features like the ability to automatically make a copy of a Google Document for each student. It also creates Drive folders for each assignment and for each student to help keep documents organized. Students can track what's due on the Assignments page and begin working with just a click. Teachers can quickly see who has or hasn't completed the work, and provide direct feedback and grades from within Classroom. To use Google Classroom, log into your ScarletMail Account and go to Google Classroom.

How does someone get Teacher Access for Google Classroom?

If you are a Faculty or Staff member at the University and require Teacher Access to Google Classroom, there are two necessary steps:

Log into your ScarletMail Account and go to Google Classroom. Once there, click on I'm a Teacher under the text Get Started Using Google Classroom.
Next go to this form and fill it out.

Once both of these steps have been done, your request will be reviewed and processed within 1 to 2 business days.

" KB0014202 Local IT Local IT - iLabs "

What are iLabs?

The iLabs are Linux-based computer systems used by the Rutgers Computer Science department to complete assignments and work on projects. These machines are located in the three rooms on the second floor of Hill Center: Command Center, CAVE, and Meltdown.

Handling iLab Incidents

Rutgers OIT does not officially support iLab machines. Feel free to assist the user with any information provided in this knowledge article; however, for any further troubleshooting, please redirect the user to the Computer Science IT department. In addition, do not assist users with compilation or runtime errors. These are things that they should ask their professors or classmates.

Logging in to the iLabs

The iLabs use different credentials from CAS. The user must be a CS student, have an active NetID, and must be registered through the activation page.

Using the iLabs

There are three ways to use the iLabs. Go to one of the rooms mentioned above and use the machines directly (except for iLab1-3 and iLabU1-4), via SSH, or via X2Go.

X2Go

This is similar to TeamViewer. The user can log in to the iLabs using this, and it is generally recommended for beginners. X2Go provides a desktop GUI, making it easy to navigate. Coding is done on the iLab when using this, but since it is similar to TeamViewer, it can be used from the user s machine to make it seem like they are coding on their own computers.

Setup instructions can be found on the Computer Science website.

SSH

Also known as Secure Shell. SSH is a protocol for remotely accessing another computer. It is a good way to use the iLabs if the user is familiar with the terminal. Just like X2Go, when the user is coding, the code is written and saved on the iLab, but the user writes it on their own machines.

There are many SSH clients available (Git Bash, PuTTY, MobaXterm, etc.). This document will go over MobaXterm, as it supports X11Forwarding (explained in How To Hand-in Assignments ).

Download and Install MobaXterm. The version doesn t matter as long as it is for the correct OS (if it is the portable version, installation is not required).
Run MobaXterm, then click the Session icon in the top-left corner.
A new window will pop up. Click on SSH.
The remote host will be any iLab hostname (see the iLab Hosts/Status section of this document).
Make sure that " Specify username is checked. Enter the user s NetID in the textbox.
Port should be 22 (SSH is, by default, on port 22).
Press OK. The terminal will pop up afterwards, prompting for the password.
Have the user enter their CS password. The password will not show at all when typing, including the * usually seen on website logins.
MobaXterm will prompt to save the password (for auto login). For convenience, save the password.
The user can now use the iLab.
Using their Own Machine

If the user is adamant about using their own machine, have them install a Linux VM or dual-boot Linux. The user should still test their code on the iLabs. It is highly recommended that the user test their code on the iLabs.

They can test their code by uploading it to Google Drive, downloading it to the iLabs, then compiling and running it using one of the methods above (X2Go, SSH, or directly using the machines in the CAVE, Command Center, or Meltdown).

Handing in Assignments
MobaXTerm

If the user is using MobaXterm, then they can forward the iLab s version of an application to their machine.

Run the command: google-chrome <website url>. This may take several seconds, depending on the user s machine.
A Chrome window will open with the requested URL. This is the iLab s Chrome instance running, so the user can use the browser as if they were directly using the iLab machine.
X2Go

If the user is using X2Go, they can run the iLab's included browser (Firefox or Chrome) and hand in their assignments through it. If the user is using a different SSH client than MobaXterm, they will need one with X11Forwarding. This is a long, generally difficult process to get working, so it is recommended to use MobaXterm. Documentation for X11 forwarding in PuTTY is available.

List of iLabs

There are multiple iLabs available for use. All of their hostnames and statuses can be found on the Computer Science iLab website.

Not all iLabs can be used by undergraduates. For example, gpu.cs.rutgers.edu is specifically for Graduate students taking a certain course. The Hadoop cluster is not considered an iLab, so regular students will not have access to it.

" KB0014131 MacOS MacOS - Consultant Manual - Americans With Disabilities Act Software (ADA) "

The Americans with Disabilities Act (ADA) requires that we provide tools to assist patrons with disabilities. Macs have many of these tools built-in. It is important that you are able to efficiently help anyone using this software, so please read the following information carefully. This guide is written using macOS Ventura, but prior versions of macOS include comparable settings and features.

Accessibility options can be found in the Apple Menu in the upper left corner of the Menu bar under System Settings. Here, the Accessibility menu holds various options under different sections based on the sense they are intended to assist with.

The options are broken into the following sections:

Vision
Hearing
Motor
General

The first group that is displayed is Vision for visual aids. Available options include:

VoiceOver - An accessibility interface that offers users magnification options, keyboard control and spoken English descriptions of what is happening on screen. VoiceOver is capable of reading aloud the contents of files, including web pages, mail messages, and word processing files. The complete keyboard navigation allows users to control the computer with the keyboard rather than a mouse.
Zoom - Allows users to enable or disable various methods of zooming in to onscreen content.
Display - Home for various visual options such as inverting colors, increasing contrast, adjusting the size of the pointer, and adding color filters.
Spoken Content - Allows users to set hotkeys and adjust various settings for on-demand screen reading.
Descriptions - Allows users to toggle the option for their computer to play available audio descriptions in visual media by default.

While these options are specifically designed to assist with the user experience for visually impaired users, keep in mind that other generic options such as increasing text size or resolution within a Mac's non-Accessibility Displays menu may also satisfy a user's needs.

The Hearing group has options intended to support users' audio experiences. Available options include:

Audio - Allows users to enable a screen flash to coincide with alert sounds, and switch audio playback from stereo to mono. Additionally, there are options for enabling and configuring background sounds to assist users with focusing and masking distracting noise in their environments.
Captions - Allows users to adjust the appearance of captions across macOS, and enable more detailed closed captions or subtitles for the deaf and hard of hearing where available.

Under the Motor group, users can find settings to adjust their control of the computer. Available options include:

Voice Control - Allows users to use their voice to control text input or interact with their computer, even while on a call.
Keyboard - Allows users to enable full keyboard control of their computer, with shortcuts available for all required actions. Also allows users to configure how long they need to hold down modifier keys, or adjust the timing between a keypress and its action. Additionally, users can enable an on-screen Accessibility Keyboard, bypassing the need for a hardware keyboard.
Pointer Control - Allows users to adjust the timing on when their computer recognizes a double-click, whether the Finder will pop out another window when a file is dragged over a folder (referred to as Spring-loading), and enable alternate control methods such as using their keyboard to move the pointer.
Switch Control - Allows users to enable and configure alternative control methods for their computer, including with a mouse, keyboard, gamepad, or other dedicated accessibility devices.

Lastly, the General group includes:

Siri - Allows users to enable the ability to type requests to Siri, macOS' digital assistant, rather than speak them.
Shortcut - Allows users to set various accessibility features to be toggled on or off with the keyboard shortcut Option-Command-F5.

For more information on macOS' accessibility features including prior versions of macOS, view the accessibility section of Apple's macOS User Guide.

" KB0016677 MacOS MacOS - Editing is Disabled on Office 365 Apps for MacOS "

This error is typically related to a licensing issue with the user's current installation of Microsoft Office applications. Follow the steps below to resolve the issue:

In order to remedy the issue, the following steps will need to be taken.

Steps to Resolve the Error

Sign Out of Office Applications

Launch each Office app installed on the Mac (e.g., Word, Excel, PowerPoint).
Click on the application name (e.g., Word) in the menu bar and choose Sign Out.
Quit the application.

Access the Library Folder

Open Finder.
Click on the Go menu and select Go to Folder.
In the box, type ~/Library and click Go.

Delete Group Containers

Navigate to the Group Containers folder.
Locate and move the following files/folders to the Trash:
UBF8T346G9.ms
UBF8T346G9.Office
UBF8T346G9.OfficeOsfWebHost

Empty the Trash

Empty the Trash to remove the files permanently.

Restart and Check

Restart the Mac.
Launch an Office application and verify if the issue is resolved.
If the Issue Persists
Reference Microsoft's instructional article: How to remove Office license files on a Mac.

" KB0014139 Mailman Mailman - Common Issues "

Owner can't post

Owners/Administrators (same thing) can't post to a list unless they are also members, or on the list of non-members permitted to post. This is the most common problem people experience when they are new to Mailman.

Member can't post

Members of a list will have their messages held for moderator/owner approval unless they are unmoderated members, or the list is configured to accept all postings.

Message size

Default message size limit on Mailman is 40kb. Messages larger than list's setting will not post. General Options page has an option to adjust the max size. A setting of 0 is equivalent to no limit.

Recommended: It is recommended that they do not send attachments through Mailman. Not only can it slow the entire system down if the list is large, but not everyone can receive attachments. Non-digest Option called "scrub_nondigest" will take attachments out of the mail, put the attachment in the list's archive (archiving must also be enabled), and then send a link to the archive where the file can be downloaded. This is the best way to send attachments through Mailman.

Wants to change name of list

Cannot be done. Must request a new list.

Changing Display Names in lists

If a user has a Mailman account setup, the display name for their email address in any list will always be pulled from the user's Profile settings. No matter what the owner of the list does. This includes if they have an account and did not specify a First and Last name in their profile, which will result in no Display Name being shown in any Mailman list.

There is no way to override a display name other than for the user to change their own.

If they do not have a Mailman account setup using the email address in someone else's list, that list owner can only specify a display name for them if they use the Mass Subscribe option to add them to the list and use one of the formats that specify a name when adding them.

There is no way to edit the display name after they have been added to a list.

Deleting lists

Users must email help@oit.rutgers.edu to have lists deleted.

" KB0016446 Mailman Mailman - Support Tool "

You can access the support tool at: https://lists.rutgers.edu/mm3/mailman3/lists/ which will present you with all current Mailman lists.

Main Page

Use Ctrl + F to search this page for a specific list. Clicking into the list will give you a subset of information about that particular list as noted below.

LISTNAME.config

Shows all current configured options for the list. Available descriptions for each field are noted below.

accept_these_nonmembers: This is a list, one per line, of regexps matching addresses that are allowed to post to this mailing list without subscribing to the list. Entries are regexps beginning with `^' and are matched against the sender addresses in the message. While non-regexp addresses can be entered here, it is preferred to add the address as a nonmember and set the nonmember's Moderation to Default Processing.
acceptablealias: This is a list, one per line, of addresses and regexps matching addresses that are acceptable in To: or Cc: in lieu of the list posting address when `require_explicit_destination' is enabled. Entries are either email addresses or regexps matching email addresses. Regexps are entries beginning with `^' and are matched against every recipient address in the message. The matching is performed with Python's re.match() function, meaning they are anchored to the start of the string.
admin_immed_notify: Should the list moderators get immediate notice of new requests, as well as daily notices about collected ones? List moderators (and list administrators) are sent daily reminders of requests pending approval, like subscriptions to a moderated list, or postings that are being held for one reason or another. Setting this option causes notices to be sent immediately on the arrival of new requests as well.
admin_notify_mchanges: Should administrator get notices of subscribes and unsubscribes?
administrators:
administrivia: Administrivia tests will check postings to see whether it's really meant as an administrative request (like subscribe, unsubscribe, etc), and will add it to the administrative requests queue, notifying the administrator of the new request, in the process.
advertised:
allow_list_posts:
anonymous_list: Hide the sender of a message, replacing it with the list address (Removes From, Sender and Reply-To fields)
archive_policy: Policy for archiving messages for this list
archive_rendering_mode: This option enables rendering of emails in archiver as rich text with formatting based on markup in the email. Currently, this option is only supported by Hyperkitty.
autorespond_owner: Should Mailman send an auto-response to emails sent to the -owner address?
autorespond_postings: Should Mailman send an auto-response to mailing list posters?
autorespond_requests: Should Mailman send an auto-response to emails sent to the -request address? If you choose yes, decide whether you want Mailman to discard the original email, or forward it on to the system as a normal mail command.
autoresponse_grace_period: Number of days between auto-responses to either the mailing list or -request/-owner address from the same poster. Set to zero (or negative) for no grace period (i.e. auto-respond to every message).

bounce_matching_headers:
bounce_notify_owner_on_bounce_increment: This option controls whether or not the list owner is notified when a member's bounce score is incremented, but to a value less than their bounce threshold.
bounce_notify_owner_on_disable: This option controls whether or not the list owner is notified when a member's subscription is automatically disabled due to their bounce threshold being reached.
bounce_notify_owner_on_removal: This option controls whether or not the list owner is notified when a member is removed from the list after their disabled notifications have been exhausted.
bounce_score_threshold: This is the bounce score above which a member's subscription will be automatically disabled. When the subscription is re-enabled, their bounce score will be reset to zero.
bounce_you_are_disabled_warnings: The number of notices a disabled member will receive before their address is removed from the mailing list's roster. Set this to 0 to immediately remove an address from the list once their bounce score exceeds the threshold. This value must be an integer.

default_member_action: Default action to take when a member posts to the list. Hold: This holds the message for approval by the list moderators. Reject: this automatically rejects the message by sending a bounce notice to the post's author; the text of the bounce notice can be configured by you. Discard: this simply discards the message, with no notice sent to the post's author. Accept: accepts any postings without any further checks.
Default Processing: run additional checks and accept the message.
default_nonmember_action: When a post from a non-member is received, the message's sender is matched against the list of explicitly accepted, held, rejected (bounced), and discarded addresses. If no match is found, then this action is taken.

id:
include_rfc2369_headers: Yes is highly recommended. RFC 2369 defines a set of List-* headers that are normally added to every message sent to the list membership. These greatly aid end-users who are using standards compliant mail readers. They should normally always be enabled. However, not all mail readers are standards compliant yet, and if you have a large number of members who are using non-compliant mail readers, they may be annoyed at these headers. You should first try to educate your members as to why these headers exist, and how to hide them in their mail clients. As a last resort you can disable these headers, but this is not recommended (and in fact, your ability to disable these headers may eventually go away).

reply_goes_to_list: Where are replies to list messages directed? No Munging is strongly recommended for most mailing lists.
This option controls what Mailman does to the Reply-To: header in messages flowing through this mailing list. When set to No Munging, no Reply-To: header is added by Mailman, although if one is present in the original message, it is not stripped. Setting this value to either Reply to List, Explicit Reply, or Reply Only causes Mailman to insert a specific Reply-To: header in all messages, overriding the header in the original message if necessary (Explicit Reply inserts the value of reply_to_address). Explicit Reply-to set; no Cc added is useful forannounce-only lists where you want to avoid someone replying to the list address. There are many reasons not to introduce or override the Reply-To: header. One is that some posters depend on their own Reply-To: settings to convey their valid return address. Another is that modifying Reply-To: makes it much more difficult to send private replies. See `Reply-To' Munging Considered Harmful for a general discussion of this issue. See `Reply-To' Munging Considered Useful for a dissenting opinion. Some mailing lists have restricted posting privileges, with a parallel list devoted to discussions. Examples are `patches' or `checkin' lists, where software changes are posted by a revision control system, but discussion about the changes occurs on a developers mailing list. To support these types of mailing lists, select Explicit Reply and set the Reply-To: address option to point to the parallel list.
reply_to_address: This option allows admins to set an explicit Reply-to address. It is only used if the reply-to is set to use an explicitly set header
request_address:
require_explicit_destination: This checks to ensure that the list posting address or an acceptable alias explicitly appears in a To: or Cc: header in the post.

send_goodbye_message: Send goodbye message to newly unsubscribed members? Turn this off only if you plan on unsubscribing people manually and don't want them to know that you did so.The text of Goodbye message can be set via the Templates tab.
send_one_last_digest_to:
send_welcome_message: Send welcome message to newly subscribed members? Turn this off only if you plan on subscribing people manually and don't want them to know that you did so. Setting this to No is most useful for transparently migrating lists from some other mailing list manager to Mailman.
The text of Welcome message can be set via the Templates tab.

" KB0016179 myRutgers myRutgers - Common Widget Issues "

Many students (either currently enrolling or already admitted) experience various issues with widgets on their My Dashboard that prevent them from viewing grades, accessing financial aid, or preparing for enrollment. The list below lists various common issues on the MyRutgers Dashboard and how to resolve them.

Red Enrollment Widget with Error contacting service. Notification

If you are not currently enrolling at Rutgers as a student:
These widgets will show "Error contacting service."
Select the X icon in the top-right section of the widget to remove it from your dashboard.
If you are currently enrolling at Rutgers as an incoming student:
If you see that you need to pay an enrollment fee, then you must pay the acceptance fee before proceeding.
If you paid the acceptance fee and still see the error, please wait 2-3 business days before escalating the issue to Rutgers One Stop Student Services. Contact the Rutgers One Stop Student Services for support.
Disappeared Widget from the Dashboard
On the My Dashboard tab, select the plus icon  in the top-right corner of the page.
You may Browse Widgets to select a category and a specific widget to add to your dashboard.
Alternatively, you may Search for a widget to add to your dashboard.
Select Add to add to your dashboard.
Blank Widget





For blank widgets, we recommend troubleshooting in the browser. Suggested troubleshooting steps are listed below:

Clearing cache and cookies on your browser
Using an incognito tab
Using a different browser
Using a different device
Restarting your device
Add a Widget
Log in to MyRutgers and navigate to the Dashboard tab.
Click on the plus sign in the upper-right-hand corner. This should pull up the Add Widget window.
Select the appropriate category for the widget you are searching for.
Alternatively, you can search for the widget itself by selecting the Search tab and entering its name.
Please note that if you already have the widget on your dashboard, it will not appear in Search.
Navigate to the appropriate widget and click the plus sign - the widget should now appear on the Dashboard.
Remove a Widget
Go to Settings > Toggle Dark Mode Off.
Expand the navigation sidebar.
Click on the down arrow to the right of the dashboard.
Select Reorder Widgets.
Utilize the Trash Bin Icon to remove widgets. Please be advised that some widgets are locked and cannot be removed.
Move a Widget
Go to Settings > Toggle Dark Mode Off.
Expand the navigation sidebar.
Click the sidebar arrow to the right of the dashboard.
Select Reorder Widgets.
Click and Drag the two horizontal lines to reorder the widgets.
Once finished, select Set Order.

" KB0014220 myRutgers myRutgers - myGuest Dashboard "

The myGuest Dashboard allows students to grant parents, guardians, and third parties access to view content and services related to their university experience. They will be able to grant access to a maximum of 3 authorized users and choose which information they can view.

Student Overview

The following guide provides step-by-step instructions for completing the user authorization process on the student's end:

Log in to myRutgers with NetID and password.
Open settings
Select Authorized Access
You are now on the My Authorized Users page. Select Add a User
Review the FERPA agreement
Check off the box > select Confirm
Fill out the fields highlighted in yellow
Select which records your authorized user should have access to
Select Submit
Your authorized user has now been added. 3 active users are allowed at one time. You can additionally perform the following actions:
Use the Edit icon to change certain information about that user, as well as permissions
Resend the Welcome Email if it was lost
Change user to inactive
How-to Guide for Authorized Users

The myGuest Dashboard enables authorized users, such as parents, guardians, and third parties, to view student information online. Students must authorize users and choose which information they can view. Once the student completes the authorization process, the user will be able to register by creating a CommunityID.

The following guide provides step-by-step instructions for creating a CommunityID and viewing student information on the myGuest Dashboard:

A student must create an authorized user before the user can create a CommunityID and view information on the myGuest Dashboard.
Once the student identifies a user, the user will receive a welcome email with information about the myGuest Dashboard.
The user should click the Register CommunityID link in the email to open the CommunityID registration site.
On the registration page, the name and email address are already filled in, as entered by the student when adding the user. The user will need to create a password, check the COPPA box, and click Register.
Once the user is registered, they can navigate to myRutgers and click CommunityID Login. They can log in with their email address and the password they created.
The user will then see a profile picture of the student(s) who authorized them, as well as the four-digit PIN assigned by the student(s). The user can select Student and launch the dashboard for view-only access.
The user will only have access to information that the student authorized for them. For example, if they were granted access to student accounting records, they would see access to pay the student's term bill. Click on the eye cover icon to reveal private information.
When calling or visiting the university, the authorized user must know the four-digit PIN, which administrators will verify before speaking with the user to protect the student's rights.

Related articles:

CommunityID - Support Info

" KB0014221 myRutgers myRutgers - myRutgers Proxy Mode "

Proxy Mode provides eligible users access to the myRutgers student dashboard as other users. Proxy Mode is only to be used when a Rutgers student has provided permission and should be noted as such in the Incident Work Notes.

The level of proxy mode access depends on the staff member's role and the permissions set by the data custodians. This service will primarily be used by Deans/Department Chairs and other administrative staff; currently, only select Help Desk Staff have access to this tool. Typically, this tool is restricted to FT only.

As a reminder, users of Proxy Mode for the myRutgers and myGuest dashboards are responsible for using student data and information in a professional, ethical, and lawful manner. In accessing and utilizing information from the dashboards, all users must ensure that student information is appropriately secured and managed. Users have a responsibility to abide by all federal and state regulations, including FERPA, GLBA, and others, to ensure the confidentiality of student data and the safeguarding of non-public customer information, and are required to complete GLBA security awareness training (if applicable).

Instructions on using Proxy Mode are in the attached PDF.

To access proxy mode in the new myRutgers UI, click on the Settings (gear) icon on the left sidebar. Go to the Security section and enter the NetID of the user you wish to proxy.

" KB0014222 myRutgers myRutgers - Support and Common Issues "

Common Issues
Access Issues

Most problems regarding accessing the myRutgers portal are related to NetID password issues. All users should log in with their NetID and password (if they have forgotten their password, please refer to the password reset procedures).

If a user tries to access employee self-service on MyRutgers Portal, they must have a faculty, staff, or student worker role; if they do not, please speak with a Supervisor.

Personal Information

To find the RUID number,

Log in to MyRutgers and navigate to the Dashboard tab.
Navigate to the Profile Widget
The RUID number is shown below the Personal Photo.

To update the Emergency Contact Information:

Log in to MyRutgers and navigate to the Apps tab.
Utilize the Search Apps tool in the upper right-hand corner.
Search and select Emergency Contacts.
You will be redirected to an external page.

Alumni cannot edit personal information in the myRutgers portal. If users cannot update their personal information themselves, please refer them to the Registrar s Office to have it manually updated.

Miscellaneous Notes

If users change their mobile device default language from English to another language, they will not be able to proceed past Duo Authentication on their phones. This issue has been escalated to Duo's administrators.

Before requesting a Balance Adjustment, please ensure the issue was not due to user error. Balance Adjustments are processed for issues beyond the user's control, such as device malfunctions, and when a print release is not an option.

OIT staff can process a print release instead of a balance adjustment. A print release is where, after errors/problems have been addressed, the user resubmits the problematic print job. An OIT staff member can remotely release the user's print job. Please contact the OIT Lab staff via Teams to request a print release. " KB0019569 NetID NetID Password Scramble Issues "

Introduction

NetID passwords can be scrambled when users click a malicious link. This security measure is taken as a precaution to protect the private and confidential data of Rutgers users. A password scramble does not mean that their data is compromised or that their account is locked; it only requires a password reset to resolve the issue.

Alternatively, a Kerberos lock and a Lock for Deprovisioning are NetID account locks and require different procedures to unlock the account. A lock for deprovisioning occurs when a user loses their active role due to not being affiliated with Rutgers or to a role transition. In this case, they would need to reactivate their account to regain access, provided they get an active role. To check for active roles, please visit Open Registry and search for the user using one of the identifiers. A Kerberos lock, on the other hand, means the account is fully locked due to potential compromise, requiring full-time intervention to unlock it. Once the user's identity is verified via an alternate email address, phone number, or CVC, the call can be transferred to a full-time staff member to unlock. A user's alternate phone number or email address can be found by clicking on one of their active roles on Open Registry.



How to check if a user's NetID password is scrambled
Visit NetID Admin.
Scroll down to the Kerberos status section after searching for the affected user's netID.
In the table under Kerberos Lockout History, verify whether the Only Password scrambled? field is marked Y.




Furthermore, note that the Reason Code and Notes fields explain why the password was scrambled.
How to assist a user whose password has been scrambled
Explain to the user that their account is not locked and their password has only been scrambled as a precaution.
Guide the user through the password reset process.
After the user resets their password, verify that the change has been processed in NetID Admin. The LDAP Password Change Date should reflect the date and time of the user's last password change. Once you confirm that the user has successfully reset their password, post their latest NetID Admin information in the ticket's work notes.

" KB0010943 Network Network - 802.1x Information and Troubleshooting "

For a device to connect to the university network, it must be configured for 802.1x authentication.

Computers can be automatically configured for 802.1x by installing RUWireless Secure.

Specific devices cannot be natively configured for 802.1x because they do not support the 802.1x authentication protocol and must be connected using the instructions on connecting your smart devices, streaming devices, gaming consoles, and wired desktops. These devices include:

Game Consoles (i.e., PS4, Xbox One, Switch)
Media streaming devices
Smart TVs
Wired devices that cannot be configured for 802.1x
Troubleshooting
No Connectivity with Secure

If users change their NetID password, they often need to reinstall Secure.

Under some circumstances, a user can be configured for Secure but is unable to authenticate. C
For OSX, check Profiles in System Preferences to confirm that Secure profiles are installed. Then, check both the Wi-Fi and Ethernet adapters in the Network Preferences: you may need to select Connect next to the 802.1x dropdown.
For Windows, you need to confirm a couple of things:
Confirm that Wired AutoConfig is running. If it is not running, open cmd and run "net start dot3svc", then re-run the installer and reconnect the ethernet cable.
Confirm that the "Authentication" tab has "SecureW2 EAP-TTLS" authentication selected for the adapters in the Adapter Settings.

There are 2 Alternative setups for RUWS, which can be found on the Windows Manual ResNet Setup KA.

No Connectivity with portal registration

It has become known that specific devices may not immediately receive Internet access, even after successfully adding the device, and unplugging/replugging the Ethernet cable.

Game consoles that are properly added (PS/XB/Switch) can receive "Timed Out" connectivity errors when testing connection or attempting to connect.

These occur because the consoles do not wait as long as computers or routers to receive an IP address. You can keep running "Test Connections" in the settings, unplugging/replugging, or restarting the device. If it fails after multiple attempts, an escalation appointment will be scheduled so a router may be tested to resolve the issue.

" KB0014235 Network Network - CIRTs "

TCPBlock (MacOS)
Overview

Part of the work of the Help Desk is helping to handle CIRT (Computing Incident Response Team) notifications. These notifications come from IPS (Information Protection and Security) when they detect a device on the Rutgers network passing unfavorable traffic, such as botnet activity. After detection, IPS contacts the party responsible for that section of the network, Help Desk is responsible for ResNet, RUWireless/eduroam, and certain VPN connections.

Descriptions for various infections and maladies encountered in CIRTs can be found in the Known CIRT Viruses document. This information can help you better understand and explain the infection to users. Please disseminate this information to users when sending the CIRT shutdown boilerplate emails.

To have their network access reinstated, the user must show that their device is free of infections. They can do this in one of four different ways:

Self-clean: The user can attempt to clean the infection by themselves. In these cases, inform the user that they will not be unblocked until they send us clean logs via the Help Desk email address. This option is only available for the first offense on a device. For repeat offenders, they will need to use an alternate method.
Walk-in: Schedule an appointment using the Walk-in Scheduling calendar.
Dispatch appointment: for dorm residents only, please call the Help Desk for more information.
3rd party: The user may opt to take their device to a third party (including Kite+Key) for cleaning or reformatting, and they will need to contact us afterward to unblock it.
Repeat Offenses

Since ResNet can only use simple methods of removing malicious software from a user s computer, the infection may still exist on the machine even after troubleshooting. The Security Operations Center will flag the user again if the infection has not been removed. Below are general guidelines for handling users who have generated multiple CIRT (Computer Incident Response Ticket) reports.

First offense:

Inform the user of infection and risks.
Users can send clean scans to help@oit.rutgers.edu.
Unban the device once we are confident no further reports will come in.
Second offense:

*BEFORE CLEANING ANY MACHINE, HAVE THE USER SIGN THE WAIVER FORM*.
The user must come into the Help Desk or have Dispatch go out to clean the machine.
Unban the device once we are confident no further reports will come in.
Third offense:

The computer must be professionally cleaned (Computer Repair, Geek Squad, etc.).
Unban the device after the user confirms the device has been cleaned/reformatted.
Identifying System Corruption

In some cases, users may suspect that they have a virus when, in fact, they merely have some kind of problem with their operating system. In other cases, viruses can even cause system corruption after removal. This is why we must make restore points for users before removing viruses. This article will address Windows only.

Generally, a system is considered to have some corruption if you cannot run or access certain Windows services even in safe mode. For example, in safe mode with networking, we were unable to access certain submenus of the control panel. This is a failure of an underlying Windows component and, therefore, indicative of some kind of problem with the OS. Other common signs can include:

Hardware failure
Non-functioning windows features as the above example
Inability to detect new hardware or update drivers
Error messages during boot

Furthermore, repairing system corruption is difficult and not something we support. In cases where we identify this issue, users have three solutions:

Copy the data off the machine & reinstall
Attempt to repair the OS with Windows install media
Take the device to a computer repair professional, i.e. Kite & Key and others

Back to top

Browser Hijackers
What is a Browser Hijacker?

A browser hijacker is a type of malware/adware that usually adds itself as a program in the Control Panel while also infecting registry files and the files for individual browsers. It adds itself as an extension and changes the home pages for the user's browsers (usually all of them, such as Firefox, Chrome, and Edge), while also making sure that any new tabs the user opens will be the home page of a strange "search engine" that looks like an ad-bloated knock-off of a regular search engine. This type of malware is usually paired with an actual computer virus but has been known to sometimes download alongside other random programs and toolbars that users install online.

Search Conduit is a popular browser hijacker you may encounter on a walk-in appointment. They get the user to utilize them as their "search" method while also filling the user's internet browsing experience with ads, so the user will have an increased chance of downloading more malware and viruses.

Removal

To remove a browser hijacker, after signing the waiver and creating a restore point, make sure to go into the user's control panel and remove the associated program. You will find many different programs considered browser hijackers, and on different systems, the same browser hijacker may not have the same name in the control panel. Use your best judgment and search online to ensure any suspicious programs you find are related to the virus.

Make sure to remove the extensions for each browser as well. Since the user's home and new tab pages will be changed, the safest way to change the home page and new tab page defaults back is to perform a complete browser reset for the user's different web browsers. Remember to ask the patron if it is okay to do this and warn them they may lose some of their settings.

A Malwarebytes full scan is the best option to remove this form of malware. Malwarebytes will also be able to locate other types of toolbars and adware that has been installed onto the user's computer as a result of the browser hijacker. After the scan is complete, the computer should be restarted to check that the browsers are no longer redirecting the users to malicious websites. Adblocker Plus is a useful Chrome extension that can help to prevent the user from accidentally clicking on ads in the future as a precautionary measure. If a Malwarebytes scan fails to remove the browser hijacker, Spybot Search and Destroy is another useful tool to remove it.

Since the user may have been infected with other types of malware due to the browser hijacker redirecting them in the past, we should also make sure to leave the user with a fully installed and updated version of Symantec Endpoint Protection (SEP). If they have a ban on RUWireless or ResNet, the removal of browser hijackers should be something we make sure to check for so that we do not have as many repeat offenders. If the user is simply a patron who walks in and is not currently banned, we should still be sending them home with a full scan of SEP running to ensure that, if they are infected but are currently under the radar, they do not end up coming back with a ban later on.

Mobile infections

There has been only one infection for iPhones for which we have generated CIRTs, XcodeGhost. Generally, the infection was caused by a specific app, and the infection is removed by having it removed. Other future infections might require a factory reset of the device.

Virtual Device or Alternate Partition

Some non-Windows operating system users may choose to emulate Windows in a virtual environment. These issues can be resolved by having the user delete their virtual device. For example, a Mac OS user with a Trojan Zeus infection probably has a Windows partition or virtual device. In the case of a virtual device, they can opt to erase it. Second partitions can hold more value for users, so they may choose to have the partition cleaned instead of erasing it.

Linux/Unix

These are very rare and will not generate CIRTs. Generally, users of these devices are savvy enough to deal with their infections. However, we may need to help such a user occasionally. In these cases, you must find an antivirus program compatible with the user s distro.

Game Consoles

Occasionally we will be notified that a user s Xbox is banned for Zeus - Gameover Variant. Traffic from the NBA2K video game series can trigger this ban. If we can verify that the device is an Xbox, we can unban it without further investigation.

Back to top

Advanced Troubleshooting
Creating Firewall Rules

Sometimes, while working on CIRTs, we must set up a firewall rule to prevent a user's computer from communicating with a specific IP address. This should not be your first step, as we want to run AV scans first to ensure malware is not responsible for the user ban. Firewalling should be a last resort, and you should consult with a supervisor before creating any new firewall rules on a user's computer. You also want to inform the user of firewall risks before setting up a new rule.

Firewalling the IP will do one of three things:

A legitimate program causes traffic, and we will be able to identify it because that program will stop working properly.
The traffic is caused by malware that only ever communicates with that IP over that port, effectively stopping the malware from moving sensitive data off the user's computer.
The traffic we see is the only indicator for the malware, but it communicates with multiple hosts/servers and may continue to harm the user's computer or steal data, and IPS will have no way of knowing if it's still there/active.

The third scenario here is really bad, so you should always run AV scans first on the user s computer.

Below are the instructions on how to create firewall rules in Windows

Open up Windows Firewall.
Select Outbound Rules > New Rule > Custom Rule > All Programs.
Leave all settings on the protocol and ports screen the same, then select Next.
Under Remote IP address, select the specific IP addresses > Add the above IP address > Next.
Ensure that the option to Block this connection is selected, then press Next.
Leave all settings on the profile page the same, then press Next.
Create a name for your firewall rule, then click Finish.
TCPBlock (MacOS)

For MacOSX, we commonly use the program TCPBlock to block a user's device from communicating with certain IP addresses. Check the USB Tools for TCPBlock. Otherwise, speak to a supervisor before downloading the program from an external link.

Back to top

" KB0014239 Network Network - DHCP Handshake "

The DHCP (Dynamic Host Configuration Profile) handshake is the network protocol Rutgers networks use to hand out IP addresses. DHCP Servers are devices configured to perform the handshake with new devices and give out addresses, such as routers and switches.

There are 4 stages in a DHCP handshake.

Discovery - Computer asks if anyone is a DHCP server
Offer - Router says that it can give out IPs
Request - Computer asks discovered router for an IP
Acknowledge - Router gives out the IP, and the computer assigns itself that IP

Extra steps include:
Information - Devices can submit an extra request for extra info about other devices
Release - Devices can notify a Server that they are no longer using their IP

Based on the user's IP, we can see what stage their device is failing to get an address in the handshake.

If a user's device has an IP of 0.0.0.0 and it is not connected to a network, it has not started the handshake at all.
If a device has an IP of 169.254.x.x, it has connected to the network but has not been able to find a DHCP server to give out IP addresses and so just gave itself one starting with 169.254.
If a device has a Rutgers 172.31.x.x or 172.30.x.x IP address, it has successfully completed the DHCP handshake, and a separate issue causes the issue.

Devices that have 192.168.x.x or 10.x.x.x IP addresses are not given out by Rutgers DHCP servers; instead, they are given out by personal routers. If you have a user with this IP unable to connect to RUWireless or the dorm's wired internet connection, they are not connected to the right network.

" KB0014149 Network Network - Escalating Called-in Outages "

Identifying these issues can be difficult, here is a general guide on how to proceed. You should always begin by asking if there is an IT staff member available to speak with.

Your objectives are to first identify:

Who & how many people do the issues affect?
Ex:
All students on Best North 3rd floor
The approximately 30 faculty and staff who work at 15 Centennial Ave
RBHS staff Dr. Xorg reports that the entire 3rd wing of the Orthopedic Building is down
James Bond, IT for the dept of Economics, reports that 60 users in the Guther Building on CAC are unable to access the network
What type of service is the issue affecting?
Ex:
All network access is unavailable.
Wired networking & phone service is down.
The wireless networks are down, but the wired one is working.
All internet is working, but phone service is down.
What is the cause of the issue?
For wireless issues, try and identify the AP(s).
For wired issues try and identify the switch(es).
Voice

For Voice issues, confirm that the issue is widespread and occurring for many users, and assign the incident to the appropriate EI - Voice Services group since we have no tools to identify these issues. However, see if it is possible to check if wired networking is working because a wired internet outage can knock out the phones. Contact Voice by calling the main TD line and selecting the Voice option.

Wired

For wired issues, identify if the issue is affecting a dorm or an academic building.

If the issue is occurring in a dorm:

Use the NAP to look up the user s authentication history and see if you can identify the problems with switches. Check with several users on several ports with several devices to see if there is an outage. Typically, if one user reports one device problem on a switch, we will send someone out and assume it is a problem with a port. Only when several users in multiple locations in a building call in about a problem do we usually realize there is an issue with the switch.

If the issue is occurring in an Academic building:

Academic buildings have different networking setups than the dorms and often do not even have DHCP enabled. We want to talk to an IT staff member for this issue if possible. Failing that, confirm with the caller that the issue affects many users and report to the NOC. We do not have the tools to identify which switch has the problem.

Wireless

Juniper Mist has access to all of the wireless APs supported by the NOC. Firstly identify which networks the issue is on. All of the RUWireless networks can be escalated to NOC, however SoE, CS, and other departmental networks should be referred to the contact people for that network.

For a NOC supported network:

Try and identify the wireless AP(s) that are experiencing problems. This can be done by looking at the user s AP connection history to see if you can identify the AP experiencing problems. Signs an AP is having problems:

Users report they are in the area and cannot connect, but the AP has no association attempts or connected users.
The AP does not appear on Juniper Mist.
Users who are reporting issues are all connected on the same AP.

For issues that are not only voice, during business hours, assign to EI - Network Operations Center with the details.

Ask if the user is departmental IT or if there is an IT person you can speak to, as these individuals may be able to provide you with more accurate information.
If there is an IT person, they should be able to speak directly to the NOC, and you can provide them the NOC phone number.
Ask how many people are experiencing the issue.
Ask what area the issue covers.
Ask if the issue affects ResNet, RUWireless, Phone services, departmental internet, or internet in an administrative/classroom building.
For RUWireless check that the issue affects both regular & secure.
RUWireless

See if you can identify the problem AP.

Lookup the user to see their association history
Check the status of the AP that they used to connect to at their location
E.x. abc123 yesterday was connected to hill-test-ap-1 in his office. However, we cannot locate that AP on Cisco Prime OR we can locate it, but no users are on it.
Once we identify which AP has the problem, we want to contact NOC. We cannot always isolate the problem AP, but we should give it our best guess.
When reporting the issue, specify that the issue occurs on secure, regular, or other.
Create a tag for the issue and tag related tickets.
Assign to EI - Network Operations Center.
RUatHome

See if you can identify the problem switch

See if you can identify the switch by checking the user s wired association history.
The problem may require some unorthodox investigation.
Check that port configurations are correct.
Check that switch configurations are correct.
Check if the issue affects 1 switch, an entire building, or several buildings.
See if only specific devices or authentication methods are affected.
EX: windows PCs, all PCs, all MBed devices, only Apple TVs
Same as above, we will reach out to NOC once we have identified the issue or we have exhausted our ability to identify the issue.

" KB0010944 Network Network - IP Address Ranges "

RUWireless (Guest) - 192.168.X.X
RUWireless Secure - 172.31.X.X, 172.27.X.X, and 172.25.X.X (faculty and staff only)
Resnet - 10.72.0.0/15
eduroam - 172.26.204.0/22
