" KB0014343 Network Network - Rebuild Network Settings "

There are some strange network issues that are caused by corruption or bugs in the device's network settings that cannot be easily reset or removed and may require you to rebuild the network settings. The method will differ depending on the device's operating system.

Remember, if the user is a walk-in, make sure they have signed the remediation form.

Windows OS

To clear these settings on Windows devices:

Open the Command prompt.
Type "netsh winsock reset".

The command will clear the network settings.

MacOS

On MacOS, it is rather involved. So, as a last resort in getting MacOS connected to RUWireless Secure, use the following instructions. Speak to a supervisor before attempting this, and if the user is a walk-in, make sure they have signed the remediation form.

Go to Wi-Fi status menu > Turn Wi-Fi Off.
From the Finder, hit Command + Shift + G then enter the following path:
/Library/Preferences/SystemConfiguration/
Within this folder, select the following files:
com.apple.airport.preferences.plist
com.apple.network.identification.plist
com.apple.wifi.message-tracer.plist
NetworkInterfaces.plist
preferences.plist
(any other .plist files related to wifi, network, internet, 802.1x, etc.)
Move all these files into a folder on your desktop and name it "Wi-Fi Backups" or anything you like.
Reboot your Mac.
Go to the Wi-Fi status menu again and turn the Wi-Fi back on.

This should force OS X to create all network configuration files.

" KB0014248 Network Network - Retrieving IP Addresses "

IP addresses are necessary to troubleshoot wired and wireless network issues properly. To retrieve IP addresses, please review the steps for the appropriate operating system:

MacOS
Open System Preferences/Settings and select Network. This can also be done by clicking the Wifi icon and selecting Open Network Preferences.
Select Ethernet or WiFi depending on connection type and click Advanced at the bottom of the dialogue box.
Select the TCP/IP tab; it will be listed as IPv4 Address.
Alternatively, it may be retrieved from the command line.
Open Terminal.
You can find it in Applications > Utilities, or search Terminal using Mac Smart Search (Command+Space).
Type "ifconfig" in the Terminal.
Under one of the interfaces (i.e., en0, en1) it will be listed as inet .
Windows
Search for command prompt on the device. Alternatively, press Windows+R and type in "cmd".
Type "ipconfig /all" and hit Enter.
Scroll down to Ethernet or Wireless adapters.
It will be listed as IPv4 Address.
Linux
If the user has Network Manager installed (has wifi/connection icon).
Right-click on the notification icon and select Connection Information.
It will be listed under IPv4 address.
If the user does not have Network Manager
Have the user open Terminal.
Enter the following command "ip addr show".
Under one of the interfaces (i.e., en0, en1) it will be listed as inet .
Android
Navigate to Settings.
Tap on WiFi.
Tap the network the user is connected to (i.e., RUWireless_Secure).
It will be listed as IP address.
iPhone
Open the Settings App.
Tap on WiFi.
Tap the blue i icon next to the network the user is connected to (i.e., RUWireless_Secure).
The IP address will be listed under IPV4 address.

" KB0014730 Network Network - RUatHome - ResNet "

Table of Contents
Requirements for using the portal
Things to note
Wireless
Wired
Escalations
Help Desk Tools
Check the status of a user's Wired device bypass (ServiceNow form submission)
Confirm a user has an active Passphrase (Pre-shared Key or PSK)
Lookup devices connected via Passphrase to a specific user

RUatHome is for smart devices not capable of installing the software to connect to RUWireless Secure. The network is only broadcast in the residence halls.

Requires each resident to visit a web portal to receive a personal Passphrase (password) that they will use on their IOT devices.

Instructions can be found at: https://ithelp.rutgers.edu/sp?id=kb_article_view&sysparm_article=KB0012481

Criteria for devices:

Smart devices
2.4 GHz or 5GHz compatible
Non-802.1x devices - it should not have Secure W2 (for RUWireless Secure) installed on the device. If it supports RUWireless Secure, it should not be on the RUatHome network.

Laptops, tablets, and phones should still use RUWireless Secure, except if the phone is being used to configure a smart device.

Requirements for using the portal

Only users with a Student role can access the Passphrase Portal. Faculty/Staff living in residence halls should be able to as well, after they are added to a specific RAD group to grant them access. If a non-student needs access to the portal:

Verify the user is a faculty/staff member, with no student role, who resides in on-campus housing.
Confirm with FT.
FT will need to put them in a specific campus specific RAD group. RAD Groups for FT reference:
Cmd RUCIT HD-RUatHome Non Student
Oit Eisd Css HD-RUatHome Non Student
Oit Esd Runit Hd-RUatHome Non Student
FT will also need to add the user to EI-NOG-Mist-FacStaffResidents regardless of which campus they are at.

The Portal can be accessed using the link in the KA or by directing the user to: go.rutgers.edu/ruathome

Things to note
Wireless
Passphrase, Passkey, Pre-shared Key, and Password are different terms, but they are all the same thing.
RUatHome supports 2.4 GHz and 5 GHz devices.
The Help Desk cannot see, change, or update a user's Passphrase. If needed, the user will have to generate a new Passphrase through the portal. Doing so invalidates their existing Passphrase after 24 hours, so connected devices will stay connected for another day.
The user cannot see what devices they have registered to their Passphrase, or which ones have an active RUatHome connection. Only Supervisors with access to MIST can see this.
Devices previously connected to RUIOT last year will have to reconnect to the updated RUatHome network following the new process.
Devices that connect to RUatHome will have to switch back to RUWireless in non-residence hall buildings (ex, phones or laptops).
Wireless Routers are not permitted anywhere.
The capability, called "personal WLAN," will be introduced at a later date. Currently, the network is one large segment with no personal segmentation. So, two devices do not need to use the same Passphrase to communicate with each other; they only need to both be on RUatHome. However, this means a user may see all devices on the RUatHome network.
If a user would like to connect a device to this network, please let them know that we cannot guarantee that their device will function properly. As an example, many smart home devices have difficulty associating with this network, and even if they can connect, they often have limited functionality.
Printers are not allowed, but can technically be set up for RUatHome. Though if it is on RUatHome, anyone would be able to discover it and print to it; therefore, it should never be recommended, and they should connect to the printer via USB.
The device limit has been set to 10 behind the scenes; however, do not state or share this with users. This limit is set under the premise that anyone who hits the 10 devices is probably abusing the system and will either back off or reach out to the Help Desk. These users can then be advised of workarounds to install Secure or move to a wired connection, where possible.
Wired

All wired devices will require users to submit a request via a ServiceNow form to get their device connected. If a device has been registered within the last year, the user will not need to fill out the form again.

Prior to submitting the form, the user needs to connect their device to a network port and power it on, to establish a connection to the network, so the MAC address can be properly bypassed by the Network team.

Please note that any user living in the Residence Halls with a student role should fill out the EI - Wired ResNet Device Bypass form.
Users living in the Residence Halls without a student role, should fill out the EI - MAC Authentication Bypass (MAB) form.
Wired Notes
Wired connections should be for desktops, and optionally gaming consoles, or smart TVs if they want. Laptops can also be wired, but we do not advertise this as they should be using RUWireless Secure instead.
Windows 10 Home and Windows 11 Home do not support native login with 802.1x using NetID and NetID Password. This is only supported on Windows Pro or Enterprise devices. They will instead need to use the ServiceNow form to submit their device's MAC address.
If it is a Windows device, they will need to disable the Wired Autoconfig service for it to connect properly.
Unmanaged Switches should not need to be registered and should work automatically.
Ethernet Adapters will need to have their MAC address added via the form if the device is not capable of 802.1x Network Authentication.
Once a request is submitted on the ServiceNow form for a Wired device MAB, the NOC will process the request, typically within 48 hours.
If someone asks when their bypass will be done, after verifying the status for their RITM is still Open in ServiceNow, you may say:
"Your device registration request is in progress and will be completed within 48 hours. We will do our best to get it done sooner. If possible, you may use the wireless network while we process your request."
There is only 1 Ethernet port available in each dorm/suite. There is no option for a second port to be installed. If there is a physical issue with the port or faceplate, please confirm the following before having a supervisor assist you in escalating to inside plant:
Make sure it is a port/faceplate that is meant to be functioning. If its a suite/apartment, there are NO ports in the bedroom.
If you have checked that this port is intended to be functional, you must get the building, room number, and user contact information before escalating.
Escalations
If users are unable to see the RUatHome SSID, these reports should be escalated to the wireless team at EI - Network Access Technologies.
Problems with the Passphrase portal or registrations should be escalated to the NAC team at EI - Network Access Technologies (NAC).
Help Desk Tools

Supervisors can access MIST to look up more detailed information for devices on the Switches.

Check the status of a user's Wired device bypass (ServiceNow form submission)
Go to the Request Item list.
Click the Filter icon in the top right.
Enter the User's NetID in the Requested for field, then choose their name from the drop-down or hit Enter.
Click Run.




Click on the Request Item Number (RITM) to view the request and check on its current State.




The State field will show:
Closed Complete if it was successfully processed.
Open indicates it has not been processed yet.
Closed Incomplete means it was not processed and closed out for some other reason, such as the device being a wireless, not a wired device.
Confirm a user has an active Passphrase (Pre-shared Key or PSK)
Log in to MIST.
Go to Organization> Passphrases.




In the search field, type the NetID and hit Enter.
Review the Created Time and Expiration fields.




We can not view a user's Passphrase, nor reset it for them.
Lookup devices connected via Passphrase to a specific user
Log in to MIST.
Go to Clients > NAC Clients.




Change the org drop-down to Entire Org and adjust the date range if applicable (defaults to Today, with a maximum history of the last 7 days).




Enter the user's NetID in the search field and hit Enter.
You'll see a list of all devices that connected to RUatHome with that user's Passphrase.
The State column will indicate whether it is actively connected.
Green: Actively connected
Orange: No longer connected.
Click on Client Insights to see more information on that device.




Look under Client Events to see a list of actions that the device has undergone. Common events may include:

NAC Client Session: This means the cloud and AP are communicating.
Authorization and Association: When the Passphrase is connected and cached.
NAC Client IP Learned: The IP is learned for data history.
Client Roamed Away: The Device may have gone to sleep, or it may have moved out of range of the access point (such as walking through or out of a building).
Transmission Failure: Could not get a good connection from the access point.
Client Session Ended: Device has been disconnected from the network (usually by switching wifi off or to another network).
NAC COA Disconnect: The cloud disconnected the client.




Scroll down to Current Client Properties for more information to help identify which device it is, such as Hostname, Device Type, and Manufacturer.



" KB0019498 Network Network - Scheduling ResNet/RUWireless Dispatch Appointments "

There will be times when troubleshooting a networking issue over the phone does not result in a resolution. If that is the case, please speak with a Help Desk Supervisor so they can determine if the issue can be dispatched. Dispatch is a service offered by labs on all campuses, in which consultants visit the caller's residence to assist with networking issues in person.

For ITS dispatch appointments, go to the knowledge article on how to schedule them.

L2/Supervisors scheduling appointment
Determine if dispatch is an option that can help the user. Typically, dispatch can handle the following issues:

Router Setup
Wireless Survey
Virus Remediation / Computing Incident Response Team (CIRT) Ticket Issue
Connecting a device to Resnet / Ruwireless
Faceplate Repair
Determining if there are physical issues with a port.
Once dispatch is deemed suitable, create an incident task for the Incident.
Incident task, be sure to include the following:

What to bring to the appointment, and full details.

Most appointments with port-related issues will require the ResNet laptop, Fluke, and an Ethernet cable.
Wireless Survey equipment should include the ResNet Laptop, a wireless NIC, and, if deemed necessary, other equipment, such as a Fluke.
Assign the task to the proper campus
NB-Labs - Busch
NB-Labs - College Ave
NB-Labs - Cook/Douglass
NB-Labs - Livingston
Dispatch hours vary depending on the time of year. If communicating with the users via email, inform them to call the Help Desk to schedule an appointment to avoid back-and-forth over email.
If it is during dispatch hours, call over to the corresponding lab. Dispatch consultants can either take the dispatch right now if the availability works out or work with the user to schedule a future date. If no dispatch consultant is available, a Lab Supervisor can assist.
Busch - (848-445-2760)
College Avenue (Academic Building) - (848-932-0310)
Cook/Douglass - (848-932-9750)
Livingston - (848-445-3465)
If no dispatch consultant or Lab Supervisor is available, the Help Desk can schedule the appointment with the user via the calendar. They should then post the appointment information and TASK number in the esd_nb_hdlabs_dispatch channel on Slack.
https://outlook.office365.com/owa/calendar/hd_appoint@oit.rutgers.edu/bookings/
Please note that wireless surveys should be scheduled for 2 hours
After the dispatch appointment:
If the dispatch consultant can resolve the Incident, they will also reassign the incident Task.
If they are unable to resolve it, they will reach back out to the HD for further escalation (HD, NOC, etc.).
Canceling/rescheduling a dispatch appointment
Verify the user's contact info and appointment information vs what's in their Incident/Task and the bookings calendar.
Add a Work Note to the Incident Task listing why the appointment is being canceled/rescheduled.
Contact Full Time and request they cancel the appointment on the bookings calendar.
Post in the #esd_nb_hdlabs_dispatch slack channel:
Incident Number/Task Number [CAMPUS] Dispatch appointment for [DATE/TIME] was canceled by the user.
Handle the Task:
If canceling, mark the Task as Closed Skipped. Resolve the Incident if the user nIncident needs assistance with their issue; otherwise, handle the Incident appropriately.
Incident scheduling, assist the user with booking a new appointment, and update the Task accordTasky. Post the new appointment info in the #esd_nb_hdlabs_dispatch Slack channel.

" KB0015324 Network Network - Whitelist Mail-Relay "

OIT provides a service called mail relay for certain devices that cannot run a Mail Transfer Agent (MTA), such as printers and switches. These devices, which normally do not have user accounts, can be allowed to send email through a special server without first authenticating to that server with a username and password. Some other examples of this that we see at the Help Desk are emails sent from the generator, or PDUs (if you ve worked in Application Support).

This Service was previously known as the whitelist service, but it s a specialized form of whitelisting that should not be confused with other types of whitelisting. Devices that use this Service must be registered with the NOC using a unique IP address/hostname pair.

More information about the Mail Relay Service and how users can request it can be found on the Mail Relay Service page.

Process
Rutgers Unit Computing Managers (UCM) will complete the request form on the Rutgers Whitelist page. This form includes information such as

IP address of the device
Hostname of the device
Type of device
Whether or not it handles Protected Health Information (PHI)
If the information is incorrect, an error email is sent to Help@OIT with the subject incomplete mail relay request . A Service now incident will be created.
Help Desk will then work with the requester to resolve the problem. See the Problems section below for example situations. The mail relay tool does not retain information about rejected requests, so requestors must submit a new request after correcting the problem.
If a device handles PHI, an email will be sent requesting more information (a response template is available for these questions). Once the requester responds with the answers, a ServiceNow task must be created with the requester's IP and hostname, along with the provided answers. This task should be assigned to EAS - Messaging and Collaboration Services.
If the information is correct, the request is submitted to the mail administrators, who typically respond within 5 business days.
A device is approved to use one of two specific servers:

whitelist.rutgers.edu -- for simple devices (e.g., printers)
smarthost.rutgers.edu -- for devices that send mail through a web form or some other type of script
Help@OIT gets a courtesy copy of the approval email with the subject [IP address] approved mail relay request for each specific IP address. A Service Now incident will be created. Approved mail-relay requests can be assigned to the requester and resolved. If the requester experiences problems with requesting, Help@OIT works with them and the mail administrators to resolve their issues.
Types of Messages
ApprovedA A A

ServiceNow incident is created, which can be assigned to the requester and resolved.
May need some clarification for whitelist vs. smarthost server, or port number.
Incomplete

Action required.
Service Now incident created for the requester.
See the "Problems" section for more information.
Denied

Usually, because the device was previously approved.
Problems

The following are example entries, and how you would troubleshoot issues. A correctly registered device is listed as an example for comparison. If you face an issue that is not listed, please bring it to the attention of a full-time staff member.

Correctly registered device
The original request has

#hostname
recscan.rutgers.edu
#ip address
172.19.63.135
Connect to RUWireless Secure or use your Rutgers desktop. If you are on a different network, then use Rutgers VPN.
In a command prompt session,n you will look up both of the listed IP addresses and the listed hostname with the NsLookup command
Use the following commands to look up the IP address/hostname

PC (You can use the command prompt)

nslookup 128.6.68.193

Server: dns.rutgers.edu
Address: 128.6.1.1
Name: mx.rams.rutgers.edu
Address: 128.6.68.193
nslookup rams.rutgers.edu

Server: dns.rutgers.edu
Address: 128.6.1.1
Name: rams.rutgers.edu
Address: 128.6.68.193
MAC (you can use the terminal)

host 128.6.68.193

193.68.6.128.in-addr.arpa domain name pointer mx.rams.rutgers.edu.
host mx.rams.rutgers.edu

mx.rams.rutgers.edu has address 128.6.68.193
IP/host is not registered
Original request has

#hostname
rnp002673e4e355.rutgers.edu
#ip address
172.20.17.200
You can see it s not requested when looking it up:

amenti > host 172.20.17.200

host 200.17.20.172.in-addr.arpa. not found: 3(NXDOMAIN)
or amenti > host rnp002673e4e355.rutgers.edu
host rnp002673e4e355.rutgers.edu not found: 3(NXDOMAIN)
You would then ask the requester to work with NOC to register the device with a unique hostname/IP pair and resubmit the request.
IP/host is not properly registered
Example 1: IP is registered to two hostnames
Original request has

#hostname
davd125nd1.its.rutgers.edu
#ip address
172.16.12.176
IP is registered to two hostnames

amenti > host 172.16.12.176

176.12.16.172.in-addr.arpa domain name pointer davd125nd1.its.rutgers.edu.
176.12.16.172.in-addr.arpa domain name pointer bue106nd1.its.rutgers.edu.
You would then ask the requester to work with NOC to register the device with a unique hostname/IP pair and resubmit the request.
If there is a legitimate reason for multiple hosts to be registered to a single IP like this, one should be the A record and the others C-names. An

A-record (Address record) is the official hostname associated with an IP address.
C-names (Canonical name records) are nicknames for the A-record.
Example 2: Two hostnames are registered to the same IP address, even though the IP address is registered to only one of them.
Original request has

#hostname
steps.rutgers.edu
#ip address
172.16.91.20
When looking it up, you will see

niflheim> host steps.rutgers.edu

steps.rutgers.edu has the address 172.16.91.20
niflheim> host 172.16.91.20

20.91.16.172.in-addr.arpa domain name pointer dr-steps.rutgers.edu.
niflheim> host dr-steps.rutgers.edu

dr-steps.rutgers.edu has address 172.16.91.20
You would then ask the requester to work with NOC to register the device with a unique hostname/IP pair and resubmit the request.
It is important to look up both items in the submitted pair to confirm the issue.
Example 3: IP is registered to a hostname, but the hostname is registered to a different IP address.
Original request has

#hostname
charlie.rutgers.edu
#ip address
172.19.25.89
When looking it up, you will see

amenti> host 172.19.25.89

89.25.19.172.in-addr.arpa domain name pointer bus.rutgers.edu.

amenti> host bus.rutgers.edu.
bus.rutgers.edu has the address 172.19.25.89
amenti> host charlie.rutgers.edu
charlie.rutgers.edu has address 172.19.25.86
amenti> host 172.19.25.86
86.25.19.172.in-addr.arpa domain name pointer charlie.rutgers.edu.
You would then respond, asking the requester to resubmit the request with the correct IP/hostname pair.
Example 4: The hostname is registered to two IP addresses, rather than a single unique one.

When looking it up, you will see

" KB0010999 Network - Wired Network - Wired - Broken Ports "

When a user in the dorms calls in about a broken Ethernet port, do the following:

Make sure the student s cable is plugged into the right port. The user should be plugged into a black (data) port, not a gray (voice) one. The port number, if visible, would also end in D or V for a data or voice port, respectively.
Verify the student is using an Ethernet cable. (To check if the cable is working properly, the user can check with a roommate s cable to observe if it s a cable or port issue.) Replacement cables of various lengths are available at many Rutgers bookstores.
If one port isn t working and the other is, advise the caller that dispatch will need to test the port. Speak to a supervisor for assistance with Dispatching a ticket for onsite assistance. After it s checked and confirmed by dispatch that the port is indeed broken, the issue can be escalated to the relevant group for remediation/repair.
If the ethernet jack is pushed in, speak to a supervisor to approve the ticket for dispatch. This also applies to broken coaxial jacks used by RUTV. You should also request the user email in a picture of the port if they are willing and able to do so.
If a dispatch or network-trained Consultant determines that the issue is with the user cabling and requires repair, we create a Task Incident and assign it to EI - Inside Plant - Cabling Repair - DORM/RESIDENTIAL for escalation.

" KB0014151 Network - Wired Network - Wired - Escalating Out Wired Dorm Issues (TD Dorm Infrastructure Repair) "

Reassign ticket to EI - Inside Plant - Cabling Repair - DORM/RESIDENTIAL.

Reasons for escalating a dorm issue to EI include:

Broken Box (the box that houses the Ethernet jacks in a room)
Faceplate replacement
Damaged In-Wall Wiring
Damaged COAX (TV port)

For more information regarding Escalations to EI - Inside Plant - Cabling Repair - DORM/RESIDENTIAL, click here.

For issues where the problem is identified to be an issue with the switch:

Email to noc@rutgers.edu with details on the switch, what the problem was, and the steps you took to identify.

This includes the following:

If a port on the switch is dead.
If a port was not configured correctly

In this case, follow the instructions to reconfigure the port.
In the email, include the new configuration you made.
A switch is not configured correctly

Again, follow the guide to identify this issue.
Do not make any changes to the switch, simply notify NOC.
If the temperature is extremely high in the switch closet.

Approximately 85 degrees fahrenheit or above.
Officially the switches can endure 105 degrees fahrenheit.
Speak to a Supervisor or FT familiar with switch closest before escalating for this, as the switch closets are usually warm.
Any other miscellaneous problem that has to do with switch behavior but not with wiring.

" KB0016873 Network - Wired Network - Wired - Ethernet Cable Types "

Ethernet Cable General Information

Ethernet cables come in several varieties, differentiated by their supported frequencies and tolerance for interference. New cables are typically Cat5e. A list of standard specifications is available at the end of the article.

There are two valid configurations for ethernet cables: straight-through and crossover however, two bad wiring configurations, reversed & split pair. Originally, straight-through cables were used for connections between two types of networked devices, while crossover cables were used for the same connections. For example, a PC router connection would be straight-through, while a router-router connection would be a crossover. However, modern networking equipment is equipped with the Auto MDI-X protocol, which enables communication between two devices regardless of straight-through or crossover. However, reversed pair or split pair cables will not work. These failure cases will be marked with red text on the yellow fluke. Cables with breaks will appear as unconnected ends and will also not function.



Category 3
UTP
10 Mbps
16 MHz
Features/Usage:
Two-line telephone systems
10BASE-T networks
Alarm system installation





Category 5
UTP
10 to 100 Mbps
100 MHz
Features/Usage:
Only for older hardware that needs outdated hardware





Category 5e
UTP
1,000 Mbps
100 MHz
Features/Usage:
Most popular cable
Supports gigabit speeds
Small space installation
Used in residential areas





Category 6
UTP or STP
1,000 to 10,000 Mbps
250 MHz
Features/Usage:
Tightly twisted, with two or more twists per centimeter
Only supports 37-55 meters when transmitting 10 Gbps speeds





Category 6a
STP
10,000 Mbps
500 MHz
Features/Usage:
Thicker than Category 6 Cables - meaning less flexible
Long-run options in offices and homes
Less susceptible to interference and crosstalk





Category 7
SSTP
10,000 to 40,000 Mbps
600 MHz
Features/Usage:
Can transmit 40 Gbps at 50 meters
Can transmit 100 Gbps at 15 meters
Extensive shielding, which results in it being relatively stiff
Used in data centers and enterprise networks
Not approved as the cable standard for telecommunication







Category 8
SSTP/Shielded
25,000 to 40,000 Mbps
2000 MHz
Features/Usage:
Ideal when the distance between units is short
Backward compatible with previous categories of ethernet cables





" KB0014261 Network - Wired Network - Wired - How to use the Fluke "

The Help Desk and Computing Labs have yellow ethernet testers called Flukes. Each location has its own Fluke, so be sure to note its availability.

The Fluke has several troubleshooting functions, including:

Determining cable length.
Detecting broken wires.
Checking if an ethernet jack is live.

The most commonly used menus on the Fluke are AutoTest and Cable.

AutoTest

The AutoTest function provides critical information about the wall port, including:

IP address of the switch.
Switch name.
Blade/port number.
Steps to Use AutoTest
Plug one end of the cable into the top port on the Fluke.
Connect the other end to the wall port.
Allow the Fluke to run its tests.
Record the displayed information to include in the incident task.
Troubleshooting
If no information is displayed:
The port might be error disabled or misconfigured.
A red X indicates that the Fluke does not detect a connection. This suggests a failure.
Retry the AutoTest using a dispatch cable:
If the dispatch cable displays the correct information, the user's ethernet cable is likely faulty.
If the dispatch cable also fails, the issue is likely with the port.
Ethernet Cable Check

The Cable menu allows you to test the functionality of an ethernet cable.

Steps to Use Ethernet Cable Check
Plug one end of the ethernet cable into the top port of the Fluke.
Plug the other end into the left side of the Fluke.
From the main menu, select Cable.
Observe the results:
A valid cable configuration will pass the test.
An invalid configuration will show:
Breaks in the lines, or
Red error text indicating an issue.
A red X indicates the cable is completely nonfunctional.
Wiremap Check

Use the Wiremap Check to detect breaks in the in-wall wiring.

Steps to Use Wiremap Check
Insert the Wireview/loopback device into the user's wall port.
In the switch closet:
Locate the cable connected to the wall port.
Disconnect it from the switch and plug it into the upper port of the Fluke.
Run the Cable Check on the Fluke.
Review the results:
Any breaks in the wiring will be identified.
Next Steps

Escalate any issues involving broken in-wall wiring to the Network Supervisor or Full-Time Staff.

" KB0018638 Network - Wired Network - Wired - Port VLAN changes "

" KB0011005 Network - Wired Network - Wired - Router Policy "

Users cannot use a wireless connection with routers since doing so will cause interference with the Rutgers wireless networks. Users can have unmanaged switches if they need additional ports in their room, but routers are not allowed in any capacity.

Previously, users residing in Marvin Apartments have been allowed to use the wireless connection. As described in this knowledge article, NOC has since fixed the wireless issues in Marvin Apartments. Personal routers are not allowed in any residential building.

Please provide the following if a user wishes to know who created the policy and why it was implemented:

"OIT and ResLife have worked together to put in a NO ROUTER POLICY that would create a healthy network, improving RUWireless coverage. Other universities have successfully implemented similar policies with 802.1X authentication. Since 802.1X already exists through RUWireless Secure, ResNet also uses the same authentication method."

" KB0011007 Network - Wired "Network - Wired - Speeds, Bandwidth and Traffic" "

What connection speeds can a user expect on the Residential Network?
The University offers Gigabit Internet, allowing a maximum speed of up to 2000 Mbps (Megabits per second) or 2Gbps (Gigabits per second), in the residence halls.
The Internet bandwidth allocated to RUWireless is capped at 2Gbps; however, internal University traffic is not metered.
Despite such a high-speed cap, users are not expected to see speeds nearing the cap. The highest expected speeds would be approaching 1Gbps on the ResNet networks or slightly over 100Mbps for wireless devices on RUWireless, RUWireless Secure, eduroam, or resnetiot.
Users connected to ResNet via Ethernet will generally receive much higher average speeds than WiFi connections.
The minimum speed the Help Desk guarantees in residences is 10Mbps on average. Any users reporting speeds below this threshold should have their tickets brought to Network Support for further investigation and may require either a Wireless Survey Dispatch or Escalation appointment.
Note that these speeds are variable. Actual network speed will vary based on several factors, including but not limited to the connection speed of the external sites, the port speed of a computer s network card, high AP utilization, or general network congestion.
What is the difference between external and internal traffic?
It is considered external traffic if you are connecting to Google, YouTube, or other such sites. Any websites hosted within the Rutgers network are internal and will be unaffected by the speed caps. A good guideline for checking if a website is internal to the Rutgers network is checking the URL of the website. Sites ending in rutgers.edu (e.g. my.rutgers.edu or sakai.rutgers.edu) are generally internal sites, and the speed cap will not affect data transmission rates to and from these sites.
It is important to note that some websites affiliated with the University are not hosted within the Rutgers network, although these sites are sometimes linked to Rutgers websites. For information that is downloaded or uploaded from these sites, the speed cap will affect any data transmitted.

" KB0014254 Network - Wired Network - Wired - Switches - Errdisabled Status "

If several MAC addresses appear on a port in rapid succession, it may error disable. For first-time offenses, restart the port on the switch. If the issue occurs frequently (i.e. every few minutes, hours, or even once a day), we want to investigate whether the user has an unusual device plugged in or if the issue occurs on a different port and escalate the issue. Routers and hubs, in particular, cause this issue more than other devices.

Confirm the following for Multihost:

User is getting media disconnect
Port shows as errdisabled on switch
Command "show interfaces status err-disabled" lists "security-violation" as reason
If user has a router connected to the port and they have confirmed the router is connected correctly and is not bridging

Solution: Have FT set multihost/port security on the port and ensure [multihost] is added to the subject.

Confirm the following for BPDUGuard violation

User is getting media disconnect
Port shows as errdisabled on switch
Command "show interfaces status err-disabled" lists "BPDUGuard" as reason
User is connecting a router to the port

Solution: Confirm router make/model and have the user check that "AP mode" on the router is disabled (may be called other things); If a user cannot find this setting, we can send dispatch. Once router is configured correctly, re-enable the port.

" KB0014256 Network - Wired Network - Wired - Switches - Multihost "

If an 802.1x port is error disabling, it may result from multiple devices being seen on the port. If this is the case, FT can enable multi-host on the port to prevent disabling.

Example:

#show int status err-disabled

Port      Name               Status       Reason               Err-disabled
Vlans

Gi4/46    3022 327 3-8367    err-disabled security-violation

" KB0014257 Network - Wired Network - Wired - Switches - Rogue DHCP Procedures "

A rogue DHCP is when a dorm student plugs their personal router into the wall jack incorrectly. Specifically, they plug the wall jack into a LAN (Local Area Network) port rather than a WAN (Wide Area Network) port. Personal routers have internal DHCP servers that assign IPs to all devices connected to the LAN ports. When a router s LAN port is plugged into the wall, it acts as a DHCP server for every computer connected to that switch. Any computer that needs to renew its network lease will receive an IP address of 192.168.x.x (most routers) or 10.1.x.x (Apple routers) instead of the typical 172.30.x.x IP that the Rutgers DHCP servers lease out.

Often, the first symptom of a rogue DHCP will appear when a user calls in complaining that they do not have internet connectivity and that they do not have a router. They will also have DHCP enabled, but will pull a 192.168.x.x or 10.1.x.x address after renewing their IP address.

While most rogues assign users an IP address in the 192.168.x.x or 10.1.x.x range, there are special cases where it may affect only the user's DNS servers or certain devices. Please see below for various scenarios.

Various Scenarios
If the user pulls a 192.168.x.x or 10.1.x.x address after renewing their DHCP lease, it is most likely a typical rogue. Please verify that the user's wireless NIC is disabled & DHCP and DNS are set to automatic on their Ethernet NIC (IPv4). Have the user run 'arp-a' to find the rogue's MAC address.
If the user is pulling a valid 172.30.x.x address but the user's DNS address is 192.168.x.x, you must statically assign the user's computer to the 192.168.x.x range. After that, ping the user's gateway address, then run 'arp-a' to find the rogue's MAC address.
If the user can get a valid Rutgers IP address and DNS name, but a router or Fluke cannot establish a lease, you will have to run Wireshark and analyze the capture to see if there are any conflicting DHCP offers on the local network.
Finding/confirming rogue

When following the call scripts, Help Desk consultants are instructed to go straight to ResNet when encountering the symptoms listed above. The ResNet Office consultant should confirm that DHCP is enabled, the wireless card is disabled, and the user is not using a proxy server. Once these are confirmed, ask the Help Desk consultant to have the user enter arp a into the command prompt and record the results in the ticket.

The arp command should display information similar to what is shown below. From here, you can obtain the MAC address corresponding to the user s current gateway address, which is the rogue DHCP server's address.

" KB0014258 Network - Wired Network - Wired - Switches - Switch commands "

Table of Contents

How to Log into the Switch:
Connect to the Rutgers VPN using Cisco AnyConnect
Open PuTTY
Select "Telnet" for Connection Type
Under Host Name, enter the IP Address of the switch
Enter your NetID
Enter your NetID Password
When you receive a Duo Push, press Allow
Cisco IOS Switches ( # prompt)

NOTE: If you re on an IOS switch, you ll need to copy any changes that you made (port names, VLAN change, etc.) to the switch s startup configuration file. This will make the change permanent and prevent loss of any changes if a power outage occurs. To do this, run the write memory command after you make your changes.

Basic show commands

[show int status] lists all the ports on the switch and their state

[show int (port)] shows detailed information of a specific port, which includes information such as the collisions that have appeared on the port

[show mac address-table] shows all MAC addresses currently detected on the switch

[show mac address-table address (MAC address)] searches for a specific MAC address

[show mac address-table int (port)] searches for what MAC address is connected to a specified port

[show cdp neighbors] shows any other switches that are connected to this one and the ports that they are connected on. A port can be specified with this command as well, and it will display the switch connected to that port.

[show version] - shows various information about the switch, including the switch model

[show auth sessions] - displays interface, MAC address, auth method (Dot1x, MAB, N/A), and status of active sessions

[show auth session int (port)] - displays detailed information for the port, including the runnable methods list, ts, which indicates whether dot1x is misconfigured

Using dot1x
Runnable methods list:
 Method State
 dot1x Authc Success
 mab Not run
dot1x misconfigured
Runnable methods list:
 Method State
 dot1x Authc Failed
 mab Not run
Using mab
Runnable methods list:
 Method State
 dot1x Failed over
 mab Authc Success
Restricted
Runnable methods list:
 Method State
 dot1x Failed over
 mab Failed over


[show aaa sessions] - displays authenticated usernames and IP addresses

[show epm session summary] - displays interface, IP address, SS,s and MAC address of active sessions

[show epm session ip (IP Address)] - displays auth method, RADIUS attributes of a specific client (useful for determining if a client is restricted because of an IPS block)

Making changes to the IOS switch ports

To make changes to any ports on an IOS switch, you must first follow these steps.

config t (This will enter configuration mode from a terminal)

On some older IOS switches, you will need to type enable instead
interface (port) (Go into interface mode for a specific port)

Note: You can interface into a range of ports by using the int range command.

Enabling and disabling ports

[shutdown] - shuts down the port that you re interfaced with

[no shutdown] - enables the port that you re interfaced with

Port security

[show port-security] - shows all ports on the switch that have port security enabled

[show port-security int (port)] - verifies your port security settings on a specific port

[switchport port-security mac-address (MAC address (optional))] - enables port security on the interface; you can specify the secure address

[no switchport port-security] - disables port security on the interface port

Port names

[description (name)] - changes port name

[no description] - leaves the description blank

VLANs

[show vlan] - shows information about every possible VLAN that ports on the switch can be configured for

[show interface trunk] - shows what VLANs are active on the switch

[switchport access vlan (VLAN #)] - assign a specific port to a VLAN

Clear counters

[clear counters (port)] - clears the counters for the specified port

VLAN shows up as routed

Run the commands:

[switchport]
[switchport host]
[switchport access vlan (VLAN #)]
Cisco Cable Test

Cisco IOS switches can run a variety of tests, including cable diagnostics. Use this to remotely determine if there is a cable break in the patch cable, wall cable, or user's cable.

Run the commands:

test cable-diagnostics tdr int gi#/#
show cable-diagnostics tdr int gi#/#

Output given will include the tested port, the rated speed, cable length (if determined), pair orientation, and line damage (if any). Please have a device plugged into the port to help ensure all lines are connected properly.

Logging

[show log] useful for finding MAC addresses that are not showing up via other methods. The first part of the show log command provides information about the logging system itself, while the part following Log Buffer contains the actual log.

Configuring a port for 802.1x

Sometimes you will encounter a port that, for one reason or another, is not configured for 802.1x. The command show running-config int Gi#/# shows the current configuration of a specified port. If a port seems to be misconfigured, do the following:

NOTE If a user port is found to be on a wireless backhaul switch (i.e. wb01) we should repatch it to an access switch (i.e. as01) instead of reconfiguring.

config t

default int <Blade/Port>

int <[Gi]Blade/Port>

switchport mode access

switchport access vlan <VLAN>

description <DESCRIPTION>

IP access-group Restricted in

authentication event fail action authorize vlan <VLAN>

authentication event no-response action authorize vlan <VLAN>

authentication host-mode multi-auth

authentication violation restrict

authentication order dot1x mab

authentication port-control auto

authentication periodic

authentication timer reauthenticate server

mab

no snmp trap link-status

dot1x pae authenticator

dot1x timeout tx-period 5

spanning-tree portfast

Be sure to change the port to the appropriate VLAN. NOC must be notified of this change.

If you come across a misconfigured switch, send an email to NOC with the switch name/IP.

" KB0014262 Network - Wired Network - Wired - When and How to Repatch "

As an escalation consultant, there are some cases where we can repair issues with wired connection without requiring NOC to rewire the wall. This article covers issues that can be resolved by repatching. Repatching is the replacement of the cable that connects the patch panel and the switch.

While troubleshooting a connection issue, you may use the Fluke to detect if a patch cable has any breaks in it. If the cable does have a break, and the port on the switch is working correctly, you can replace the cable with one of the HD cables. Use the new cable to connect the same ports on the patch panel and switch.

There are also cases where the cable is working fine, but the port on the switch is not working. In this scenario, if there are open ports on the switch that are not being used for anything, you can move the cable from the dead port on the switch to an open port. Then, log into the switch, reconfigure the port to the correct port settings, and label it with the correct room. Label the original switch as dead. After your appointment is complete and the user confirms that this resolved the issue, send an email to NOC to report the changes that you made.

If there are no open ports on the switch, under no circumstances should you unplug wires to make space. Send an email to NOC to let them know that the port on the switch is dead and is causing a problem for the user s port, noting that there were no open ports to which to repatch. NOC will handle the issue from this point.

" KB0014264 Network - Wired Network - Wired - Wired Escalations "

Using the fluke (Yellow Linkrunner)
The fluke is a device that gives relatively detailed information about what it is plugged into
Running auto-test while plugged into a switch will return information about the switch and connectivity.
All flukes are bypassed, so if the fluke is getting access-rejects but the bypass is still valid, there is likely an issue.
Plugging both ends of a wire into the fluke and running the cable test will find if the wire is damaged internally or no.t
If a wireview is plugged into a wall and the fluke into the other end of the wall, you can run the cable test to evaluate if the wall s internal wiring is still intact.
Steps to take during a wired escalation
First, always ensure the user is plugged into the correct data port.
Next, check the user s cable for damage.
Next, double-check that the fluke is working correctly.
If the fluke works, you can check the RN Laptop + ethernet adapter
If the RN Laptop can 802.1x through the ethernet adapter, the issue may be with the user s device
If the RN Laptop cannot 802.1x, the port may be misconfigured for 802.1x
If not, then continue to investigate the port configuration
This will entail checking the port on the switch and the switch configuration

If the port does not appear to be the source of the problem, there may be wiring issues.

The switch may have ports that are simply dead. This can be fixed by moving the cable from that port on the switch into an empty one and configuring it. Email should be sent to NOC detailing the fix and broken port. This is typically called a re-patch.
The cable from the patch panel to the switch may have gone bad. Simply replace the cable with one of ours.
Using the wireview or loopback, a break may be detected in the wall. EI - Inside Plant will need to repair the wiring.
If the faceplate is damaged, dispatch or escalation can fix it. However, if the box itself is damaged or hanging off the wall again EI - Inside Plant will need to be contacted.
Things to bring on a Wired Escalation

Most things will be helpful during a wired escalation, and it is recommended to err on the side of bringing more than less. A full kit entails the following:

Laptop
Ethernet NIC
Wireless NIC
Tools USB
Fluke
Multiple ethernet cables
Extra faceplate
Screwdriver
Wireview or loopback
Router
Consent form

We have access to the following switch closet keys, kept in our safe: - BCM, Campus master, dorm specific, and 2AA

The following items are helpful for extreme edge cases:

Locust SSID finder - to track down wireless networks
Minihub - to look at packets from a target device

" KB0014265 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - Academic Buildings (capacity issues) "

If a user reports slow speeds in a non-residential building, confirm the following:

User is having RUWireless issues at classroom/lecture hall, but not at other locations.
User reports others in class are experiencing similar issues.
Both RUWireless and RUWireless Secure networks are visible.

If this is the case, collect:

NetIDs of users affected.
Mac addresses of devices affected.
Building and room numbers where the issue is occurring.

Inform the user that we will report the issue to our service provider to determine if there's a problem with the access point, but the Access Point may be at capacity. Refer the incident to the Network Operations Center (NOC) for escalation.

" KB0014268 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - AP behavior & configuration "

Wireless APs should be configured to have all antennae vertical; on the current 4 antenna APs, the upper pair should be directly up and the lower pair directly down.

Clean Air: Wireless APs automatically change their network channel to channels to prevent interference with other wireless networks. This is generally not relevant as a cause of problems.

Signal strength fluctuation: An additional step that APs take to prevent interference. They automatically reduce their power level if they can see other RUWireless networks. This can result in unintended dead spots for rooms immediately between APs if both APs back down simultaneously.

Cisco Prime offers the ability to view the following helpful information about an AP, inclding:

Currently associated users
Nearby wireless broadcasts
Previously associated users
Power level of AP (10 is lowest, 1 is highest)

Prime offers maps of APs at some locations. This can be useful for evaluating signal strength issues in an area. Note that not all maps are complete or present. Some of the full-time staff have access to additional floor plans however, AP information is not available for those.

When using Prime, consider the difference between the Ethernet MAC and the Base Radio MAC. The Base Radio MAC is generally the address that interests us.

" KB0014266 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - Damaged or Broken Access Point (AP) "

If a user reports that an Access Point (AP) is damaged or broken inside a residence hall, they will need to provide proof of damage to the AP with pictures emailed to help@oit.rutgers.edu or in an incident. Common issues would include missing antennae from the APs with four antennae.

Once the damage to the AP has been determined, Network Support can schedule an Escalation appointment to have the AP repaired.

For broken or damaged APs in non-residential buildings. Confirm the location of the AP and the damage done as would be done for a residential building. Once this information has been confirmed, move the incident to the EI - Network Access Technologies queue with a request to have the AP repaired.

" KB0014267 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - Eduroam "

Eduroam is an intra-university network that is available at supported institutions. Faculty, Staff, Students, and Emeritus can access and use Eduroam.

Users log into Eduroam using their own home university credentials. As a result, we cannot install Eduroam for members of other universities visiting us; they will need to speak to their universities for support.

Rutgers Guests and retirees are not eligible for Eduroam.

If Rutgers students, faculty, staff or emeriti want to set up their devices for Eduroam, they will need to run the installer on the Eduroam setup page. Setup can be done from anywhere they have an internet connection; they do not need to be on campus to install, but it's highly recommended that they test the connection on campus before heading to another institution.

Our public-facing documentation is here: RUWireless Eduroam page. Eduroam users log in with netid@rutgers.edu and their NetID password to log in. Other than using netid@rutgers.edu in the installation instead of just the NetID, the rest of the installation is the same. Manual setup instructions are available in the article: RUWireless & Eduroam - Manual Setup.

" KB0014269 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - General Information "

Allowed Roles

Users without student, faculty, staff, alumni, or guest roles cannot access RUWireless Secure. They can instead use the guest RUWireless network.

Speed limits

RUWireless in the dorms does not have a guaranteed minimum speed. However, due to how the APs allocate bandwidth, in some cases wireless speeds can be significantly better than wired speeds. For our Cisco APs, the full bandwidth of the AP is divided between the users. An AP with 50 Mbps with 2 users would give 25 Mbps to each while the same AP with 100 users would give 0.5 to each. This is why users report slowness most in the evenings because many users are connecting at once. Generally, if an AP is being flooded with users and all nearby APs are functioning normally, the only solution is installing more APs. APs generally have an unthrottled gigabit connection.

Where do APs come from?

RUWireless APs are installed by the NOC. For dorms, the housing department pays for the AP. For academic buildings, the department that controls the building pays. APs for other networks are installed and maintained by that department.

Coverage

RUWireless offers full coverage in all Rutgers. However, not all academic buildings have coverage. For an uncovered department building to get covered, the department must submit a request to the Telecommunications Division and fund the installation of APs.

For this reason, we only perform wireless surveys in dorms. If a user requests a survey in a building other than a dorm, speak with a supervisor. Personal Routers are not permitted in any residence hall.

Supported devices for RUWireless Secure

Other than computers and smartphones, any device that can authenticate on 802.1x is supported on RUWireless Secure. Any device with a browser that can store cookies should support the browser for RUWireless.

RUatHome

Game consoles, smart TVs, streaming devices, smart devices, and wired (ethernet) desktops can be connected to the network (RUatHome). For information, go to the knowledge article detailing how to Connect your smart devices, streaming devices, gaming consoles, and wired desktops.

" KB0014270 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - Google Homes on RUAtHome "

Although it is not guaranteed that all Google Homes can connect to RUAtHome, here are some steps to attempt a connection:

The user must have the Google Home application on their phone. If they don't, have them install it.
The device must be factory reset to get the Google Home's MAC address.
Then go to the Google Home app, select Devices > Add device > Set up a new device.
Tap on the gear icon, and the MAC address should be at the bottom of the page.

Note: Some devices, like Google Home, may require your phone to be on the same network as your device to complete the setup process or change settings on your device. In this case, you will also have to add your phone to the RUAtHome Registration portal.

For more information, please refer to the following article regarding connecting your smart devices.

" KB0014274 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - Manual Setup "

In some cases the RUWireless installer is not an option due to various issues or device/OS incompatibility. In these cases manual configuration of the device can be required.

Several important notes regarding the secure setup:

In order to download the two listed certificates, the user will need to either have access to internet or have a USB port to get the certificates from the tools USB.
EAP is not the same as EAP-TTLS if both are an option.
TTLS stands for Tunneled TLS.
WPA2 is not the same as WPA2 Enterprise.
While the Identity or Username is where the the user enters their NetID, the Anonymous or Secret Identity field should be left blank.

Installing NetworkManager

Some Linux distributions support NetworkManager but do not have it installed by default. These are the steps necessary to make it work correctly, though they may need to be modified to suit the specific distribution.

Install NetworkManager
Use the distributions package manager to find the Network Manager package. In Debian based distributions, it can be installed with the following:
sudo apt-get update; sudo apt-get install network-manager network-manager-gnome
Disable any unnecessary services
This includes dhcpd and ifplugd and wicd. Research what is appropriate. Not all of the services mentioned above may be installed. But usually this usually this can be accomplished with the following:
sudo systemctl disable dhcpcd; sudo systemctl disable ifplugd; sudo systemctl disable wicd;
Set up NetworkManager

Both commands boot up the Network Manager service and configures the device to start NM for every boot up
sudo systemctl enable NetworkManager; sudo systemctl start NetworkManager
Reboot Device
Remove Old NetworkManager Icon
Right click the icon and click remove.
Start NetworkManager:
This command starts the NetworkManager applet.
nm-applet 
Linux without NetworkManager
Using wpa_supplicant (a unix command-line utility for connecting to wifi networks):
Open /etc/wpa_supplicant.conf with a text editor such as Leafpad, Nano, or Vim.

sudo leafpad /etc/wpa_supplicant.conf
Insert the following into the file, replacing the text marked with angle brackets (<>) with the appropriate content:

network={
      ssid="RUWireless Secure"
      scan_ssid=1
      key_mgmt=WPA-EAP
      eap=TTLS
      identity="<netid>"
      password="<NetID password>"
      ca_cert2="<path to downloaded copy of https://cloud.securew2.com/public/09325/RUWirelessSecure/certificates/usertrustrsacertificationauthority.cer>"
      phase2="auth=PAP"
      }

The path may be found by going to file properties and adding /<name of file> onto the end. In this case, the file name will be /RUWireless_Secure.crt
In order to find out the correct interface for the connection, you must run either of the following commands:
ifconfig

or
ip a

Note: the second command checks for an IP address.
The command will usually show you three or more interfaces (wired, loop, and wireless).
You can choose between the wired and wireless adapters according to which interface you wish to use.
For example, if your wireless adapter is named "wlan0", the following would be the command to run in order to make the connection:
sudo wpa_supplicant -Dwext -i wlan0 -c /etc/wpa_supplicant.conf &

NOTE: Be sure to substitute -Dwext with -Dwired if using a wired connection, and "wlan0" with the correct interface (usually "eth0" for wired connection).

You must then run the following, to run DHCP:
sudo dhclient -v -i wlan0
You may replace "wlan0" with the correct interface.
Check for IP using:
ip a

If there a still issues, check the DNS servers by opening /etc/resolv.conf

sudo leafpad /etc/resolv.conf

and insert:

nameserver 128.6.1.1

into the file, and try again.

You can automate the connection by editing /etc/network/interfaces like so:

sudo leafpad /etc/network/interfaces

and insert the following into the file:

auto wlan0
iface wlan0 inet dhcp
wpa-conf /etc/wpa_supplicant/wpa_supplicant.conf


Reboot the device to test.

Substitute the interface "wlan0" for whichever is appropriate.

" KB0010955 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - Radiation questions "

Radiation concerns related to wireless signals

Concerns about the potential dangers of wireless signals such as those from cell phones and wireless routers occasionally arise.

In short, wireless routers emit weaker signals than cell phones and, therefore, present even less potential risk. Additionally, cell phones have not been shown to present health risks based on current scientific research.

For more information, refer to the FCC s page on wireless devices and health concerns.

" KB0014340 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - RBHS Wireless "

RUHealthSciences exists across the RBHS campuses (Newark, Piscataway, New Brunswick, Scotch Plains, and Stratford). This network is dedicated to faculty, staff, students, and Guest account holders only. All computers and devices that are using these networks must be configured.

"RUHealthSciences" for any RBHS faculty, staff, and students (broadcast in all RBHS and UH locations)
RUWireless and RUWireless Secure (broadcast in all RBHS locations)

" KB0010956 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - Redirect to Login Page "

Occasionally, users connected to RUWireless will not be redirected to the correct login page and will get a blank page or an error message.

HTTP vs HTTPS

To access the login page, you can try accessing any web pages that use HTTP instead of HTTPS.

To understand why this solution works, we must examine how devices get to the RUWireless login page normally:

The computer attempts to perform a Domain Name System (DNS) lookup for a URL (Google.com might be translated to the 216.58.219.238 IP address, for example).
The browser requests to access the webpage at IP address 216.58.219.238.
RUWireless will attempt to redirect the browser s request to the RUWireless and Wireless Secure login page instead.
At this point, one of two things can happen:

If the target website uses HTTP, it will be redirected to the login page without issue.
If the target website uses HTTPS, the redirect to the login page will be considered insecure and be blocked by the browser.
Some HTTP web pages that can be used are:
Rutgers.edu
NJ.gov
Common HTTPS sites that can cause problems are:
Google.com
Reddit.com
Facebook.com

The issue can occur on many popular websites. Therefore, if someone cannot get to the login page, have them try one of the HTTP sites instead.

DNS Issues

If the user is not getting redirected to the login page, check if they can access wifi.rutgers.edu.

If they have a non-rutgers DNS, they may not be able to get through to these pages. Therefore, ensure their DNS is set to automatic, and that they get 128.6.1.1 while automatic. Once this is set, they can access these two pages. Users cannot download the RUWireless Secure installer if they have a custom DNS server set.

If the user s DNS is correct, they can download the Secure installer either from the wifi.rutgers.edu link or directly from this RUWireless Secure page.

Tools USB

If the user still cannot get RUWireless to download the installer, you can assist walk-ins or lab patrons with installing Secure via the tools USB. The installer should be there by default.

Other Notes
The student worker role does not grant access to Secure.
Anyone having trouble connecting can log onto the RUWireless guest network first and use it to access the secure download.

" KB0014247 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - Removing SecureW2 Secure Profile "

A majority of Secure issues can be resolved by removing and reinstalling a freshly downloaded Secure installer. To remove the SecureW2/Secure profiles, please see the following:

iOS
Open the Settings app, then go to General.
Scroll down to Profile & Device Management.

If prompted, enter the device passcode.
Tap Delete to confirm.
Mac OSX up to 10.6
Open System Preferences and select Network.
Choose Airport or Ethernet and click the Advanced button.
Go to the 802.1X tab.
Select RUWireless Secure and click the button to remove the profile.
MacOS 10.7 - 12.7
Open System Preferences and select Profiles.
Choose the RUWireless Secure profile.
Click the button to remove the profile.
MacOS 13+
Open System Settings and select Privacy and Security.
Scroll down to Profile.
Select the RUWireless Secure profile and tap Delete.
If prompted, enter the device passcode.
Tap Delete to confirm.
Windows
Open Control Panel or use the Search function to locate Uninstall Programs.
Scroll through the list to find the SecureW2 program.
Select SecureW2 and click Uninstall.
Android
Using Settings:
Open the phone s Settings and navigate to Apps.
Locate the JoinNow MultiOS app (Secure W2 app).
Select the app and tap Uninstall.
Using the App Drawer:
Open the App Drawer and find the JoinNow MultiOS app.
Press and hold the app icon, then drag it to the Delete/Uninstall section on the screen.
For Devices with Manual Configuration

If the JoinNow MultiOS app is not installed, the device may have been manually configured. Follow these steps:

Open the phone s Wireless Settings.
Select the RUWireless Secure network.
Choose the option to Forget Network.

Note: Not all Android phones will have the JoinNow MultiOS (Secure W2) app installed. Some devices may have been manually configured instead. In such cases, simply forget the network through the wireless settings as outlined above.

" KB0010959 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - RUIOT "

RUIOT (Rutgers Internet of Things) is a WPA2-PSK wireless network broadcast across the University to supplement the existing wireless bypass services offered on RUWireless.

This network is only available to departments and IT professionals, it is not offered for your typical end user.

Connection to this network (with password: resnetiot); use the IP Space 172.30.X.X and does not have the same speed and firewall restrictions as RUWireless so devices on this network should offer greater usability for devices. These devices can connect to the network without being stopped by the captive portal. There is a speed cap in place of 10Mbps per device and firewall restrictions on this network.

The RUIOT network should only be used for departmental non-802.1x devices.

If a user reports issues with specific applications or devices, bring this to the attention of a Supervisor for tracking and escalation.

Departmental IT must follow the procedure to connect to RUIOT, if it is a streaming device; otherwise, they should submit a request via the EI - MAC Authentication Bypass (MAB) form (only visible to IT staff).

Important Notes:

Any reports of missing RUIOT SSID should be escalated to the wireless team assignment group: EI - Network Access Technologies.
Problems of devices not connecting should be escalated to the NAC team assignment group: EI - Network Access Technologies (NAC).

" KB0010951 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - RUWireless Secure Service Account "

Purpose of an RUWireless Secure Service Account

RUWireless Secure (RUWS) Service Accounts are used to install RUWireless Secure on a device which is not assigned to a single user. For RUWS Service Accounts, there are no MAC address limits on Service accounts since they are not MAC-restricted.

Examples of typical use cases might include departments with sign-in kiosks, departmental tablets, or departmental iPads.

Note: The form below is only visible to IT Staff.

Requesting an RUWS Service Account

To request an RUWireless Secure Service Account, navigate to the EI - RUWireless Secure Service Account form.

If the link above does not bring you directly to the form:

Navigate to https://ithelp.rutgers.edu/
Select Request Service > Desktop and Mobile Computing > EI - RUWireless Secure Service Account.

The request form will ask for the following information:

Requested for

By default, it will populate your name.
This would be the contact who is responsible for the account. In those cases where this request is being submitted for someone else, please indicate it in the form.
i.e. For a guest or non-IT personnel requesting it for their department, they'd need their IT representative to submit it on their behalf.
You can search for the user via NetID or name.
Contact email
By default, it will populate the email of the contact listed in the Requested for field.
Contact phone number
By default, it may populate the phone number of the contact listed in the Requested for field.
Department Name
By default, it may populate the department of the contact listed in the Requested for field.
Username
Must be at least 9 characters.
Description
Type of device(s) and business purpose.
Department contact address
Contact address for the IT department responsible for the Service Account.
This e-mail address will be used for Service Account lifecycle communications going forward so this should not be a single point of contact.
Additional Comments

Note: There is an ability to attach files to the request but is not required. The button is located below Additional Comments.



For issues where all basic and intermediate troubleshooting has been exhausted regarding RUWS Service Accounts, Shaun Manaois can assist further.
