" KB0014276 Network - Wireless Network - Wireless - RUWireless Survey Guidelines "

Below is a general guideline of what to do when a Level 2 is out on a RUWireless Survey.

Table of Contents
Before heading out to the appointment
Upon arrival at the appointment
The wireless survey process
How to locate driver information
MacOS
Windows
Sample write-up for ServiceNow work notes
Before heading out to the appointment
Access point (AP) information: Before going out to conduct a survey, speak with a supervisor to get information about the closest access points and put that information into the ticket. This information will include the name, location, and the MAC address of the AP. You will be able to reference this information in MIST to see if the AP is running properly when you are on site.
Understanding the situation: Read over the ServiceNow ticket to fully grasp the issue that the user is facing. Remember that the main reason for these surveys is to examine whether the issue lies with the user's device(s) or with the APs of the location. Sometimes the issue can be device-specific. For example, the user s phone may be getting a perfect wireless signal, but the user s laptop is not. After reading the ticket, you should be aware of the user's specific issue (drops in connection, slow speeds, or both) and the user's devices.
The wireless network interface card (NIC): While on site, you will be conducting the survey with both the user s laptop (if it is a laptop issue) and the ResNet laptop. Be sure to use the wireless NIC on the ResNet laptop.
You can disable the internal wireless card by going to Network and Sharing Center > Change Adapter Settings > Disable the current Wi-Fi adapter.
When you plug the wireless NIC into the laptop, it will be listed as Wi-Fi 2.
Things to bring: For a wireless survey, the only required equipment is the ResNet laptop, wireless NIC, and the liability waiver form. Other equipment can be taken if deemed necessary, particularly the fluke.
Upon arrival at the appointment
Get a detailed account of the user s issue by asking the following questions:
What is the user s issue?
Does it occur on both the RUWireless Secure and the guest network?
Does the issue resolve after a short period?
How long does the issue last?
Times that the user experiences issues
Is the network stable at other times?
Does this happen in certain locations or rooms?
Is this local to their apartment or across campus?
Note: If the user asks about having a wireless router, please refer to the router policy for further guidance.
Get information about the user s devices
Include model info as well as software info for all devices the user has, even if they work. Please collect info on the wireless card and the installed driver information.
Please collect the information for all devices the user has, and whether they work. If connection issues are on certain devices, it is probably not an issue with the wireless network.
Initial troubleshooting questions:
Does the user have low signal strength?
Is the signal strength the same with the USB wireless NIC?

(Check driver version for USB NIC)
Are the drivers up to date for the device's wireless card?
Does the ResNet laptop have a similar issue?
Does it work closer to the AP?
Does the device have issues connecting to the network?
If the user is connecting via the Open network, install RUWireless Secure.
If the user is connecting via RUWireless Secure, try reinstalling.
If this does not work, check the driver version and try the USB NIC.
Does the device drop the connection?
Is there low signal strength when the device drops?
Is it able to reconnect? How long does it take?
Confirm the driver version is up to date, and try with a USB NIC.
Slow speeds?
Low signal strength?
Does it work in other locations?
Does it work closer to the AP?
Connected, but nothing will load
Does the user have a valid IP (172.31.x.x)?
Check to make sure that the DNS settings are set to auto.
Does the connection show as limited?
Run "ipconfig /release".
Run "ipconfig /renew".
Test the connection.
If issues continue, please run ipconfig /all and copy the data.
Is the device a support device?
Please refer to the How to connect your smart devices, streaming devices, gaming consoles, and wired desktops knowledge article for a list of supported devices.
For unsupported devices, we will not permit a wireless router for these devices alone. You can tell the user that the service provider is working on a long-term fix for these devices, but we do not have an ETA for the release of this fix. If the user has any further issues, they may contact the Help Desk.
Is the device FCC compliant?
The wireless survey process
Checking the AP: The first thing to do when you get to the user s residence is to check the closest access point to see if it is on, running, or damaged. If the AP is damaged, note how it is damaged and take a picture of it. Damaged APs can be reported via the protocol outlined in RUWireless - Broken & Damaged Access Point. For wall-mounted APs, antennae should be completely vertical, with the top two facing the ceiling and the bottom two facing down. Ensure that all antennae are securely screwed in.

The following list was created for the Cisco Aironet 2700 APs (Some RUWireless APs are of this model), but is still a good quick reference for other APs as well.

LED Status Indications by Message Type:

Boot Loader Status Sequence
Blinking green:
DRAM memory test in progress.
DRAM memory test OK.
Board initialization in progress.
Initializing the FLASH file system.
FLASH memory test OK.
Initializing Ethernet.
Ethernet OK.
Starting Cisco IOS.
Initialization successful.
Association Status
Green: No wireless client associated.
Blue: At least one wireless client is associated.
Operating Status
Blinking blue: Software upgrade in progress.
Cycling green, red, off: Discovery/join process in progress.
Rapidly cycling blue, green, red: Access point location command invoked.
Blinking red: Ethernet link not operational.
Boot Loader Warnings
Blinking blue: Configuration recovery in progress (MODE button pushed for 2 3 seconds).
Red: Ethernet failure or image recovery (MODE button pushed for 20 30 seconds).
Blinking green: Image recovery in progress (MODE button released).
Boot Loader Errors
Red: DRAM memory test failure.
Blinking red and blue: FLASH file system failure.
Blinking red and off:
Environment variable failure.
Bad MAC address.
Ethernet failure during image recovery.
Boot environment failure.
No Cisco image file.
Boot failure.
Cisco IOS Errors
Red: Software failure; try disconnecting and reconnecting the unit's power.
Cycling blue, green, red, and off: General warning; insufficient inline power.


Network cards: Before surveying the area, it is imperative to check the user s make/model of the network adapter and its corresponding drivers. Drivers are out of date in some cases. Directions on where the driver information is listed in the Device Information section of the survey printout.

Windows: In Device Manager, right-click the card and select Properties, then go to the Drivers tab.
MacOS: Go to Apple menu > About This Mac > More Info... > System Report... > WiFi and look for Card Type.
Updating drivers: To manually update the drivers for Windows, type the network card name into Google.
For example, type Intel(R) Dual Band Wireless-AC 3160 driver and this will direct you to the Intel website to download a newer version of the driver.
Sometimes drivers are downloaded as an .exe file, which will automatically update the driver when the file is run, but sometimes websites provide the driver in a folder of files. In that case, you would need to manually install the driver.
Note: For MacOS, the only way to update drivers is to update the OS. You may do so with the user s permission. If you cannot get a solid internet connection with either the user s laptop or by using the Wireless NIC with the user s laptop, please update the OS using a Wired NIC.
Surveying the area:
You will be surveying the user s entire residence using the following tools:
MIST
Windows Snipping Tool
(Command+Shift+4 on Mac OS)
Google Speed Test
inSSider
Before starting, create a folder on both laptops desktops labeled as [Ticket #] user and [Ticket #] Wireless NIC. You will be taking a snapshot of inSSider and the Speed Test on both devices. Using MIST is not necessary on the user s device. There should be a minimum of 3 screenshots per location. It should take roughly 5 minutes to obtain the information necessary in each location. Name each screenshot with the ticket number, the location where the survey was done, and which laptop was used in the filename [i.e., "INC0818290 Living Room user (INC0818290 Living Room Wireless Nic).png"]. At the end of taking screenshots, be sure to copy the folder on the user s laptop onto the Tools USB so that you have the screenshots to upload.
In the Silvers Apartments, for instance, there are three main living quarters: the living room and two bedrooms. With three rooms to survey, there should be a total of at least 9 screenshots with signal strength information. The reason for so many screenshots is that the information is readily accessible for the people who review the surveys.
The ServiceNow write-up and RUWireless survey webform: A thorough ServiceNow comment at the end of a survey is essential to completing a solid RUWireless Survey. The consultant going out to the site knows what happens at the user s residence more than any other consultant, so please be sure to be as specific as possible in what is done on site. A sample write-up is provided in the next section.

Please also fill out the RUW Survey Webform, which can be found in the staff portal.
In addition, please upload the survey screenshots to the incident in ServiceNow.
Nuances & troubleshooting: There are many unusual issues that require special instructions.
The following are general troubleshooting tips throughout the survey:

If the issue seems specific to the user device, check if the user s device is connecting on the 2.4 GHz or the 5 GHz band. While we can ask the NOC to increase the power of a specific band, it is sometimes not viable to increase the power of the 2.4 GHz band due to interference with other APs. In such cases, we may recommend that the user obtain a NIC that supports the 5 GHz band instead.
If the issue seems to disappear when the ResNet NIC is connected to their device, it is possible that the user s network card is old, underpowered, damaged, or misconfigured. Some network cards do not have their default settings optimized for 802.11n, which can be corrected by looking up the ideal settings online. However, the other cases can only be fixed by a new network card, either internal or as a USB.
It can be the case that the theoretical nearest AP is powered off, mislabeled, or otherwise deficient. In cases where there appears to be no signal at all, or the AP information from MIST does not appear at all, it is helpful to call the Help Desk and see if they can identify which AP is the true nearest that is being connected to.
In some cases, network issues are caused by interfering nearby wireless networks. These can sometimes be identified by looking for bypassed devices with similar MAC addresses. Another possible way to identify these networks is to see what devices are connected to nearby ResNet switches.
Speed Test results are often not the focus of our surveys, so we do not require quite as many pictures of these. Signal strength is much more important. Getting a picture of the inSSider at many locations inside and just outside the dorm helps us get a better understanding of the issue.
In very specific cases, it may be necessary to use Wireshark to inspect wireless packets.
Another very rare tool is the Locust, a network hunter that helps track down the exact source of an SSID. There are phone apps that replicate this functionality.
How to locate driver information
MacOS

MacOS cannot update drivers without updating/upgrading the operating system, but it is still important to include this information in the survey. Here is a screenshot of MacOS to illustrate where to look for driver information. From the desktop, click the Apple button on the top right of the screen. Then go to About this Mac > System Report > Network > Wi-Fi.





Windows

While Windows has built-in driver updating, it is almost always better to retrieve the drivers directly from the manufacturer if possible. To find the driver information for Windows devices, open the Device Manager by clicking Start and selecting Device Manager from the Control Panel. Alternatively, you may use the shortcut for Run (Windows+R), then type devmgmt.msc and hit Enter.





If you searched the network card on Google and found an updated driver for the specific network card, go to Browse my computer for driver software > Let me pick from a list of device drivers on my computer > Have disk... > Browse to locate the driver folder. Then, you will have to find the driver folder wherever you downloaded it. Sometimes the drivers download as a .zip file, so you may need to extract the folder first.

As long as the user has internet access, you can use the Tools USB to update the user s drivers. Make sure to check Create a restore point before updating any drivers in case of failure.

Sample write-up for ServiceNow work notes

Upon arriving at the user s apartment, I checked the closest AP. It is located in the next room, which is a lounge, but it is roughly 20ft away from the nearest room in the apartment.

First, I checked the user s network card drivers. I found a newer version on the web, so I went ahead and updated it.

As I was updating the user s drivers, I went to the closest AP and took a screenshot of inSSIDer.

In the living room, both the Wireless NIC and the user s laptop were able to pull an average connection. The user could stream on her laptop, and there was no delay in getting to websites. The issue is when moving to either of the two bedrooms.

In the main bedroom, the wireless NIC could not reach any internal or external sites. The user s laptop could access websites, but it took roughly 10-15 seconds for websites to load. I tried streaming a video on YouTube, but the video would merely buffer.

In the guest bedroom, it took the wireless NIC roughly one minute in order to reach any external website. It took roughly 20 seconds in order for the user s laptop to reach any external websites.

" KB0014280 OneSource OneSource - HR and Payroll "

OneSource is the Rutgers University Human Resources (HR) s Employee and Faculty Service Center. They are a single point of contact for employees (including student employees), faculty, managers, and retirees to have their HR & Payroll questions answered and issues resolved.

Leave Administration and Insurances all medical and non-medical leave requests and documentation are handled exclusively by OneSource.
CM-Personal Data Changes updates to personal information such as name, date of birth, address, social security number, etc.
Benefits Administration manage enrollments, deductions, tuition reimbursement/remission, and verification requests (e.g., State, Medicare, etc.)
Kite and Key Technology Store Deductions
Former-Employee Services provide needed information and forms/documents to former employees
Pending Job Change Submissions monitor pending job changes to ensure pay accuracy.

A case number is assigned to each incoming inquiry/issue, and it is tracked until resolved so the customer can check the case's progress. The system also facilitates service by providing OneSource representatives with a customer s role, union, benefits, and other relevant factors at the point of contact.

Note: The OneSource self-service portal is available 24 hours a day, 7 days a week. The center does not offer walk-in service; however, the existing walk-in areas for UHR and Payroll will remain open to serve faculty and staff on matters requiring in-person support.

You can contact OneSource via the service center portal, by email, or by phone: 732-745-SERV (7378).

Hours of Operation: 8 AM - 5 PM, Monday - Friday

__________________________

What does OneSource offer you?
A single point of contact.
When you call, knowledgeable professionals will assist you with accurate, up-to-date answers to your questions. You have the added convenience of calling one phone number, (732) 745-SERV (7378), for end-to-end service resolution.
On-demand access 24 hours a day, 7 days a week.
Policies, procedures, and forms are available on the OneSource self-service portal. You can access them whenever you need to find human resources and payroll information.
Consistent and accurate information.
You can browse an extensive collection of articles on the self-service portal. Articles include the latest information on topics such as leave administration, disability insurance, benefits, and payroll matters.
A clear view of your inquiry status.
When you call, a OneSource s ff member will open a ticket to start the process. You will receive email notifications prompting you to take action when additional documentation (e.g., a doctor s note) is required to complete your request.
The focus is on you the customer!
We will check in with you periodically to see how we re doing and to ensure we're providing best-in-class service.
How do you contact OneSource?
Phone
Call (732) 745-SERV (7378) to speak with a customer service representative Monday through Friday from 8:00 AM to 5:00 PM.
OneSource Self-Service Portal
Access the OneSource self-service portal to initiate a request or read articles on human resources and payroll topics.
Check out the OneSource Portal navigation video under the Resources title.
OneSource Website
Browse the OneSource website for updates on the service center, videos, and leadership messages.

" KB0014281 OR OR - Emeritus and Retiree roles "

These roles were created to provide more granularity on the roles that Rutgers-affiliated people have and to better define the services available for these groups of people. Information about these roles in relation to their email accounts can be found in the Rutgers Connect Emeritus and Retirees knowledge article.

If any new retirees who are not in the HR system request services, they will need to contact their retiree relations representative based on the information on the Rutgers Retiree Information page. Their retiree relations contact should assist them by entering their information directly into HR's systems.

For more information on benefits, visit UHR's page for Retiree Benefits.

If a user does not already have an EMERITUS or RETIREE role but they would like to be granted one, then they should contact the HR of their department in order to receive the role. Information about the various HR departments can be found in the Find Your HR office website.

" KB0014283 OR OR - Information Changes "

Individuals can modify some of their own Student and University Employee information in the Online Directory. To do this they should:

Go to the Personal Info page and log in with their NetID and password.
Use the appropriate tab to modify the information.
Click the Save/Verify button to save any changes.

Different types of people will have different types of access.

Faculty/Staff

There are various ways for Faculty/Staff to change their name depending on the purpose.

If the user wants to change their directory listing on Rutgers People Search they can log into the Personal Info page. This will allow them to create a preferred name in the online directory, but their official name will still also be listed. This official name comes from HR.

If the user wants to update their name, date of birth, and job title with University Human Resources, it is best to suggest that they call the OneSource Rutgers Faculty and Staff Service Center at (732)-745-SERV (7378) or visit the HR Service Portal.

If they want to change their name on beneficiary forms, pension/retirement plans, or health benefit plans, follow these steps:

Log in to to MyRutgers.
On the Dashboard, users should see an icon for "Name Change" under their profile picture.
There are options for changing one's Chosen name or Legal name, and both will direct users to separate pages on the UHR website.
Selecting Faculty/Staff - Change your legal name will direct users to the UHR page with instructions to submit a form to change their names on documents like pension plans, health benefit plans, and beneficiary forms.
Students

Students can update their information fully using the Personal Info page. There are several tabs at the top of the page listing info to be updated.

Users with dual roles

Users with both Student and Faculty or Staff roles will be prompted when going to personalinfo.rutgers.edu to choose which role they want to edit.

People without a NetID

These users would have to first ensure they have an active role. Once they have an active role, they should be able to activate their NetID. Part of the process offers the users the option to create or update their e-mail address in their Directory entries. After their accounts are active, they should be able to use the normal procedure.

New Students

Students with only an Admit Coming role cannot use the online form until they gain a current Student role.

New Employees

People who have submitted their New Hire form to create their NetIDs will not be able to use the online form until Payroll grants them their Faculty or Staff roles.

Guests

Help Desk and Labs FT can update guest information via OR.

RBHS guests should contact their patron to make desired changes. To determine an RBHS guest's patron, locate the guest in OR, in the Source: or-webapp area, click on audit > select Audit Roles > View Audit. Look at the first line of the results and the User listed in red is the NetID of the person that entered this guest's information. For RBHS people ONLY this person is their patron. This can then be used to give the name of the patron to the guest. If the guest needs contact information for their patron, give out ONLY the information that is in the public directory for that person.

If RBHS guests do not know their patron it is ok for them to reach out to their sponsor to have the change made. If it is deemed easier or if there are time constraints, we can contact the patron on behalf of the guest to request the change.

Type 6 employees

Type 6 employees do not have access to this tool. To update their information, the HD Consultant should direct the user to OneSource.

Alumni and former University employees

Alumni and former employees may still have active accounts but would not be able to make directory changes as they would not have current Student, Faculty, Staff, or Guest roles. As such, their entries would not turn up in ROD searches.

" KB0014284 OR OR - OpenRegistry "

OpenRegistry (OR) is a central directory listing faculty, staff, and students at Rutgers University. It lists personal identifying information for users, provided alternate contact information, along with roles (for example, Staff, Guest, Student, Alumni, etc.). Not all information in the OR is made public. Therefore, information in OR should not be given out, but users may view some of the information contained within using the Rutgers Online Directory (ROD), available through search.rutgers.edu. This information is limited to name, office number, office location, department, and official Rutgers email address. However, not all information will be available for all users. This tool is utilized to copy roles & dates and paste them into work notes for an incident.

Layout of OR
Under Manage People on the left-hand side, select View Person.
Under Find Person, there is a selection of Identifier Types, the Identifier field, and the Name field.
Identifier Types in the dropdown menu include NETID, SSN, RUID, RCPID, IID, EXTERNALID, EMPLID, RCN, ANUMBER, PIDM.
You can provide either an identifier or one or more search criteria such as name, but not both.

Ex. Putting the Identifier Type = NetID, Identifier = "ond3", and Name = "Olivia" will display an error.
You can select Show Advanced Search Options to search with role & department name.

Search Results
Search results will show Name, NetID, IID, RCPID, RUID, EMPLID, ANUMBER (if applicable), Birth Date, and Roles respectively.
Click on the hyperlinked name for personal details.

ServiceNow
Copy PHI flag (Y or N), roles and dates and paste them into ServiceNow Work Notes in your incident.
DO NOT COPY:

Social Security Number (SSN)
Notes on Services, Roles, and Requests
Services that go inactive with an inactive role: ScarletMail, Connect, Employee Self Service.
Guest Role: affiliated with the university but not paid by the university or not enrolled as a student.
Sponsor Request FAQ
The retiree role is NOT a guest role.
If something is incorrect on OR, review the Identity Mismatch Article.

" KB0014329 OR OR - PHI - Restrictions "

There is an LDAP flag indicating whether a user has access to Protected Health Information (PHI). Under HIPAA regulations, there are very strict guidelines on how PHI can be stored or sent via email. As such, users with access to PHI have encrypted email accounts and are subject to restrictions on which email accounts they are eligible to activate and on the email configurations they can set.

You can confirm if an individual has a PHI flag through the NetID Management lookup tool or Open Registry, but all students, staff, faculty, and guests associated with RBHS should be listed with a PHI flag.

Users with PHI flags are subject to the following restrictions:

Users are ONLY allowed to set a delivery address that is a Connect email account (which is all encrypted)
Users are ONLY allowed to use their alias or a Connect email account as an official address
Users are not permitted to forward their Connect email to another account

If a user states they are no longer affiliated with RBHS or do not have access to PHI, we may be able to override the PHI flag and make specific changes on their behalf. If a user claims they do not have access to PHI and need to make a change that is restricted for PHI=Y users, confirm their current affiliation with RBHS/Rutgers and the exact change they are trying to make, and speak with full-time staff.

" KB0014286 OR OR - Rutgers Online Directory FAQs "

Some Frequently Asked Questions about Rutgers Online Directory entries (from the people who maintain it):

Can HD staff give out NetIDs?
Generally, no. For specific guidance, see Policies and Procedures - Giving out NetIDs
Why can't I find myself (or my friend) on the Rutgers Online Directory?
Current Students, Faculty/Staff, and Guests are displayed on the public Directory.
Students: Students can hide all or part of their information displayed in the Rutgers Online Directory by accessing the Personal Info page.
Faculty/Staff: It may take a few weeks for new hires to appear in the system until they are entered into the payroll system.
Guest: All information is displayed immediately when entered by an Administrator.
How can I hide or update my personal information on the Directory?
Students can use the Personal Info site to:
Hide or update their Home Address, Phone Number, Email, or URL.
Hide Academic Major, College or Year of Graduation.
Hide their Entire Records.
Note: Students can only hide their information if they have an active student role, which requires registration.
Faculty/Staff can use the Personal Info site to:
Update their email, home address, and Phone numbers.
Local Rutgers Address/Phone number cannot be hidden, and can be changed through a user's departmental HR.
I am unable to create an accounting web application.
The information used to verify what you enter in the web application comes from the Open Registry (OR). You must have an active role in the OR and match on name, SSN, and date of birth. The Help Desk can view data in the OR using our OR view and verify it with a user.
There are 2 entries for me in the Directory. How can this be corrected?
This usually happens because the source systems have had the name and/or date of birth entered differently. The Help Desk can view data in the OR using our view to OR and verify it with a user to confirm that both records are for the same person. If they are, they should submit a ticket with as much detail as is available.
My name is incorrect. How can I have this corrected?
Corrections need to be done at the data source office. Have the student, faculty member, or staff member contact their local One Stop office.
Faculty and Staff:
Have the user reach out to their department's HR for further assistance with updating the info.
Contact OneSource at 732-745-7378 or via the HR self-service portal for further assistance.
How can I correct my job title or university office information?
Faculty and Staff - Users should contact their departmental HR or OneSource at 732-745-7378 or via the HR self-service portal for further assistance.
How do I contact my Campus Registrar's office?

New Brunswick One Stop - 848-445-4636
Newark One Stop - myRUN Supportal
Camden Registrar, Phone: 856-225-6053
I am receiving this message when using the Rutgers Online Directory: "You are no longer able to access the Rutgers Online Directory."
Queries to ROD are limited by IP address due to screen scraping used for spamming. Send a query to the Help Desk for your campus requesting the ACS-PDB queue with the IP address so ESS can unblock this address.

" KB0018780 Policies and Procedures Labs - Pitch Room TV Instructions "

Pitch Room TV

Patrons can screen share from their devices to the Pitch Room TV using Mersive Solstice or Apple AirPlay. The TV can be powered on using the remote, which should always remain within the Pitch Room.

If the TV is reset, the source will need to be changed to display the Mersive Solstice page. On the remote, select the "Source" button and then navigate to HDMI1 to return the TV to the Mersive Solstice page.



" KB0018779 Policies and Procedures Labs - TV Screen Share Guide "

Lab TVs

There are TVs available for patrons to use within the computer labs. Patrons can connect their devices and share their screen with the TV. They may connect using Apple AirPlay or the Mersive Solstice application.

AirPlay

To assist users with connecting to the TV with Apple AirPlay, utilize the following steps:

First, ensure the device is connected to RUWireless Secure. Then, select the AirPlay button on the device and choose the name of your TV from the list of options.





Enter the code displayed on the TV screen on the device when prompted.





Mersive Solstice

Mersive Solstice is an application that can be downloaded onto the user's device, allowing them to screen share with the TV. To assist users with connecting to the TV using Mersive Solstice, utilize the following steps:

First, ensure the device is connected to RUWireless Secure. Then, navigate to https:// [The IP address listed on the TV screen] and download the Mersive Solstice application on your device or search for the app in your device's application store



 



Open the Mersive Solstice app, and enter the IP address in the search bar at the top of your screen.





Finally, enter the code that is displayed on the TV, and the patron's device will be connected.

" KB0014432 Policies and Procedures Policies and Procedures - DocuSign (Walk-in) "

DocuSign is a cloud-based document signing system licensed by Rutgers University. At the labs & Help Desk, DocuSign Power Forms are utilized in cases where a client has to sign a document authorizing the staff to perform a task, or to acknowledge a procedure (such as the Software Remediation and Router forms).

Software Remediation and Troubleshooting

Router Use Agreement

How Do I Use DocuSign?

Location: Links to the forms are located on the front station computer's homepage and in the bookmarks, in addition to being listed above.

On the DocuSign Power Form page, the user will need to enter their name and email address to proceed with the signing process.

Check off I agree to use electronic records and signatures and select Continue.

Scroll down on the document to the Signature Section.

Confirm the name, initials, and signature by selecting Adopt and Sign (Client can draw or upload their signature).

Select Finish to complete the signing process.

The user will receive a copy of the form to the email address they entered after they have completed their portion.

Once the user has selected finished and submitted the form, you can go to the ECS DocuSign Intermediary shared folder within Connect (sc_docusign@oit.rutgers.edu).

See How to view a shared mail folder if you need to add this folder.

All new forms will be in the Inbox folder, with the subject form name - user's name to allow you to find the relevant form.

Select Review document to complete your part of the signing process, where you will be required to enter additional information (i.e. MAC addresses, user's NetID, your NetID, and ticket number).

Once you have completed the signing process, please drag the email to the Completed folder.

" KB0019426 Policies and Procedures Policies and Procedures - Help Desk Mentor Team Games "

The Help Desk Mentor Team Games run each semester from the first day of classes through the last day of finals.

The current status of the games can be viewed on the Merit Board or the TV located at the back of the Help Desk.

How Merit Scores are calculated

Scores are based on your Rating times the Merits you earned during the current semester.

Example: You have a Rating of 90 and have earned 30 Merits this semester. Your score is 27 (0.9 x 30 = 27).

Notes:

The Rating and Merits used to calculate scores are only those that are finalized. If you have any pending Infractions or Merits, they will not be reflected in your Score. Follow up with your Mento Team to finalize any outstanding items to ensure your score is up to date.
The Merit Board is updated manually by Supervisors. If the "Last Updated" time noted on the Merit Board is old, you can ask a Supervisor to run the numbers and update it.
How Team Scores are calculated

Since the number of people on a Mentor Team can vary across Teams, the Team Score is the average per person for that Team.

Example: The total of all individual Scores on an 8-person Mentor Team is 600. The Team Score is 75 (600 / 8 = 75).

Ways to increase your score
Earn Merits! Aim for high-value Merits, like emergency coverages or volunteering for tasks and projects.
Keep your Rating up! Infractions will lower your Rating and, by extension, your score, so ensure you are always putting your best foot forward on shift.
Ensure you're getting credit for your hard work! Follow up with a Supervisor to ensure they enter your Merits in PTS when you complete something on the Bounty Board.

" KB0014891 Policies and Procedures Policies and Procedures - Phone Call Scripts "

Phone Greeting
REMEMBER!.. Always ask for personal information, never hand it out!

"Thank you for calling the OIT Help Desk. My name is [name]. May I please have the spelling of your NetID?"

"May I have a phone number where you can be reached in case we get disconnected?"

"Thank you, and may I have a current email address where we can send your ticket confirmation to?"

"Have you called about this issue before, and if so, do you already have a ticket number?"

Hold Reminders

Start writing your ticket during the call, and use Slack to send ticket numbers to supervisors before asking users to hold!

When asking users to hold

Could I ask you to hold for about two minutes?

If more time is needed after 2 minutes, ask

We are still looking into your issue. Would you like to continue to hold, or would you prefer a callback?

If the user would prefer to stay on hold:

Thank you, I ll check back with you within 5 minutes.

At the end of the call

If you need to call back about this issue, your Ticket number in our system is [ticket number]. You should be getting an email that includes your ticket number and a feedback survey.

Voicemail Script

"Hello, this call is for [user's name]. My name is [your name] from the OIT Help Desk.

You previously contacted us on [date] regarding issues with [problem]."

[Mention further instructions here if needed]

"If you are still facing issues, please call us back at 833-648-4357 and reference your ticket number [ticket number].

We are open today until [closing time]. If we do not hear back from you within 7 days, we will assume the issue is resolved.

Thank you for contacting us. Have a nice [day/night]."

*If we want the user to call back, then only give them the Help Desk number.

" KB0016743 Policies and Procedures Policies and Procedures - PTS Overview "

Table of Contents
What is PTS?
What are Incident Reports?
How is one's Performance Rating recalculated?
Types of Merits
50 Merit Incidents
30 Merit Incidents
10 to 30 Merit Incidents
3 Merit Incidents
Calculation of Coverage Merits
Types of Infractions
Minor Infractions - (-10) Merits
Moderate Infractions - (-30) Merits
Major Infractions - (-50) Merits
Egregious Behavior
PTS Terms and the Calculation of Values

What is PTS?

PTS is short for Performance Tracking System, a system used by consultants and supervisors that allows supervisors to monitor consultants' positive and negative behavior as merits and infractions, respectively.

What are Incident Reports?

When a supervisor enters an entry into the Performance Tracking database, a report will be generated. This report will show the relevant incident data. Consultants must meet within 7 days, regardless of extenuating circumstances, to discuss any infractions; otherwise, it will be finalized as-is. Failure to cooperate with the resolution of a Performance Tracking report will result in the given Performance Rating reduction being applied, with the possibility of additional disciplinary action.

How is one's Performance Rating recalculated?

Recalculation only occurs on the specified dates at the end of each semester. Merits are applied to your Performance Rating until it reaches 100% or no more merits remain. All remaining merits will be held until the next recalculation. CITs not promoted in the Fall will be recalculated if promoted in the Spring. Consultants on leave will be recalculated if their leave crosses a recalculation date. The recalculation dates are as follows:

Summer Date: 8/21/2025

Fall Date: 1/5/2026

Spring Date: 5/19/2026

Types of Merits
50 Merit Incidents
Consultant of the Semester Award
Permanent assignment for a hard-to-fill shift
30 Merit Incidents
Consultant of the Month Award
Promotion
10 to 30 Merit Incidents
Volunteering for tasks or projects
Special Projects
Exceptional handling of a situation
Exceptional service
Exceptional attitude or work ethic
Emergency Coverage (Please see Calculation of Coverage Merits for more information)
Exceptional Performance during training
3 Merit Incidents
Completing extra work at management discretion
Exceptional service based on customer feedback
Taking Coverage
Volunteering to staff the Help Desk front station
Arriving early (15 minutes or more) to a Help Desk shift
Calculation of Coverage Merits
Taking Coverage: 3 merits - Any coverage for another consultant
Emergency Coverage Low: 10 merits - When a consultant stays after their shift for 10-20 mins or until their relief arrives late, or when covering a day in advance (if applicable)
Emergency Coverage Medium: 20 merits - Coverage for the next day (non-opening shifts)
Emergency Coverage High: 30 merits - Coverage for the day of (even if it s partial) - Coverage for the opening shift the next morning
Types of Infractions
Minor Infractions - (-10) Merits
Lateness under 15 minutes
Failure to follow coverage procedures
Failure to sign in/out properly
Inattention to responsibilities and/or users
Poor work ethic or attitude (Minor)
Poor communication
Failure to use Slack as intended
Failure to wear a badge or display a nameboard
Minor substandard ticket writing
Failure to perform or record lab rounds
Failure to submit shift reports
Failure to clean according to schedule
Failure to take stats properly
Failure to set up/shut down IML on time for class
*Failure to comply with the max work hours policy
*Lateness of 15 minutes or more, with notice
*Lateness to staff meeting/training
*Failure to follow shift turn-over protocol - leaving without relief/unauthorized breaks
*Minor failure to enforce/follow procedure or rules
*Minor failure to cooperate with management and coworkers
*Failure to address or escalate a problem correctly
*Failure to read/respond to work email promptly
*Failure to complete the assigned task/project by the deadline
*Misuse of mailing lists
*Failure to follow uniform policy (Minor)
* First instance during a semester is noted as a warning
Moderate Infractions - (-30) Merits
Missed training/staff meeting without notice
Shift lateness of 15 mins or more, without notice
Lateness of 1 hour or more
Poor customer service
Poor work ethic or attitude (Moderate)
Failure to follow uniform policy (Major)
Failure to enforce/follow procedures or rules
Failure to cooperate with management and coworkers
Substandard ticket writing
Failure to report to a dorm visit promptly
Failure to follow monitoring procedures
Failure to complete machine room rounds/console checks
Major Infractions - (-50) Merits
Missed shift/mandatory training
Egregious Behavior

The following are examples of infractions that may result in disciplinary action, including a reduction in your Performance Rating, probation, suspension, or termination. This list is by no means exhaustive, and full-time managers can terminate the employment of any OIT employee at any time for egregious behavior, regardless of that employee's Performance Rating/probation status. Minimal disciplinary action for egregious behavior will be a 50 Merit Performance Rating reduction. Some incidents in this category may also require legal action or penalties under university policy. Any incidence of egregious behavior, including violations of the Acceptable Use Policy, University Harassment Policy, and University Code of Conduct, can affect an employee's standing at OIT, even if the violation occurred outside of work. Terminated consultants are not eligible for rehire by OIT.

Abuse/Unapproved use of staff accounts, privileges, resources, or equipment
Abuse of staff telephones (liable for all unauthorized toll charges)
Abuse of staff/user personal information
Compromising equipment warranty/damaging equipment
Excessive bandwidth usage on the staff machine
Failure to meet professional expectations
Falsifying official records
Going on probation multiple times
Insubordination
Mistreatment of others
Sleeping while on duty
Theft
Unapproved use of OIT facilities
Unauthorized work without a uniform
Violation of the Acceptable Use Policy
Violation of the University Harassment Policy
Working under the influence of illegal substances or alcohol
PTS Terms and the Calculation of Values

Performance Rating: This indicates your current status. As infractions occur, your Performance Rating will decrease by the percentage points indicated for the infraction. At 0%, you are on probation.

Merits: Merits may be earned for activities exceeding the expected level of Performance. These merits will accumulate until a recalculation is reached. The accumulated merits will be applied to your Performance Rating until the rating reaches 100% or all merits are exhausted. Any remaining merits will be held until the next recalculation.

Excused: Circumstances surrounding this infraction do not lead to a reduction.

Tracking: Used to monitor patterns of behavior. Does not result in a decreased performance rating per incident. Does not affect perfect Performance.

Warning: Used to record the first instance during a semester of certain infractions. This does affect perfect Performance.

Breaks: Any extended period of time you are not scheduled to work, including Winter Break, Summer Break, Spring Break, and other approved leaves of absence.

Probation: Occurs when the Performance Rating reaches 0%. Any more than 1 minor infraction or any major infraction while on probation will result in automatic termination. The consultant will be ineligible for the annual raise in the year they were placed on probation. Probation lasts for 60 days, excluding breaks. The consultant will be ineligible for any promotional opportunities and certifications during the probationary period. Upon completion of probation, the performance rating rises to 30%. If probation falls within a recalculation period, the recalculation will be done after probation.

Termination: Loss of all shifts with all groups. No longer eligible for rehire by any group within OIT.



" KB0019149 Policies and Procedures Policies and Procedures - Research Guidelines "

Step 1: Collect information and identify user issue

Collect all relevant user information and input it into the Service-Now ticket.

Identify the user issue and specify what they are seeking assistance with in work notes.

Always provide a detailed explanation for what the user is asking for and ask for clarification if necessary.

Check the user's related tickets to see if the issue is ongoing or if they already have an open incident. To do this: In the Service Operations Workspace in ServiceNow, click the "i" icon to the right of the user's name in the Subject Person field. Then, in the new tab that opens, click on Related Records > Incidents.

Step 2: Check Knowledge Articles

Search the Knowledge Article database for solutions to frequently asked questions and step-by-step guides.

Step 3: Search ServiceNow for related tickets

Search Service-Now using keywords to find related tickets and check the steps that were taken to resolve the issue.

Step 4: Search Slack for related tickets

Search Slack using keywords to find related tickets and review the steps taken to resolve the issue.

Step 5: Use online research

Use online research through search engines, such as Google, to explore potential solutions for more general questions.

Step 6: Ask Level 2/Assistant Supervisor

If available, ask peers for guidance.

Ask in #esd_nb_hd_all for further assistance.

Step 7: Ask CSq

If all other steps did not help to resolve the issue, ask FT/L3 in CSq for assistance after posting the ticket in #esd_nb_hd_all and including all relevant information in the incident.

" KB0015945 Policies and Procedures Policies and Procedures - Resources to Handle a Difficult User "

Have the Right Mental Attitude

The user is probably not mad at you specifically. They are more likely frustrated by the situation as a whole. By putting yourself in the right mental state, you can stay calm and professional when responding to the user.

Develop Thick Skin

Along with having the right mental attitude, developing thick skin can prevent the user from pushing your buttons and riling you up. Let go of your fear. By viewing demanding customers as a new challenge rather than a condemnation.

Empathize with the User

People who are upset need to be heard, so let your customers talk, and don t interrupt them.

Things to keep in mind
Practice active listening

It is essential to engage in active listening, which is the practice of consciously assimilating what the other party has to say instead of just standing silently in front of them.
If the user is in person, active listening also entails listening with your whole body. This means using positive body language, such as having an open stance and nodding, to show the other person you re listening.
Make them feel that they re taken seriously.
Make sure that the user knows that you are taking their concern seriously. This can be done by maintaining eye contact and exhibiting the right non-verbal behaviors (like not smiling, excessively nodding, or rolling your eyes). In addition, refer to your customer by name, as it makes people feel they re being heard and could help calm them down.
Respond with empathy
Instead of responding defensively, respond with empathy. Saying things like;

I can see why you re disappointed
Oh dear, that s not what you would have expected
I can understand why you re upset can show the customer that you understand where they are coming from.
Explain Yourself Clearly

Once you have gathered your information, explaining the problem and solution as clearly as possible is crucial. This will help you avoid a customer asking you to repeat yourself, saving valuable time.

Here are suggestions on how to convey information as comprehensively as possible:

Speak at an even pace, and don t hurry.
Use common terms that the customer will understand and avoid technical jargon.
Pause between sentences.
Practice reciting policy procedures during your downtime to relay them flawlessly.
Reduce Hold Times

Lengthy hold times can dramatically increase handle times and leave the user more time to think of more things to discuss. Thus, shortening hold times can go a long way to reducing chit-chat with users.

Be Mindful of Verbal and non-Verbal Cues.

The things you say and don t say can significantly affect the outcome of any customer interaction. Signs of boredom, impatience, or aggression will only escalate the situation. So, be very mindful of your words and the body language you project.

Verbal Cues:
I apologize. I didn t hear/understand what you said.
Will you? rather than You will.
Yes, rather than Yeah.
Sir.
Ma am.
I ll check and be right back.
Will you hold for a moment while I check on that?
Thanks for waiting.
Mr./Mrs./Ms. _____. (Address by the first name only if you know what s appropriate)
Non-Verbal Cues:

Body language is an important tool that can give the right or wrong signals depending on your appearance. Good non-verbal cues can be:

Nodding
Eye contact
Note taking

Make sure to avoid queues such as;

Closed fists
Folded arms
Eye rolling

These can aggravate the user.

Ask Direct Questions

When you have a talkative user on the line, don t ask open-ended questions that require lengthy answers unless necessary. This will open up the door for them to slip into unrelated topics. Asking direct questions that need yes or no answers will keep the conversation straight and to the point and significantly reduce tangential discussions.

Communicate What You Can and Can t do About Their Situation

Instead of saying things like there is nothing I can do, say what you can and cannot assist them with.

Being upfront with the user is also recommended.

Addressing a user s concerns quickly may turn their negative experience into a positive one.

Act Quickly

If you can resolve the customer s problem immediately, then, by all means, do so. However, the longer an issue takes to resolve, the more frustrated the user can get, resulting in more aggravation.

Resources to Use
LinkedIn - How to deal with Difficult Customers.
LinkedIn Learning - Customer Service: Working in a Customer Contact Center.
Talk Desk - How to Handle a Talkative Customer on the Phone.

" KB0014316 Policies and Procedures Policies and Procedures - Sensitive information and media/press inquiries "

As a member of OIT you are trusted with information and internal communications (especially concerning policy and procedures) that is NOT meant to be shared freely. Do not divulge any potentially sensitive, confidential, or internal OIT information that is not already known to the user, either voluntarily or if requested. If you notice a policy or procedure that was not properly followed by staff, or notice a circumstance that could cause a perception of liability for OIT, do not pass this information along to the user and instead escalate it internally as appropriate. If you are unsure of what can and can not be provided to a user, refer to FT for guidance.

As a staff member, you are not permitted to represent or speak on behalf of OIT on social media or any other website or forum.

If contacted by Media (Targum or other news outlets) about OIT issues, state that you are not authorized to speak on this matter. Take down contact information for this person and the topic they are asking about. Pass this information onto the full time staff by sending an email to hd_mgr@oit.rutgers.edu and copying itcomm@rutgers.edu with the details of the request.

Know that OIT crafts public messages very delicately, as we want to ensure information is being presented correctly and tactfully. If you see any OIT related conversations in social media getting heated, bring the matter to FT staff's attention rather than getting involved. Engaging in the conversation to attempt to clarify policy, or even to direct users to publicly posted policies, puts you at risk for misrepresenting OIT.

Getting Started (Before Shift)

Before starting your shift, please be mindful of the space around you. It is important you are working in a quiet private space, as you will be accessing confidential information.

Slack Channel

Slack will be used to communicate with your colleagues and supervisors about any issues or questions you may have while on shift. Log in to Slack at https://rutgers.slack.com/ with your netid@rutgers.edu.

You will need to download the VPN client in order to access and utilize certain sites and tools.

If you have activated the VPN service and have downloaded the client, please skip to the Setting Up Voicemail section.

To get started, go to https://it.rutgers.edu/virtual-private-network/.
If you have not done so already, you will need to Activate VPN through NetID Service Activation before downloading the client.
After activating the VPN platform service, go to the General VPN Connectivity Details and Instructions for installation.

For further information and guidance on the VPN, please refer to the Virtual Private Network page.

Be sure to sign into the VPN before you sign in to your shifts on Zed, your sign-in will be flagged.

Setting Up Voicemail

The phone system should not send calls to your personal voicemail. If the call goes to voicemail, ensure your voicemail greeting is professional.

Although your voicemail will be set up, we prefer that calls not go to voicemail. Please read below for additional information.

Whitelisting the OIT Help Desk number

When using your personal phone with Five9, incoming calls are routed to you via the main OIT Help Desk number. iPhones and Androids have settings that either silence or "auto screen" calls from an unknown number. To keep users from getting an automated screening prompt when they are rerouted to your phone, ensure to add the OIT Help Desk number as a contact on your phone:

848-445-4357

Missed Calls and Voicemail

While working remotely, it is essential to be attentive to phone calls. Unfortunately, missed calls may result in a voicemail that you can only access when using your personal phone number to take incoming calls.

If you are stepping away from your duties for any reason, please keep the following in mind:

Make sure to communicate your being away to supervisors and FT in the esd_nb_hd_all Slack channel.
Set the appropriate Five9 status to ensure you do not receive calls while you are away
Do not decline calls. Declining calls will result in a voicemail. If you are finishing up your shift and receive a last-minute call, accept the call and transfer to the next available consultant.
Declining/avoiding/refusing/disconnecting calls will result in probation/suspension/termination of your position

If you miss a call or receive a voicemail, please notify the on-duty supervisors on Slack. You should then proceed to review the voicemail and reach out to the caller. As stated earlier, please ensure your voicemail is set up and professional.

Faculty/ Staff Help Desk

During this time, Faculty/Staff Help Desk will be open and operating their normal business hours M-F 7:30 AM - 5:00 PM.

If the Faculty/Staff Help Desk experiences an overflow of calls after a 2-minute hold time, callers will be given the option to speak with a student consultant, who may direct them to a Level 1 consultant for further assistance. Upon receiving the call, you should hear, Faculty Staff Overflow. If a call cannot be handled by the Student Help Desk, it can be redirected to the FSHD using the "Faculty Staff Overflow Return" option. This option allows you to redirect the call back to FSHD without placing it at the back of the call queue. This should *ONLY* be used to return those calls and should *NOT* be used to transfer calls that reached the student help desk in another way. NetID /Password
Scarletmail
WebEx
Connect

" KB0014384 Policies and Procedures Policies and Procedures - Slack "

Slack is a messaging tool used by OIT staff.

Channels

Please read the descriptions below to understand the purpose of some channels.

ESD_General: This channel is for team-wide communication.

ESD_Outage_Degradation: Announce/discuss service outages and degradations that can potentially impact users. Only FT and Sups should post in this channel.

ESD_NB_HD_All: Designated for all NB Help Desk/Labs staff for general questions when assisting users. Use @here when posting in this channel, and always include the Incident number.

ESD_NB_HD_Email: Designated for all NB Help Desk/Labs staff for questions regarding incidents in the email queue. Use @here when posting in this channel and always include the Incident number.

ESD_Random: Casual, non-work-related conversation.

ESD_NB_HD_Food: Casual food-related conversation.

ESD_NB_HD_News: Used for announcements, reminders, or general FYI. Only FT should post in this channel.

Using Slack

Please log in to Slack at the start of every shift. The desktop version of Slack has been installed on all Help Desk computers. If you are experiencing issues with the desktop version of Slack, you can also access Slack on a browser at https://rutgers.slack.com/. Slack uses CAS, so please log in with your NetID and password.

Please note that our team's Slack URL is https://rutgers.slack.com/. You will need that information to log in to Slack.

If you are using Slack in a browser, you will need to enable Slack notifications.

Please be sure to check the Outage Degradation channel and review the NB HD All and General channels during your shifts.

Do not use @channel when posting.

While on shift, please update your Profile to include our location (Help Desk, Application Support, ARC, Loree, AB, DLC, Tillet, etc.) This can be done by selecting your icon in the top-right corner, then editing your Profile > Profile > School/Department/Unit.

Outage Degradation

As a Consultant, please do not post in ESD_Outage_Degradation for general banter or troubleshooting issues.

If you have additional information to provide about a known outage, please reply to this thread.

If you have information regarding an unreported outage while on shift, please speak with a Supervisor.

Enabling Slack notifications on the browser
On your desktop, click your profile picture in the top-right corner.
Select Preferences > Notification.
Under Notify me about... , choose your notification riggers. It is recommended to choose Direct
messages, mentions & keywords for getting all important updates.
To use different triggers for your mobile notifications, check the box next to Use different settings for
my mobile devices, then select your preference from the drop-down menu.
To allow Windows for notifications, please allow Slack permission to enable desktop notifications by accepting the notification alert at the bottom of the screen.

Back to top



As a student IT worker, you hold a unique position at the intersection of technology and academia. You are the go-to person for your peers and faculty members when it comes to solving technical issues, providing support, and maintaining a reliable computing environment. This manual is designed to equip you with the knowledge and skills necessary to excel in your role and make a meaningful impact on campus

Policies And Customer Service
Mission, Vision and Values Statement
Mission
The mission of The Office of Information Technology (OIT) is to provide university-wide leadership in information technology and quality information technology services in support of Rutgers University's mission of instruction, research, and service.
Vision
We will be a role model within the higher education community for customer satisfaction and service excellence using innovative strategies and advanced technologies, through a unified multi-faceted approach.
Values
We believe that the organization's greatest asset is its staff. We encourage and support professional development activities that meet the goals of the organization. We embrace a set of values, take personal responsibility, and are accountable for our daily actions.
Teamwork
We extend cooperation and support to our colleagues and customers throughout the Rutgers University community. We hold team goals paramount while encouraging and recognizing both individual and team achievements.
Innovation
We embrace creative solutions, different perspectives, and new opportunities. We provide leadership for the university in introducing and applying information technology.
Professionalism
We are knowledgeable in state-of-the-art information technology and apply our knowledge in accordance with the best professional practices. We empower each other and use good judgment in performing our duties and activities. We value the benefits of diversity and treat all individuals with respect.
Integrity
We are trustworthy and honest in all of our actions. We adhere to a high standard of ethics in our professional relationships.
Initiative
We identify opportunities and promote appropriate uses of information technology. We proactively take steps to increase our customers' awareness of information technology as a means to achieve their objectives.
Code of Conduct

OIT consultants' responsibilities are to provide aid to the Rutgers patron community: the students, faculty, and staff who are affiliated with Rutgers University. The Consultant Code of Conduct, which is stated below, guides consultants on providing this service: Consultants are OIT s most visible representatives. The way in which they conduct themselves in dealing with students, faculty, and fellow consultants is just as important as the support they provide. The images that they are expected to project are those of helpful, courteous, and technically competent individuals. While on duty, they have the authority and responsibility to operate their sites in an equitable, impartial, and orderly manner. They are further entrusted with the proper operation and security of the resources available to them at their sites. Finally, they are expected to answer patron questions to the best of their ability. Every consultant who is employed by OIT must follow the Code of Conduct.

Those who cannot fulfill the responsibilities expected of them are subject to disciplinary action. Consultants are required to wear their Rutgers, Office of Information Technology polo shirts during every shift. Consultants will not be permitted to work a shift without their provided work attire. Failure to comply will subject the consultant to disciplinary action.

Customer Service

OIT consultants should be polite and understanding when answering questions. Everyone has been in a position in which they were not quite sure of something and were afraid to ask questions. Consultants should consider how patrons feel and try to understand that not everyone is a computer expert. The following strategies may be helpful in providing the exemplary level of customer service that consultants are expected to offer:

Consultants should listen carefully to patrons' questions. Listening passively is not sufficient; consultants must actively listen. They must be sure to understand the problems patrons present to them in order to provide the correct solutions.
Consultants can begin to express this understanding simply by repeating questions back to patrons using their own words.
Consultants should give patrons their undivided attention. Answering patron questions is their number one priority and they should not allow themselves to become distracted.
At times, patrons may ask questions that do not address the actual problems that are occurring. It may be necessary to interpret a patron s concern by asking pointed questions or by walking to their machines to discern these problems.
It is very important for consultants to be friendly and polite to all patrons. They will hear many questions being asked more than once. Although these questions may become redundant, consultants are expected to remain courteous.
When redirecting questions to other staff members, consultants should introduce patrons to the new staff members and assure them that their problems will be resolved (e.g., This is Erica. She will assist you with this problem. ). If problems must be handled by other departments, consultants can make referrals or escalate tickets through ServiceNow. They must be certain that the problems can be resolved by these departments and must notify the departments of patron referrals. Inappropriate referrals constitute extremely poor customer service and must be carefully avoided. If one is unsure, consult with a Supervisor.

Consultants are expected to answer patrons questions thoroughly. Consultants are discouraged from spending excessive amounts of time with patrons, particularly when others are waiting for assistance. No patron deserves to be neglected. During off-peak hours, consultants are permitted and encouraged to take more time to answer patrons questions.

In the computer labs, patrons may ask consultants to do their homework for them. Consultants are not permitted to do other people s work, but should refer patrons to their instructors for assistance.

Work Hours & Scheduling

Each semester is comprised of three working periods:

Temporary Schedule

At the beginning of the semester, consultants choose shifts that they can work during this time. Different campuses have shift restrictions due to the demand of shifts. Check with your desired campus on the restrictions. Also, during this time, consultants have to request their permanent schedules.

Consultants are required to work minimum weekly hours during the temp period each semester. If unable to meet the minimum, consultants are required to contact management. If consultants do not meet minimum hourly requirements and do not reach out to management, they risk not being placed on the permanent schedule.

Permanent Schedule

The permanent schedule runs from the end of the temporary schedule to the first reading day before finals. These shifts are requested using the online shift requester during the temporary schedule.

Consultants are advised to request 3 times as many hours as they prefer, but not required to do so. If they submit minimal requested hours, they risk not being placed on permanent schedule. Once a consultant is placed on a permanent schedule, ownership of the shift(s) becomes theirs, and are expected to follow all coverage protocols. If a consultant places the same permanent shift up for coverage more than 3 times in a row, they will be contacted and may be dropped from the shift

There are two instances when consultants are allowed to drop permanent shifts:

During the announced add/drop period. This is during the last week of the temporary schedule. If you were assigned a shift that you can no longer work, you may drop the shift using Zed during the Add/Drop period. This is the only time shifts may be dropped without consequence. You will receive a confirmation email stating that your shift has been successfully dropped. Once you drop a shift, it is final. You will have to request it again if you want it back. Open shifts on the permanent schedule will be listed on the Open Shifts page. To pick up these shifts, request the shifts on Zed using the Request tool. Shortly after the Add & Drop period ends, you will receive an email from the Scheduling Team if you were assigned to any shifts.
If consultants can no longer work specific shifts, they must contact their supervisors for approval in order to drop them. Then they must find replacements for their shifts and have supervisor approval. A shift remains the original consultant s responsibility until the management team approves the change.
Finals Schedule

The finals schedule starts when the permanent schedule ends and lasts through the final exam period. The procedure for picking up finals shifts is similar to that of picking up temporary shifts; however, the finals shifts are selected during the last few weeks of the permanent schedule

Hours Requirement

To maintain your position on the schedule, you are required to work at least 85% of your permanently assigned shifts. Failure to meet this threshold by posting or calling out of your shift for any reason may result in removal from the current schedule and ineligibility for the following semester's schedule.

Level 1 Consultants are expected to work at least ten (10) hours per week but no more than thirty (30) hours per week (twenty (20) hours for international students) throughout the semester.

Level 2 Consultants are expected to work at least twelve (12) hours per week but no more than thirty (30) hours per week (twenty (20) hours for international students) throughout the semester.

Level 3 Assistant Supervisors are expected to work at least fifteen (15) hours per week but no more than thirty (30) hours per week (twenty (20) hours for international students) throughout the semester.

Level 3 Supervisors are expected to work at least eighteen (18) hours per week but no more than thirty (30) hours per week (twenty (20) hours for international students) throughout the semester.

For more information on these schedules, consultants should contact the management team of their groups.

Consultants in Training who would like to be promoted to Consultant status should do the following:

Completion of all required training
Satisfactory performance during temporary schedule
Remain in good standing

There are always some, often many, shifts available. If Consultants in Training in good standing did not receive shifts during this process, they may be invited to pick up shifts throughout the fall semester as they open up or in the spring semester.

Campus Limitations

All consultants and supervisors are limited to working on a maximum of three (3) campuses per semester. The eligible campuses are College Avenue, Livingston, Cook/Douglass, Busch, and Help Desk. This restriction applies to all working periods, including the temporary schedule period at the start of each semester.

Consultants who wish to switch campus rosters for the following semester must reach out to the supervisory groups of the desired campus.

Consultants should plan accordingly when selecting shifts to ensure they remain in compliance with this campus limit policy.

Back to top

Attendance

All OIT consultants must attend all scheduled shifts as they are posted and are expected to work these shifts until their relief arrives. If a shift has ended and relief has not come in - immediately notify the Supervisor on duty. Supervisors should be aware of any absences from a scheduled shift for other duties as soon as possible.

Commitment to Your Role

This is a real job, and your commitment is essential. You are expected to be present and punctual for every scheduled shift. Please understand that your presence is vital to our operations, and any absences or lateness can significantly impact our team.

Your shifts and trainings should be a high priority. Please do not schedule other activities with the expectation that you can miss or be late for work.

Transportation Responsibility

You are responsible for arranging your transportation to and from work on time. Not having transportation or leaving early enough is not an acceptable reason for missing a shift or arriving late. Please plan accordingly to ensure you can fulfill your obligations.

Training Attendance

Attendance at all scheduled trainings is mandatory and holds the same importance as your shifts. Training is critical to help you perform your role effectively, and you are expected to attend them.

Sign Ins

All consultants are expected to sign themselves in when they arrive on duty and sign themselves out when their shift is over. Consultants are to sign into and out of the Zed payroll system upon the start and end of their shift. The time they sign in or out should coincide with the time their shift starts or ends. Consultants are responsible for their own sign ins and sign outs. If there are any problems during a consultant s shift with regards to Zed sign ins and sign outs the consultant is expected to make a comment in the space provided in Zed and should also send email to the campus lab Supervisors or notify the Supervisor on duty.

Consultants should arrive a few minutes early to their shifts to allow time for signing into computers and the Zed payroll system. Arriving early also allows consultants to be ready with the necessary tools open when their shift time begins. Consultants should be signed into the Zed system when their shift is scheduled to start so that they are not considered late.

Falsification of sign ins or sign outs with the intent to receive pay or assist other employees in receiving pay for time not worked constitutes theft and will be treated as such.

Breaks

If consultants want to take breaks other than to use the restroom, they should ask the supervisor on duty. If a break is approved, it must be accounted for in Zed.

Consultants can take quick 15 minute breaks during long shifts without signing out, with their supervisor s permission.

Extended breaks in which consultants leave and go to lunch (for over 15 minutes) is unpaid and must be accounted for in Zed in the appropriate field.

For shifts that are less than 8 hours, a break is not necessary, but may be considered if calls or workloads permit.

For shifts that are 8 hours or longer, a 30 minute break is strongly recommended. You should inform the supervisory staff that you will be going on break before you leave.

Consultants cannot take a break at the immediate start or end of a shift, or use breaks to start a shift late or end a shift early.

Lateness

Consultants are expected to arrive to all shifts and sign in on time. In the event that consultants believe they might be late, they should call the supervisor team of the location their shift is on as soon as they know they are going to be late. Alternatively, they may post in the designated Slack channel at their location for reporting lateness. The supervisor line for each location is as follows:

Help Desk
848-445-4357 then dial silent option press 6 then #
Busch
(848) 445-2760
Cook/Douglass
(848) 932-8498
College Avenue
(848) 932-0319
Livingston
(848) 445-3215

Peer coverage exception: If you know you will be late and secure coverage for the beginning of your shift (e.g., asking a colleague to cover until you arrive), no infraction will be issued if you inform the leadership team at the location where you are working of the lateness and who will be covering that time (before the shift start time). You must note the NetID of the consultant who covered your lateness on your Sign-In. And the consultant who covered your lateness must note your NetID on their Sign-Out.

Managers may only excuse up to a maximum of three (3) absences or missed shifts, and three (3) latenesses, per student per semester. This includes all locations and schedules worked.

Excusals are strictly reserved for genuine, extenuating circumstances that render attendance or being on time absolutely impossible.

You must follow the excusal process noted below.

Calling Out/Emergency Call Outs

Calling out should happen infrequently and only be used in emergency situations. If it happens too often, a meeting with your mentor or a Supervisor may be arranged to discuss the issue.

Certain circumstances require people to take off without advanced notice. In the event that you must call out of a shift with 24 hours notice or less, please abide by the following procedures.

Call the supervisors as soon as possible to inform them you are not able to attend your shift. The number you are calling will depend on where your shift is located.

If it is outside of operating hours, you can call the Help Desk to see if an after-hours consultant is available to take your call they can let the location know as soon as possible if you won't be attending your shift.
If you are unable to get in contact with a Supervisor via phone, notify your management team via the appropriate Slack Channel, or email.
Earned Sick Leave (ESL)
What is ESL?

Employees may accrue Earned Sick Leave (ESL) at a rate of one hour per thirty hours worked, up to 40 hours in a benefit year (The benefit year is July 1 through June 30). Employees can use up to 40 hours of accrued ESL per benefit year for the purposes listed in the NJ ESL (see below). Up to 40 hours of accrued and unused ESL may be carried over to the next benefit year, but a maximum of 40 hours of ESL may be used in a benefit year. Employees can begin using their earned sick time 120 days after their start date.

How can I tell how many ESL hours I have?

Login to my.rutgers.edu, then locate the "Employee Self Service" tab. There, you should see many options on the left side. Under "Benefits" there's a link titled "Employee NJ Earned Sick Leave for Class 4 Causal/Temporary or Seasonal Employee and Class 5 Student Workers". Click that, and you should see how many ESL hours you have earned and used. Please keep in mind that you are responsible for keeping track of how many hours you have earned and used. Supervisors cannot see this information.

When can I use my ESL?

New Jersey Earned Sick Leave (NJ ESL) can be used for:

An employee s or employee s family member s medical condition or preventive care
Absences due to circumstances related to domestic or sexual violence
Closure of the employee s workplace, or employee s child s school/daycare, by order of a public health official (This does not apply to a weather closure, only a public health related closure)
Time to attend employee s child school meetings/functions (This does not apply to your own school meetings/functions)
