" KB0016890 Policies and Procedures Policies and Procedures - Verifying Identity "

If you need to verify a user's identity for password resets, Duo bypass codes, or to unlock a locked account, the following are five acceptable methods. Any of these five methods are acceptable in any situation where identity needs to be confirmed. There is no requirement for one of these methods over another for any particular situation.

Never share the information you see in OR with the user. The user should be providing you with the information, and then you verify that it matches one of the methods listed below.

Always post how you verified the user's identity. If identity has been verified via an alternate phone number or email in OR, post the following in the work notes:

"Verified email/phone: email/phone number from role: role"

Ways to Verify
Alternate phone number
Part 1
Part 2
Alternate (non-Rutgers) email address
Part 1
Part 2
CVC
Verify in person
Verify over Zoom (only when approved and offered by FT)
Emailing in a photo of a Rutgers or a government-issued ID card is not an acceptable form of ID verification.
Alternate phone number
Part 1
Ask the user if they have any non-Rutgers phone numbers that would be on file with Rutgers.
In OR, click on each role to view any listed phone numbers and confirm a match.
User must currently have access to this number.
Alternatively, verify the phone number matches the one found in the Emergency Cell Query tool (only higher level student consultants/supervisors, or Full Time staff have access to this tool; ask for assistance if needed).
User must currently have access to this number.
After confirming a match, post a Worknote in the incident:
"Verified email/phone: email/phone number from role: role"
Verbally tell the user their Interaction (IMS) number, do not use the Incident number.
Part 2
The second consultant should call the user back at the verified number. Do not transfer the call.
Have the user repeat their Interaction (IMS) number provided by the first consultant for verification.
After verification, continue assisting the user as needed.

Please note that providing the number does not constitute verification. The user must be able to receive a call from us at the verified number for the verification to be successful. Activation keys, bypass codes, or NetID unlocks should not be given out or performed until the user has been called back at the verified number.

Alternate (non-Rutgers) email address

Please note that providing the email does not constitute verification. The user must receive an email from us at the verified address for the verification to be successful.

Activation keys or bypass codes must be emailed to the verified Alternate (non-Rutgers) email address only.

NetID Unlocks should only be performed after the user has confirmed the number sent to the alternate email address.

Part 1
Through email

If the user emails us from their non-Rutgers email address listed in OR, this is sufficient to verify their identity and proceed with assistance through email.

Through phone

If the user is on the phone, follow the instructions below.

Ask the user whether they have any non-Rutgers email addresses on file with Rutgers.
In OR, click on each role to see any non-Rutgers emails listed.
User must currently have access to this email.
The consultant should not verbally disclose the Interaction (IMS) number to the user. Instead, email the verified Alternate (non-Rutgers) email address, the Interaction (IMS) number.
You must use the Compose Email feature in ServiceNow, not Additional Comments, to ensure the email only goes to their Alternate (non-Rutgers) email address, and NOT their Rutgers email address (in case their Rutgers email was compromised).
Have the user read the number from their email back to you over the phone.
After confirming a match, post a Worknote in the incident:
"Verified email/phone: email/phone number from role: role"
Add the user's alternate email address to the ticket's Watch List.
Part 2

After verification, continue assisting the user as needed.

For part 2 of a password reset or Duo bypass, have the user confirm their (non-Rutgers) email address and send the Activation Key/Bypass code generated by the supervisor to the alternate (non-Rutgers) email address.

CVC
Ask the user if they have their RUID card. If they do, ask them to provide the CVC number on the back of the card.
This is a six-digit number located on the back of their physical RUID card.
Contact a supervisor and provide them with the CVC number the user provided so the Supervisor can verify it is correct.
NEVER post CVCs in the Incident.
After confirming a match, post a Worknote in the incident:
"Verified CVC"
After verification, continue assisting the user as needed.
Verify in person
Instruct the user to visit the nearest location based on https://it.rutgers.edu/help-support/
Ask the user to provide their Rutgers ID card or a valid US government ID card
Confirm the details match the details in OR, including name and birth date
If there is a photo in OR, confirm that the user and ID match the photo.
After confirming a match, post a Worknote in the incident:
"Verified ID" and note what type of ID was used to verify
After verification, continue assisting the user as needed.
Verify over Zoom (only when approved and offered by FT)
This should be done only if they can not verify via one of the other 4 methods, and when approved by FT - do NOT offer with this being approved by FT.
FT will need to verify the user has a photo in OR for them to verify against during the Zoom, in order to proceed.
Reach out to FT for further guidance. After they confirm that there is no other verification method available, FT will arrange to do the following:
FT will send them a Zoom invite from a departmental OIT Zoom account.
Once on Zoom, FT will record the meeting to the cloud.
They must hold up government-issued ID to the camera and show their face in good lighting.
They must also provide the last 4 of their SSN to the FT staff member (or RUID number if they have no social) during the Zoom call.
If they do not want to do this, they can come in person to verify their ID.
If FT feels that they are not the person in question, they should end the call and send the incident number and recording to abuse for further investigation.
Emailing in a photo of a Rutgers or a government-issued ID card is not an acceptable form of ID verification.

If you have any questions, please ask a Full Time Supervisor.

" KB0014142 RAMS RAMS - Rutgers Automated Mass-mailing System "

RAMS stands for Rutgers Automated Mass-mailing System. It allows an e-mail list to be generated based on demographic information, and provides for automated updates to the list membership based on changes in the demographic. These emails are sent to a member's official Rutgers email, which can be found in the Cloud Admin Tool (CAT) listed as their User Principal Name (UPN).

As of Spring 2026, RAMS supports ad-hoc mailing lists, allowing users to quickly create temporary mailing lists for specific events, projects, or one-time communications. Owners can manage membership by bulk importing members using their NetID or email address, and can also include members from RAD and Rutgers Connect unified or distribution groups. Currently, only Rutgers individuals can be added to ad-hoc mailing lists; support for external email addresses will be added in the future. If a user is interested in requesting an ad-hoc RAMS mailing list, they will need to use the RAMS New List Request form. In the file "Targeted demographic group", the user should specify "ad-hoc".

The RAMS main page is located at rams.rutgers.edu. Authorized users can find tools on this page to manage their existing lists and create class lists. If you are helping a caller with RAMS, you can find answers to many common questions using the RAMS Documentation page.

In assisting users, it is useful to find out their degree of control over the list.

Administrators can have one of three roles for a RAMS list.

The Owner role makes the user an additional owner of the list. List owners can make any changes to the list. To contact the owner of a list, send mail to <listname>-owner@rams.rutgers.edu.
The Moderator role gives the user the ability to moderate messages that are sent to the list through email or by using the web interface.
Users with the Editor role will also be able to moderate messages sent to the list, but they will only be able to do this through the web interface and not through email. Editors will also not receive an email notifying them that messages are pending moderation.

Owners can go to the website and make any changes using the links for each list.

Moderators are the only ones to get the approval e-mails. Owners do not get them. This is intentional. A faculty member may want to own a list (have full control over its configuration) but doesn't want to be bothered with the daily approval of messages. That might go to a secretary, who can be made a moderator.

Editors can also approve messages, but only through the web interface. An Editor might be a grad student who won't be reading e-mail every day but will just access the web interface as needed and approve/deny messages. Some web links are unavailable to an editor but will be to an owner or moderator.

When troubleshooting a RAMS list problem and unsure how to proceed, or believe the issue needs escalation, be sure to speak with a supervisor as they have extra access to the RAMS system and may be able to spot a problem with these tools that you would not have discovered otherwise.

" KB0014326 RBHS RBHS - CINJ After Hours Support "

Otherwise please direct them to call the CINJ Help Desk during normal business hours.

" KB0014333 RBHS RBHS - Remote Access Troubleshooting (Citrix) "

UH VPN

Some RBHS\UH users have a misunderstanding that VPN is required for all services.

Inquire what application or service will be used that needs VPN
Ask: What are you trying to access remotely?
If they answer: My office PC
Ask them to be more specific: What do you need to access or run from your office PC?
If they say my network share(s)
Ask: Which network share or which letter drive?
If they say an application
Ask: Which application?
If its a UH or RU Citrix application -
If it's one of the UH Clinical applications below --> Instruct them to register for remote access at istreg.rbhs.rutgers.edu (select Clinical Remote Access)
Epic, Logician, PACs, Muse or Sovera
If its any other UH Citrix application -->
If it's an RBHS application -->

If RBHS\UH user states they are trying to get to Citrix, they do not require VPN, direct the user to a web browser citrix.uhnj.org

If UH user states they are trying to get to network shares (H, J, or K - do not suggest the shared drive letters but let the user tell you).

Inquire if this is the first time accessing the network drive/s. If yes, UH building or remote? Is this a desktop or a laptop? Is this a University Hospital-owned device or a personal device?
First time document the above questions.
HEAT assignment to UH Citrix
Previous user have the user reboot.
If the same result assign UH Desktop
UH Email

If a UH user looking to access email, guide the user to a browser and go to exchange.uhnj.org

Access UH Citrix citrix.uhnj.org (web)

Accessing Citrix remotely using the "Local Citrix Client" (requires VPN)

Users who connect to VPN remotely (for other reasons) may access Citrix from their web browser (citrix.uhnj.org) or from their local Citrix Receiver\Workspace (new name).

If they wish to access Citrix from their local Citrix client, these are the steps:

Log on successfully to VPN
Open Citrix Receiver\Workspace (Start > Programs > Citrix )
If they are already logged on (you will see CORE\Your_Username)
Click on the small button with an arrow pointing down (to the right of CORE\username)
Select Log Off
Click on the same button
Select log on
Log in using your NetID or Core credential
Launch desired Citrix application
If they are not logged on, they will see LOGON instead of CORE\username
follow steps 3c thru 3f.

" KB0015912 RBHS RBHS - School of Dental Medicine (SDM) Call Handling Procedure "

The OIT Help Desk has coordinated with the School of Dental Medicine (SDM) to receive their first-level calls. The SDM phone number 973-972-8200 has been routed to the OIT Help Desk.

Below is the process for receiving and escalating calls.

Support Overview
Call Coverage is Monday Friday, 7:30 am to 5:00 pm.
Priority Dedicated Coverage will be 7:30 am 9:30 am.
A majority of calls will come between this time.
Be familiar with the escalation process.
All Level 2 and above are responsible for handling daily call activity.?
The skill in Five9 is called SDM General.
The consultant will hear the call is from the Dental School High Priority when answering a call from the skill.
Please note that calls from SDM users may be received from ANY skillset. Please follow the same procedures listed regardless of skill if the user is from SDM.
Utilize the standard OIT Help Desk greeting.
SDM has no IT support for nights and weekends.
Handling Call Activity
Be sure to ask the caller if they have an existing ticket, as SDM has communicated to their community that routine request activity is directed to it@sdm.rutgers.edu and will generate a ServiceNow incident assigned to RSDM-Triage.
If the user has an existing incident in an RSDM assignment group, please do NOT move it out of the current assignment group. You may leave work notes on the incident in its current assignment group.
Any standard enterprise-related call (i.e., NetID password, Connect, DUO, etc.) can be handled through standard OIT Help Desk procedures.
If you receive a call that requires SDM-specific support, mark it as New and assign it to RSDM - Triage.
Please be sure to include the location and any specifics of the issue. On-campus and off-campus locations can be found under the location heading in this article.
Escalation Procedure

Escalate all activity where an SDM customer indicates they need someone right away, it is urgent, or needs someone to respond immediately. This includes but is not limited to urgent matters related to classes, exams and patient care. If there is any doubt, proceed to escalate the issue using the following procedure:

Level 2 staff should seek assistance from FT/Sup to escalate the call.
Full-time staff and supervisors will have access to the Microsoft Teams Channel "RSDM IT Support & OIT Help Desk Coordination".
Use the RSDM tag and post in the channel.
Include the INC number, the user requesting assistance, the location of the user if relevant, and any other pertinent information.
Please do not keep user on the line. You can inform the user that their call has been assigned to the SDM team and will be followed up with as soon as possible.

Fac/Staff support will handle call activity from 7:30 am to 8:30 am and provide backup up to 5 pm.
Please follow the same procedures listed regardless of the Five9 skill it came from, if the user is from SoN.
Utilize the standard OIT Help Desk greeting.
SoN has no IT support for overnights, weekends, and university holidays.
Handling Call Activity
Be sure to ask the caller if they have an existing ticket, as SoN has communicated to their community that routine request activity is directed to email support@sn.rutgers.edu which will generate a ServiceNow incident assigned to the group: SON - IT.
If the user has an existing incident in an SoN assignment group, do NOT move it out of the current assignment group. You may leave work notes on the incident in its current Assignment group.
Any standard enterprise-related call (i.e., NetID password, Connect, DUO, etc.) can be handled through standard OIT Help Desk procedures.
If you receive a call that requires SoN-specific support, mark it as New and assign it to the group: SON - IT.
Please be sure to include the location and any specifics of the issue. On-campus and off-campus locations can be found under the location heading below.
Escalation Procedure

Escalate all activity where a SoN user indicates they need someone right away, it is urgent, or needs someone to respond immediately. This includes but is not limited to urgent matters related to classes, exams, and patient care. If there is any doubt, proceed to escalate the issue using the following procedure:

Level 2 staff should seek assistance from FT/Sup to escalate the call.
Full-time staff and supervisors will have access to the Microsoft Teams Channel "SoN & OIT Help Desk".
Use the SoN tag and post in the channel.
Include the INC number, the user requesting assistance, the location of the user if relevant, and any other pertinent information.
Please do not keep the user on the line. You can inform the user that their call has been escalated to the SoN team and will be followed up with as soon as possible.

Can you access the internet?

" KB0014370 ScarletMail ScarletMail - Account Disabled "

Callers may have issues with logging into ScarletMail. They may report that they get the error:

This account has been disabled. We are unable to process your request at this time. Please try again later.

This usually means there is a security issue with the account, and Google has locked it. The caller will not be able to log into Google services at that time. Look the caller up by NetID in the NetID Admin interface. If you do not see the lock on NetID Admin, please update the ticket and bring it to a supervisor.

If the user is calling regarding a ScarletMail audit, please see the ScarletMail - Audit and Deprovisioning page.

" KB0013858 ScarletMail Scarletmail - Additional Info "

If a user needs a Scarlet Mail password reset, they will follow the same procedure as for their NetID.

ScarletApps Mobile/POP/IMAP Password

Previously, there was a separate password for ScarletMail to configure it on third-party mail clients. As of September 2024, this separate password no longer exists, and users should use their NetID password.

Google Transfer Quotas

The link below details the daily transfer quotas for the Gmail account. This affects the Gmail web interface, as well as IMAP and POP Upload/Download limits.

https://support.google.com/a/answer/1071518?hl=en

If a user is setting up a ScarletMail account with Outlook and receives Over Quota or Outlook can not synchronize subscribed folders for scarletmail.rutgers.edu error messages, the issue may be related to the Google Quota limits.

" KB0014371 ScarletMail ScarletMail - Audit and Deprovisioning "

As of Monday, December 9, 2019, we will be updating the ScarletMail account audit process. We have traditionally run these audits once a semester to clean up the accounts for those who no longer hold a role that entitles them to a ScarletMail account. ScarletMail account audits will now follow an automated procedure that more closely aligns with the Rutgers Connect account audit process. Please note that the ScarletMail audit not only affects ScarletMail but the entire ScarletApps Suite.

ScarletMail Audit and Deprovisioning Process:

Please read the new guidelines for the ScarletMail audit process:

These automated audits will run every week.
Once a week, users who no longer hold a valid Rutgers role will be notified via email of the closure of their ScarletMail account. Please keep in mind that, under the new deprovisioning policies, the user's role end date may not coincide with the day their ScarletMail account is closed. The off-boarding process page will provide additional details (RBHS students keep authentication for 120 days past their role end date, and legacy students keep authentication until the following semester).
The notification will include the date the account will be locked.
Every day, a check is performed to determine whether a user has regained a proper Rutgers role that entitles them to a ScarletMail account again. If they do, they will be automatically removed from the audit list. If the user is already locked, their account will be unlocked.
If the user is under audit, you will see a red box with their deletion date and the date they were notified via email.
Example of the audit email that users will receive:

From: Scarletmail Help <help@scarletmail.rutgers.edu>
Sent: Friday, December 6, 2019 5:19 PM
To: Scarletmail Help <help@scarletmail.rutgers.edu>
Subject: Rutgers email accounts subject to closure

Dear [USER],

We are contacting you to let you know that your [NETID]@scarletmail.rutgers.edu account is currently scheduled for deletion the morning of [DATE].

According to official University records, either:

You are no longer registered as a Rutgers student.
You are no longer working as faculty/staff for Rutgers University.
You are not listed as a Rutgers alumni/retiree/emeritus.
Your university guest status has expired.

If this is incorrect or you have exceptional circumstances, please learn who to contact by visiting the ScarletMail audit self-service page, choosing your current role, and following the instructions that are provided for your specific role.

Once your ScarletMail account is removed, you will no longer have access to your ScarletMail emails or ScarletApps data (including documents, photos and other data stored in Drive).

If you wish to verify the validity of this message, or if you still require assistance after following the directions on the self-service page above, please email help@scarletmail.rutgers.edu, or contact your local Help Desk.

Thank you,

OIT Help Desk

The ScarletMail audit self-service form:
The audit email will include a self-service Google Form that users can use to find instructions for retaining their ScarletMail account based on their current situation.
This form contains an option for the user to request further assistance. Full-time staff will monitor this form and respond to each request for additional assistance.
Assisting a user with keeping their ScarletMail account:
Please use this knowledge article as a reference when assisting users with retaining their ScarletMail account, if needed.
After reviewing the options above with the user, they should be able to determine their next step in retaining their account. If the user still needs further assistance, please escalate to a supervisor.

" KB0016212 ScarletMail ScarletMail - Handling Requests for Email Spoofing Help "

Request From Systems

There will be times when the systems groups will send an email asking us to follow up with a user suspected of spoofing (faking) the from address of a Rutgers email from a non-Rutgers account. The request from systems will be sent to hd_ft and look similar to the following:

From: Basil Kmech <kmech@oit.rutgers.edu>
Sent: Friday, May 5, 2023 12:25 AM
To: ECS Help Desk FT Supervisors <hd_ft@oit.rutgers.edu>
Cc: Super-User <root@hoover.rutgers.edu>
Subject: NetID spoofing help needed

HD

RU_acct_info:
netid:##netid:832705:42000:ZHANE CAVE:/hoover/netid:/bin/tcsh

NOTE: They might be sending an email to their own account

If they are the "Actor:", see the "Date:" and "Received by:" info too in Google's alert details below; their email actions are getting the attention of Gmail and their automated protection/security systems. They should probably change what they are doing and stop.

## This is all we know. Google does what it does.
This Gmail potential employee spoofing alert informs you that a sender has sent messages that may be spoofing employee names in your domain.

The alert details include:

Summary: non_rutgers account@somewhere.com sent 1 message that may be spoofing users with a similar display name in your domain. There wwas1 rrecipient
Date: Thursday, May 4, 2023, 8:41:36 PM (UTC)
Actor: non_rutgers account@somewhere.com
Total messages: 1
Received by: netid@scarletmail.rutgers.edu
Severity: MEDIUM

Thanks,
kmech c/o root@scarletmail.r.e

Handling Requests

To handle these requests, please do the following:

Create an incident.
Caller: Guest
Service Offering: ScarletMail
Business Service: Email and Collaboration Services
Category: Communication and collaboration
Assignment Group: NB-HD-Level 3 (or any group you have access to)
Assigned to: yourself
Short Description: Google Email Spoofing Alert
Copy and paste the email received from the systems group into the Work Notes of the incident.
Using the email feature in ServiceNow, send the following email to the netid@scarletmail.rutgers.edu email address. Copy and paste the email below or use the Help@OIT Email Spoofing response template.


Hello,

Google has notified us that the address [non_rutgers account@somewhere.com] is attempting to send email out as: [name of the spoofer or netid@scarletmail.rutgers.edu]. This is setting off their automated protection and warning system, so we wanted to let you know. If there is a specific reason why you need to do this, please let us know, and we can try to assist you in accomplishing this through alternate means.

Your issue has been marked as resolved but not closed. If you are still experiencing issues related to this incident, call the OIT Help Desk at 833-OIT-HELP (648-4357) or visit the Rutgers IT Help Portal. If you are not encountering any more issues, your response is not required, and this incident will close automatically in 5 days.

Best,

OIT Help Desk
833-OIT-HELP (648-4357)


After that is done, you can Reply All to the email report from systems to confirm the user has been followed up with, and resolve the incident.

" KB0014372 ScarletMail ScarletMail - Mobile configuration for iOS and Android devices "

There are several ways for a user to configure ScarletMail for their mobile devices. The user may use the default mail app that comes with their device or users may use the Gmail app itself.

If a student is under 18 years old, they may be unable to access ScarletMail through mobile mail apps or third-party mail clients. They should access ScarletMail through a web browser.

For setting up ScarletMail for an iOS device, please reference the user-facing documentation.

For setting up ScarletMail for an Android device, please reference the user-facing documentation.

Utilizing Gmail
Have the user open the Gmail app on their mobile device. If the user has not installed the Gmail app, walk the user through downloading the application from their respective app store.
If the user has an existing email added to the Gmail app, they will select their profile picture > use another account > and then log in using netid@scarletmail.rutgers.edu
If the user is logging in for the first time, they will select Sign In and then log in using netid@scarletmail.rutgers.edu.
The user will then be prompted to log in via CAS. The user will enter their NetID and NetID password.
If the user is not enrolled in Duo, they will be prompted to set up Duo. You may walk the user through Duo Enrollment. If the user is already enrolled they will need to approve the login.
Once Duo is approved/setup the user will be able to see their emails within the Gmail app (it may take 5-10 minutes for all emails to load)

Google offers additional documentation if you need further materials to assist the user.

" KB0014199 ScarletMail ScarletMail - ScarletMail and ScarletApps (Google@Rutgers) "

ScarletApps is the implementation of Google Suite services at Rutgers University, accessed through a user's ScarletMail account. ScarletMail accounts are provided to anyone with Faculty, Staff, Alumni, Retiree/Emeritus, Guest, or Student roles. ScarletApps services include Google applications such as Gmail, Docs, Calendar, Video, Sites, and Groups. To see if a user has a ScarletMail account, consultants may check the CAT tool.

If a student is under 18 years old, they will be unable to use third party apps or setup ScarletMail on any mail client. They will need to use the web browser to access their account.

Google Links:

ScarletApps FAQ

Google Terms of Service

Google Apps Privacy Policy

Domain Administrator Data Access

" KB0018415 ServiceNow ServiceNow - Chat Support "

General Policies
Professionalism

Response Templates should be used as much as possible to ensure a uniform and professional chat experience for all users.

Address users in a professional manner.

Avoid everyday texting slang, IT jargon, or acronyms.

Expectations

When assigned to a chat shift, you should be working on emails when not actively in a chat. Pay attention to ServiceNow during your entire shift so you do not miss an incoming chat before it times out. When one comes in you should immediately accept it and pause working on emails.

You should only be engaged in 1 active chat at a time.

Logging into Chat
After logging into ServiceNow, ensure you are in Service Operations Workspace.
On the left side toolbar, click the chat icon to open your Chat Inbox.




Click the dropdown box and select Available. This is also where you would set your status to Away if you are not in an active chat and need to step away from the computer, similar to what you would do in Five9.


Enabling Notifications

The first time you log into chat, you must enable desktop notifications. Once enabled, you should not have to do so again.

Click the gear icon in your Chat inbox.
Ensure all notification options are selected.


Accepting Chats
When an incoming chat is in your Queue, you will have 20 seconds to click Accept. Chats will only appear in 1 agent's queue at a time; they do not show up for multiple agents simultaneously. ServiceNow allows you to accept multiple chats and have several active at one time, however you should only have 1 active chat.




After accepting, the chat window will open, and you can see the user's entire Chat Bot history before it was routed to you. Review the history to understand the user's issue.




On the right side will be the Chat Interaction's fields and Work notes, similar to taking a phone call.




For the initial message, use the Greeting message response template.
Follow up with the Request existing incident number response template to see if they have contacted us about this issue before. You should also review the user's Related Records for any open incidents.
Ask for a phone number and email if you get disconnected using the Request contact info response template.
Using Chat
Mandatory Fields
You must enter a Service Offering for the Chat since it is a required field, and one is not automatically selected.
The Short Description will be pre-populated with a generic description; however, you must change this field to be more descriptive to accurately reflect the user's issue.
After updating those 2 fields, make sure to click Save.
Assignment Group is set to ITSM - New Brunswick Chat Support by default and carries over automatically to any Incident created from the chat. Leave the Assignment Group as is.
Available Chat Features

The chat window has various built-in features. They can be accessed by typing / in the chat window or clicking the lightning icon on the chat bar. The only features we will be using at this time are:

Sending a Response Template
Searching the Knowledge Base (KB)
Transferring to another Agent

There is an option to have a private chat with other agents that is not visible to the user. However, it should not be used. Continue using Slack for communication with other consultants/supervisors.

Using Response Templates

Response Templates should be used as much as possible to ensure a uniform and professional chat experience for all users.

Bring up the response templates by typing /r or clicking the lightning icon and choosing Response Templates.




Enter keywords to search the available templates, and a list of related ones will appear. Hovering over the template in the list will display a pop-up preview of the whole template.




Choose the desired template to have it populate as your draft chat message. You can alter it before it is sent, for example, if you need to replace the [INCIDENT NUMBER] found in the message with the actual number.




Hit enter or click the send icon, and it will be sent to the user.




Sending Knowledge Articles
Bring up the knowledge base search by typing /search-kb or clicking the lightning icon and choosing Search KB Article.
Enter keywords to search the knowledge base, and a list of related articles will appear.




Click a knowledge article to add it to your draft chat message. Send the Sending a knowledge article (KA) response template with the KA.




After sending the knowledge article, it will be shown in the chat history. Both you and the user can click the link in the chat window to open it.


Creating Incidents from a Chat

Similar to phone calls, if a chat can not readily be resolved and requires escalating to another group or additional follow-up, you will need to create an incident.

Ensure you have added a Service Offering, updated the Short Description, and clicked Save on the Chat Interaction before proceeding.
Click Create Incident in the top right corner. Do not use the /create_incident chat feature as it does not copy any work notes from the chat into the incident.




For any new work notes, add them to the incident moving forward and not the chat interaction.
Use an appropriate response template and replace the [INCIDENT NUMBER] text in the response template with the user's actual incident number.




After sending, the incident will appear in the chat history. Both you and the user can click on it to open it.


Transferring Chat to another agent

You can transfer an active chat over to another agent if necessary. Few groups provide chat support, so transferring is typically limited to your group.

The agent you want to transfer to must be set to Available in chat before you can transfer.

Confirm with the other agent over Slack before attempting to transfer.
Send the user the Transfer chat response template.
Start a transfer by typing /ta or clicking the lightning icon and choosing Transfer to Agent. You can also click the transfer to agent icon on the chat toolbar.




Search for an agent by name, NetID, or choose one from the available list.




After selecting, their NetID will be shown in quotes.




Click the send icon to initiate the transfer. The inbox on the left will show both agents that you have a pending sent or incoming transfer request.

Sent transfer request:



Incoming transfer request:




Once accepted, both agents will remain in the Chat. The user does not see any notice of the transfer, and if either agent comments, it appears to be coming from one agent.
The accepting agent should send the Accept a transfer response template after updating their name in it.
The accepting agent must set the Assignment group back to ITSM - New Brunswick Chat Support.
The original agent can leave the chat by clicking the X on the interaction's tab in the Service Operations Workspace.
Transferring to another group

Due to the limited groups supporting chat currently, transferring a chat to another group's chat queue is not an option. If the issue needs to be transferred to another group or escalated further, you must create an incident for the user.

Ensure you have created an incident for the user using the Create Incident button in the top right. This will copy all worknotes from the chat over to the incident.




Ensure all relevant information from the chat is included in the incident since other groups cannot view the chat history.
Select the appropriate response template to send the user in the Chat:
Reassigning Incident if another group in ServiceNow will handle the incident, and they will address the issue with the user directly.
Created incident task if you are creating a task for another group for escalation, with the expectation that the Help Desk will follow-up with the user.
Contact local IT (DAs) if they need to contact their local DAs for assistance and the DAs do not have an assignment group in ServiceNow.
Replace the [INCIDENT NUMBER] text in the response template with the user's actual incident Number.




After sending, the incident will appear in the chat history. Both you and the user can click on it to open it.


After ensuring the user has no further questions or concerns, click End Chat to mark it as Closed Complete.




Continue any further work on the incident that was created.
Handling situations
Request the user call/walk-in

Sometimes an issue can not be troubleshooted over the phone, or they must verify their identity.

Create an incident for them.
Ensure you provide the user with the incident number in the chat.
Instruct them to call the Help Desk or walk into one of our locations using the Request user calls in or Request user walks in response templates.
Ask the user to hold

There may be times when you need to do further research into their issue or consult a supervisor. If this is the case, continued follow-up with the user is essential while they are waiting. This ensures they know the issue is being looked into and the chat doesn't timeout.

Initially putting the user on hold

Use the Place on hold first notice response template when first asking them to give you time to look into the issue further.

Ask for more time

After a certain period, with no response from the user or agent, the chat will prompt the user if they want to continue the conversation. If this happens and you need additional time, send the Place on hold continuing response template.

Ask to follow up with the user at a later time

If enough time has passed to warrant offering the user a follow-up when you have more information, send the Place on hold offer follow-up response template. This will allow them to continue waiting, get a callback, or receive updates via email about their incident.

If they request a call back or follow up email, ensure you:

Create an incident
Have their phone number and preferred email address in the incident
Note in the incident whether they prefer a phone call or email follow-up
Provide them with the incident number in the chat before ending it
User provides sensitive info in the chat

Please contact a Full Time Staff member.

User is irate

If a user is disrespectful, angry, or inappropriate in their conversation, notify a Supervisor or FT. You can then send the Irate user first notice response template.

If they continue to be aggressive or disrespectful, consult with a Supervisor or FT and then send the Irate user end chat response template. Once you do, immediately end the chat.

Escalate to a Supervisor

After consulting with a supervisor or FT to determine whether the situation warrants it, use the Escalate to supervisor response template. The expectation is that the Supervisor calls the user to address their issue.

Chat ends before the issue is resolved

In the event a user ends the chat before you have confirmed their issue is resolved, or the chat timeouts due to a lack of response from them, do the following:

Create an incident from the Chat.
Ensure you copy all relevant information from the chat as a work note on the incident.
Resolve the incident using the Help@OIT Chat Disconnected response template.
Ending Chats
User ended

A user may choose to end a chat at any time. You should receive a notification in the chat window if they do. Follow the instructions in the Chat ends before the issue is resolved section above on how to proceed.

This will automatically mark the chat State as Closed Complete.

User did not respond

After a certain period of time with no response from the user or agent, the user will be prompted if they would like to continue the conversation. If the user does not respond, the chat will automatically close and you will see a corresponding message. This will automatically mark the chat State as Closed Complete. Follow the instructions in the Chat ends before the issue is resolved section above on how to proceed.

Agent ended
Confirm with the user they have no further questions using the Any further questions response template.
Once they confirm, send the Final message response template.
Click End Chat which will automatically mark it as Closed Complete.




The chat history will show a message that the chat has ended.




Finish wrapping up any work notes or incident if one was created.
Timeouts

You have 20 seconds to accept an incoming chat, which is only shown to 1 agent at a time.

The idle chat reminder, to ask if the user would like to continue the chat, is displayed to the user anywhere between 3 to 5 minutes, depending on when the user last messaged.

The chat timeout, when the chat is closed due to inactivity, is anywhere between 6 to 8 minutes, depending on when the last message was posted in the chat by the user or agent.

" KB0014749 ServiceNow ServiceNow - Creating Incident Tasks "

You can maintain ownership of an Incident to coordinate communications with the user, but create a Task for escalated action items or to request input from other OIT related groups (Connect, Box, Mailman, etc..)

Service Operations Workspace view
In the top right corner of an incident, click on Create incident task.




A new tab will open.
Fill out the fields on the Task:

Short Description: Brief summary of the issue.
Description:

Clearly specify the action that is needed by the support group, or the specific question that needs input on.
Include all relevant information from the Incident that the support group would need to complete the Task. Do not assume they will go back and look at the Incident.
Configuration Item: Typically this should be the same as the Incident's Service Offering/Business service/Category. Should correlate with the group you will be assigning the task to.
Assignment group: The support group that you are requesting additional input/escalation from.
Assigned To: Usually left blank.
Work Notes: Assign yourself to the Work Notes Watchlist for the Task. (?)
Click Save when complete.
Return to the Incident. Make any necessary Work Notes and put the State to Awaiting Internal Partner.
Traditional Incident View

To create a task:

After the Incident has been created and saved, scroll down to the bottom of the page.
Select the Incident Task Tab.
Select New to create a new Task.
Fill out the fields on the Task:

Configuration Item: Typically this should be the same as the Incident's Service Offering/Business service/Category. Should correlate with the group you will be assigning the task to.
Assignment group: The support group that you are requesting additional input/escalation from.
Assigned To: Usually left blank.
Short Description: Brief summary of the issue.
Description:

Clearly specify the action that is needed by the support group, or the specific question that needs input on.
Include all relevant information from the Incident that the support group would need to complete the Task. Do not assume they will go back and look at the Incident.
Work Notes: Assign yourself to the Work Notes Watchlist for the Task.
Click Save when complete.
Return to the Incident. Make any necessary Work Notes and put the State to Awaiting Internal Partner.

Service Operations Workspace (SOW) view
Creating an Interaction

For phone calls, if you have the Workspace view open already, a new Interaction should automatically be created and some fields auto-populated when you answer the call. In this case skip to step 4.

Open the Workspace if you haven't already done so by clicking on Workspaces > Service Operations Workspace.




Create a new Interaction by clicking + > New Interaction






Choose the Interaction Type from the drop down menu.
Search for a relevant Service Offering which is a Categorization of the incident, if you don t find a specific service offering use the most appropriate category/business service.
Search for the user's NetID in the Opened For field, or use Guest if the user does not have a NetID.
If the user is calling on behalf of someone, search for the NetID of the person they are calling for in the Subject Person field.
Search to see if the user has an existing Incident:
For the Caller
Once a NetID is entered in the Opened For field, if they have any recent Incidents or Tasks it will show in the right bar under Requestor Information > [USER's] Tasks. Click View all.




Alternatively you can click the i icon to the right of the user's name in the Opened For field. Then in the new tab that opens, click on Related Records > Incidents.




For the Subject Person
Since these do not automatically show under Requestor Information, click the i icon to the right of the user's name in the Subject Person field. Then in the new tab that opens, click on Related Records > Incidents.
If an Incident exists you can associate it with your interaction by doing the following.
Open the Incident.
Click the three dots in the top right corner, then Associate Record.




Enter the Interaction number in the Interaction field.




Click Save.
Collect alternate contact information and enter the phone number in the Callback phone number field.
Choose the appropriate Assignment Group.

NB-HD for Walk-ins or Level 1 phone calls, a supervisor will assist if it needs to be escalated further.
NB-HD - Level 2 for ITS or SDM calls
The Assigned To field should already be populated with your name.
Enter a Short description, which is a brief sentence on the user s issue. This will be sent out to the user via email.
Enter a Description, which is a longer more thorough explanation of the user's issue (i.e. issue environment, troubleshooting already performed). This field is visible to the user.
Category/Business Service/Service Offering: Categorization of incident, if you don t find a specific service offering use the most appropriate category/business service
Click Save in the top right if you have not already done so.
You may add Work Notes in that field which are not visible to the User.
Closing the Interaction

All Interactions should be Closed at the end of every call/walk-in.

If no further action is needed

If you are able to resolve the issue over the phone with no other assistance needed you can mark down any final Wok Notes and then click Complete or Close in the top right corner. No further action is necessary.

Further action needed
Create an Incident from the Interaction if you need to do any of the following:
You need to send the user any additional information thru the Incident/over email (links to knowledge articles, bypass code, AK, etc..)
The user's issue was not fully resolved and they need to have the Incident Placed On Hold.
The user needs to be routed to another Assignment Group, or have an Incident Task open with another Group
To create an Incident first click Complete or Close in the top right corner.
Then click Create incident.




A new tab for the Incident will open under the existing Interaction and most of the fields should automatically be copied over.
If the person is a Guest, make sure to add an email for them under Alternate Contact Information > Caller Alternate Email.
Proceed to handle the Incident as you normally would.
Handling an Incident
Sending Emails

To Send an email to the user, find and use a Response Template from the side panel. Alternatively, a list of common responses can also be found on the ServiceNow - Response Template KA.

Click the Response Template icon from the side panel.




Search for and select the appropriate template.
Scroll down under the template that is displayed and click Copy to clipboard to copy the template.
Under Compose make sure Comments is selected and paste in the response template you copied.
If you are resolving the Incident, put the template into the Resolution Notes field instead.
Edit the response as necessary and proceed with the instructions below to place it On Hold or Resolved.
On Hold
If we are putting the issue on hold (such as awaiting information from the customer or a vendor), change the state to On Hold and set the On hold reason and the Hold until fields as applicable (7 days by default).

Additional comments are required when setting the On hold reason to Awaiting Caller
Work notes are required when On hold reason is set to Awaiting Vendor
For the OIT Help Desk when adding Additional comments for any of the above cases you may use the following template or edit a response from Responses Templates:
A list of common responses can also be found on the ServiceNow - Response Template KA.

Hello [Name],

Thank you for contacting the OIT Help Desk.

[Information regarding the user's case/answers to additional questions]

[IF PLACING INCIDENT ON HOLD:] Your issue has been marked as on hold. If you are still encountering issues regarding this incident, please respond to this email with more information or call the OIT Help Desk at 833-OIT-HELP (648-4357). If you are not encountering any more issues, your response is not required, and this incident will resolve in 7 days.

[IF RESOLVING INCIDENT:] Your issue has been marked as resolved, but it has not been closed. If you are still encountering issues regarding this incident, call the OIT Help Desk at 833-OIT-HELP (648-4357) or visit the Rutgers IT Help Portal: https://ithelp.rutgers.edu/. If you are not encountering any more issues, your response is not required, and this incident will close automatically in 5 days.


Best,
[Your Name]
OIT Help Desk
833-OIT-HELP (648-4357)




Save the Incident.
Resolved
When the Incident is resolved, change the State to Resolved.

Resolving gives the customer 5 days to reply to the incident in the event their issue is not resolved before it is closed by the system.
After closing, should the issue reoccur you can create a new incident for the user and reference the original ticket.
In the Resolution Information tab, enter the Resolution code and Resolution notes as applicable and save/resolve the incident.




Escalating an Incident
If the responsible party is in ServiceNow
Assigning the entire Incident to the Group

If instructed to do so, you may assign the Incident to another Group.

Ensure the state is set to New
Change Assignment Group to the appropriate team, but do not set an Assigned To unless the other team/our standard operation procedures instructs you to do so.
A list of all onboarded groups and their correct triage group can be found in the following knowledge article.
When changing Assignment Group you will be required to set Work Notes, use this to indicate why the incident is assigned to their group and what action is required of them.
Save the incident.
Creating an Incident Task

If the Help Desk is maintaining ownership of the Incident and communication with the user, but we need input from other groups, follow the instructions on creating an Incident Task.

If the RP is not in ServiceNow
If normal escalation is not via ServiceNow, follow existing Standard operating procedures. (ex: OneStop or OneSource) Typically this involves providing the user with a public facing phone number/email for another support group.
Update Work Notes and Additional Comments as needed.
Resolve the Incident if applicable.
If the normal escalation path is email to another group (not normally used):

You may use the Email functionality in ServiceNow ONLY if the user would normally be cc d in those escalations as replies will be marked as Additional comments. (ex: for Supervisors emailing IAM for Identity Merges).
You may use the Compose Email functionality found under the Handling an Incident > Sending Emails section of this KA. However instead of copying the response template back into the Additional Comments field, you would send the email through this interface.
Subject for these emails should typically be the Incident number.
You may also escalate outside ServiceNow if applicable, and post relevant information into Work Notes and Additional Comments

You wouldn t want to subscribe the address to the work notes list at this time since those emails contain links to Service Now which may confuse parties.







Traditional Incident View
Creating an Incident
Create a new Incident.
Search for the user's NetID within the Caller field

Alternatively, you may use the magnifying glass to search the user s table in its entirety, or use an alternate system applicable to your department to locate the user s NetID
If the user is not a Rutgers affiliate (or does not yet have a NetID), enter the Caller as Guest

When the caller and the subject person are the same (i.e. not calling on behalf of someone), please clear the Subject person field as it generates unnecessary required fields
For Guests you will need to complete the Alternate Contact Information tab
You can select the Show related incidents icon to the right of the Caller field if the user mentions they have called about this previously or if you want to see if they have other incidents open.
It is important to ensure you have the right caller, some users may have similar names/NetIDs so always double check if you are unsure.




Collect alternate contact information

Enter the phone number in the Callback phone number field.
Enter their email address (if they would not like to use their Rutgers address), in the Alternate Contact Information tab.
If they re using an alternate (non Rutgers) email address AND they are a Rutgers affiliate please add that alternate address to the Watch List , this will send additional customer visible comments to them.

If they are a Guest caller (non-affiliate), this field is automatically populated from the Alternate Contact Information Tab.
Fill in the required information before submitting the incident
Short description: is a brief sentence on the user s issue. This will be sent out to the user via email.
Description: is a longer more thorough explanation of the user's issue (i.e. issue environment, troubleshooting already performed). This field is visible to the user.
Category/Business Service/Service Offering: Categorization of incident, if you don t find a specific service offering use the most appropriate category/business service
Choose the appropriate Assignment Group.

NB-HD for Walk-ins or Level 1 phone calls, a supervisor will assist if it needs to be escalated further.
NB-HD - Level 2 for ITS or SDM calls
Assigned to

If you are resolving this issue yourself, you may assign it to yourself, provided you are in the relevant assignment group
Note: Incidents will automatically change to In Progress when they are assigned to an individual
At this point the incident should be Saved , which enters the record into the system and keeps you on the screen (Submitting will cause you to return to the previous screen instead)
Other fields are not required for initial submission, but SHOULD be completed for reporting ASAP:

Channel: Method of user contact interaction
Impact & Urgency : Used to determine order in which incidents should be resolved. These are set to normal by default and only need to be changed for high priority issues.
Comments ( Notes Tab)

Work notes

Should be used to document the call for internal use, and is not visible to the user.
Comment throughout the interaction (for HD staff this is where we would post NetID admin/OR/information stream during the interaction).
Additional comments

Are customer visible, and should only be used once or so per interaction to summarize the events that occurred and provide information to the user (i.e. to provide a knowledge article, link, or contact information for another group)
Every additional comment sends the user an email based on the emails configured in the Watch list and the users default Rutgers email address.

" KB0011360 ServiceNow ServiceNow - Customer Survey Follow-ups "

When you are asked to do a Customer Survey Follow-up, please use the response template Help@OIT Follow-up After Negative Survey when sending the email. It can be edited to best match the incident and/or request from the user.

If the user's incident is already closed, you can choose Copy Incident from the Additional Actions menu in Service Operations Workplace. You will need to fill in most of the information again, but this will leave a note in the work notes that this new incident is a copy of the previous incident. After responding, leave the incident "On Hold" and "Awaiting Caller" for one week. If someone requests you to follow up, it is best practice to let that person know the incident number of the new incident.

Service Operations Workspace (SOW) view
Answering Emails
Open the Workspace if you haven't already done so by clicking on Workspaces > Service Operations Workspace.
Change to Your Teams Work from the drop down to see Incidents an Tasks for all assignment groups you are a part of.




You'll see a serious of pie charts. Go through the following lists to address each type of request. Click on the chart for each to bring up the list view underneath.

Unassigned Incidents
Go through each level from highest to lowest severity.
Sort the Updated column from oldest to newest when addressing these.
Incidents assigned to your team
In Progress
Sort the Updated column from oldest to newest when addressing these.
There should not be any In Progress incidents that are not actively being worked on by staff at that moment.
Any In Progress incidents with an Updated date before today should be moved to an On Hold or Resolved state.
On Hold
Sort the Updated column from oldest to newest when addressing these.
Separately, you should also sort the Hold until column from oldest to newest to determine if the Hold until date has passed, if so, the incident should be handled as noted in the Housekeeping section below.




For Supervisors: Click the right arrow to display additional charts relating to Incident Tasks and Catalog Tasks. Make sure to address these as well during your shift. More information on handling the Catalog Tasks can be found on the Handling Generic Requests KA.
After selecting one of the sections noted above make sure to sort from oldest to newest. Click on Updated to sort that column from oldest to newest. To do this you may have to add the Updated column to the list view the first time you use SOW.
Click on the Settings (gear) icon above the list of incidents.
Then click Edit Columns.




Highlight the Updated option in the list on the left, then click the arrow to move it to the list on the right.
You can arrange the order of columns here by clicking and dragging on each field. (Alternatively when viewing the list of incidents, you can hover over a column, click the six dots that appear and drag columns left and right.)
Click OK to save.
Tip: Change the display to remove empty space in list view by clicking on your initials in the top right, then Preferences > Display > toggle Use compact spacing mode to be on.
If you want to view Incidents only for a specific group (ex: Help@OIT to see only emails or NB-HD to see only phone call incidents) you can do so by grouping the Incidents by Assignment Group.
Click the three dots on Assignment Group.
Click Group by Assignment Group.




Click the Number of the first incident to open it and read through it. Click the Details tab on the Incident to see more info.
When opening for the first time you may have to click the dropdown next to Incident to see all the standard fields.




Assign it to yourself by clicking Assign to me so other staff don t address it. If its already assigned to another staff member from previous follow-up, reassign it to yourself.
Note that all emails stay in the NB-HD - Help@OIT assignment group and all phone calls/walk ins stay in the NB-HD assignment group.
Any ITS or SDM calls/emails go into the NB-HD - Level 2 assignment group.
If it is a new email, under the Impact section you will need to set the Category, Business service, and Service offering to the best of your ability; if the specific Business service/Service offering doesn t exist use the Category that would fit best




In some cases this may auto-set the Assignment Group, please change it as appropriate. To set the Assignment Group:

If the name is not typed exactly (i.e. NB-HD - Level 2) it will not select, as it searches for an exact match, starting with what you type. You may use the magnifying glass to the right of the List Editor to search against a list of all values.
You may also start with an asterisk * to search for an assignment group that includes the characters you type anywhere in the group name, and not just starting with. (ex: *Duo will bring up Two-Step Login with Duo)
When updating assignment group you will have to add Work Notes when assigning an incident put a brief sentence about why you assigned it to that group. Do not save the work notes at this time wait until all steps are completed and save all at once.
If the email is a spam email (received spam that doesn t require a report to Abuse, not a user report of a phishing message) The email is clearly not an IT related issue, the individual is not sending from a Rutgers email address, and we can reasonably assume the sender knew this was not the right way to share this info - for example, complaints about a faculty member - process this as spam. If it comes from a Rutgers email address, please reach out to FT for guidance.
Assign it to yourself.
Clear out the Watchlist field and update the Alternate Contact Information to be: "N/A" or simply put a dash "-"
Update the following:
Category: IT Professional Services
Business Service: IT Service Delivery and Support
Service Offering: IT Student Help Desk
Additional Comment: Incident is Spam
Make sure to Save your changes.
Click the Mark as Spam button in the top right. This will auto cancel the ticket.
Set the Impact and Urgency fields

Knowledge Article on those fields
Internal HD article on those fields
If the user is a guest, you will need to complete the Alternate Contact Information tab with the information available.
If the user emailed an RUID/NetID but it was not associated with their profile you can manually update the Caller field.
If the user emailed from a non-Rutgers address, please set the Watch list accordingly to include their email.

This should be done even if they are a Rutgers affiliate (for emails received as Guest it will automatically pull the Caller Alternate Email after saving
If the email was received with a blank subject the Short Description will be blank, please enter a brief description of the issue
Save the incident and proceed with handling accordingly.

You may use the Compose Email functionality found under the Handling an Incident > Sending Emails section of this KA. However instead of copying the response template back into the Additional Comments field, you would send the email through this interface.
Subject for these emails should typically be the Incident number.
You may also escalate outside ServiceNow if applicable, and post relevant information into Work Notes and Additional Comments

You wouldn t want to subscribe the address to the work notes list at this time since those emails contain links to Service Now which may confuse parties.
Housekeeping
On Hold past due

Review the On Hold incidents to ensure none have passed their On Hold date. You may need to add the Hold until column to your list view if it is not already there by doing the following:

Click on the Settings (gear) icon above the list of incidents.
Then click Edit Columns.




Highlight the Hold until option in the list on the left, then click the arrow to move it to the list on the right.
You can arrange the order of columns here by clicking and dragging on each field. (Alternatively when viewing the list of incidents, you can hover over a column, click the six dots that appear and drag columns left and right.)
Click OK to save.

Review the On Hold past due incidents: This is a list of all incidents that are passed their Hold until, confirm the user has not responded.

Under the Incidents Assigned to your team pie chart, select On Hold.
Click the Hold until column name to sort from oldest to newest.
For those whose Hold date has passed, confirm the user has not responded and no further action is needed from us on the incident.
Change State to Resolved and update the Resolution Notes tab, then Save the incident.
The Resolution code is Solved Remotely for emails.
Resolution notes should use the "Help@OIT Resolving After End of Hold" Response Template

It is a main responsibility of a Labs Level 1 consultant to answer emails sent to the help@oit.rutgers.edu email address in ServiceNow. There is an expectation for Labs Level 1 consultants to answer at least two emails per hour and note them down in the Level 1 Consultant End-Of-Shift (EOS) report for tracking. Level 1 consultants must have their emails approved by a supervisor to check for correctness before responding to a user.
