Getting started in ServiceNow

Labs Level 1 consultants can use the Level 1 Consultant Dashboard in ServiceNow to see an overview of the emails assigned to the NB-Labs - Level 1 assignment group in ServiceNow.

Supervisors will review the Lab Supervisor Dashboard to review tickets that require a supervisor's attention.

Level 1 consultants will answer emails sorted into the NB-Labs - Level 1 assignment group in ServiceNow by a supervisor. The emails selected for the Level 1 consultants to review will include topics appropriate for a Level 1 consultant and can be answered using a ServiceNow response template.

Some topics that a Level 1 may encounter can include:

Standard NetID activation questions
NetID locked for deprovisioning
Admit Coming/Student Role access troubleshooting
Lab-specific questions related to printing, hours, locations, and quotas
OneStop redirects
Service activation issues
OIT Virtual Lab offerings
General Rutgers questions
Term bill login
Simple RUWireless secure questions
Straightforward RUatHome questions
Standard/phone-verified DUO bypass codes

Supervisors will leave emails with questions on the following topics in the primary Help@OIT filter for Level 2s to review:

CommunityID issues
Applications questions
NetID merges
NetID password resets
RBHS or Rutgers Health issues
Citrix questions
Kerblocks
International/email-verified DUO bypass codes
Taking ownership of a ticket

To maintain efficiency and ensure no emails are overlooked, tickets should be addressed in the order they were received. This process helps move the email queue forward and prevents delays. To view the oldest emails first, sort the filter by the Updated column in descending order.

Steps to take ownership of a ticket:

Open the ticket by clicking on the incident number.
Assign the ticket by selecting Assign to me in the upper right-hand corner
Once assigned, responsibility for resolving the ticket remains with the assigned consultant for the duration of their shift.

If the ticket cannot be completed before the end of a shift:

Add a work note explaining why the ticket could not be completed
Unassign the ticket by removing the name from the Assigned to field
Change the ticket's state back to New
Click Save

This process communicates to other consultants that the ticket is available for continued work and preserves Progress.

Filling out the incident fields

When a ticket is opened, the system defaults to the Overview panel, where the user's original message can be found under the Summary section.



For additional ticket information, click the Details tab. The complete set of incident fields may be accessed by expanding the down arrow next to Incident if it is not immediately visible.

Under the Impact section, complete the following fields based on the context of the issue:

Category
Business service
Service offering

Once the service offering is selected, the business service and category should auto-populate. If they do not, it is then the consultant's responsibility to ensure these fields are filled accurately. The accuracy of these fields is important, as they determine which response templates will be available during ticket resolution. For assistance with these fields, refer to the ServiceNow: Using the Category, Business Service, and Service Offering fields knowledge article, which includes a list of all available service offerings.

Checking related records

It is essential to check whether the user has any other open tickets related to this matter to determine if another consultant has already addressed their concern.

To check related records:

Select the Related records tab at the top of the page




Go to the Incidents by Caller/Subject section
This page will show all incidents in ServiceNow where the user is in the Caller or Subject person fields.
Please take note of the date and time the incidents were opened, their State, and the Short Description.
Click on a ticket number to open it in a new tab.

After reviewing the tickets under Related records, it may be determined that no other relevant tickets were recently opened for this user for this issue. If so, please close out of this view and answer the original email ticket in the Details panel as usual.

Parent/Child tickets

When a recent relevant ticket is found, proceed in one of the following ways:

If the recent relevant ticket has been resolved:

Review the ticket to understand the solution
Set the email ticket as a child ticket and the resolved related ticket as the parent.
This action will cross-reference the two tickets, indicating they were related contact points about the same issue.
All child tickets will automatically resolve when the parent ticket is resolved.

To designate an incident as a child ticket in the ServiceNow Operations Workspace:

Navigate to the Related Records section of the Details panel
Enter the parent ticket's incident number in the Parent Incident field




Click Save

If the recent relevant ticket is marked as New and has not been assigned to a group:

Confirm with a supervisor whether the tickets should be cross-referenced as Parent/Child
This configuration ensures that all work notes and additional comments will be centralized in the parent ticket and automatically shared with the child ticket.

If the recent relevant ticket is assigned to another group and is In Progress:

Review the ticket to understand the troubleshooting steps already taken
Check with a supervisor for guidance on how to proceed
Posting tool information

If the user has a NetID, information from the following sources needs to be posted:

NetID Admin Management
All information from this search can be posted as a work note.
CAT Tool
Only post the relevant information, such as their roles or which services they are requesting access to.

For NetID users, always post the NetID Admin and role information in every ticket. If the user is a guest without a NetID, mention this in the work notes, as they will not have a record on our systems to use for troubleshooting.

Using the response templates

To select a response template:

Select the correct Category, Business service, and Service offering
Click Save
Select the Response Template icon from the right panel




Choose the corresponding response template from the dropdown menu
Please note that some templates will only appear if the service offering is updated to the corresponding topic.
Click Copy to clipboard.
Paste the template into a work note or a separate document to draft the response.
Review all notes written in brackets and erase any irrelevant sections.
Enter the drafted response into the ticket as a work note

In some situations, the template may not provide enough information to satisfy the question the user is asking. In this case, the template can be used as a base, and the relevant information can be added manually. Links to public-facing knowledge articles can be shared with users by copying the article's permalink into the response.

Using other available resources

ServiceNow

The IT Service Management Knowledge Base contains information on various internal and external topics and procedures.
If a relevant article is missing or insufficient, notify a supervisor so it can be reviewed and potentially updated.
When referencing Knowledge Base articles during troubleshooting, include article links in the ticket's work notes to allow supervisors to retrace the troubleshooting process.
The keyword search feature in ServiceNow can also be used to locate tickets with similar issues.
Searching for related tickets, like exploring a user's Related Records, is a helpful way to find examples of how similar problems were handled.
Referenced ticket numbers can also be included in a work note for documentation and review purposes.

Grammarly

Grammarly is a tool that provides grammar assistance and can help make email drafts sound more professional when appropriately used.
While Grammarly includes a generative AI feature for brainstorming and advanced writing support, this feature is not permitted for Level 1 email communications.
Instructions for activating Grammarly can be found on the Grammarly support page.

Google

Online searches often provide helpful information related to the issue being addressed.
However, because publicly available information may not be specific to Rutgers systems or procedures, verify any findings with a supervisor before relying on them for resolution.
Incident escalations

When additional guidance is needed for a ticket, the following escalation paths are available:

Consult with an on-site Level 2:

Level 2 cons are email certified and can provide direction and support.
They may also share relevant documentation to aid in understanding internal resources and processes.

Consult with an on-site assistant supervisor:

If a level 2 is not available, an assistant supervisor may be consulted.

Post questions in the #esd_nb_level1_email Slack channel:

Use the @here tag to notify all currently active users.
Before posting, perform a keyword search in Slack to check if the question has already been asked and addressed.
Previously answered questions often contain valuable insights and solutions in their comment threads.

Technical questions should be asked in public Slack channels rather than private messages to support shared learning and knowledge building. This approach enables consultants to learn from one another and reference past solutions when similar issues arise.

How to escalate tickets

While Level 1 email support is provided by the consultants in the Labs, additional Level 1 email support is provided by the Help Desk. Sometimes, an email that is triaged to Labs Level 1 support may require more in-depth troubleshooting or require tools that the Labs consultants do not have access to. For these cases, please speak with a supervisor or reach out in Slack so that the ticket can be reassigned.

To reassign a ticket to the Help Desk, a supervisor will take the following steps:

Change the Assignment Group to NB-HD - Help@OIT
Unassign the Level 1 consultant from the ticket so it is blank
Change the State of the ticket to New
Save the ticket
Reach out to the Help Desk supervisors on the #esd_nb_hdlabs_sups channel in Slack to let them know that a ticket was just escalated from Level 1 support and requires their attention
Draft approval process

A supervisor must approve all emails before they are sent to the user.

Before requesting approval, ensure the following actions have been completed:

Ownership has been taken of the oldest ticket in the NB-Labs - Level 1 Assignment Group
All appropriate fields are completed
All relevant information from tools such as NetID Admin and CAT is included

Methods for requesting approval:

Speak directly with an on-site supervisor
Post in the #esd_nb_level1_email Slack channel using the [draft review] tag

Upon review, a supervisor may:

Provide comments or recommend edits
Approve the draft

After review, supervisor approval must be documented in the ticket work notes. Approval in Slack alone is not sufficient. While it is the supervisor s responsibility to record this, if they do not, the consultant must include the NetID of the approving supervisor in the work notes.

Once approved, the email may be sent to the user by either resolving the ticket or placing it on hold.

Guidelines for ticket status:

The ticket may be resolved if the issue is fully resolved and no further assistance is required.
The ticket may be placed on hold if there is potential for the issue to recur.
In cases of uncertainty, placing the ticket on hold is preferred to allow the user to respond or ask follow-up questions before the ticket is automatically closed.

To place a ticket on hold:

Change State to On Hold
Select the on-hold reason as Awaiting Caller
Paste the email draft into the Additional Comments field
Click Save

To resolve a ticket:

Select Resolve on the top right of the page
Paste the email draft into the Resolution Notes
Click Resolve
Criteria for draft approval

Supervisors are evaluating tickets for correctness based on the following criteria:

The correct service offering is selected.
The consultant is assigned appropriately.
Work notes clearly describe the troubleshooting process.
Information from NetID Admin and CAT is included when relevant.
The response is technically accurate and complete.
External links are correct, active, and relevant.
Tone is professional and free of jargon and suited to the audience.
Supervisor housekeeping

A supervisor is responsible for assisting Level 1 consultants with email support. To accomplish this, supervisors will be asked to do the following tasks:

Check the Help@OIT Triage filter for emails that a Level 1 consultant can answer and assign them to the NB-Labs - Level 1 assignment group in ServiceNow.
Answer questions and approve email drafts in the #esd_nb_level1_email Slack channel.
Level 1 consultants will tag their draft approval requests using the [draft review] keyword.
Supervisors can set up the keyword notification in Slack by going to their profile in the lower right-hand corner and selecting Preferences. Under the My Keywords section of the Notifications menu, you will find a list of all the keywords that trigger a notification.




Check if there are any replies to tickets that were previously put on hold
If there is a reply, unassign the ticket from the consultant and change the state to New so the next available Labs Level 1 consultant can address the reply.
Check if any tickets have been on hold for 7 days or more
Have Level 1 consultants review these tickets and resolve them if needed.
Escalate tickets to Level 1 email support in the Help Desk when needed.
Review the Level 1 Consultant EOS responses and follow-up as needed.
Link brings you to the folder; Select the tracking sheet that has the semester you're looking for.
Delegate tickets to a Level 2 if a Level 1 consultant cannot address it.

Help@OIT Activation Key

Hello [USER'S FIRST NAME],

Thank you for contacting the OIT Help Desk.

Your activation key is [ACTIVATION_KEY]

Instructions to unlock security questions and reset password:

1. Visit https://netid.rutgers.edu/displayForgottenPasswordForm.htm
2. Enter the following information:
- NetID: Your NetID in all lowercase letters
- First Name: Your first name, with the first letter capitalized
- Last Name: Your last name, with the first letter capitalized
- Identifier: Select the bubble next to the identifier you wish to use, then enter the digits of the identifier.
If you are a guest of Rutgers University, be sure to enter your pseudo-Social Security Number given to you by your sponsor.
- Date of Birth: Your date of birth in the MM/DD/YYYY format
- Activation Key: The activation key listed above
3. Click I m not a robot, You should see a green check mark after clicking this.
4. Click Continue to submit the form.
- If you receive an error message, please verify that your information has been entered correctly.
5. After submitting the form, you should now be able to change your security questions and answers in case you need to reset your password in the future. Click Update Questions once you are finished.
6. Enter your new password, then click Reset Password to confirm your change.

After successfully resetting the password, log into personalinfo.rutgers.edu and update:

- Your emergency cell phone number in the "Emergency Contacts and Notification" tab.
- Your personal (non-Rutgers) email address in the "Address Update" tab.

This will allow you to use your mobile number or your alternate email address to reset your password remotely, should that be necessary for the future.

[IF PLACING INCIDENT ON HOLD:] Your issue has been marked as on hold. If you are still encountering issues regarding this incident, please respond to this email with more information or call the OIT Help Desk at 833-OIT-HELP (648-4357). If you are not encountering any more issues, your response is not required, and this incident will resolve in 7 days.

[IF RESOLVING INCIDENT:] Your issue has been marked as resolved, but it has not been closed. If you are still encountering issues regarding this incident, call the OIT Help Desk at 833-OIT-HELP (648-4357) or visit the Rutgers IT Help Portal: https://ithelp.rutgers.edu/. If you are not encountering any more issues, your response is not required, and this incident will close automatically in 5 days.

Help@OIT DUO Bypass Code

Hello [USER'S FIRST NAME],

Thank you for contacting the OIT Help Desk.

In order to update your DUO devices please reference the following webpage: https://ithelp.rutgers.edu/sp?id=kb_article&sysparm_article=KB0012519

Your DUO Bypass Code is as follows: <Bypass Code>

To use the code, please enter it when prompted with the DUO Authentication Method within the Enter a Passcode option. Please note that this code only lasts 2 hours since its generation and can only be used twice. If you do not utilize this code within 2 hours, you will need to contact the Help Desk again for another code.

Upon adding your new device to DUO, we recommend adding a secondary device. You may add another device such as a mobile phone by following the instructions on this webpage: https://ithelp.rutgers.edu/sp?id=kb_article_view&sysparm_article=KB0012446

You may also opt to obtain a Duo hardware token as described here: https://ithelp.rutgers.edu/sp?id=kb_article&sysparm_article=KB0012534

Your issue has been marked as on hold. " KB0016176 ServiceNow ServiceNow - Sharing Knowledge Article (KA) Links "

There are 4 different links that can take you to various versions of an article.

Portal PermaLink

This is the link that should always be used whenever referencing an article to a user.

It is the link on the self-service Portal that takes the user to the most updated version of the article.

Navigate to the article on the Portal and click Copy Permalink located at the top or bottom of the article.

Example: https://ithelp.rutgers.edu/sp?id=kb_article_view&sysparm_article=KB0012508

Note: it will always start with ithelp. and end with an article number such as KB1234567

Portal sys_id link

When used, this will take the user to this specific version of the article. It will not bring them to the most current version of the article if it has since been updated, which is why this one should not be used.

When viewing an article in the Portal, this is the link in the URL bar on the browser.

Example: https://ithelp.rutgers.edu/sp?id=kb_article_view&sys_kb_id=08138ef71bbe4110743c54a51a4bcbf0

Back End PermaLink

It is the link in the back end of ServiceNow that takes the user to the most updated version of the article.

This link can be used for internal documentation.

Navigate to the article in the back end (our internal view of ServiceNow) and click Copy Permalink located at the bottom of the article.

Example: https://rutgers.service-now.com/kb_view.do?sysparm_article=KB0012508

Back End sys_id link

It is the link in the back end of ServiceNow that takes the user to a specific version of the article.

Navigate to the article in the back end (our internal view of ServiceNow), this is the link in the URL bar on the browser.

Example: https://rutgers.service-now.com/kb_knowledge.do?sys_id=08138ef71bbe4110743c54a51a4bcbf0&sysparm_referring_url=kb_view.do



Permalinks will always have a URL that ends in the KB number

" KB0019410 Software Installation Upgrade SmartView to the latest version "
What is SmartView?

Smart View enables you to integrate ERP, enterprise performance management (EPM), and business intelligence (BI) data directly from the data source into Microsoft Word, PowerPoint, Excel, and Outlook. Using Smart View, you can view, import, manipulate, distribute, and share data in Office-based interfaces.

How to upgrade SmartView to the latest version

Please note that if your default browser is Mozilla Firefox or Google Chrome, a browser add-on is required.

Verify that all Microsoft Office applications, including Excel, Word, and Outlook, are fully closed.
Uninstall the current version of Oracle SmartView from your device.
Install the latest version of SmartView.

Once the zip file download is complete, extract its contents: Compressed Folder Tools ribbon > Extract all. If you lose track of the download file, check the Downloads folder.




The Select a Destination and Extract Files pop-up will appear. Select Extract. After the pop-up, confirm the default save location as is, then click OK.




Once the zip file is extracted, navigate to the folder containing the extracted files, and Double-Click SmartView.exe to start the installation wizard as the user.




When installation is complete, click OK. The Smart View ribbon is displayed the next time you open Microsoft Excel, PowerPoint, or Word.





If you encounter any issues during this process, please contact the OIT Help Desk for further assistance.

" KB0014408 TD TD - Voice & Telephone Services "

EI - Voice Services - Newark
EI - Voice Services - Piscataway
EI - Voice Services - Newark RU

If the person who calls is not a UCS, please check with a supervisor prior to distributing the NOC phone number. Enter their NetID and password > Login
Select which school their request is for.
This may either be Rutgers or RBHS.
Select I need to report a problem > I can't retrieve my voicemail - please reset my password
Select New Service > enter their manager's name.
Enter their request number
This will be their office phone number or the phone number for which they need to reset the password.
Select Contact number is the same as the request number unless the user wishes to be contacted on a different number.
Enter the building location, room number, and keep the priority low.
Select Finish.

Once the user submits the request, TD will follow up with them at their contact number.

" KB0014118 Webex Webex - Information and Troubleshooting "
Table of Contents

Webex Support Quick Tips
If users are unable to log in to Webex, have they activated their accounts? Directions on the Rutgers Webex site.
If they are unable to join a meeting, please follow the below troubleshooting options:
Have they had trouble joining all meetings or just specific ones?
What OS are they using? Are they joining from the browser or with the downloaded application? Are their computer and browser up to date?
Are they able to join the Webex test meeting? Directions on the Cisco Webex site.
If so, they should confirm the details of the meeting they are having trouble joining. This could include reaching out to the meeting host or professor to confirm start time, that the host has joined the meeting, that the meeting time or joining link/meeting number has not changed, that the user contacting us is listed as an attendee if the meeting is locked, etc.
If they are unable to select Join Meeting or it is greyed out, they must select an audio connection. It may say "Call me" under the greyed-out Join Meeting button. If they select this and change to "Use computer for audio," they can use their computer's speakers and microphone to join. They will also need to give their browser permission to use the microphone and camera, which should pop up in the top left when they first try to join the meeting. If they are using the app, you can find more information about configuring audio and video connections at the Cisco Webex site.
After we troubleshoot all of this, please get the meeting number (7XX XXX XXX) before escalating the issue so we can look into the specific meeting!
For other issues, please consult the resources on the Rutgers Webex site and the Cisco Webex Help site to find an answer before escalating the issue. Many common questions have answers on these sites (e.g., recording a meeting, setting alternate hosts, etc.)
General Information
What is Webex?

WebEx is a set of tools designed for personal and corporate collaboration. It s used to connect with others, typically through the internet, and allows you to communicate via audio, video, text chat, file sharing, whiteboards, and other features.

How many people can Webex host?
Our current Enterprise License Agreement provides all of the following services in one package:
All Webex accounts include licenses: Webex Meetings and Webex Events. This will apply to any user who activates a Webex account through the Service Activation portal on netid.rutgers.edu OR when a Domain Admin activates a Webex account through the CAT tool.
Currently, users are assigned a Webex Meetings license when they activate a Webex account, and Domain Admins can assign additional license features when needed.
Domain Admins will be unable to turn on individual license features through the CAT tool; however, they will still be able to look up users within their domain to confirm whether they have activated a Webex account.
A Support Center license will be issued only upon special request, after review by EITC.
Meeting Center - Fully participatory meetings with up to 1025 attendees.
Webinar Center - A one-to-many application for large-scale events with up to 1,000 attendees.
??????Support Center - Technical support through a WebEx platform with 8 concurrent sessions with 5 participants in each.
The links above lead to Cisco's descriptions of each service if you need more information.
The catch is that not all 1,000 members will be able to click a link to the meeting.
The standard meeting should support
Up to 500 attendees using the WebEx software on a device.
Up to 1000 attendees dialing in over VOIP.
Up to 500 attendees using phones.
Up to 25 room systems.
The pages on collaboration help are supposed to be the most up-to-date information:
https://help.webex.com/en-us/article/ne50jcfb/Start-a-Webex-Meeting
What are we expected to support?

The OIT Help Desks are expected to provide first-level support for Webex software and hardware issues. This includes general account troubleshooting (account creation, password issues, etc.), general use (setting up meetings, joining meetings, etc.), general hardware troubleshooting (settings, connections, etc.), and other issues to be determined. The OIT Help Desks will also serve as an escalation point for issues beyond our support level.

Who is eligible to use Rutgers Webex?
All faculty/staff and students can obtain a Webex license.
Guests can also obtain a license;se, however, the license must be provisioned by a domain administrator.
How to escalate issues beyond your troubleshooting ability.

When confronted with an issue that exceeds the scope of this document or your troubleshooting capabilities, you should bring it to the attention of the next level of support. The second level of support for Webex issues is the Knowledge Management Team. Should you not be able to reach them, you can reach out to the Tech Team. Issues that exceeded either level of support will be escalated to EITC by emailing eitc@oit.rutgers.edu.

" KB0017428 Webex Webex - UCM Calling "

Webex calling enables a user to have their Webex app function as their office desk phone. This is referred to as a soft phone and replaces the old Jabber soft phone system. They can place and answer calls on the Webex app on their desktop or mobile device using the phone number associated with their office phone. Other features, such as hunt group calls (calls to a central number that rings on multiple phones), work with this as well.

When viewing a user's profile on CAT, you can determine if they have already been set up for this service. If they have, you will see a feature labeled "Unified Communication Manager (UCM)" under Webex > Services.

Requirements

User must have a Cisco office phone and an active Rutgers Webex account. Confirm this before escalating.

If using it on their mobile device, ensure the Webex app was downloaded from the Intune Company Portal, and not from their phone's default app store.

Escalation

Voice Services handles setting up the user for this feature to be enabled. After verifying the requirements above, any issues or requests to set it up can be sent to EI - Voice Services.

Additional Information

See the public knowledge article on Webex Calling for additional information.

A copy of the setup instructions that the user will receive is attached to the linked article. Voice Services must set up their phone before they attempt these instructions.

" KB0014125 Windows Windows - Americans With Disabilities Act Software (ADA) "
Ease of Access Center

Numerous applications are available to assist users with disabilities that may affect their ability to use Windows computers. The Ease of Access Center in Windows 10 and Windows 11 offers magnification and speech for the visually impaired, using text or visual alternatives for people who are hard of hearing, and an on-screen keyboard and mouse keys for those who have trouble using the keyboard or mouse.

To reach the Ease of Access Center, open the Control Panel and select Ease of Access. The shortcut Windows Key+U can be used to quickly get to this screen.







Magnifier

An application to assist users with slight visual impairments. It will display a small, magnified window along the top of the screen that follows the movement of the keyboard focus or the mouse cursor. It will take up large parts of the screen to make it easier to read. The zoom and the magnification can be adjusted bigger or smaller. The screen view will follow the mouse.







Narrator

A text-to-speech application that can assist patrons with visual impairments in using the computer. It will say the words on the screen or verbally describe what is on it (such as a checked or unchecked box).

Display Accessibility Options

The screen can be changed to high contrast by selecting Set up High Contrast. Turn the high contrast on or off by pressing Left ALT+Left SHIFT+Print Screen.

Select Use text or visual alternatives for sounds to replace the auditory alert with a flash. The visual warnings include the Flash active caption bar, the Flash active window, and the Flash desktop. Additionally, users can select Turn on text captions for spoken dialogue. The volume can be adjusted by selecting Adjust Volume.

Input Accessibility Settings

Enabling the On-Screen Keyboard assists patrons with mobility impairments. It allows patrons to type on the screen using the mouse instead of the keyboard. To turn on Sticky Keys so that one can press keyboard shortcuts one key at a time, go to Make it easier to focus on tasks and select Turn on Sticky Keys.

By selecting Make the mouse easier to use, the user will be prompted to choose from a few options to configure the mouse. The color and size of the mouse pointers can be changed, and Mouse Keys can be turned on, allowing the user to use the number keypad to move the mouse around the screen.

The Ease of Access folder also has the option for Speech Recognition. From this selection, the computer can be configured to follow commands using one s voice. The program has to be trained to the user s voice; this process may take a while.

" KB0014127 Windows Windows - Common Keyboard Shortcuts "
Common Windows Keyboard Shortcuts

It is not required to memorize these shortcuts, but it will aid your computing experience.

Keystroke	Action
CTRL+A	Select All
CTRL+B	Bold selected text
CTRL+C	Copy
CTRL+F	Open the Find dialog box
CTRL+I	Italicize selected text
CTRL+N	Open a new file or window
CTRL+P	Open Print dialog box
CTRL+S	Save the active file
CTRL+T	Open a new tab
CTRL+V	Paste
CTRL+W	Close active window or tab
CTRL+X	Cut
CTRL+Z	Undo
CTRL+Y	Redo
CTRL+SHIFT+ESC	Open Task Manager
CTRL+ALT+DELETE	Open the Windows Security dialog box
F2	Rename the selected item
F3	Search for File or Folder
F5	Refresh
ALT+ENTER	View the properties for the selected item
ALT+F4	Close the active item, or quit the active program
ALT+F	Open File menu drop-down
ALT+TAB	Switch between open applications
BACKSPACE	View the folder one level up in Windows
SHIFT+D	Delete the item without placing it in the Recycle Bin
ESC	Cancel the current task
PrtSc (Print Screen)	Takes a screen capture of the entire desktop
ALT+PrtSc	Take a screen capture of the active window
Win+Home	Minimize all but the active window
Win+Up Arrow	Maximize the active window
SHIFT+Win+Up Arrow	Maximize the active window vertically
Win+Down Arrow	Minimize the active window
Win+Left/Right Arrows	Dock the window to each side of the monitor
SHIFT+Win+Left/Right Arrows	Move the window to the monitor on the left or right
Win+CTRL+D	Create a new virtual desktop and switch to it
Win+CTRL+F4	Close the current virtual desktop
Win+CTRL+Left/Right Arrows	Switch to the virtual desktop on the left or right
Win+A	Open the Action Center
Win+C	Launch Cortana in listening mode (for voice commands)
Win+D	Show Desktop
Win+I	Open the Settings app
Win+S	Launch search
Mouse Gestures
Keystroke	Action
Drag the window to the top/Double-click the top of the window	Maximizes the selected window
Drag the window left/right	Snaps the selected window to fill half of the screen
Shake the window left/right	Minimize everything but the selected window
Double-Click top window border (edge)	Maximize the window vertically
Move the mouse to the bottom right-most corner of the taskbar	All windows become transparent, revealing the desktop
Click the small button on the far right of the taskbar	All windows are minimized, and the desktop is shown
Taskbar shortcuts (Mouse)
Keystroke	Action
SHIFT+Click on a taskbar button	Open a program or quickly open another instance of a program
CTRL+SHIFT+Click on a taskbar button	Open a program as an administrator
SHIFT+Right-click on a taskbar button	Show the window menu for the program
SHIFT+Right-click on a grouped taskbar button	Show the window menu for the group
CTRL+Click on a grouped taskbar button	Cycle through the windows of the group
Other Useful Shortcuts
Keystroke	Action
CTRL+SHIFT+N:	Creates a new folder in Windows Explorer
ALT+Up	Goes up a folder level in Windows Explorer
ALT+P	Toggles the preview pane in Windows Explorer
SHIFT+Right-Click on a folder	Adds Command Prompt Here, allowing the user to open a command prompt in that folder easily
Win+P	Adjust presentation settings for the display
Win+(+/-)	Zoom in/out with the mag'ifi'r
Win+E	Open a new Windows Explorer window
Win+R	Open the Run prompt
Mac Keyboard Translations for Windows
Mac Key	Windows Equivalent
COMMAND	CTRL
CONTROL	CTRL
OPTION	ALT

Additional shortcuts can be found at Microsoft's Keyboard shortcuts in Windows page.

" KB0014124 Windows Windows - Consultant Manual - Windows 10 "
The Windows 10 Operating System

Windows 10 is an operating system developed by Microsoft for business and home users. It provides a graphical user interface (GUI) that makes it easy to navigate and perform day-to-day tasks.

In our lab, many Macintosh computers can boot either macOS or Windows 10 using Boot Camp. PCs will only boot into Windows 10. For basic Windows 10 operations, please review the tutorials available in LinkedIn Learning's Windows 10 literacy course.

LinkedIn Learning content is accessible free to Rutgers University faculty, staff, and students via https://linkedin.rutgers.edu/.

Desktop

The desktop is the main screen that appears when the computer is turned on and logged into Windows 10. Folders and icons on the desktop serve as links to programs, files, web browsers, etc.

Taskbar

The Taskbar is located at the bottom of the desktop screen and displays currently running programs. It allows patrons to switch easily between various running applications. New features that set Windows 10 apart from its predecessors include the ability to search for programs and files using the integrated search bar, as well as greater customization and usability.

The icons on the far right of the Taskbar together make up the System Tray, which displays programs that are generally running in the background or minimized (not displayed). For example, these may include instant messaging, antivirus programs, network connectivity status, and notifications.

Start Menu

The new Windows 10 Start Menu includes a wider array of Microsoft Live Tiles. Some useful features include the Mail client, the Cortana assistant, and easy access to commonly used programs and files. The integrated search bar now searches the internet and local files. All programs are listed in the Start Menu's center list. Keep in mind that the Control Panel and the Devices and Printers tab are no longer listed on the Start Menu and must be searched for, or accessed by right-clicking the Start Menu. The power options can also be accessed from the Start Menu.

Note: You can see who is logged into the computer by clicking the three bars in the top left corner and looking at the name above Settings in the bottom left, or by clicking the profile image on the left side of the Start Menu. This is very useful when troubleshooting why a user's print job is not showing up in the queue.

Pin To

Programs can be pinned to the Start Menu, where they appear as a permanent square panel, or to the Taskbar, where they remain as permanent icons at the bottom of the screen.

To pin any desired program to either the Start Menu or the Taskbar, search for it, and under its search result, you should see the Pin to Start and Pin to Taskbar options.

To pin an open program to the Taskbar: Right-click the icon > Pin to Taskbar.





Show Desktop (Aero Peek)

Another useful feature in the Taskbar is the Show desktop option, which can be used by clicking the thin rectangular box on the far-right side of the Taskbar. This quickly minimizes all open windows and displays the desktop.

Alternatively, press Windows key + D to minimize all open windows and quickly access the desktop.

Opening Applications

Open the Start Menu to see a list of all the computer's programs. Scroll to find the program or program folder you wish to open, then click it. Alternatively, typing the application's name in the bottom Start Menu search bar provides quicker access.

Alt+Tab Shortcut

The Alt + Tab shortcut lets you navigate through open windows without using the mouse. Pressing this key combination cycles through each window and displays a preview. Releasing the Alt key while highlighting a window brings it to the front and makes it the active window.

This shortcut is handy for quick, easy navigation among open windows and programs. It is especially handy when many windows are open and immediate access to a particular one is needed.

Alternatively, use Windows key + Tab to open Task View, which displays all running apps and windows in a list. Unlike the previous Alt + Tab function, Task View will remain open even after releasing the keys.

Accessing Software and Files with Run

To access Run, type "Run" into the Start Menu's search bar or press Windows key + R. This box lets you enter a specific name to open programs, files, folders, and networks. For example, consultants can open Google Chrome by opening Run and typing in "Chrome".

Using the Command Prompt

The command prompt is a text-based interface for running commands and applications, as well as for manipulating system settings. Most tasks that can be performed via the command prompt can also be performed via the Windows graphical user interface (GUI). To access the Command Prompt, search "Command" or "cmd" in the Start Menu's search bar and open the program by clicking it or using the Enter key.

File Explorer

The File Explorer is a shell, much like the Command Prompt. It is a file manager used to access and manipulate files stored on the local drive, removable media, and some remote file servers. On the left-hand side of the window is the task pane, which displays a list of common actions and destinations relevant to the current directory or selected file(s).

Locking Desktop

As an OIT employee, protecting patron information is crucial. Windows 10 allows you to lock your computer, requiring a password to regain access. This feature should be used whenever you step away to keep data secure. To lock your desktop, you can use one of two methods:

Click your user icon on the Start Menu and select Lock
Pressing the Windows key + L.
Logging Off

Due to administrative settings, the lab computers cannot be logged off via the power button on the Start Menu to avoid accidental device shutdowns during update periods. To log off a machine, one can take three approaches:

One can log off by utilizing the Logoff shortcut found in the Start Menu.
By clicking your user icon on the Start Menu and selecting Sign out.
Pressing Ctrl + Alt + Delete at the same time and selecting Sign out.
Deep Freeze

A user's files will remain in their user folder until the computers revert to their default image before the lab opens the next day. This process will result in the files being wiped from the user folder. If a user forgets to retrieve their files from a lab computer, remind them they have until the lab closes to do so. This is NOT a 24-hour cycle; all files are routinely removed in the morning.

KeyAccess

KeyAccess is the client software that enables access to the Key Server and runs the Key Server software. A Key Server is a system for managing our limited number of licenses (also called "keys") for each application, such as Microsoft Office or Photoshop. A client such as KeyAccess will notify the server when a program requiring a key is opened, and the server will provide a valid key so long as the key is in use. For example, if the Labs have only 5 keys for an application, only 5 machines on the network can access that program at a time. Occasionally, all keys may be used, and an error message will pop up stating, "All licenses for the program are checked out." In this situation, a patron must wait until a key becomes available. In addition, if a patron leaves a program idle for a certain period of time, KeyAccess will send a message asking the patron g to close the program for others' use.

Rarely, the display message "Searching for shadows" will appear. This occurs when the KeyAccess program cannot connect to the Key Server. A "shadow" is a local server that handles the Key Server duties while the connection with the main Key Server is being established. If this is occurring, it is important to notify an available supervisor.

" KB0014128 Windows Windows - File storage/sharing "
USB Flash Drives

USB (Universal Serial Bus) flash drives are easy and convenient ways to store and transfer files, thanks to their relatively large storage capacity. When the drive is connected, the operating system recognizes it as an external hard drive. With a flash drive, files can be saved, recovered, and erased. It is important to remove flash drives safely, as physically disconnecting one while it is transferring data can result in corrupted files.

To safely disconnect a USB flash device:

Select the Safely Remove Hardware and Eject Media icon in the system tray.
Select the device (as in the screenshot below)
Select Eject Flash Drive.

This will cause a pop-up message to appear stating that the USB device can now be safely removed. Sometimes, an error message will state that the USB device cannot be safely removed because it is still in use. In this situation, verify that no programs access data on the flash drive before trying again.

SD Cards

SD (Secure Digital) Cards can be used as an easy and convenient way to store and save files. Like flash drives, SD Cards have relatively large storage space and are not limited to storing pictures in cameras. Insert the SD Card into the device's corresponding slot. Just like a flash drive, the computer will recognize the SD Card as an external drive, and you must safely disconnect it using the steps described above.

One notable downside of using SD Cards for file storage is the frequent lack of device input compatibility. USB hubs and adapters exist to increase this compatibility, but it is unlikely that most users will have one on hand.

Compressing Files

Windows 10 natively supports .zip file compression.

To compress a file, right-click on the file and select Send to > Compressed (zip folder). Additionally, files may be added to the archive by dragging them to the compressFolder.
To extract files from the zipped archive, right-click the archive and select Extract All. A wizard will open to guide the user through the extraction process step by step. Alternatively, one can view the contents of the compressed folder by double-clicking the folder s icon, then dragging the file(s) from the folder to any drive location or the desktop. Windows will then automatically decompress the file.
Box Drive

Box Drive is a cloud-based storage solution for Rutgers community members, accessible with their NetID and password. When files are uploaded to a user's Box Drive, they are made available to them from anywhere they can access Box. In addition to accessing files through the Box website, lab machines are imaged with a software copy of Box Drive, which is integrated with Windows File Explorer or the Mac Finder.

PuTTy

PuTTY is an SSH, login, and telnet terminal emulator that can be utilized to provide secure access to a UNIX shell environment.

Starting a Session:

When the software starts, a window titled PuTTY Configuration should open. This window has a configuration pane on the left, a Host Name (or IP address) field, and other options in the middle, and a pane for saving session profiles in the lower right area.

Select the PuTTY application from the Start menu.
When the program opens, select the Session list item in the upper-left corner of the window.
In the Host Name box, type apps.rutgers.edu (without the quotes), then under Username, enter your NetID and select Connect.t
Click Ok and enter your NetID username/password into the terminal
Snip & Sketch

Snip & Sketch is an easy way to take a screenshot of all or part of your PC screen, add notes, and send or save it. Navigate to the application by searching for "Snip & Sketch" in the Windows Search bar and selecting it, or use the hotkey Windows key + Shift + S. This application has replaced the previous Snipping Tool offered by Microsoft. Though the Snipping Tool remains accessible, Snip & Sketch is generally more user-friendly and the more current software.

When taking a new snip with the hotkey, a notification appears in the bottom-right corner of the screen. This is added to the clipboard and can be pasted into documents or messages, and can be saved by selecting the notification and clicking the Save icon in the top-right corner of the Snip & Sketch window that opens.

More information can be found at Microsoft's Snip & Sketch blog post.

Bluetooth Connectivity

Bluetooth is an open wireless technology for exchanging data over short distances between devices. This creates a personal area network (PAN) with a high level of security. This allows patrons to file-share between computers, use wireless Bluetooth headphones, or connect to their cell phones and other devices if desired. Data transfer is possible; however, Bluetooth data speeds are slow and not recommended for large files. Some of our lab computers support Bluetooth use. Macs and Dell computers in the labs can connect to Bluetooth, while HPs cannot. The following instructions outline the basics of connecting to Bluetooth for the lab's computers:

Bluetooth must be enabled for a device to connect. Before trying to connect any device, check whether the Bluetooth icon is present in the icon tray.

If the icon is absent, you can enable Bluetooth by navigating to Settings > Devices > Bluetooth & other devices > Bluetooth, then turning it on. If the Bluetooth subheading is not present, the machine is likely not compatible with Bluetooth.

Once the Bluetooth icon appears, you can connect a Bluetooth device by tapping the notifications icon in the bottom right of the screen, then tapping Connect.

A sidebar should pop up with options to connect to available Bluetooth devices within range of the computer. It may take a few seconds to find all the devices.
Click the device, then confirm the device you want to connect to via Bluetooth.
" KB0014129 Windows Windows - Task Manager "

The Task Manager displays the applications and processes that are currently running on the computer. The utility is very useful for managing programs when the computer is behaving unexpectedly, or an application is not responding. There are several methods to access the Task Manager, including:

CTRL+SHIFT+ESC.
Right-click on the Windows taskbar > Task Manager.
CTRL+ALT+DELETE > Task Manager.

Once opened, the Task Manager will have several tabs. The Processes tab shows a list of the computer s open applications, and can be expanded with the More details option to include usage information and background processes. It will display each program s status, including whether it recognizes that one is not responding. To close an application that is frozen or not responding, select it and choose End task.





Note: Ending a task will attempt to end all associated processes in that application s process tree. Sometimes, even End Task will not terminate a frozen program. For that, you will need to consult the Background Processes list found under the Processes tab.

The Background processes list under an expanded Processes tab lists all running processes. These include active applications, which are also listed under Apps. The user can end a targeted process associated with an application that is running, not responding, or stopped. Using this method can have unexpected consequences and should be done only as a last resort, after a user is aware that they may lose unsaved changes to open documents. To end a process, select the process from the list and select End task.

" KB0014130 Windows Windows - Temporarily Installing Programs "

Most computing needs can be met with the programs installed on the computer lab machines. However, if a required program is unavailable, users should note that only simple applications, those not requiring administrative privileges, can be easily installed. Complex installations are generally not possible.

If a program cannot be installed, users can check for a portable version. A portable executable placed on a flash drive or other external storage can bypass installation issues and retain user settings.

Note that any programs installed onto a lab machine by a user will be deleted after they log out.

" KB0018465 Zoom Zoom - Enable Webinar license on Zoom Service Account "

Enabling Zoom Webinars for Service Accounts should primarily be done by the EITC group (EI - Telepresence Collaboration assignment group in SN). In urgent cases, a FT that is also a Zoom Admin can add the Webinar by doing the following.

Log in to CAT
Go to Zoom Licenses
Select the Zoom Accounts tab
Search for the name of the Service Account
Click on the number listed under the Webinar Capacity column
Select 1000 for Webinar Capacity and click Save.

Due to the limited quantity of licenses, Webinar 1000 license usage will be reviewed quarterly. Webinar 1000 licenses will be removed to free up the license for others if:

If no webinar has occurred in the last 3 months before
And no events are scheduled for the next 3 months.

No information for the Service Account is lost if the license is removed and re-added at a later date.

Due to the limited number of Webinar licenses, the EI - Telepresence Collaboration group may limit departments to a single Webinar license, assigned to a single Service Account. However, they may sometimes have 2 or more. Guidance on multiple Webinar licenses per department should have the department's local IT deferred to contact the EI - Telepresence Collaboration assignment group in SN.

" KB0014971 Zoom Zoom - General Information "
General Information

Zoom has been launched for Rutgers University guests, faculty, staff, students, and emeriti. Information regarding Rutgers Zoom accounts can be found on the Rutgers IT Zoom Site. You may also use the Rutgers Zoom FAQ, which is user-facing. For a Rutgers user to access Zoom, they will need to activate it. Please reference the knowledge article on creating a Rutgers Zoom account.

Zoom account status in CAT

The CAT tool displays various information about a user's Zoom account, such as whether the user has activated their account and their last login date. Always reference the CAT tool before and after instructing a user to activate their account. An active Zoom will look similar to the following image:





If you are unfamiliar with the CAT tool, reference the CAT getting started documentation.

Logging into Zoom

There are several ways for a user to log in to Zoom. Users may use the Rutgers-specific Zoom page, the generic Zoom Website, or the Zoom desktop application. For assistance with logging in, please refer to the knowledge article on logging in to Rutgers Zoom.

Zoom HIPAA Compliance

Rutgers Zoom is HIPAA compliant.

Zoom Webinar

Zoom Webinar information can be found using the Rutgers IT Zoom Webinar Article.

Departments are typically limited to one Webinar 1000 license per department, and therefore coordination is needed to determine which Service Account holds between local IT and the Telepresence Collaboration group. For Requests for these licenses, the DA should have an incident opened with the EI - Telepresence Collaboration group in ServiceNow.

Meeting 1,000, Webinar 1,000, Webinar 3,000, Webinar 10,000, and Live Streaming requests should fill out the Bookings Calendar in the Rutgers IT Zoom Webinar Article.

Their Webinar/Meeting capacity or Live Stream capacity will only be increased the day before the event, and removed the day after. Webinar 1,000 can remain on a Service Account and are not subject to the 1-day limit, but are periodically checked for active usage.

A separate Events or Sessions license would need to be purchased in advance for an account to use breakout rooms in a Webinar. The Events license is not free (very expensive). The Sessions license is cheaper than the Events license. The Events license cannot be shared between accounts; it is tied to a specific account when purchased. Should go through EI - Telepresence Collaboration to obtain.
General Troubleshooting
If a user cannot see the two preloaded Rutgers backgrounds, please have them update their Zoom client.
Users must be on the same site to schedule a meeting for one another; i.e., if one user has a PHI flag and the other does not, they will not be able to schedule on behalf of one another. This is also the case for setting alternative hosts for further meetings. Both users must be in the PHI instance, or both must be in the non-PHI instance.
